        Ida and Edwin Bauer and their grandchildren

FRED AND KATIE BECK

Fred Beck was born in North Dakota July 23, 1903 to Gottlieb and Maria
(Metzger) Beck.  Katie Beck was born near Bowdle September 26, 1906 to William
and Rosina (Oster) Schick.

Fred and Katie were married November 24, 1924. They started married life near
Hosmer.  They lived in various places in Walworth and Edmunds counties.  In the
fall of 1942, they moved to a farm south of Roscoe (now the Peter Baumgartner
farm).  In October of 1946, Fred purchased the Phillip 66 service station and
bulk plant from Mr. Ladner.  Fred, Katie, and family moved into Roscoe in
December of 1946.  They ran the station until January 1, 1966 when one of their
sons, Reuben, bought the inventory and bulk truck.  Fred continued to help out
around the station.  In March 1966, he had his first heart attack while
delivering fuel to the John Thomas farm.  Fred's health continued to fail and on
August 11, 1971 he died.

Katie continued to live in their house just west of the station until October
1981, when she moved to the Senior Housing in Roscoe.  Another son, Gerold,
purchased the house and station from her.

        Back row: Reuben, Harry, Gerold, Lorraine and Ramona; front row: Katie
        and Fred Beck

Fred and Katie had five children: Harry, Reuben, Loraine, Ramona, and Gerold.

Harry married Irene Rohrbach and lived in Aberdeen. They had one child, Pam
(married to Ron Rohrbach). Harry died July 3, 1978.

Reuben: see history.

Loraine is married to Gordon Diedtrich and lives in Aberdeen.  They also have
one daughter, Debbie (Mrs. Keith Norman) and two grandchildren.

Ramona is married to Roman Guthmiller and lives in Aberdeen.  They have four
children.  Mork is a minister in Glencoe, Minnesota.  He and his wife Linda
have one child, Rachel.

Peggy (Mrs.  Dale Martens) lives north of Aberdeen. Ronald operates a gas
station and Kathy attends school in Aberdeen.

Gerold: see history.

REUBEN AND BERNICE BECK

Reuben, son of Fred and Katie Schick Beck was born on a farm near Hosmer, S.D.
May 8, 1929.  They later resided on a farm near Java, S.D. until 1942 when they
purchased a farm southwest of Roscoe.  Reuben attended Roscoe High School.  In
1946 the family moved to Roscoe, when Fred bought the Phillip 66 Station.
Reuben was employed by his father at Beck Oil until 1965 when he bought the
business.

Bernice was born to John and Martha Wiederrich Roesch December 6, 1928, on a
farm north of Roscoe. She lived there until her marriage.  She is a graduate of
Roscoe High School and Northern State College.

        Back row: Richard, Gregory and David; front row: Bernice and Reuben Beck

They were married at St. Paul's Lutheran Church June 21, 1950.  They have three
sons: Gregory, born September 29, 1951, is married to Rita Grote and has three
children, Shelley, Shown, and Timothy; Richard, born August 8, 1953, is married
to Deborah Flogtvedt.  They have one daughter, Stacy, and are expecting their
second child in September of 1982; and David, born August 21, 1955 is presently
working on his Master's Degree in Wildlife Biology at South Dakota State
University in Brookings.

Reuben discontinued the Beck Oil business in 1976 and devotes his full time to
farming and ranching on the farm which he purchased in 1958 from John Laib.

Bernice has taught in area rural schools, Loyalton, and for the post 12 years
has been teaching fifth grade at Roscoe.

They have both been involved in many local organizations through the years.
Reuben has served on the city council for several years.  Bernice is a member
of the local, state and National Education Association.

GEROLD AND PATRICIA BECK

Gerold J. Beck was born July 24, 1942 to Fred Beck and Katie (Schick) Beck on a
farm near Java.  In the fall of that year his family moved to a farm south of
Roscoe. In December, 1946, they moved into Roscoe where his father owned and
operated the Phillips 66 Station. Gerry attended school in Roscoe and graduated
in 1961.  He attended Northern State College and received his Bachelor of
Science Degree in 1965 and his Masters of Science Degree in 1968.

Patricia (Lapka) Beck was born February 14, 1945 in Aberdeen to Gerold and
Elenor (Hoeft) Lapka.  She attended St. Mary's Grade School and graduated from
Central High School in 1963.  Pat then attended Presentation College and
received a two-year business degree in 1965.

        Gerold, Billy, Bo, Pat, and Joel Beck

Gerry and Pat were married June 10, 1965.  They moved to Hosmer where Gerry was
business teacher and principal.  In 1971 they moved to Aberdeen where Gerry
taught at Presentation College.  He also worked at Farmers and Merchants Bank
and taught at Central High School and the Vocational School.  In 1978 Gerry and
Pat moved to Roscoe where Gerry is principal and business teacher of Roscoe
High School.  Pat works at the First State Bank of Roscoe.

Gerry and Pat have three sons.  Joel was born January 1, 1966 in Aberdeen.  He
will graduate from Roscoe High in 1984.  Bo was born January 5, 1967 in
Aberdeen. He will graduate from Roscoe in 1985.  Billy was born April 16, 1969
in Aberdeen and will be a freshman at Roscoe High in the fall of 1983.

JOHN AND ANNA BECK

John Beck was born February 12, 1901 at Mound City, S.D. to Gottlieb and Maria
Metzgar Beck.  He married Anna Laib February 3, 1927 at Bowdle, S.D. She was the
daughter of Jacob and Maria Hoffman Laib who were both born in Russia and came
to the United States in 1899.  Anna was born March 18, 1908 at Bowdle.

John and Anna farmed in the Bowdle and Java area until 1937 when they moved to
a farm near Roscoe (the present James Weisser farm).  From there they moved to
a farm five and one-half miles northeast of Roscoe.  In 1944 they bought a farm
three and one-half miles north of Ipswich.  Mr. Beck retired in 1974.  They
continued to live on this farm until his death September 3, 1977.  Mrs. Beck at
that time moved to Ipswich where she is presently residing.

CHRIST AND AUGUSTA BEYERS

        Back row: Adolph, Albert, and Hedwig; Front row: Christ, William, and
        Augusta Beyers

Christian Beyers was born in Lechevo, Wastpreizin, Germany December 18, 1853.
He was married to Augusta Heiman February 5, 1882.

In May 1883 he came to the United States, living in Minnesota a short time
until his wife joined him and together they came to Dakota territory.  They
filed a homestead in Edmunds County, Montpelier Township, where they resided
until their deaths, Mr. Beyers December 2, 1943 and Mrs. Beyers July 17, 1937.
They are buried in Morningside Cemetery.

They had four children, Hedwig (Mrs. Adolf Jeschke), Albert, Adolph and William.

ADOLPH AND ELIZABETH BEYERS

        Back row: Aaron, Oscar Isaak; middle row: Sheelah, Emma, Mercedes, and
        Sharon; front row: Adolph and Elizabeth Beyers

Adolph Beyers was born on September 7, 1892 in Montpelier Township, southeast
of Roscoe.  He was the son of Mr. and Mrs. Christ Beyers who migrated to the
United States and the Roscoe area from Germany in May 1883.  He married
Elizabeth McNaughton from Staples, Minn.  May 17, 1917.  They settled on the
Beyers family farm where they lived all their lives.

They had three children: Aaron, Mercedes and Dale, who died in infancy.

The Beyers were members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church.  Mrs. Beyers passed away
on January 20, 1968 and Mr. Beyers passed away on April 9, 1968.  They are both
buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

AARON AND EMMA BEYERS

Aaron Beyers was born near Roscoe, the son of Adolph and Elizabeth McNaughton
Beyers.  He married Emma Dabbert on January 17, 1942.  She was the daughter of
Christ and Margaret Geist Dabbert, also of Roscoe.

They have two daughters, Sheelah, an R. N. who married Robert Prevost and lives
in Lambert, Mont. and Sharon a beautician, married to Richard Beitelspacher and
lives near Bowdle, S.D. The Beyers have ten grandchildren.

Aaron and Emma sold the family farm in October 1981 and built a new home in
Roscoe where they are now living.  This ended a continuous Beyers farm operation
on the original homestead.  Christ Beyers settled on the land in 1884 and was
joined by his son Adolph in 1917, who later was joined by his son, Aaron, in
1942.

The Beyers are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe where Emma is a
member of the Ladies Aid. Aaron served several years as Treasurer of Montpelier
Township.

ALBERT AND MARIE BEYERS

Albert Beyers, the son of Christ and Augusta Beyers, was born on a farm in
Montpelier Township, Edmunds County, Dakota Territory, October 10, 1887.  He
attended a country school for several months in the fall and again in the
spring.  School was not held during the winter months.

Albert acquired a tractor and plow and broke up the native sod for the
neighbors so that they could raise grain.  He and his brother, Adolph, did
threshing in the neighborhood for many years until the combines took over.

April 9, 1913 Albert Beyers and Marie Schurr, daughter of Martin and Anna
Schurr, were united in marriage.  Marie was born in Chicago, Ill.  June 27, 1894
and came to the Beebe area with her parents in 1906. They raised four children,
Helen (Mrs.  Perry Downer), Ernest, Raymond and Neva (Mrs.  Helmuth
Kirschenmann). The children established homes in the  area.

Their first car was a Model T Ford touring car.  They did not have anti-freeze
to put in the radiator in the winter so hot water was put into the radiator to
help warm the motor so it would start and also to help to keep it from freezing
before the motor was warm enough to keep the water from freezing.

Mr. Beyers served on the Montpelier Township board and the school board for
many years.

Mrs. Beyers was a member of the BBB Extension Club and held the various
offices.  She was a member of the Ladies Aid of St. Paul's Lutheran Church.
Her hobby was crocheting and making pieced baby quilts.

Albert Beyers died June 11, 1972.  Mrs. Beyers died April 18, 1980.  They are
both buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe, S.D.

ERNEST W. AND ANNA BEYERS

Ernest Beyers was born December 4, 1915 to Albert and Marie (Schurr) Beyers, on
a farm in Montpelier Township.  He attended a country school for his grade
school education, and went to high school at Brookings, taking the agricultural
course.

He served in the United States Army during World War 11 including 18 months in
the European Theater of Operations.  After his discharge from the Army, he
married Anna Mischke, of Hazel, S.D. They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran
Church where Ernest has served on the church council as chairman and also
elder.  He has also served on the Montpelier Township board of supervisors for
many years.

The couple have three children, Russell, who married Arvis Naasz of Aberdeen;
Melvin, who married Janice Harms of Willow Lake, and Eileen, who married Tom
Schultz of Spearfish.

Russell and Melvin both farm in the Roscoe community, while Eileen lives in
Worthington, Minn. and Tom teaches school in nearby Round Lake, Minn.  With the
exception of four years of military service, Ernest has lived his entire life
on his home farm in Montpelier Township.

MELVIN AND JANICE BEYERS

Melvin Beyers was born April 27, 1951 in Ipswich, S.D. He is the third and
youngest child of Ernest and Anna Beyers who live eight miles southeast of
Roscoe.  A few months and several miles in between, his future wife, Janice
Harms, was born December 22, 1951, in Estelline, S.D. Her parents are Tebbo and
Vesta Harms from Willow Lake, S.D.

Melvin and Janice were married May 28, 1972 in Willow Lake and lived in Rapid
City, S.D. about one year where Mel was employed as a carpenter.  In the spring
of 1973, they moved to the Pete and Josephine Kotschevar farm, five miles south
and one mile east of Roscoe.  Since that time they have been farming.  Melvin
is also on the Cenex Board of Directors, Montpelier Township Board and the
church council of St. Paul's Lutheran Church at Roscoe.

November 3, 1974 their first daughter, Tracey, was born and April 3, 1978 is
when Heather, their second daughter, was born.

RAYMOND AND RUTH BEYERS

        Back row: John, Robert and Mark; front row: Patricia, Raymond and Ruth
        Beyers

Raymond Beyers was born December 26, 1921 to Albert and Marie Schurr Beyers at
rural Roscoe.

Ruth Beyers was born September 16, 1921 to John and Martha Wiederich Roesch
also of rural Roscoe.

Raymond graduated from Brookings Ag. school in 1940 and Ruth graduated from
Roscoe High School the same year.  They were married in 1944 at the Plymouth
Congregational Church in Aberdeen and have been farming south of Beebe (no
longer a town).  They ore members of St. Paul's Evangelical Lutheran Church.

Their children are John, married to Patty Ulmer, Robert, married to Margurette
(Peg) Meier, Mark, married to Carlo Meier and Patricia, married to Floyd Weisz.
Raymond and Ruth have been blessed with 12 grand- children.

JOHN R. AND PATTY BEYERS

John Raymond Beyers was born October 26, 1945 in Aberdeen, S.D. to Raymond and
Ruth Roesch Beyers. They resided on a farm southeast of Roscoe at the time.
When he was very young they moved to the farm two miles south of Beebe where
they still reside.  John is a graduate of Roscoe High School and attended the
National School of Business in Rapid City for one year. He began working at
First State Bank of Roscoe in 1964 where he is still employed.

        Back row: Allen, Patty, John and Mary; middle row: Evan and Brian;
        Front row: Emily and Dennis Beyers

John married Patty Ulmer July 25, 1965 in Hosmer, S.D. Patty is the daughter of
Rupert and Orma Gross Ulmer who reside eight miles north of Roscoe.  Patty was
born July 4, 1945 at the farm home.  She is a graduate of Hosmer High School
and attended Northern College in Aberdeen for two years.

John and Patty lived in Roscoe until 1970 where she taught elementary school
for two years and where John, along with his job at the bank, did some farming
with his dad. in the spring of 1970, they purchased the farm two and one-half
miles northeast of Loyalton where Bert and Freida Ham had lived.  They moved
there with their growing family in October of 1970. They have been fortunate to
have Bert Ham working as their main field hand on their grain farm.  Three of
the last five years have been less than favorable in the farming venture.  In
1976 drought caused complete crop failure.  In July of 1980 their crops were
mostly destroyed by hail and in 1981 high winds and heavy grasshopper
infestation took their toll.

John and Patty have been blessed with six healthy children: Mary Ellen, born
August 9, 1967; Allen John, born September 28, 1968; Emily Ruth, born May 30,
1970; Dennis Albert, born November 29, 1972; Evan Raymond, born December 12,
1976; and Brian Rupert, born April 15, 1980.

The family attends St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe where John has served
as an usher and a trustee and Patty is presently serving as a Saturday School
teacher.

MARK AND CARLA BEYERS

Mark Beyers was born November 2, 1949 at Aberdeen, S.D. to Raymond and Ruth
Roesch Beyers.

Carla Meier Beyers was born July 21, 1950 at Aberdeen, S.D. to Henry and Clara
Geier Meier.

Carla and Mark were married February 15, 1969, while both were attending NSC.
They started farming in the fall of 1970.  They reside on a farm seven miles
east and one mile south of Roscoe.

The Beyers are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church where Mark is presently
treasurer of the church council.

Their children are: Denise Marie, born August 5, 1969; Dionne Marie, born
February 17, 1971; David Mark, born April 13, 1972; Danielle Marie, born
December 5, 1973 and Douglas Mark, born December 29, 1981.  All the children
are students at Roscoe School except Douglas, who is a pre-schooler.

LEROY AND MARY BIEBER

LeRoy K. Bieber was born August 11, 1922 at Roscoe to Solomon and Ida Holmberg
Bieber.  Mary Alice Buechler as born February 2, 1931 at Hague, N.D., to
Valentine and Elizabeth Hilt Buechler.  In 1908, her mother (Elizabeth) came
from Russia and settled at Hague which was Valentines' birthplace.

Roy and Mary were married at Roscoe October 12, 1956.  Roy has been an owner of
Roscoe Auto Co. for 33 years, until his retirement in October 1982.  He served
in the United States Navy for three years during WWII. He is a member of the
Roscoe American Legion where he has served as commander.  Mary worked for
Scherf's Super Valu for eight years and as secretary for the Selective Service
System in Edmunds County for 21 years.

They have three children, Kenneth, living in Sioux Falls, S.D.; Cathy, married
to Robert Stricker, has three children and lives in Watertown, S.D. Cathy does
part- time work in the Watertown High School and is a Mary Kay dealer.  Pamela
is married to Michael Morgan and lives at Eagan, Minn.  They have three
children.  Pam is a nurse and works at Children's Hospital in St. Paul, Minn.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.  In November 1982,
Roy and Mary moved to Watertown, S.D.

RAYMOND AND MARILYN BIEBER

Raymond V. Bieber, son of Solomon Bieber and Ida R. Holmberg was born December
12, 1924 at Roscoe, S.D. He was raised on a farm south of Roscoe and attended
the Roscoe Public School until he graduated in 1944. Later he started a service
station business, known as "Biebers Service", with his brother LeRoy.  He
entered the Korean War in November 1950 and was discharged in August 1951.
Upon return from service, he continued in a partnership business, known as the
Roscoe Auto Company, with his brother LeRoy and Martin Scherf.

He married Marilyn Kaven June 9, 1956, at Zion Lutheran Church, Aberdeen, S.D.
She was born August 15, 1935 at Leola, S.D. to Idris Pete Kaven and Alma Anna
Hering.  She lived on a farm in the Wetonka area in her childhood days and
later moved to a farm northwest of Aberdeen, S.D. She attended rural schools
and graduated from Westport High School in 1953.  She was employed at Socony
Vacuum Oil Co. in Aberdeen from 1953 until June 1956, at which time she was
married and moved to the Roscoe area.  She has since been employed at the
Roscoe Auto Co. as bookkeeper.

July 25, 1961 a little girl, Shelly Dawn, was born to Roy and Marilyn.  She has
lived in Roscoe her entire life and graduated from Roscoe High School in May,
1979.  She is presently attending Northern State College in Aberdeen, S.D. and
plans to graduate in May of 1983.

Another daughter, Lora Lee, was born August 28, 1966.  She is presently
attending the Roscoe Public School.  She will graduate from high school in 1984
and plans to attend college after graduation. The Bieber family are members of
St. Paul's Lutheran Church at Roscoe, S.D. and plan to live in Roscoe until
retirement.

PAUL AND CHRISTINA BINFET

        Paul P. Binfet

Paul Binfet was born February 23, 1920 to Peter and Elizabeth Binfet on a farm
near Onaka, S.D. He grew up and went to school in Hillside Township where he
farmed with his dad.

Christina Uhrich was born September 13, 1922 to Stanis and Clara Glatt Uhrich
on a farm near Roscoe, S.D.

Paul and Christina were married November 16, 1943 at St. Thomas Church in
Roscoe.  They resided on a farm 10 miles north of Onaka until they retired in
1979 and moved into a new home in Roscoe.

Through the years they participated in church and community activities.  They
were members of St. John's Catholic Church in Onaka where Christina is a member
of the Christian Mothers and also taught CCD religion class for 10 years.  Paul
was a church usher.  Christina is a member of St. Thomas Catholic Church and a
member of the Altar Society.  Paul served on the township board in Hillside
Township for many years.

One son was born to this union.  Paul was born February 25, 1964 in Bowdle,
S.D. Paul is a 1982 graduate of Roscoe High School.  He is enrolled at the Lake
Area Vocational Technical Institute at Watertown in Automotive Maintenance.

Paul, Sr. died November 29, 1980 and is buried in the church cemetery in Onaka,
S.D.

JOHN AND ELIZA BISHOP

The Bishops lived on the farm on the west side of the road about two miles
north of Roscoe.  The Roscoe school and the three schools in Glen Township were
under the some school board.  Mr. Bishop for many years was a member of the
school board.  The new school house that was finished in 1919 was built under
his wise guidance as president of the board.  Eliza Bishop was the daughter of
the George Chappells.

John Bishop was born in 1872 and died in 1956.  Eliza Chappell Bishop was born
in 1878 and died in 1948. They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.

LAVERN AND HELEN BOBBY

        Roger, Duane, Shirley, Albert, Helen, LaVern, Bill and Dale Bobby

LaVern Bobby was born at Sisseton, S.D. May 19, 1923, to Emil and Emelia
Schwislenberg Bobby.  They moved to the vicinity of Beebe, S.D. until he
enlisted in the Navy November 15, 1942.  He took his training at San Diego,
Calif. and served his sea duty to Hawaii, New Hebrides, New Zealand, Australia
and various other places.  He was discharged January 23, 1948.

He married Helen Oberlander, daughter of Paul and Sophia Kallenberger
Oberlander, June 2, 1946 at Hosmer, S.D. They farmed in the Beebe and Ipswich
area for nine and one-half years and September 15, 1955, they moved to Roscoe
where they bought the "Community Bar" from Harold Ellwein and Willard Kukrall.
They operated it at that location until they had the old Milwaukee Bar
remodeled and they moved to that location January 8, 1956.  In July of 1964
they sold it to Mr. and Mrs. Al Zacher.

In October 1964, they moved to Aberdeen where they bought and operated
"Luckies" on South Main.  After three years in this business they sold it and
moved to Edgeley, N.D. where LaVern was section foreman for the Milwaukee
Railroad.  In August 1971, they moved to Bowdle, S.D. taking over the section
foreman job there. In May 1973, they moved back to Roscoe as foreman of the
Roscoe and Ipswich rails.  Helen worked at the City Liquor Store and at
Donnie's for three years.  In May 1982, they moved to Aberdeen where LaVern
took a truck driver job for Burlington Northern Railroad.

They have six children, Roger, married to Tillie Buechler, has one son Zach and
lives at Grosse Pointe Woods, Mich.

Duane, married to Linda Nelson, has one son, Joel, and lives in Aberdeen.

Albert is married to Lynn Carpenter, has two children, Paul and Kristie, and
lives in Hoffman Estates, Ill.

Bill, married to Margaret Gross, has three sons, Matt, Sam and Jacob, and lives
in Roscoe.

Dale works on the steel gang.  All the boys are employed by the railroad.

Shirley is married to Jim Nissen, and they live in Aberdeen where she works at
3M.

LaVerne Bobby passed away December 29, 1982 and is buried in Bethany Cemetery
in Hosmer.

BILL AND MARGARET BOBBY

William E. Bobby was born September 5, 1953 at Ipswich, S.D. to LaVern and
Helen Oberlander Bobby.

Margaret Gross was born March 15, 1953 at Eureka, S.D. to Erv and Ann Thommen
Gross.  They were married May 25, 1974 in Bowdle, S.D. They came to Roscoe
first in 1977 and again in 1980.  They had previously lived in Montana, Idaho,
and Washington working with railroad tie gangs.  Later they moved to Bath,
S.D., Hague, N.D., Roscoe, Aberdeen, Wisconsin, Indiana and finally Roscoe
again.

During all their moving, railroading with the Milwaukee Road was their
occupation until just recently.  Bill now works for the Burlington Northern.

They have three sons, Mathew, Samuel and Jacob.

JACOB BORKIRCHERT (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Jacob Borkirchert was born in Menno, S.D. in 1870.  He moved to Roscoe in 1887
where he worked on ranches north of town.  In 1890 he was united in marriage to
Magdalina Burrer who was born in South Russia in 1880.  Her family moved to a
farm near Eureka in 1882. In 1912 the Jacob Borkirchert family moved to the farm
that had been homesteaded by John Hepperle.  Their children were Otto, Helen,
Bertha, Emil, Mary and Adelia.  Otto took over the farm when Jacob died in 1934.

EMIL AND IRENE BORKIRCHERT

        Irene, La Villa Pearl and Emil Borkirchert

        Back row: LaVilla and Steve; front row: Michael, Chrystal and Erica
        Martens

Emil Borkirchert was born near Roscoe, S.D. October 14, 1912 to Jacob and
Magdelina (nee Burrer) Borkirchert.  He attended school in the Roscoe
Independent District.  He was confirmed at the Reformed Church in Hosmer.

Mrs. Emil Borkirchert (Irene Voegele) was born October 1, 1910 to Christian and
Christiana (nee Ladner) Voegele east of Hosmer.  She was baptized into the
Lutheran faith at the rural Lutheran church east of Hosmer, S.D.

When she was five years old, the family moved to Eureka, S.D. (where her mother
had been born to John and Margarueta Ladner (nee Schaffer).  The Ladners had
come from southern Russia and homesteaded two miles north of Eureka, S.D.

Irene started public school at Eureka, and also attended Lutheran Sunday School
for three years.  Irene was confirmed at St. Paul's Lutheran Church at Roscoe,
S.D. by Pastor Scherf.

Irene attended the rural school until eighth grade graduation.  She attended
high school in Roscoe for two years and attended Ipswich High School where she
graduated with the class of '30.  She graduated from Northern State College
with a major in Elementary Education and minors in Music and Political Science.
She joined the teaching field as a life career.

June 1, 1941 Irene Voegele and Emil Borkirchert were united in holy wedlock at
St. Paul's Lutheran Church at Roscoe, S.D. by Pastor Lau.  They farmed
northeast of Roscoe until January 14, 1971, when Emil passed away at the age of
58 years.

In April 1942 Emil Borkichert was drafted into the military service.  He served
his country for three and one half years.  Thirty-two months of this time he
spent in New Guinea.  He was attached to the Army Medical Department, 362nd
General Hospital.  He was discharged on October 13, 1945, at Camp McCoy, Wis.
He returned home October 14, 1945.  He had never had a furlough from the time
he was inducted April 12, 1942.  Irene had spent all the time that Emil was in
the service at their farm northeast of Roscoe.  Emil continued to farm, and was
still farming on the same farm at the time of his death.

Emil and Irene have one daughter, Mrs. Steve (LaVilla Pearl) Martens, of
Osmond, Neb.  LaVilla Pearl graduated from the Roscoe High School and the two
year accountant course of the National School of Commerce at Rapid City, S.D.

Irene resigned from the Roscoe Public School May 1975, after having taught the
sixth grade for 12 years. She had taught 33 years, 28 years of teaching was all
in the Roscoe Independent District.  She was given special recognition by the
Roscoe Board of Education, Roscoe School employees and the student body.

Irene moved to Roscoe after Emil had passed away, July 1971.  She has been a
member of St. Paul's Lutheran Church since 1922.  She has three grand-
children, Michael, Chrystal and Erica Martens.

Emil Borkirchert was preceded in death by his parents, Mr. and Mrs. Jacob
Borkirchert, one brother Otto Borkirchert.  One sister Helen Cunningham has died
since Emil died.  Three sisters survive: Mrs. Albert Wiepking (Mary) of Bowdle;
Mrs. Elmer Docter (Adelia) of rural Eureka; and Mrs. Bertha Kaul of Rapid City,
S.D.

June 16, 1977 Irene Borkirchert and John Nehlich from rural Ipswich were united
in holy wedlock.  They continue to live in Roscoe, S.D. John has three boys:
Lester of Roscoe; Art of rural Ipswich; Curtis of Oakes, N.D. and one daughter,
Mrs. Jim McIntosh (Jaci).  John also has 12 grandchildren and one
great-grandchild.

Irene is a member of the Lutheran Ladies Aid, Roscoe Senior Citizens, American
Legion Auxiliary, Roscoe Commercial Club, American Association of University
Women, The Retired Teacher's Association and a post Sunday School teacher and
member of the Lutheran Choir.  She is a member of St. Paul's Lutheran Church at
Roscoe, S.D.

EMIL AND EMMA BRANDNER

Emil Brandner was born at Zeeland, N.D., the son of Daniel and Katherine
Wittmeier Brandner.  He grew up there and went to country school.  As a young
man he married Emma Hieb at Ashley, N.D. She was the daughter of Wilhelm and
Christina Hieb, born at Hague, N.D., where she grew up and attended the country
school near her home.  They farmed near Hague for some time until 1928, when
they moved to a farm eight miles northeast of Hosmer.  In about 1944, they moved
on a farm two miles south of Roscoe where they lived until 1967 when they
retired and made their home in Roscoe.

They had seven children, Irene, Francis, Virginia, Doris, James (deceased in
1969, buried in Onaka, S.D.), Judy and Nancy, who died in infancy.

Emil passed away July 27, 1977 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

A. JAMES AND REGINA BREITLING

        Back row: Joel, Kim, Brenda, Rick, Jackie, Rodney and Cheri (Gillick);
        front row: Gina, holding Charity, Bryon, James, and Kristi Breitling
        (Kneeling)

A. James Breitling was born December 7, 1934 to Arthur and Relina Haas
Breitling of Venturia, N.D. James attended grade school in rural Venturia and
attended high school and graduated from Ashley, N.D. He also attended college
at Ellendale, N.D.

Regina E. Gross was born August 24, 1936 to John A. and Barbara Stahlecker
Gross of Bowdle, S.D. Regina attended and graduated from Bowdle High School.
She attended college at Northern in Aberdeen, S.D. Both James and Regina taught
elementary school for three years.

James and Regina were married August 26, 1956 in Bowdle, S.D. Farming was their
main occupation at different locations, three years in Venturia and ten years
in Bowdle, after which they moved to Pierre, S.D. where Regina received her
nurses training.  They then relocated at Roscoe where they are presently
residing. Regina is now a nurse at the Ipswich Community Hospital.  James

Six children were born to the Breitlings, they are as follows: Jacqueline

is active in various organizations and is organist of the church.  James is

JAMES AND HARRIET BRENTON FAMILY

James Brenton married Harriet Frazier of Anoka, Minn.  In 1910 they came with
their little daughter, Ethel, to live north of Roscoe on the farm just west of
the Johnston homestead where the Robert Brentons and their three other sons
lived.  A few years later the James Brentons moved to Grand Marais, Minn. but
they returned to Roscoe and were living on the Decker farm when Mr. Brenton
became carrier on rural route three about 1924.

Soon after that Ethel became the telephone operator. At that time telephones
could be used only when the operator was in the office, from nine in the morning
until nine in the evening.  The office was closed a half hour at noon and from
six to seven for dinner.  Florence or Ruby Borsborry had been the operator
before Ethel. About 1930 the off ice was moved to the little house two blocks
west of the auditorium and there was continual service.

Four years later Ethel was transferred to the Ipswich office and Rose
Schumacher Wiedenmeyer become the operator.

Mr. Brenton transferred to Norbeck, and when he retired the family moved to
Springdale, Ark., where Reed had a poultry farm.  Eventually both Ethel and
Hazel, who had also become an operator, located in that area.  Mr. and Mrs.
Brenton, Ethel and Reed are all deceased, as is Hazel's husband, Vernon
Homesley. Hazel and her married daughter and family live in Rogers, Ark.

ROBERT AND LUCINDA BRENTON FAMILY

        Back row: Ethel, James, Ira, Samuel, and Frank Brenton, Jessie Brenton
        Decker, and Evelyn Decker; front row: Reed, Harriet, Hazel, Robert and
        Lucinda Brenton, Laurence, Ernest, Charles and Raymond Decker

In 1897 Robert and Lucinda Brenton, their four sons Frank, James, Samuel and
Ira and one daughter, Jessie, came by covered wagon from Wolcott, Ind. to the
Walnut Grove, Minn. area.  In 1908 Jessie married Charles Decker and had gone
to live on the Decker homestead near Roscoe, S.D. Two years later the parents
and the three single sons, Frank, Samuel and Ira, moved to the Wm.  Johnston
farm just north of the Decker homestead.  Ira had come in the fall and he was
teaching the school west of Roscoe.  James had married and was living in
northern Minnesota.

The Brentons were prosperous and respected farmers. In 1919 they built the
garage just south of where Donnie's Cafe is now located.  The parents and Frank
remained on the farm.  The garage was a large one with the newest equipment
including a light plant.  At this time electricity for the city was furnished
by direct current.  Jake Wold operated the light plant and the electric power
could be used only from seven in the morning to eleven at night.  Brenton
Brothers had the agency for the Elgin automobile.  Charles Decker was the
mechanic for a couple of years.  In 1923 the garage burned down.

For many years Frank directed the City Band.  He taught many of the young
people a great deal about music.  Among the music groups he organized and
directed was a male quartet in 1926.  The members of this were James Brenton,
Sam Fountain (Mrs.  George Wilson's brother) the Reverend Cecil Allin (minister
of the Presbyterian Church) and Frank.

In 1934 Ira married Elsie Klicko.  The Klicko family had come from Illinois in
1916 to live on the farm just north of the Brentons.  Ira lived the longest of
any of the Brentons.  He died in Everett, Wash. a week before his hundredth
birthday.  His family all live on the west coast.  Elsie still lives in Everett.

PHILLIP AND OLGA BUECHLER

Phillip Buechler was born November 5, 1905, northwest of Bowdle, S.D., the son
of Fred and Elizabeth Stotz Buechler.  He grew up in the Bowdle area.

Olga Winkler was born on January 27, 1908, near Roscoe, to Karl and Magdalena
Dobler Winkler.  She grew up here and went to the rural school, living with her
parents until her marriage on December 8, 1929 at the Lutheran Church in
Bowdle.  They settled on a farm southwest of Roscoe and lived there until 1942
when Phillip passed away.  He is buried in the cemetery one mile south of
Bowdle.  Olga moved to Roscoe in 1943 where she lived with her mother for a
year, until she bought her own home.

They had five sons: Melvin died at age two years and nine months; twin sons
died in infancy; Verlin, married to Virginia Amons, has two children, Phillip
and Amy, lives in Wiboux, Mont. where he is administrator in the nursing home.
Arvin, married to Linda Moffitt, has two children, Terry and Gary, lives in
Rancho LaCosta, Calif. where he is a CPA.

Olga is a member of St. Paul's Lutheran Church where she is a member of the
Ladies Aid and also a member of the Roscoe Senior Citizens.  She is a resident
in the Senior Housing Unit in Roscoe.

VALENTINE AND ELIZABETH BUECHLER

his retirement.

died in May 1982.

his daughter, Betty and her family.

PHIL AND ZELDIA BUKASKE

Phil, son of Simon and Mary (Ignatowski) Bukaske was born in 1897 at Buffalo,
N.Y. He came to South Dakota in 1911, to the Loyalton area.

Zeldia, daughter of Thomas I. and Lula (Barton) Oban was born in 1903, at
Loyalton, S.D.

Phil and Zeldia were married in 1920 and farmed in the Loyalton area until
Phil's death in 1956.  They became members of the Loyalton Wesleyan Church in
1950, and Zeldia now attends the Ipswich Wesleyan Church.  Zeldia has worked as
a cook at different places, and retired in 1966, and now resides on the
Anderson farm near Loyalton.

To this union four children were born.

Junior, of New Orleans, La., married Letha Stann from New Orleans.  They live
there where Junior is a boilermaker by trade.  They farmed in the Loyalton area
10 years before moving to New Orleans.  They have three children.

Stella, of Watertown, S.D., married Floyd Hilden of Hager City, Wis.  They have
two daughters.  They farmed near Loyalton until 1980, and are now retired at
Watertown, S.D.

Bonnie, of Loyalton, S.D. married Harold Anderson of Hager City, Wis.  They
have four children, and farm near Loyalton.

Darrell of Loyalton married Velda Kleffman of Faulkton, S.D. They have seven
children and are farming near Loyalton on the home place where he was born.

DARRELL AND VELDA BUKASKE

Darrell Leroy Bukaske was born May 12, 1935 at the farm home where he now
lives.  He is the son of Phil and Zeldia Oban Bukaske.  Phil was born December

Darrell and Velda were married on June 21, 1956 at Ipswich, S.D. They made

OLIVE (WILKERSON) BULLIS (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

The C. H. Wilkerson family came to Roscoe in a covered wagon from Boyd County,
Neb. in 1904 where Mr. Wilkerson made an unsuccessful attempt at the dray
business and later became a railroad employee. They left Roscoe in about 1906
and returned in 1909, when Mr. Wilkerson went to work as a stable man at the
Jamieson Hotel.  In spring of 1912, they loaded in a covered wagon for the last
time and settled near Miles City, Mont.

WILL AND BERTHA BURKETT (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

After Will and Bertha Burkett were married, they lived seven miles northeast of
Roscoe for 24 years and eight months.  Bertha came to Roscoe from Iowa in the
spring of 1893.  She received her education here after which she taught school
for five years.  She recalled water being a problem as they had to haul it
three-fourths of a mile on a stone boat.

RUBY M. CALLIN (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Ruby was the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Elijah Cole, who lived on a farm
northeast of Roscoe until 1901. Mrs. Martha Penfield also listed in this book
was her sister.  She recalled life on their farm with many fires and a severe
snowstorm the winter of 1896-1897 when they couldn't leave their farm for three
months because of the heavy snow.

Their closest neighbor was Jonathan Brewer. (Her son, John, and his wife Vivian
are residents of Roscoe in this Centennial year.)

SAMUEL CHAPPELL (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Samuel Chappell came to Roscoe in the spring of 1887 with his mother and two
sisters.  His father came the year before to help build the railroad.  The
family also ran the dray and livery stable.  He attended school through the
ninth grade.  The family moved in the fall of 1919 to western Montana.

CHARLES W. AND EMMA CHRISTEN

Charles W. Christian was born September 18, 1877 at Albany, Minn.  He married
Emma Heier who was also born at Albany October 21, 1872.  They had three
children: Dr. Harold Christen of Lemmon, S.D.; Mrs. Edwin (Esther) Bock of
Brainerd, Minn.; and Mrs. Erwin (Inez) Haerter of Roscoe.  They had nine
grandchildren and 17 great-grandchildren.

He attended the Park Region Lutheran College at Fergus Falls, Minn. where he
took a business course.  He graduated in 1898, always having enjoyed singing and
baseball.  His first job was as a hotel clerk at Melrose, Minn.  He later took
a position as manager of the Farmers Elevator in Albany, Minn.  He did not like
the grain business and became associated with Theisen & Benolken in the
implement and Livestock business, and as a side line, Tom Schaefer and Mr.
Christen built the first Telephone Exchange in Albany.  After severing his
connection with the Implement and Livestock business, he become the assistant
cashier of the First State Bank of Albany.

It was in October 1906 that Mr. Christen, E. Keller, and H. A. Warner,
purchased the stock of the first State Bank of Roscoe, and Mr. Christen become
its cashier. He held this position until 1927 when he was elected president.
Charlie continued as president until 1954 when the controlling interest was
sold to A. N. Johnson of Walhalla, N.D. At that time he became chairman of the
Board of Directors.

Some of the other positions he held were: County Commissioner for eight years,
member of the Roscoe Independent School District, member of the City Council
for many years, a member of the Fire Department when the first chemical fire
engine was purchased.  He was an elder of the Lutheran Church for 41 years and
served nine years on the Executive Board of the State Banking Commission.

After his retirement, Charlie continued to live in his home in Roscoe.  He
enjoyed his hobbies which were hunting and fishing.  At the age of 90 years he
went to Lemmon, S.D. and lived in the Five Counties Nursing Home where he
remained until October 1977 when he passed away at the age of 100.  Mrs.
Christen had passed away in October 1948 when she was 76.  They are both buried
in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

WILLIAM AND ADELINE CHRISTEN

William H. Christen was born in Albany, Minn.  October 8, 1884.  He grew up in
that area, graduated from St. Cloud Business College, worked in the First State
Bank of Albany for three years before coming to Roscoe May 5, 1909.  He came to
work in the First State Bank of Roscoe where his older brother Charles was
working.

Adeline Abeling Christen was born in Long Prairie, Minn.  July 3, 1886.  She
attended school there and come to work in Huck's General Store in September
1909.

William and Adeline Christen were married in Long Prairie, Minn.  August 27,
1913 and came to live in Roscoe shortly thereafter.

William continued to be associated with the bank in various capacities until
1954 when he retired.  He was a 50 year member of the S.D. Banker's Assn.  He
was one of the first to help organize the Roscoe Volunteer Fire Department in
1918, served on the town board, was mayor and always interested in civic
programs and projects, including planning the City Park.  His special interests
included planting trees, fishing, hunting and music.  He played the clarinet in
the City Band and enjoyed playing his violin at home.

Mrs. Christen was active in church activities at St. Paul's Lutheran Church
where she helped organize the Sunday School with Rev. J. P. Scherf, played the
organ for church and worked with the choir.

The Christen's daughter, Rhea, was born May 19, 1915. After completing school
in Roscoe, she graduated from the University of South Dakota at Vermillion,
taught two years in Parkston, S.D. High School and married A. Edwin Karlen in
1939.  They live in Columbia, S.D. Their family is Robert Edwin, Anchorage,
Alaska; Ruth Ann, LeMars, Iowa and Richard William of Columbia.

William Christen passed away June 14, 1978 and is buried in Morningside
Cemetery in Roscoe.  Adeline resides at the Colonial Manor in Ipswich, S.D.

EUGENE AND LYNN CONN

        Back row: Wendy, Eugene, Jr., Eugene, Sr., and Tonia; Front row: Lynn
        and Crystal Conn

Eugene Francis Conn was born in Aberdeen, S.D. October 19, 1939 to Francis and
Erna Brick Conn.  He graduated from Westport, S.D. High School in 1959, then
worked on construction until November, 1961. November 24, 1961 he married Lynn
Christianson at Columbia, S.D. He entered the Army in April of 1962 and they
spent the next two years at Fort Leonard Wood, Mo.  Upon his discharge April 3,
1964 they farmed in the Wetonka area with his father and brother for two years.
In January of 1966 they went on their own and moved to a farm in the Roscoe
area, the farmer Alvin Houck farm.

Lynn Christianson Conn was born and raised on a farm northwest of Columbia,
S.D. December 22, 1942 to Elmer and Esther Krege Christianson.  She graduated
from Barnard High School in 1960 and attended the Aberdeen School of Commerce
and worked at Production Credit Association until August of 1962 at which time
she joined her husband at Fort Leonard Wood.

They were blessed with four children: Eugene, Jr. was born at Aberdeen, S.D.
June 17, 1964.  He graduated from Roscoe High School in 1982 and is presently
attending Bismarck Junior Collage; Tonia Lynn was born at Ipswich, S.D.
February 15, 1967 and is a sophomore at Roscoe High School.  She is presently
one of the arsonists at St. Paul's Lutheran Church; Wendy Sue was born at
Aberdeen, S.D. March 21, 1968.  She is a freshman at Roscoe High School;
Crystal Rae was born at Ipswich, S.D. May 29, 1976 and is in the first grade at
Roscoe.

IVA COVEY (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Iva Covey, daughter of R. H. Wardle, came to Roscoe in 1905.  That year and for
five years they had wonderful crops, then came the hot winds which took the
grain. She remembered the winter the elevator burned.  It burned for weeks and
when the wind blew, the sparks would fly, many of which had to be put out
before they started a fire.  She also remembered the first year they threshed
by horsepower.

CHRIST AND MARGARET DABBERT

Christ Dabbert, Sr. was born July 15, 1881 in South Russia.  He was the son of
August and Carolina Schott Dabbert.  Margaret Geist was also born in South
Russia, March 21, 1886.  She was the daughter of Christ and Gottliebina Singer
Geist.

They were married January 31, 1908 and in the fall of 1911 they came to the
United States.  They worked on a farm west of Bowdle, S.D. until they settled
on a farm near Tolstoy, S.D. Later they moved to the Faulkton, S.D. area where
they farmed until 1920 when they bought a farm two and one-half miles north of
Roscoe.

They were blessed with seven children, Christ, Jr.; Martha (Mrs.  Adam
Schumacher); Elsie (Mrs.  Adolph Finkbeiner), Emil, Emma (Mrs.  Aaron Beyers),
Edwin and Arthur.

There were a lot of hardships along the way, especially in the summer of 1937
when Mrs. Dabbert had the misfortune of losing her right arm when a tornado
touched down on their farm and destroyed several buildings.

Mr. Dabbert passed away June 6, 1946.  Mrs. Dabbert remained on the family farm
until the fall of 1949 when she sold the farm to her son, Emil, and retired
into the town of Roscoe where she lived her remaining years. She passed away
September 17, 1970.

They were members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church and are buried in Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe.

EMIL AND MINNIE DABBERT

        Back row: Debra and Donna; front row: Minnie and Emil Dabbert

Emil Alvin Dabbert was born February 16, 1918 in Faulk County, S.D. He came to
Roscoe, in October 1920, with his parents, Christ Dabbert Sr. and Margaret Geist
Dabbert.  Emil was one of seven children.

Emil spent a few years in the Armed Service from February 24, 1942 until
November 4, 1945, of which 41 months were spent overseas.  He saw combat action
in Guadalcanal, in the Solomon Islands and also in the Liberation of the
Philippine Islands.  He also served time in the Hawaiian Islands, New
Caledonia, New Zealand and the New Hebrides Islands.  Emil was wounded twice in
action and received the Purple Heart and the Oakleaf Cluster.

After returning from the service, Emil married Minnie Hintz on March 3, 1946.
Minnie was born near Bowdle, S.D. in Walworth County January 10, 1916, to John
Hintz and Magdalena Merkel Hintz.  Emil and Minnie have two daughters, Donna
Diane, and Debra Darlys.

Minnie and Emil are members of St. Paul's Evangelical Lutheran Church, where
Emil is serving his second term as elder and Minnie is a member of the Lutheran
Ladies

After several years of renting farms, Minnie and Emil got tired of moving
around, so they decided to buy Emil's home place where they are still living at
the present time, two and one half miles north of Roscoe.

EDWIN AND MARJORIE DABBERT

        Marjorie, Rita, Mary, Michael, Michelle, Edwin and Richard Dabbert

Edwin Dabbert was born September 9, 1920 at Faulkton, S.D., to Christian and
Margaret Geist Dabbert, who came to America in 1911 from South Russia. He moved
two and one-half miles north of Roscoe when he was one month old.  They lived
in the Bowdle, Tolstoy and Faulkton area before moving north of Roscoe.

Eddie attended the Bishop School for eight years and graduated from Roscoe High
School in 1938.  He then went to C.C.C. Camp.

Eddie held township offices as well as being secretary and treasurer of the
Voluntary Fire Department.  He is also a member of the Cemetery Board.

He suffered a severe heart attack in 1964 and had poor health until he had open
heart surgery at Northwestern Hospital January 19, 1977.  This surgery proved
to be very successful and he enjoys many outdoor sports.

Marjorie Fischer Dabbert was born April 17, 1925 to John and Christina Woltz
Fischer, who came to this country in 1900 and 1901 from South Russia.

Marj attended rural school in Bryant Township and graduated from Roscoe High
School in 1943.  She received a B.S. degree in Elementary Education in 1970 and
has taught for many years.  She is presently teaching third grade in Roscoe.

Marj is a member of R.E.A., S.D.E.A., Lutheran Ladies Aid, Ivy Circle Garden
Club, as well as holding offices.

Eddie and Marj are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church and the Roscoe
Commercial Club.

They have two children: Richard, who graduated from Roscoe High School in 1963
and entered the Air Force the same year.  He has been stationed in Okinawa,
Florida, South Carolina, Taiwan, Rapid City, California and the South Pole.  He
is presently in Altus, Okla., where he is a flight engineer and an instructor.
In May of 1983 he will have served 20 years.  Richard married Mary Jane Geier,
who also graduated from Roscoe High School and the Aberdeen School of Commerce.
She has a Civil Service job on the some base as Richard.  They have two
children, Michelle who is 14, and Michael 8.

Rita graduated from Roscoe High School in 1969 and attended Watertown
Vocational School, graduating in 1970.  She worked in Watertown for six years
and since then has worked in Sacramento, Calif., as a Dental Secretary.

DENNIS AND ELOISE DAYTON

Dennis Lee Dayton was born September 27, 1944, in Aberdeen, S.D. His parents
are Ray and Esther Dunker Dayton, of Stratford, S.D. He grew up on a farm
between Warner and Stratford.  He graduated from the Warner High School in 1962
and joined the Navy that fall.  In the spring of 1966, he returned to civilian
life with a medical discharge to join with his father in farming.

Eloise Mueller Dayton was born October 21, 1949, in Mobridge, S.D. Her parents
are Otto and Anna Hofmann Mueller, of Java, S.D. She grew up on a farm north of
Java.  Eloise graduated from Java High School, in 1967 and Stewarts School of
Hairstyling in 1968.

Dennis and Eloise were married in Java May 26, 1968. They farmed near Stratford
until 1972.  Dennis started working for the South Dakota Wheat Growers in Bath,
S.D. in 1973 and was transferred in 1976 to the Roscoe elevator.

The Daytons now reside on what used to be the Jake Vogele place, just north of
Roscoe.  They have three children, Loren Conrad, 14, Valerie Ann, 13, and Lionel
Edward, 11.

THE PERRY ADDISON AND EMILY JOHNSTON DECKER FAMILY

        Perry Addison Decker

In the spring of 1888 the Decker family moved from the Plankinton, S.D. area to
the Roscoe area.  Four years previously they had come by train from Hartsgrove,
Ohio.  After two moves near Roscoe, they settled on the homestead three miles
north, a half mile west and a quarter mile south of Roscoe.  Two big cottonwood
trees still mark the site.

In this family there were four girls, Carrie, Nellie, Julia and Alice and one
boy, Charles.

The trip from Plankinton to Roscoe, a distance of about 200 miles cross country
travel was made by ox team. This trip took about a week and it was necessary to
make three trips to move the family and their belongings to Roscoe.  To be able
to drive an ox team one used a willow stick about six feet long and the word
"Gee" for right turn and "Haw" for left.  The ox team was trained to be driven
without the use of reins.

Some of those winters the family burned twisted straw or cow chips in a stove
Mrs. Decker called "the Animal". For a year or so Carrie and Julia operated a
cafe where the auditorium stands today.  Later Carrie married Edward Stitt and
moved to Hitchcock. (in 1936 she was chosen "Eminent Homemaker" by S.D. State
College in Brookings.). Julia married Ed Cook and moved to Plankinton and after
teaching a few years Nellie married Oliver Vanderpool and moved to Woonsocket.
Alice, the youngest daughter married Adney Downer and lived on a farm southwest
of Roscoe until her death in 1920.

        Back row: Laurence, Charles, Raymond and Jessie Decker, Carrie Decker
        Stitt, William Johnston, his sister Mrs. Emily Decker, Alice Decker
        Downer with Ivan, Nellie Decker Vanderpool, and Mildred Vanderpool;
        front row: Evelyn Decker, Margaret and Rhea Stitt, Edna, May, and Perry
        Downer, Muriel and Doris Vanderpool

Charles started his first venture in business in the late 1890s by opening a
bicycle shop in Roscoe.  During this time bicycles were the common mode of
transportation. He operated this shop for about a year, and then in 1905 went
to college in Valparaiso, Ind.  His education was cut short by the death of his
father, and he came home to manage the farm and to care for his mother.
Because of his interest in God's work, and his knowledge of the Bible, he
received a license to preach in the Methodist Church.  He, also, traveled and
lectured for the Prohibition Party in which his father had been so vitally
interested.  As a drawing card, an Edison phonograph was used in these
lectures.  Phonographs were a new item on the market.  While engaged in this
work he met Jessie Brenton who was living at Walnut Grove, Minn. and they were
married October 11, 1908. To this union were born five children.  One girl died
in infancy.  Evelyn, Laurence, Raymond and Ernest are the other children. in
1910 Mrs. Decker's parents and three sons, Frank, Sam, and Ira, came to live on
the Wm. Johnston farm just north of the Deckers and a married brother, James,
and his family moved to the farm west of them.

The Deckers spent the year of 1913 and 14 in Venice, Calif. and though they
returned to the homestead, they moved to Roscoe in 1919 when he and the Brenton
brothers went into the garage business.  The Deckers first lived in the Basford
house across the street from the Wilson Lumber Yard.  Several years later they
purchased a house located on the present school grounds and moved it to the lot
across the street to the west where the I. S. Mahon's were the last to live in
it.  A few years ago it was torn down.

In 1921 when Mr. Decker took over the duties as postmaster, the post off ice
was in a small building and there was one triweekly route. in a few years he was
successful in having the building completely remodeled and all new equipment
installed.  By this time he had succeeded in having three daily routes
established.  He was, also, active in civic affairs and he was a member of the
Masonic Lodge.

        Jessie and Charles Decker

In 1935 after leaving the post office Mr. Decker and his sons, Raymond and
Ernest, leased the Texaco Station from Carl Swanson in Aberdeen.  This station
had been operated by Wm.  Hild.  Two years later Ernest went to work for K.O.
Lee in Aberdeen.  From there he went to Douglas Aircraft in Santa Monica.  He
married Florence Collbohm of Santa Monica.

Their son, Paul, named their son after his great-great-grandfather, Perry
Addison.  In 1947 Ernest and his family moved to San Jose where he worked for
Sears Roebuck and Florence worked as bookkeeper for the city at the airport.
Since they retired, they live in Honolulu most of the time.  They have three
children.

Laurence married Dorothy Holland of Scotland, S.D. For many years he taught in
Dakota schools.  He left the superintendency in Scotland to move to Anaheim,
Calif. where he and Dorothy both taught.  In 1975 he was named the outstanding
teacher in Anaheim High School.  They have four children.

Evelyn and Lynn Light were married.  Among other positions he was manager of
The Lemmon Drug Store and later assistant manager of the Walgreen Drug Store in
Aberdeen.  From there he went as a pharmacist to the Canal Zone.  After
spending 10 years in the Canal Zone, he accepted the job at the Air Force
Hospital in Minot, N.D. After he retired he worked with a realty firm.  He died
in 1979.  Evelyn taught and in 1934 she was dean of girls at the high school
dormitory here in Roscoe.  In Minot she taught at the Minot Business College.
She still lives in Minot.

Raymond married Vera Heiser whose father for several years managed the Roscoe
Farmers' Elevator. After leaving the Texaco Station, Raymond worked as an
inspector for the state and then worked at Montgomery Wards.  After retiring he
owned and managed the East Side Monument Co. Last year Vera retired from her
job as a secretary in the Aberdeen Public Schools. They have four children.

In 1942 Mr. and Mrs. Charles Decker moved to Santa Monica where he was employed
by Douglas Aircraft. While he was employed there, he became ill and died three
years later.  Mrs. Decker lived in Santa Monica until her death in 1953.  They
and both of their parents ore laid to rest in the Roscoe Cemetery.

Ray passed away on February 7, 1983 and burial was at the Riverside Memorial
Park Cemetery.

        Raymond Decker

LUIE DEDEN (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Luie Deden was well known to early Roscoe citizens. He worked for the railroad
on their north and south passenger cars as a baggage checker and janitor.  He
later delivered mail from the depot to the post office with a hand push cart.
Mr. Deden moved to California in the early 30s and passed away there.

ADNEY P. DOWNER FAMILY

Adney P. Downer, who lived eight and one-half miles southwest of Roscoe for 68
years, was born August 27, 1881 in Iowa.  In 1882 he moved to Freeman, South
Dakota with his parents, John M. and Allie (Simpson) Downer.  After graduating
from the eighth grade in the Turner County public schools, and attending one
term of school at Dakota Wesleyan in Mitchell, he homesteaded in Glover
Township in 1902.  Except for teaching a winter term of school in Glover
Township one year, he engaged in a farming career.  He was active in township
affairs and served as assessor for a number of years.

December 20, 1903, Adney Downer married Alice R. Decker at the Decker home near
Roscoe.  She was born in March, 1879 in Ohio and came to Roscoe with her
parents, Perry A. and Emily (Johnston) Decker.  She died January 14, 1920 and
is buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

        Mabel, Ivan, Edna, Perry, May, Adney and Alma Downer

Four children were born at Roscoe to this marriage: May, who married George
Novak in 1922 and Tron Andersen in 1943 (both deceased), resides in Lemmon,
South Dakota.

Edna, who married John Brucklacher of Freeman, South Dakota in 1927.  They
still reside in their home near Freeman.

Perry, who married Helen Beyers (daughter of Albert Beyers of Roscoe) in 1934.
He resided on his farm southwest of Roscoe until his death in 1969.  He is
buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

Ivan, who married Ida Adam (daughter of Martin Adam of Roscoe) in 1936.  They
now reside in Scotland, South Dakota.

June 29, 1921, Adney Downer married Alma A. Hutchinson of Ipswich, S.D. She was
born March 25, 1886 in Wisconsin and in 1893 moved with her parents, Albert G.
and Helen (Covey) Hutchinson, to Loyalton, S.D. Later the family moved into
Ipswich.  She taught public school a number of years in the Ipswich, Loyalton,
and Roscoe areas.  Alma Downer died January 1, 1971 and is buried in the Roscoe
Cemetery.

Two daughters were born to this marriage: Ella, who died in infancy and is
buried in the Roscoe Cemetery; and Mabel, who married Paul Williams of
Hagerstown, Maryland in 1961.  They now reside in Superior, Wis.

In 1971, Adney Downer returned to Freeman, S.D. where he remained until his
death December 22, 1979. He is buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

The A. P. Downer family first joined the Methodist Church in Roscoe.  When that
church was closed, they transferred to the First Presbyterian Church where they
retained their membership as long as they lived at Roscoe.  Adney Downer was
active in church affairs and was sent to the national meeting in Philadelphia
of the Presbyterian Church (General Assembly) in 1937 as a district
representative.

In 1910, Adney Downer had the first International automobile in the area,
(there were one or two cars of other makes ahead of his in Roscoe).  This car
was unique in that it had a chain drive.  On the way home from town one day, a
neighboring farm dog chasing the car got too close and caught the tip of his
tail in the chain drive, resulting in a shorter tail.

PERRY AND HELEN DOWNER

Perry Downer was born January 15, 19 10 to Adney P. and Alice nee Decker Downer
on the northeast one- fourth of Section 17 in Glover Township, Edmunds County.
He attended the rural school which was one mile north of the farm home.  He
also attended the School of Agriculture, South Dakota State College at
Brookings.  He and his brother, Ivan, farmed with their father until they
established their own farms.

Perry Downer and Helen Beyers, daughter of Albert and Marie nee Schurr Beyers,
were married January 2, 1934.  Their first home was a small, 12'xl6', house
located on the Adney Downer farm.  October 31, 1935 the small house was moved
to the northwest one-fourth of Section 17 in Glover Township where they
gradually built their farm.

Roger Perry Downer was born May 12, 1936 and died October 1, 1936.

Lois Marie Downer was born September 26, 1939.  She attended the rural school
and was looking forward to starting high school when she became ill and died
October 25, 1953.

Leslie Raymond Downer was born February 26, 1943. He and Lois attended the some
rural school as their father had.  He attended Roscoe High School, South Dakota
School of Mines in Rapid City for two years, transferred to South Dakota State
University in Brookings, graduating in 1966.  He taught industrial Arts in
Hosmer High School until 1969 when he took over the family farm which he still
operates on a limited basis due to a health problem.  He is not married.  His
mother lives with him.

Besides farming Perry did plumbing, heating, air conditioning, and electrical
work.  He served on the Glover Township board and the school board.  He was
active in the Presbyterian Church, both in the local church and in Presbytery.
In 1968 he and his wife visited mission stations and were briefed on the work
in Japan, Hong Kong, Thailand, India and at the World Council of Churches in
Geneva, Switzerland.  February 27, 1969 he died in a truck accident.  He, Roger
and Lois are buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe, S.D.

Helen Beyers Downer was born January 1, 1914 in Montpelier Township.  She
attended the rural school about a mile from the parental home and Roscoe High
School, graduating in May of 1932.  She is active in the Presbyterian Church.
She is an elder, the clerk of the session and church treasurer, and is
treasurer of the Womens Organization.  She has held Presbyterial and Synodical
offices.  Since 1969 Helen has been serving on committees of the Presbytery of
South Dakota.

She likes to travel.  She has been on tours to Alaska, Hawaii, Brazil, Peru,
Ecuador, Venezuela, Colombia, Mexico and a number of the European countries,
also The Lands of the Bible.  She has traveled in the mainland 48 U.S.A.

JULIUS Z. AND IRENE DOWNER

Julius Z. Downer and wife Irene, homesteaded nine and one-half miles southwest
of Roscoe in 1902.  Later they moved to the farm two miles south of Roscoe (now
owned by C. Geier.) In addition to farming, he operated a creamery in Roscoe a
few years before moving to Ohio.

An infant son is buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.  Three other children were born
at Roscoe and moved with their parents to Ohio: M. Harold, Margaret, and Ralph
(now deceased).

W. SIMPSON AND EDNA DOWNER

W. Simpson Downer, wife Edna (Downing), and five children (Allie, William,
Jesse, Leona, and Hazel) moved onto a farm nine and one-half miles southwest of
Roscoe in 1925.  Edna served as a midwife and practical nurse to neighbors.
When the family moved to Arizona in the late 1930s, William remained on the
farm in Glover Township.  He had married Elodie Wiens of Marion, S.D. in 1936.

Three children were born to them at Roscoe: Orville, Joyce, and Velera.  In the
late 1940'5, William and family moved to Oregon.

They are both deceased and buried in Salem, Ore.

BALTHASSAR AND MARY DUTENHOEFFER

Balthassar (B.K.) Dutenhoeffer was born in Russia August 15, 1898 to Michael
and Regina Dosch Dutenhoeffer.  He immigrated with his parents to the United
States at the age of two.  He was raised in the rural Ipswich area along with
three brothers and nine sisters.

Mary Heiszler Dutenhoeffer was born in Russia June 29, 1901 and came to the
United States with her parents, Peter and Veronica Meier Heiszler at the age of
five months.  She was raised in Hillside Township, Edmunds County.

Balthassar and Mary were married January 31, 1920. They lived in rural Ipswich,
S.D. and two years later moved to rural Hillside Township, south of Roscoe.
They built a farm there and lived there until retirement in 1951 when they
moved to Roscoe.

They had four children, Ann, George, Stanis, and Angeline.

Ann married Mel Henry and lives in Peru, Neb.  They have three children and
three grandchildren.

George is married to Jayne Hartzel and lives in Huron, S.D. They have two
children.

Stanis married Leone Sauer and they live in Roscoe. They have two children.

Angeline married Walter Lammle and lives in Shenandoah, Iowa.  They have five
children and seven grandchildren.

B.K. died in 1963 and Mary in 1967.  They belonged to St. Thomas Catholic
Church and are buried in the church cemetery.

STANIS AND LEONE DUTENHOEFFER

        Back row: Eric and Janis; front row: Leone and Stanis Dutenhoeffer

Stanis Dutenhoeffer (also known as Dennis) was born August 26, 1925 in rural
Edmunds County to Balthassar and Mary Heiszler Dutenhoeffer.  He grew up on his
parents farm which was nine miles south of Roscoe in Hillside Township.  He
attended rural Hillside Township School, Loyalton School and graduated from
Roscoe High School in 1943.

Leone (Leocadia) Sauer Dutenhoeffer was born January 11, 1932 in Aberdeen, S.D.
to Michael and Amelia Schwan Sauer.  She attended St. Mary's and Central High
School in Aberdeen, graduating in 1949.

Stanis and Leone were married September 11, 1951. They have two children, Janis
and Eric.

Janis was born September 19, 1963, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1981,
and is presently attending Northern College in Aberdeen.

Eric was born October 29, 1966 and will graduate from Roscoe High School in
1984.

Stanis has been a farmer all of his life, taking over his parents 'farm in
1951.  Due to illness, they sold the farm in 1981 and have since lived in
Roscoe.  His first attempt with a kidney transplant was unsuccessful and he is
presently waiting for another one.

The Dutenhoeffers belong to St. Thomas Catholic Church where Leone is a member
of St. Thomas Altar Society.  They also belong to Roscoe Commercial Club.

HENRY AND MARGARET EHNES

        Back row: Nick, Tony, Maggie and John; middle row: Alice, Henry, Jr.,
        Henry, Sr., Kate, Margaret and Barbara; front raw: Matt, Roy and Simon
        Ehnes

Henry Ehnes, son of John Ehnes, was born March 15, 1875 in Crimea, Russia.  He
married Margaret Kelsch, daughter of Fredrick Kelsch, born March 12, 1876 in
Crimea, Russia.  In 1899 they immigrated to the United States.  For two years
he worked on the railroad in Bowdle.  They homesteaded seven miles west and one-
half mile south of Roscoe.  After farming for some years, with the crops
getting worse each year, Henry decided to move to Canada.

He rented out his farm and shipped his machinery and livestock to Bow Island,
Alberta, Canada.  From Bow Island, he moved his family to Faith, Alberta where
he farmed until 1915.  He moved his family to Montana where he farmed for two
years before returning to his original homestead near Roscoe.  He remained on
this farm until his death October 3, 1940.

He son, Simon, took over the farm for several years while his wife, Margaret,
lived with their daughters. Their 12 children are Anton (Tony), married Regina
Fiest who died in 1937.  He   married Hannah Stoecker and they now live in
McLaughlin, S.D. Magdalena (Maggie) married Andrew B. Meier and lives in Roscoe.

Nickodemus (Nick) married Isabelle Slack.  He lived in Great Falls, Montana
until his death.

John was born in 1902 and died in 1927.

Catherine married John Bitz and now lives in Portland, Ore.

Leontina (Alice) married John Roth and lives in Aberdeen, S.D.

Rochus (Roy) married Anna Roth and lives in Ipswich, S.D.

Simon married Anna Mary Leboldus and lives in Roscoe.

Mathew married Delores Scheuffele.  He farmed near McLaughlin until he moved to
Great Falls, Mont. where he now resides.

Barbara married Joseph Volk and farmed near Aberdeen until they moved to
California.

Henry married Theora Bulesen and farmed near Lakeville, Minn. until his death
in 1982.

Margaret married Benedict Reinbold and farmed near Aberdeen until they moved to
Son Diego, Calif.

SIMON AND ANNA MARY EHNES

        Back row: LeRoy, Henry, Paulette, Phyllis and Marian; front row: Simon
        and Anna Mary Ehnes

Simon Ehnes was born near Roscoe December 14, 1908 to Henry and Margaret Kelsch
Ehnes.  Henry Ehnes was born March 15, 1875 in Crimea, Russia and Margaret was
born March 12, 1876, also in Russia.  They came to the United States in 1899
and settled on a farm west of Roscoe.  Henry died October 3, 1940 and Margaret
died December 6, 1955.  They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

Anna Mary Ehnes was born September 4, 1913 at Selz, N.D. to Andrew and Marion
Keller Leboldus, who come to this country from Odessa, Russia in 1903.  Her
father died of the flu August 11, 1919 and is buried at Selz, N.D. Her mother
married Constantine Fuhrman March 6, 1923 and moved to Onaka, S.D. She died in
March 1963 and is buried in Aberdeen, S.D.

Simon and Anna Mary were married October 24, 1932 at St. Thomas Catholic Church
in Roscoe.  They settled on a farm near Roscoe.  They raised a family of five
children.  They are: Marion, who was born April 21, 1934 and married to
Sebastian Scherr June 1, 1954 in Roscoe.  They live on the farm seven miles
west of Roscoe where her grandfather, Henry Ehnes, homesteaded when he came to
South Dakota.  They have eight children.

LeRoy was born September 14, 1935.  He married Angie Heinz May 1, 1963 in
Ipswich, S.D. They live on the farm four miles southeast of Roscoe where his
father farmed from 1941 to 1960.  They have eight children.

Henry was born March 15, 1939 and married Shirley Lunders June 2, 1964 in
Hoven, S.D. They live in Mobridge, S.D. and have three children.

Phyllis was born March 23, 1946 and married Roger Guthmiller July 1, 1968.
They have one son and live in Roscoe.

Paulette was born May 13, 1951 and married Tony Kub November 27, 1971.  They
have two children and live on a farm southeast of Roscoe.

Simon and Anna Mary retired from the farm in 1960 and moved into Roscoe where
they still reside.

LEROY AND ANGELINE EHNES

        Back row: Nancy, Anthony, Cheryl, Sandra, Mark and Jamie; front row:
        Angie holding Kerry and LeRoy holding Darcy

LeRoy Ehnes was born at Onaka, S.D. September 14, 1935 to Simon and Anna Mary
Leboldus Ehnes.

Angeline Heinz was born at Aberdeen, S.D. November 8, 1944 to Mary Heinz.

LeRoy and Angie were married May 1, 1963 at the Holy Cross Catholic Church in
Ipswich, S.D. LeRoy spent from November 1956 to November 1958 in the Armed
Forces including tour of duty in Korea. After his discharge from the Army he
worked for Mardian Hide and Fur in Roscoe for many years.  He then worked for
Northern Farm Supply in Aberdeen until 1965.

December 12, 1965 LeRoy and Angie moved to the home farm two miles south and
east of Roscoe, where they are presently residing.  They do grain farming, are
in the milking business, and do custom haystack moving.

LeRoy and Angie were blessed with eight children throughout their married life,
Cheryl, who is 17 and is a senior at Roscoe High School; Mark, 15, a sophomore;
Sandra is 12 and a seventh grader; Anthony is 11 years old and is a sixth
grader; Jamie at 10 is a fifth grader; Nancy is nine and is a fourth grader,
all at Roscoe Elementary School; Darcy is two and one-half and Kerry is 10
months old.

LeRoy and Angie and their family are members of the St. Thomas Catholic Church
in Roscoe.

LeRoy is presently the treasurer for Montpelier Township and Angie belongs to
the Altar Society.

PAUL AND CLARA ELFORD

Paul Elford was born April 26, 1895, on a farm one mile east of the cemetery,
north of Roscoe.  He was the son of William Elford, who was born in England on
June 24, 1852, and Metla Schwartz, who was born March 25, 1860 in Denmark.
They were married at Naperville, Ill. in 1880, and soon moved to Cherokee, la.,
where he had a paint shop.  In the spring of 1893, they came to Roscoe.

Clara Witter was born in Mt.  Vernon, la.  June 20, 1887.  Her father was
Franklin Witter, who was born April 7, 1848 at Chagrin Falls, Ohio.  Her mother
was Francis Foster who was born at Fountain Prairie, Wis. on July 7, 1852.

Paul and Clara were married May 10, 1921, in Aberdeen.  They made their home in
Roscoe where Paul was a pointer and decorator until 1931, when he started
carrying mail on Route 1 out of Roscoe.

They have two daughters: Jean (Mrs.  Edward Beebe) who lives in Ridgefield,
Conn. and has two sons, two daughters and five grandchildren; and Mary (Mrs.
C. L. Scheier) of Verdigrie, Neb.  Mary has three sons, two daughters and three
grandchildren.

Paul has held many different offices in Roscoe: Commander of the American
Legion, Chairman of the Town Board, Clerk of the School District, Vice Chairman
of the Edmunds County Ration Board, Advisor to Draft registration during WW II.
Paul is a veteran of WW I and belongs to the WW I Barracks for 63 years,
Woodmen and Masons.  His name is listed in Who's Who for South Dakota.

When Paul and Clara were very young, there were only two churches in Roscoe,
the Presbyterian and Methodist.  The Lutherans and Catholics had services once
in awhile in the school house.  Clara taught school and was working in the
First State Bank at the time they were married.

Paul retired in 1961 and they decided to settle in a warmer climate, moving to
Harlingen, Texas where they are living now.  They make an annual visit back to
Roscoe to see their old friends and are planning to join the citizens of Roscoe
in celebrating their 100th birthday.

CHRISTIAN AND REGINA ELLWEIN

Regina Ellwein              Christian Ellwein,

Christian Ellwein was born in Grimm, Russia.  His parents are unknown as is
Christian's birth date and year.

Christian married Regina Haar in Russia.  Their oldest son, Matt Ellwein, was
ten months old when they come to America.  They settled in the Hutchinson
County, Freeman, S.D. area where they farmed.

In about 1900 or so, Christian moved his family to Roscoe, S.D.; at that time
they could still homestead land.  They moved on a farm II miles southwest of
Roscoe, known as the Mertz farm.  At the present time, Julius Schneider is
living on the farm.

They lived there about three years, then homesteaded some land in Cottonwood
Lake Township.  The land was six miles west, three north and one-half mile west
of Roscoe.  They started a sheep ranch and had as many as over 3,000 head of
sheep.

        Tina, Ben, Anna, Matt, Ted, Josie, Albert, Katherine and Alex; seated:
        Regina Ellwein

They had seven sons and five daughters: Matt, Alex, Ben, Albert, Fred, Ted,
Gottlob, Katie, Frieda, Josie, Tina and Anna.

When they retired, they built a new home in Roscoe; the house that Bill Bobby
lives in now.

Mr. Ellwein passed away in 1936 at the age of 86 years.  Mrs. Ellwein moved in
with her daughter and husband, Mr. and Mrs. Emil Mayer in Aberdeen.  She died
at the age of 94 years.  They are both buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.  At the
present time, oil the children and their spouses have passed away.

ALEXANDER C. ELLWEIN FAMILY

        Anna and Alexander Ellwein

Alexander C. Ellwein was born in Hutchinson County February 28, 1874 to
Christian Ellwein and Regina Haar Ellwein of Freeman, S.D. He grew to manhood in
that area and also went to school there.

Alexander farmed with his father.  In 1898, he married Christianna Huber who
also was born in Hutchinson County.  They homesteaded in the Gregory, S.D. area.
They had four sons: Robert, Ervin, Helmuth, and Harold.  Ervin and Helmuth
passed away as small boys. Robert lives in Santa Barbara, Calif. and Harold
lives in Roscoe, S.D.

Alexander s first wife passed away in 1911. As a widower, Alexander moved to
the Roscoe area and was married to Anna Miller.  Anna was born in Grimm,
Russia, and came over to America as a grown girl in the twenties.  They rented
the Herman Baer farm located seven miles west, one mile north, and one mile
west of Roscoe.

They had two daughters: Rose, married to William Wudel and now lives in
Aberdeen, S.D. and Nora, also living in Aberdeen, S.D.

The Ellweins lived on the Baer farm for many years, then decided to buy their
own farm which was located four miles south and one and one-half miles west of
Roscoe.  They lived there until 1935, then they retired and moved to Roscoe.
Alexander bought 10 acres of land which was the Roscoe fair grounds at that
time. They moved a house on it and other buildings and they raised poultry and
a few animals.  The place now is owned by Lloyd Larson.

In 1942, they moved to Aberdeen, S.D. Alexander died in 1949 at the age of 75
years.  He was buried in an Aberdeen cemetery.  Mrs. Ellwein made her home with
her daughter, Rose, for a few years.  She passed away at the age of 81 and also
is buried in an Aberdeen cemetery.

        Back row: Loretta, Robert, Agnes and Harold Ellwein; middle row: Anna
        (Mother), Nora and Bill Wudel; front row: Alexander, Loren and Rose
        Ellwein

HAROLD AND AGNES ELLWEIN

Harold Ernst Ellwein was born February 17, 1905 in Gregory County, Gregory,
S.D. to Alexander Ellwein and Christianna Ellwein nee Huber who both were born
in Hutchinson County, Freeman, S.D. area.

Harold had four brothers Robert, Ervin, and Helmuth. Two of the brothers died
as young children, Ervin in Gregory and Helmuth in Freeman.  Robert lives in
Santa Barbara, Calif. and Harold in Roscoe, S.D. He also has two half sisters.

Harold's mother passed away in 1911 and his father then moved to Roscoe, S.D.
when he was seven years old.  His father then married Anna Miller.  They had two
daughters, Rose and Nora.  Rose married William Wudel and they live in Aberdeen.

The family moved on a farm located four miles south, one and one-half miles
west where Harold grew up to manhood and also went to school there.  He farmed
with his father until he started farming for himself.

On August 20, 1933, Harold married Agnes Stern of Freeman, S.D. Six days later
they moved to the Roscoe area.  Agnes was born to Stephan Stern and Minnie Stern
nee Frank.  Both parents were born here in America.

Their first home was four and one-half miles south of Beebe, S.D., known as the
Henry Sporer ranch.  They had a lot of grazing land and raised white faced
cattle.

In 1940 they sold the ranch to Elmer Loss of Ipswich and bought a farm one mile
south of Beebe, known as the Huntley farm. in 1953, they sold the farm to
Ferdinand Schmaal and moved into the city of Roscoe. They started a lunch
counter and bar with Mr. and Mrs. Solomon Reede as partners.  It was called the
Community Bar which was located in the building that is now the old liquor
store.  A year later the Ellweins sold their share to Mr. and Mrs. Willard
Kukrall.

Harold then worked with the Gorder Company from Aberdeen as their farm salesman
for 18 years.

On January 1, 1959, Elmer Mayer and Harold started an insurance business along
with the real estate business.  The office was located in the old hotel and was
known as the Roscoe Real Estate and Insurance. The two worked together for 19
