years until they sold the business to Leland Treichel from Bowdle, S.D.

Harold served several terms on the town board and also as an elderman on the
church council.

Agnes belonged to various clubs, and is active in Ladies Aid.  Since they
retired, Harold and Agnes have done quite a bit of traveling in the U.S.,
Europe and Hawaii.

They enjoy going to the Senior Center, and taking care of their yard and home.

GOTTLOB and SOPHIE ELLWEIN (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Gottlob Ellwein was born in 1886 in Hutchinson County to Mr. and Mrs. Christian
Ellwein.  He came to the Roscoe vicinity in 1900.  In 1909 he married Sophie
Hafner, also born in Hutchinson County in 1882 to the Martin Hafner's.  They
farmed northwest of Roscoe until 1945 when they moved to town.  Sophie passed
away in 1958 and Gottlob in 1970.  They are both buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

MATHIAS AND HANNA ELLWEIN

Mathias Ellwein was born April 25, 1872 in Crimea, Russia, the son of Christian
and Regina Haar Ellwein.  He came to the Roscoe area and settled in Cottonwood
Lake Township in 1889.  His wife, Hanna Wagner Ellwein was born March 4, 1880
at Bessarabia, Russia and came to the Selby, S.D. area in March 1894.

Mathias and Hanna were married January 4, 1910 at Selby.  They had three
children: Bertha, Elmer and Helen.

Mathias died March 5, 1946 and Hanna died January 11, 1966.  They are both
buried in Morningside Cemetery, Roscoe.

ELMER AND MELLITA ELLWEIN

Elmer Arthur Ellwein was born May 28, 1912 in Cottonwood Township northwest of
Roscoe, the son of Mathias and Hanna Wagner Ellwein who were both born in
Russia.  Mathias came to the Roscoe area in 1889 and Hanna in 1910, at the time
of her marriage.

Elmer married Mellita Gorne October 23, 1938 at Roscoe.  Mellita Fredricka
Gorne was born January 4, 1913 in Glover Township southwest of Roscoe, the
daughter of John J. and Katherina Scherer Gorne who were born in Crimea, Russia
and came to this area in October 1905.

They have two sons: Larry who is married to Bettie Geditz.  They were married
June 30, 1968 at Ipswich and have two children.  They live on the farm northwest
of Roscoe.

Dennis married Joan Ulmer June 12, 1962 at Aberdeen, S.D. They live on a farm
near Barnard, S.D. and have five children.

LARRY AND BETTIE ELLWEIN

Larry Ellwein was born September 19, 1941 at Bowdle, S.D. to Elmer and Mellita
Gorne Ellwein.  Bettie Louise Geditz was born November 15, 1943 at Ipswich,
S.D. to John and Bettie Steen Geditz.

Larry and Bettie were married June 30, 1968 in Ipswich and live on a farm
northwest of Roscoe.  They have two children Dolly Louise, born October 2, 1975
and Jason Elmer, born April 17, 1978.

Larry was in the military service from 1960 to 1962.

        Dolly, Bettie, Larry and Jason Ellwein

TED AND BERTHA ELLWEIN (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Ted was born in 1894 at Freeman, S.D. and after living in Freeman and Bowdle,
he moved to Roscoe.  Before starting a cream buying business in 1915, which he
operated for many years, he clerked in the Huck Variety Store.

In 1920, he married Bertha Ernst and they had two daughters, Mrs. Jess, (Rena)
Barton, and Mrs. Delbert, (Dorothy) Sonnenfeld.

Mr. Ellwein belonged to the Fire Department and Commercial Club.  He also
served on the city board, was the city auditor and was treasurer of the school
board for 25 years.  After he retired, they moved to Aberdeen and later to the
Colonial Manor in Ipswich where he died in 1979 and she died in 1981.  Both are
buried in the Morningside Cemetery.

JACOB AND KATHERINE ERNST

Jacob Ernst was born in 1861 in Gallant, Crimea, South Russia.  His parents,
John Jacob and Christina Ernst (nee Rabe, born in 1833), were married in 1854
and had 11 other children.  Jacob married Katherine Blumhardt, the daughter of
Mr. and Mrs. Karl Blumhardt, who was also born in Crimea, Russia in 1868.
While living in Russia, they had five children: Katherine, the former Mrs. M.
J. Baer; Charles; Robert; Bertha, the former Mrs. T. J. Ellwein; and one son who
died at the age of six months.

In 1898, Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Ernst immigrated to the United States looking for a
new land of hope and freedom in America.  Along with them came their four
children and his mother, Christina, who died in this country in 1902.  Children
born to them in the United States were Emil and Eugene, the only surviving son
who now lives in Aberdeen, S.D. Three of the Ernsts' sons, Robert, Emil, and
Eugene, married Schick sisters.

Mr. and Mrs. Ernst homesteaded four and one-half miles west of Roscoe in Glen
Township.  They built a sod house at that location during the summer of 1899.
They were charter members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church of Roscoe.

Jacob Ernst died during the flu epidemic in March of 1918.  Katherine remained
living on the farm until 1920 when she moved to Roscoe where she died in August
of 1951.

Their sons continued to farm the homestead until about the mid 1930's after
which Emil remained farming and later retired there.  His son, Morris, presently
farms on the homestead.  His children are the fourth generation to live there.

        Standing: Eugene, Robert, Bertha, Emil, and Charles; seated: Katherine
        Ernst

EMIL AND ROSE ERNST

Emil Ernst, son of Jacob and Katherine Ernst, was born October 18, 1900 on the
Jacob Ernst homestead four and one-half miles west of Roscoe where he lived his
entire life.  He married Rose Schick December 13, 1925. Rose was born August
29, 1905 to Mr. and Mrs. William Schick of Bowdle.  Emil and Rose Ernst had
four sons: Morris, who has a separate history in this book; Lloyd; Wilmer; and
LeRoy.

        Back row: Wilmer, Morris, Lloyd, and LeRoy; front row: Rose and Emil
        Ernst

Lloyd Ernst was born July 1, 1930.  He married Ardella Gab of Eureka, S.D. and
is presently living in Vienna, Va. He is manager of operations for Western Fuels
Association, Washington, D.C. They have two daughters: Charlene and Krista.
Charlene is married to Rick Acuna.  They have three children and also are
living in Vienna.

Wilmer Ernst was born August 9, 1933.  He married Darlou Schaible of Hosmer,
S.D. They are living in Sacramento, Calif., where he is a civilian employee at
the Air Force base.  They have two sons: Steve and Monty.  Steve is married to
Myrna Blackburn.  They live in Sacramento with their child.  Monte is also in
Sacramento and works in a fire department.

LeRoy Ernst was born June 25, 1941.  He married Arlene Schuck of Aberdeen, S.D.
They now live in Bismarck, N.D. where he is Executive Secretary of the North
Dakota Motor Carriers Association.  They have three children: Michelle, Jason,
and Michael, all of whom are still at home.

Emil and Rose farmed the homestead until their retirement in 1965.  They rented
and later sold the farm to their son, Morris.  They were members of St. Paul's
Lutheran Church of Roscoe and the Roscoe Senior Center.  Emil also served as
foreman on a W.P.A. road project, various township and church committees, and
the county F.H.A. committee.  He enjoyed living on the farm, fishing, and
visiting with his many friends.

Emil died very suddenly October 1, 1981, of a heart attack at his home on the
farm.  Rose has since then moved into on apartment in Roscoe.  She is still a
member of the Roscoe Senior Center and St. Paul's Lutheran Church and the
Ladies Aid there.

MORRIS AND DOROTHY ERNST

Morris Ernst, son of Emil (deceased) and Rose Ernst, was born March 1, 1928 in
Roscoe.  He attended rural Dunn School and Roscoe High School.  June 4, 1960, he
married Dorothy Pedersen, daughter of James (deceased) and Lillie Pedersen of
Ipswich.

Dorothy Ernst was born on April 12, 1934 at Faulkton, S.D. She attended rural
school in Faulk County and graduated from Ipswich High School.  She worked for
the Edmunds County A.S.C. office for eight years before she married Morris.
She now enjoys raising her family and being a homemaker.

        Sonia, Sandra, Kari, Dorothy and Morris Ernst, Lori and Dale Hettick

Morris and Dorothy are engaged in farming and ranching on the Jacob Ernst
homestead four and one half miles west of Roscoe where they built a new home in
1960.  Morris farmed in partnership with his father until 1965.  He then rented
the land until 1978 when he purchased the farm from his father.

Morris and Dorothy have four daughters.  Lori was born July 5, 1961, and is
presently a R.N. at the Ipswich Community Hospital.  She married Dale Hettick
in 1981. Kari was born July 23, 1963, and is presently attending Northern State
College and working part-time in Aberdeen, S.D. Sandra and Sonia, identical
twins, were born January 22, 1966.  They are at home attending Roscoe High
School.

Morris has served on various committees and boards including: A.S.C. Community
Committee where he met Dorothy, Roscoe Cenex Oil Company Board, Edmunds County
F.H.A., St. Paul's Lutheran Church Council, and is presently on the F.E.M.
Electric Association Board of Directors.

Dorothy is a member of the How and Why Extension Club and St. Paul's Lutheran
Ladies Aid and has taught Saturday School for nine years at St. Paul's.  Both
ore members of the Roscoe Commercial Club and St. Paul's Lutheran Church of
Roscoe.

ROBERT AND IDA ERNST

Robert Ernst was born July 15, 1896, in Crimea, South Russia, the son of Jacob
and Katherine Blumhardt Ernst. He came to the United States and the Roscoe area
with his parents in 1899.  They settled on a farm west of Roscoe.

        Roman, Vella, Lorna, Doris, Roger, Ida, Robert and Douglas Ernst

Ida Schick was born October 19, 1907 in the Roscoe- Bowdle area, to William and
Rosina Oster Schick.  She grew up in the rural Hosmer area.

Bob and Ida were married in the Congregational Church in Hosmer November 23,
1926.  They lived on the family farm west of Roscoe until about 1933, when they
moved to Roscoe.  Bob was employed as the city "Cop" for some time until he
started an auto mechanic business known as Ernst's Garage.  He operated this
business until the time of his illness and death in 1975. He is buried in
Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

They had six children: Doris, married to Clarence Kukrall, lives on a farm
south of Roscoe; Lorna, married Floyd Ulmer, lives near Hosmer; Roman, married
Virginia Blumhardt, is a mechanic and lives in Hartford, S.D.; Vella, married
to Al Zacher, died in July 1980; Roger, married to Caroline Hieb, is an
architect in Shakopee, Minn.; and Douglas, married to Peggy Daschle, is a
credit manager in LeSeuer, Minn.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe, where Ida is a member
of the Ladies Aid.  She is also a member of the Roscoe Senior Citizens.

GEORGE AND CHRISTINA FISCHER

George Fischer was born March 29, 1870 in Bergdorf, South Russia.  His wife,
Christina Walz Fischer, was born March 31, 1873, also in Bergdorf.  They came to
America in 1901.  They homesteaded a farm eight miles northeast of Roscoe.

        Back row: Charles, John, Fred, Bill, Bertha and Amelia; front row:
        Albert, George, August, Edward, Ethel, Christina, and Luella Fischer

They had eleven children: John, Carl, Fred, Amelia and William, all born in
Russia; Bertha, Albert, Edward, August, Luella, and Ethel, all born in Roscoe.
Of the 11, Bertha, August, Edward, Ethel and Luella are still living.

George and Christina retired from farming in 1935 and moved to Roscoe where
they resided until their deaths.  George died June 30, 1943 and Christina died
November 18, 1952.  They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

MR. AND MRS.  EDWARD  FISCHER

        Jim, Sharon, Loren, Cheryl, Stacey and Edward Fischer

Edward G. Fischer was born April 18, 1907, to George and Christina Walz
Fischer, eight miles northeast of Roscoe.  He received his grade school
education in a country school and farmed with his family until he married
Minnie Wiederhoft February 28, 1931.  At that time they bought a farm five
miles north of Beebe.  To Ed and Minnie, two children were born, Sharon, born
December 8, 1940 and Jim, born January 16, 1944. Sharon married Loren
Kirschenmann in 1959.  They have two daughters, Cheryl and Stacey.  Loren works
for Texas Instruments and they live in Richardson, Texas.

Jim lives in Minneapolis where he has worked for General Mills since 1969 as an
accountant.

Minnie Wiederhoft Fischer died June 21, 1966.  Ed stayed on the farm until
1969, then sold out and moved to Roscoe.  March 24, 1971 Ed married Nilda
Schumacher in Roscoe, where they still reside.

        Edward and Nilda Fischer

JOHN AND CHRISTINA FISCHER

John Fischer was born July 26, 1892 in South Russia, the son of George and
Christina Waltz Fischer.  He came to the United States with his family in 1900
and settled on a farm northeast of Roscoe.

Mrs. John Fischer (Christina Waltz) was born December 6, 1893, the daughter of
Jacob and Margaret Bauer Waltz.  They also came to this country from South
Russia and settled in 1901 near Colharbor, N.D.

John and Christina were married January 4, 1916 and lived on the farm northeast
of Roscoe until 1962, when they retired and moved into Roscoe.

They raised their family of five children on the farm. They are: Leah (Mrs.
Glen Jackson) Colorado Springs, Colo.; Bernice (Mrs.  Cecil Wiarda) Castlewood,
S.D.; Marjorie (Mrs.  Edwin Dabbert) Roscoe; Ellwood, Ipswich, S.D. and Jackie
in Geneva, Neb.

They celebrated their 50th wedding anniversary in 1966.

John passed away January 6, 1971 and is buried in Roscoe's Morningside Cemetery.

Christina is a member of St. Paul's Lutheran Church and the Ladies Aid.

WILLIAM (BILL) AND ALVINA FISCHER

Bill Fischer was the son of George and Christina Walz Fischer.  He was born May
8, 1901 in South Russia.  He came to the United states with his family when
only a few months old.  They settled in the Roscoe area, homesteading in
Huntley Township, which is the farm where Dennis Sauer now lives.  Bill had 10
brothers and sisters.  He was confirmed in 1916 at the Lutheran Church in
Roscoe.

Bill married Alvina Hettich, daughter of Michael and Elizabeth Scheuffle
Hettich November 1, 1925.  Alvina was born in South Russia December 19, 1905.
She came to this country in 1911 with her family.  Bill and Alvina lived on a
farm four miles east and one mile south of Roscoe for 35 years.

Alvina was confirmed in July 1920 in the Lutheran Church and has been a member
of St. Paul's all her life. She is a member of the Ladies Aid and the Senior
Citizens and has held offices in both.  Bill was a school board member and
township supervisor for many years.

They had five children, four of them died in infancy. Their only living child
is Jerome William, born April 30, 1937, who is married to Beth Grau of
Faulkton, S.D., living in Roscoe and operating a farm implement business.  They
have three sons.  After Bill's retirement in 1959, they moved to Roscoe.  Bill
died in July 1974 and is buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.  Alvina is still living
in Roscoe and enjoying her "golden years".

JEROME AND BETH FISCHER

Jerome Fischer is the only living child of Bill and Alvina Hettich Fischer.  He
was born April 30, 1937, baptized and confirmed in St. Paul's Lutheran Church.
He attended rural school, four miles east and one mile south of Roscoe in
Bryant Township; graduated from Roscoe High School in 1955.  He entered the
Army in November 1956 and received an honorable discharge in November 1958.

        Kent, Both, Curtis, Jerome and Chad Fischer

After his return from the Army, he started courting a young school teacher from
Faulkton, S.D. who was teaching in the rural school he had attended and who was
staying at his parent's home.  June 21, 1959 this schoolteacher, the former
Beth Grau and Jerome were married.  Beth was born July 28, 1939, at Miranda,
S.D. to Albert and Gladys Bent Grau.  She received her education in rural Faulk
County schools and graduated from Faulkton High School in 1957.  She received
her degree in elementary teaching at NSC in Aberdeen, S.D. Jerome's parents
retired and Jerome and Beth continued to live on the family farm until October
1967 when they had an auction sale and moved to Roscoe.

Jerome worked at Sundstrand Aviation in Belvidere, Ill. for a short time.  He
worked for Spitzer Implement before purchasing the Super Valu Store from Arthur
Scherf in 1968.  Jerome and Beth operated this store for 14 months when an
unfortunate fire destroyed the business.  He then worked for Spitzers until
1976.  At that time he entered into his own business which is called J. Fischer
Sales, and deals in new and used farm machinery.  He is still operating this
business today.

Jerome and Beth have been active in community affairs.  Jerome served as city
councilman for six years, Mayor for two years, Commercial Club president, Fire
Chief, Development Corporation chairman, Chairman of Church Council, and
General Chairman of the Roscoe Centennial.  Both has been teaching most of the
years since their marriage.  She is currently the fourth grade teacher at
Roscoe, having also taught kindergarten and remedial reading.  She has belonged
to various organizations and is at the present time a member of the Roscoe
Commercial Club, Ivy Circle Garden Club, SDEA and NEA.

Three boys were born into this family.  They are Curtis Jerome, born May 7,
1960; Kent Allan, born April 10, 1967 and Chad William, born December 11, 1972.
Curtis graduated from Roscoe High School in May 1978. He attended Black Hills
State College in Spearfish, S.D. for one year.  He owns and operates his own
truck which he leases to Waggoners Trucking Service in Billings, Mont.  Kent is
in High School and Chad is a grade school student at Roscoe.

MATHEW AND ANNA FUHRMANN

Mathew and Anna Usselmann Fuhrmann were born in Russia.  They grew up and were
married there. in the spring of 1906 they brought their family to the United
States and settled on a farm 10 miles southwest of Roscoe.

Mathew was killed in a horse and buggy accident as he was returning to his home
from a trip to town (Roscoe) in 1910.  He is buried in Onaka.

Anna was married to a Mr. Klein and mode her home in North Dakota until her
second husband died. Some time later she was married to Frank Schaeffer and
lived in Aberdeen until his death.  After that she went to live with her oldest
son, Constantine, in Onaka. She died in the fall of 1934 and is buried in Onaka.

Mathew and Anna had five children: Constantine, Joseph, Margaret, Pius and
Elizabeth.

JOSEPH. SR. AND HELEN FUHRMANN

        Back row: Julia, Joe, Barbara and Ottilia; front row: Joseph Sr., Anna
        and Helena Fuhrmann

Joseph Fuhrmann was born March 22, 1887 to Mathew and Anna Usselmann Fuhrmann
in the Odessa area of Russia.  He came to the United States in the spring of
1906 and settled with his parents on a farm about 10 miles southwest of Roscoe.

May 9, 1909 he married Helena Mangold in Bowdle. Helena was the daughter of
Frank and Barbara Marquardt Mangold, and was born October 29, 1890 at Odessa,
Russia.

As a young girl Helena worked for the Mathew Fuhrmann family and came to the
United States with them. After arriving here she took a job at the Ward Hotel in
Aberdeen until her marriage.  Joe and Helena settled on a claim near Bison,
S.D. but returned to the family farm when Joe's father was killed in a horse
and buggy accident.  They remained there until their retirement in 1944 when
they moved to Roscoe.

They have five children: Joseph Jr. who married Helen Reinboldt.  They have two
sons and after having lived many years on the family farm they moved to Aberdeen
where they still live; Julia, who married Conrad B. Menzia and lives in Roscoe.
They have one daughter; Barbara, who married Anton Mardian, Jr. and has one
daughter.  They live on a farm west of Roscoe; Tillie, widowed, is living in
Aberdeen; Anna, married to Steve Malsom, has six children and lives in Council
Bluffs, la.

Joe and Helen were members of St. Thomas Catholic Church where Helen was a
member of the Ladies Altar Society and Joe served as a trustee.

Joe died of a heart attack May 4, 1954 at the Martin Scherf farm where he was
working part-time.  Helena passed away May 1, 1974 at the Colonial Manor in
Ipswich, where she had been living.  Both are buried in Morningside Cemetery.

JOE AND HELEN FUHRMANN

Joe Fuhrmann, Jr. was born September 28, 1909 in the Roscoe area to Joseph and
Helena Mangold Furhmann. Joe's parents were born in Romaina, Russia and came to
the United States in May 1907 settling in the Roscoe area.

Helen Reinbold Fuhrmann was born September 3 in the Hosmer area to Adam and
Kundegunda (Cora) Kraft Reinbold.  Helen's parents were born in Odessa, Russia.
Her father came to the United States in 1880 settling near Bowdle, S.D. and
her mother in 1884 to the Webster, S.D. area and later to the Bowdle area.

Helen and Joe got married November 7, 1935 and settled on a farm southwest of
Roscoe where they raised their family of two boys, Jerome and Norbert. They
retired in 1971 and moved to Aberdeen, S.D. They are of the Catholic faith.

Their oldest son, Jerome, married Joan Vogel of Onaka, S.D., lives in Antioch,
Calif., and works with Pacific Gas and Electric Co. His wife, Joan, works in
real estate and is office manager in an orthodontist's office. They have four
children.

Their youngest son, Norbert, married Sharon Volk of Ipswich, S.D. and farms on
the family homestead south- west of Roscoe.  They have three children.

NORBERT AND SHARON FUHRMANN

Norbert Fuhrmann was born December 24, 1945 in Aberdeen, S.D. to Joe and Helen
Reinbold Fuhrmann. Both of his parents were born in the United States and
always lived in the Roscoe area.  They are presently living in Aberdeen.

Sharon Volk Fuhrmann was born September 2, 1948 in Ipswich, S.D. to Jake and
Frances Mitzel Volk.  Both of her parents were born in the United States and
lived in Hague, N.D.; Bowdle S.D.; and Ipswich, S.D. where they presently
reside.

Sharon and Norbert were married June 25, 1966 at Holy Cross Church in Ipswich.
They settled on his folk's farm southwest of Roscoe.  In 1972 they took over
ownership of the farm.

Fuhrmanns are members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.  Norbert is a
past school board member and is supervisor of their township board.  He served
in the National Guard from 1963-69 after graduating from Roscoe High.  Sharon
is a member of St. Thomas Altar Society.  She is a graduate of Ipswich High.

Three children were born to this union: Steven, born December 11, 1966; Rick,
born November 23, 1967; and Diane, born November 11, 1968.  They are students
at Roscoe High.

PIUS FUHRMANN FAMILY

        Pius and Caroline Fuhrmann

Pius Fuhrmann was born to Matthias and Anna Usselman Fuhrmann February 14, 1897
in Odessa, Russia.  At the age of 10, he came to the United States with his
parents, homesteading southwest of Roscoe. October 27, 1919, Pius married
Theresa Roth, daughter of Michael and Mary Schnur Roth.  They had four
children.  Lena, married to Thomas Schwan who passed away in 1969, has two
children, Tom and Ronnie, and lives in Aberdeen.  Helen, married Paul Salwei,
they have three children, Jim, Therese and Richard, and they live in Aberdeen.
Anna, married Clarence Banik, has six children, Gene, Bill, Theresa, Clarence,
Jr., Timothy and Johnny, and they live in Spokane, Wash.  Moth, married LaVern
Grismer, has eight children, Bernard, Jack, Cleo, Bruce, Ava Marie (deceased),
Loren, Sherry and Carol Ann.  They live in Roscoe.

Pius's wife Theresa, died in 1930.  He then married Caroline Leboldus, daughter
of Andrew and Marian Keller Leboldus.  They had ten children.  Ramona
(deceased) was married to Wilbert Weron; Raymond (deceased); Madonna, married
Tony Grismer, has seven children, Robin, Ramona, Rhonda, Roy, Russell, Rodney
and Roger (deceased).  They live on a farm west of Hosmer.  Luella married
Albert Hopfinger, has four children, Brenda, Dale, Linda and Shelly.  They live
in Aberdeen.  Ray married Angie Schwingler, has two children, Greg and Bonita,
and they live in Arroyo Grande, Calif.  Jim, married Darlene Geier, has three
children, DeAnn, Cindy and Debbie, and they live in Billings, Mont.  Kenny
married Wanda Zahalka, they have two children, Sheila and Sandra, and they live
in Aberdeen.  Ronnie, married Ruby Martell, has two children, Christopher and
Brent, and they live in Omaha, Neb.  Virginia, married Tony Binfet, they have
three children, Troy, Tracy and Tanya and live in Grandview Wash.  Marcella
lives in Dallas, Texas.

Pius was in the military service during WW I.  He was a charter member of the
Roscoe American Legion.  Pius and Caroline retired from farming in 1963 and
moved to Roscoe.  Pius died July 17, 1967 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery
at Roscoe.  Caroline moved to Aberdeen in 1978 where she lives at the present
time.

MATH AND LAVERN FUHRMANN

        Back row: Bruce, Bernard, Jackie; middle row: Cleo, Loren, Sherry;
        front row: LaVern, Carol Ann and Moth Fuhrmann

Math Fuhrmann was born March 18, 1929 in rural Roscoe, to Pius and Theresa Roth
Fuhrmann.  Pius was born February 14, 1897 in Odessa, Russia, and came to South
Dakota when he was 10 years old.  Theresa was born May 18, 1900 in the Roscoe
area.  She died when Math was 18 months old.  Pius was remarried to Caroline
Leboldus, who was born March 10, 1910 in Selz, N.D. Pius was laid to rest July
17, 1967.  Caroline is presently living in Aberdeen.

LaVern Grismer was born January 14, 1937 in rural Hosmer, S.D. to Jacob and Eva
Heilman Grismer, who were both born in the Hosmer-Bowdle area.

Math and LaVern were married October 11, 1954, in the Holy Trinity Catholic
Church in Hosmer.  They bought a farm one and one-half miles west of Roscoe
where they raised grain, milked cows and raised stock cattle for 28 years.  Due
to problems with their health, they sold the farm in October 1981 and built a
new home in Roscoe which they moved into in March of 1982.  They still do some
farming and have stock cows.

Math was on the Glen Township board for several years, on the Roscoe School
board for one term, director at the Roscoe Cenex for the post 10 years and is
still on at this time.

Math and LaVern are both active members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in
Roscoe, where LaVern has been a member of the Altar Society for 28 years, held
offices for several years and taught CCD for eight years.  She belongs to the
How and Why Extension Club for four years and was Secretary-Treasurer the post
year and Secretary-Treasurer of the Parents Music Ass'n.  They both belong to
the Roscoe Commercial Club.

They have nine children: Bernard, born August 28, 1955, attended Roscoe High
School, is a graduate of NSC and is an Underwriter for Farmers Union Insurance
in Denver; Jack, born July 23, 1957, attended Roscoe High School and is a
graduate of NSC.  He is an Insurance Adjuster for Farmers Insurance Co. in
Boulder, Colo.; Cleo, born March 20, 1959, attended Roscoe High School, then
Aberdeen School of Commerce for a secretarial course.  She married Roger
Arneson and has a daughter and a son.  She is employed by the K. O. Lee Co. in
Aberdeen; Bruce, born June 24, 1960, attended Roscoe High School.  He bought a
farm in the fall of 1981 where he raises grain and livestock; Ave Marie was
born November 9, 1961 and became an angel November 10, 1961; Loren, born April
2, 1963, attended Roscoe High School and will be a sophomore at NSC.  He is
working at Bauer farms.  Sherry was born December 11, 1964, is a senior at
Roscoe High School. Carol Ann, born January 9, 1970, will be in the seventh
grade at Roscoe School.

ROBERT AND MARJORIE GARNER

Robert Garner was born at Wessington, S.D. to James and Sadie Garner, August
31, 1925.  He comes from a family of five children, two brothers and two
sisters.  His Dad and Mother came from Minnesota, his Mother's maiden name was
DeVrient.  They came to South Dakota and farmed most of their lifetime.  Robert
married Marjorie Langbehn July 10, 1946.  Marjorie came from a family of eight
children, some were born in Iowa and the rest in South Dakota.  Marjorie was
born at Wolsey, S.D. May 10, 1930 to Arthur and Anna Longbehn who originally
came from Iowa.  Marjories' Mother's maiden name was Dammann.  Marjorie had two
brothers and five sisters, one brother died in infancy and two more are
deceased now.

Robert and Marjorie lived on a farm near Wessington for a few years before
moving into town.  He started working for Cenex in 1953 and in 1956 was
transferred from Wessington to Lake Preston, S.D. as assistant manager.  In
1957 they moved to Roscoe, S.D. where Robert was manager of Cenex.  Marjorie
has been bookkeeper at Cenex since that time too.  They have three children.

Reed Alan Garner was born April 20, 1949, started school in Wessington and came
to Roscoe starting third grade.  He finished high school in Roscoe and went to
the School of Commerce in Aberdeen, S.D. for awhile and then he went to work
for the Mel Pickard Bee Farm. Reed married Pamela Akkerman June 4,1976.  Pam is
a teacher at the Roscoe School.  They have two daughters, Megan, who was born
March 16, 1977 and Bethany, who was born January 29, 1979.

Ricky Lee Garner was born May 8, 1957 at Huron, S.D. He went all twelve years
of school at Roscoe.  He worked for the Mel Pickard Bee Farm a few years and
then went to work for Wiedrich Construction where he is still working.  Rick
married Jackie Breitling June 4, 1978.  Jackie is a nurse at the Ipswich
Hospital and they live in Ipswich.

Robin Roe Garner was born November 19, 1963 at Bowdle, S.D. She graduated from
Roscoe High School in 1982 and her plans include attending Black Hills State
College in Spearfish, S.D. She has been employed part time at Donnie's Cafe in
Roscoe for the last two years.

REED AND PAMELA GARNER

Reed was born April 20, 1949 to Robert and Marjorie Garner in Huron, South
Dakota.  They lived in Wessington until 1957 when they moved to their present
home in Roscoe.  Reed attended school in Roscoe and graduated in 1967.  He has
mainly been employed by the Pickard Bee Farm of Roscoe since that time.

He married Pamela Ackerman June 4, 1976.  Pam was born in Watertown February
18, 1950 to Ernest and Phyllis Ackerman.  She attended the Watertown Public
Schools until graduation in 1968.  After high school she attended NSC and
obtained a BS degree in elementary education.  Pam has taught for 11 years in
the Roscoe School System and has returned to NSC for her masters degree in
guidance and counseling.  She is presently teaching English and Social Studies
at the Junior High level and is guidance counselor for K - 12.

Pam and Reed have two children, Megan born March 16, 1977 and Bethany born
January 29, 1979.

Reed is a member of the Volunteer Fire Deportment and an avid sportsman.  Pam
is a member of SDEA, NEA and Garden Club.

ADAM AND THERESA GEIER (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Adam Geier was born in Strasburg, Russia in 1886.  He came to America in 1908,
where he settled near Bowdle and married Theresa Heintz in 1912.  In 1916, they
moved to a farm five miles southwest of Roscoe where they farmed for 30 years
until they retired in 1946 and moved to Aberdeen.

Their children are Frank, Clemens, Joseph, Wendlin, Pius, Katherine and Rosalie.

CLEMENS AND MINNIE GEIER

        Leo, Mary, Darlene, Minnie, Andrew, Clemens, Sr., Clemens, Jr., and
        Theresa Geier

Clemens Geier was born January 18, 1918 near Roscoe to Adam and Theresia Heintz
Geier.  He grew up on a farm southwest of Roscoe where he attended school.  He
married Minnie Vetch, daughter of Antone and Mary Kraft Vetch, at St. Thomas
Catholic Church in Roscoe.  They started farming southwest of Roscoe where they
still reside.

Clemens entered the United States Army in April 1942 and was stationed in the
Pacific Islands until his discharge in July 1945.  During his years of farming,
Clemens served as Glover Township assessor, treasurer, supervisor and school
board member.  He also served as a Roscoe school board member.  He served six
years on the Edmunds County selective service board.  He is a member of the
Aberdeen VFW and the American Legion Post 259 in Roscoe where he was once a
commander.  He is also active in the Republican Party and is a notary public.

Clemen's father, Adam Geier, was born near Odessa, Russia October 20, 1886 and
came to the United States September 27, 1908.  He lived with his brother John in
Bowdle, S.D. where he was employed.  He married Theresia Heintz in Bowdle
September 23, 1912. Theresia Heintz was born near Odessa, Russia August 19,
1892 and came to the United States with her parents in 1911. They moved to a
farm southwest of Roscoe in 1916 where they raised their family of nine
children.  They moved to Aberdeen in 1946.  Adam died December 3, 1961 and
Theresia died June 22, 1972.

Minnie Vetch Geier was born June 19, 1923 near Roscoe to Antone and Mary Kraft
Vetch.  She lived on various farms before moving to Roscoe in 1931 where she
attended school.  She married Clemens May 27, 1941 and has lived on a farm in
Glover Township her entire married life.  She is a member of St. Thomas Altar
Society.

Minnie's father, Antone Vetch, was born in Ipswich, S.D. May 1, 1899.  He
farmed until 1931 when he moved to Roscoe where he was employed.  He married
Mary Kraft April 3, 1923 in Aberdeen.  Mary was born September 11, 1905 near
Onaka, S.D. They moved to Igloo, S.D. in 1942 where they worked at the army
depot.  In 1952 they moved to Edgemont, S.D. where they still reside.

Clemens and Minnie had six children.  Darlene was born October 31, 1941 in
Bowdle, S.D. She married James Fuhrmann of Roscoe.  She resides in Billings,
Mont. where she works in the hospital as an obstetrical technician.  They have
three children, DeAnn, Cindy, and Debbie.

Mary was born May 25, 1946 in Aberdeen.  She married Richard Dabbert of Roscoe.
They presently live in Altus, Okla. where Richard is stationed with the U.S.
Air Force.  Mary works for the civil service as a secretary. They have two
children, Michele and Michael.

Theresa was born September 4, 1949 in Aberdeen. She married Robert Malsom of
Roscoe.  They farm four miles north of Roscoe.  They have four children,
Melissa, Daniel, Lori and Darin.

Clemens A. was born January 3, 1951 in Aberdeen.  He married Bonnie
Kirschenmann of Ipswich.  They are farming two and one-half miles south of
Roscoe.  They have four children, Steven, Craig, Troy and Stacey.

Leo was born July 15, 1953 in Aberdeen.  He married Celeste Schnaible of
Roscoe.  They live in Hardin, Mont., where Leo works as an auto mechanic.  They
have three children, Jason, Chris and Nikki.

Andrew was born February 27, 1959.  He married Cynthia Winkler of Roscoe.  He
works as a tune-up technician in Aberdeen where they live.

WENDLIN AND MAGDALENA GEIER

Wendlin Geier was born in Roscoe June 26, 1926 to Adam and Theresia Heintz
Geier.  January 13, 1947, he married Magdalena Malsom at Hillsview, S.D. She was
the daughter of Kasper and Margaret Fischer Malsom. She was born October 12,
1925 at Hillsview.  They took over the Geier homestead farm and have lived there
ever since.  They are members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.

They had 12 children: Donald, lives in Custer, S.D.; Rosie, married Norbert
Hoerner and lives near Bowdle, S.D.; Charles, married Carol Staley and lives in
Strasburg, Colo.; Margaret, married to Kasper Unser and lives in Ipswich, S.D.;
Dorothy, lives in Aurora, Colo.; Gary, lives at home (Roscoe) at this writing;
Michael, married to Kim Keller and lives in Rapid City, S.D.; David, married to
Lori Williams and lives in Yankton, S.D.; Bruce, married to Cindy Kosters and
lives in Aberdeen, S.D.; and Kenneth is a student at Roscoe High.  Donald's
twin brother, Adam, died at birth and Peter died when he was eight days old.

CHRISTIAN AND MARIE GEIST

Christian Geist was born September 28, 1873, at Bender, Russia.  In 1898 he
married Marie Mertz, who was born in January 1873 in Odessa, Russia.  They came
to the United States via Hamburg, Germany, arriving in New York City aboard the
"Celtic" in 1907.  They lived in the Tolstoy, Hoven, Faulkton, Bowdle and
Roscoe areas before moving to Aberdeen in 1936.

They had 12 children of whom only nine names are known, Bertha (Marks)
deceased; Jacob, deceased; Christian in Wisconsin; Leopold, deceased; August,
deceased; Emil in Faulkton; Fred in South St. Paul, Minn.; Mary Ahlman in So.
St. Paul and Gottlieb in So. St. Paul.

They were members of the Lutheran Church.

Christian died in August 1955 and Marie died in March 1956.  They are buried in
Morningside Cemetery, Roscoe.

JACOB AND LYDIA GEIST

Jacob Geist was born April 28, 1900 in South Russia and came to South Dakota
with his parents in 1907. They were Christian and Marie Mertz Geist.  They
moved to Roscoe in 1916, coming from the Bowdle area.

Lydia Knittel was born October 4, 1902 in Russia, the daughter of Conrad and
Regina Guenthner Knittel.  They came to South Dakota in October 1903, arriving
here on the Sioux Railroad line.

Jacob and Lydia were married March 20, 1920 at St. Paul's Lutheran Church.
Jacob was the Roscoe City Marshall from April 1931 to December 1935.  He often
told of those years as being a time when many "Bums" rode the freight trains
and would get off here.  It was the City Marshall's job to deal with them which
sometimes become quite a problem, once even having to pull one of them off the
railroad tracks in the path of an oncoming train, where the "bum" had laid down
in an effort to "end it all" and refused to move.

In 1936, they moved to a farm nine and one-half miles south of Roscoe where
they lived until retirement in 1955.  He moved back to Roscoe and worked for the
Edmunds County Highway Department and was also a part-time city cop.

They had eight children, Leontina, died in infancy; Margaret died at age 13;
Minnie died in infancy; Alvina (Mrs.  Donald Hoilien) of Ipswich; Reinhold died
in 1966 at Amherst, S.D.; Irene (Mrs.  Joe Braun) of Ipswich; Vivian (Mrs.
Herbert Wiederich) of Roscoe and Herbert of Roscoe.

Jacob died May 31, 1970 and Lydia died December 6, 1955 and both are buried in
Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

They were members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church.

HERBERT AND DORIS GEIST

        Kurt, Herbert, Doris and Clayton Geist

Herbert C. Geist was born January 4, 1935 at Roscoe to Jacob and Lydia Knittel
Geist.  He lived in Roscoe until 1936 when he moved on a farm south of Roscoe
with his family.  He attended the rural school near his home.

Doris Brandner was born May 12, 1934 in McPherson County, the daughter of Emil
and Emma Hieb Brandner. In 1944, she moved to a farm two and one-half miles
south of Roscoe with her parents.  She attended the Roscoe school, graduating
from high school in 1953.

Herbert and Doris were married January 24, 1954 in St. Paul's Lutheran Church
in Roscoe.  They made their home on the farm Herbert grew up on and are still
living there.

They have two sons, Clayton, who farms with his father and is a musician with a
dance band, and Kurt, who works in the Human Services Center in Yankton, S.D.

They are members of the St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

LEOPOLD AND PAULINE GEIST

Leopold Geist was born May 16, 1909, in the Tolstoy, S.D. area, to Christian
and Marie Mertz Geist.  Pauline Aman was born in rural Hosmer, S.D. July 30,
1915, to John and Barbara Kolienberger Aman.  She came to Roscoe at the age of
nine.

Leo and Pauline were married March 20, 1931, at St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Roscoe.  They settled on a farm southwest of Roscoe and later farmed west of
Roscoe until 1947 when they moved to town.  Leo worked for Spitzer's Implement
for 18 years and for a short time for the Edmunds County Highway Department.
Leo and Pauline worked as custodians at the Lutheran Church for 24 years.

They had one daughter, Edna, who was married to Milbert Schumacher and is
widowed.  She has two children, Marlys and Ronald.

Leo passed away June 23, 1977 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

Pauline is a member of the Lutheran Ladies Aid and the Roscoe Senior Citizens.

SYLVESTER AND AGATHA GLATT

Agatha Englehart Glatt was born December 22, 1875 and Sylvester Glatt was born
November 1, 1872.  They immigrated to the United States from Russia in 1900.
They settled on a farm near Venturia, N.D. May 1, 1909 they moved to a farm
south of Roscoe, where they lived until March 1940, when they retired and moved
to Roscoe.

Sylvester died September 4, 1940 and Agatha died February 2, 1958.  To this
union were born 11 children: Katherine, Annie, Clara, Walburg, Sebastian, Fred,
Caroline, Tony, Sylvester, Jr., Lawrence, and Lena.

Katherine married Micheal Steffans.  They lived in Devils Lake, N.D. They had
11 children.  They are both deceased.

Annie married Frank Roth.  They farmed south of Roscoe.  After their
retirement, they moved into Roscoe. They had five children: Clara, Sebastian,
Andy, Eva, and Alice.  Sebastian died in 1979, Frank in 1950 and Annie in 1981.

Clara married Stanis Uhrich November 6, 1921.  They farmed south of Roscoe.
Following their retirement in 1969 they moved to Roscoe.  They had seven
children: Christina, Walburg, Rose, Eva, Larry, Tom, and Jean. Stanis died
April 10, 1981.

Walburg married Adam Meier.  They farmed near Venturia, N.D., moving to
Zeeland, N.D. after their retirement.  They had five children: Sylvester,
Wilfred, Wilbert, Martina, and Micheal.  Walburg died March 29, 1980.

Sebastian was born in 1906 and died in 1924.

Fred married Eva Meier.  They lived on a farm south of Roscoe.  They later
moved to Billings, Mont.  They had five children: Loretta, Deloris, Arnold,
James, and Larry.  Deloris was born November 19, 1933 and died April 27, 1936.
Fred died August 1, 1973. Caroline married Frank Geier.  They farmed near
Roscoe, later moving to Aberdeen.  They had three children, Jerome, Beverly and
Phylis.  Jerome died at the age of 15.  Frank died July 1, 1970 and Caroline
died in 1971.  Both are buried in St, Mary's Cemetery in Aberdeen.

Tony served in the Armed Forces from 1939 to 1946. After retiring he resided in
Roscoe and Aberdeen.  He died November 23, 1970.

Sylvester Jr. married Minnie Voeller.  They farmed south of Roscoe, moving into
Roscoe in 1940.  Sylvester, along with his brother Lawrence, bought and operated
Benders Pool Hall in April 1940.  They sold the pool hall in 1943 and Sylvester
bought the Clothing Store, also from Bender.  In 1950, he sold the clothing
store and they moved to Aurora, Colo., where they still reside. They had two
children Sylvia and Gloria Ann.  Gloria Ann was born May 23, 1940 and died
September 19, 1944.

Lawrence operated the Pool Hall along with his brother Sylvester until March
1941, when he went into the Army.  He received his discharge in 1945.  He bought
Kohler Drug at Iroquois, S.D. and re-named it Glatt Corner Store.  He married
Bonnie Warner in June 1948. In 1951, he sold the store and moved to Reno, Nev.
He died May 23, 1973 and is buried at Willamette National Cemetery in Portland,
Ore.

Lena was born March 6, 1919.  She worked for Sylvester at the pool hall until
after he sold it.  She then worked for National Tea Store in Aberdeen.  Later
she worked for Lawrence at the Corner Store in Iroquois, S.D. Lena married
Wayne Augenbough June 25, 1949. They farmed near Iroquois until September 1956.
They moved to Oregon City, Ore. where they still reside. They had five
children: Donnie, Hope, Jean Marie, Peggy, and Mark.  Jean Marie died May 19,
1954 and is buried at Iroquois.

THEOPHIL AND JOHANNA GOETZ

Theophil Goetz was born in Germany in 1839, one of two sons of Joseph and
Elizabeth Goetz.  In 1854, they migrated to Odessa, Russia, and later to
Kleinlabenthal, Russia, where they were farmers.  Theophil worked for his
father until 1868 when he married Johanna Sahli, who was also born in Germany
in 1844. At this time he become a harness maker.

Theophil and Johanna came to the United States in 1904 and settled in the
Hillsview, S.D. area.  They moved to  Hoven, S.D. for a time and in 1909, they
moved to  Roscoe.  In 1910, they sold their farm and moved to  Bow island,
Alberta, Canada.

Theophil passed away in 1926 and Johanna passed away November 7, 1937.  They
ore both buried in Canada.  Their children were Michael, Siegfried, Valentine,
Joseph, Theophil and Ignatz.

MICHAEL AND ALOISA GOETZ

Michael Goetz was born in Russia January 1, 1870, the oldest son of Theophil
and Johanna Sahli Goetz.  They were farmers in Kleinlabenthal.

Michael joined the Russian Army in 1891 and served until 1895.  Soon after his
discharge, he married Aloisa Meier, who was born July 15, 1871 in Russia.  He
worked in a flour mill until 1898 when he and Aloisa came to the United States.
They settled near Hillsview, S.D. Michael worked as a farm helper in this area
until 1904 when the family moved to Ipswich, S.D. Later that same year they
moved to Mina, S.D. where they remained about a year.  They then returned to
Ipswich where they lived until November 1906, when they moved to Millard, S.D.
In 1908, the family moved to a farm southwest of Roscoe where Michael lived
until he retired and moved to Roscoe in 1935.

When Michael and Aloisa arrived in this country, they began to save money to
help the rest of the family to leave Russia.  In 1901, enough money had been
saved so that his brother Valentine could join them.  In 1904, the two brothers
had saved enough to bring their parents, Theophil and Johanna, and brothers
Joseph, Theophil and Ignatz to this country.

Michael and Aloisa had ten children.  Two sons and one daughter died as
infants.  The other seven were George, Johanna, Katherine, Margaret, Roy, Peter
and Michael.

Aloisa passed away December 25, 1934 and is buried in Onaka, S.D. Michael
passed away December 28, 1943, and is buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

ROY AND KATHERINE GOETZ

        Jerome, Elroy, Joe, Katherine, Roy, Betty, Leonard, Elmer and James
        Goetz

Roy Goetz was born March 13, 1907 at Millard, S.D., the son of Michael and
Aloisa Meier Goetz, who came to the United States from Russia in 1898.  He came
to the farm southwest of Roscoe with his parents in 1908. Roy received his
education in the rural school near his home and learned his trade as a farmer
working with his father as he was growing up.

He married Katherine Deis November 7, 1928 at Onaka, S.D. She was born at Onaka
September 21, 1909 to Michael and Eva Feist Deis.  They settled on a farm
one-half mile east of the Goetz family home.

Roy and Katherine had seven children.  They are: Leonard, who is living on the
home farm.  Elmer, who is married to Aileen Ahern and living in Aberdeen.  They
have four children.  Joseph, married to Anna Geier, lives on a farm in Hillside
Township.  They have five daughters.  Elroy, married to Jean Alwin, lives on a
farm west of Roscoe and has four daughters.  Betty, married to Donald Ellwein,
lives in Auburn, Wash. and has two children. Jerome, married to Linda Hoven,
lives in Minneapolis, and has three children.  James, married to Angie Salwei,
and lives in Aberdeen, S.D.

Roy served as treasurer of Hillside Township for many years.  They were always
active members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.  Katherine is a member
of the Ladies Altar Society.  They retired from the farm in December 1959 and
moved to Roscoe.

Roy passed away June 16, 1979 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

JOSEPH AND ANNA GOETZ

        Joe, Ann, JoAnn, Jane, Mary, Francine and Kristie Goetz

Joseph Goetz was born August 7, 1934 near Roscoe to Roy and Katherine Deis
Goetz.  He attended the some rural school as his father had before him and like
his father, he too learned the farming trade as he was growing up.  Joe served
in the Army from February 1957 to December 1958.  He received his basic training
at Fort Chaffee, Arkansas and served 15 months in Korea.

Joe married Anna Geier November 11, 1958 at Bowdle, S.D. Anna was born April
10, 1936 to Anton and Mary Malsom Geier at Bowdle.  Anna is a graduate of
Bowdle High School and Presentation College in Aberdeen, S.D. She taught in
rural school for four years. Joe and Anna settled on the farm just one mile
east of his boyhood home.  Here they raise livestock and crops of small grain.
They built a new home in 1970 to provide more space for their family of five
daughters.

The daughters are: Mary Antonette, born October 18, 1959, married to Walter
Grismer and living on a farm near Hosmer, S.D.

Jane Ann and JoAnn Marie, twins, born November 27, 1960.  Jane is married to
Chip Fuhrman and living in Mansfield, S.D. JoAnn is married to Monte Troske and
living in Turton, S.D.

Francine Katherine, born November 21, 1963, is a student at NSC in Aberdeen.

Kristie Lee, born May 4, 1968, is a student in the Roscoe School.

Joe is a director at the Roscoe Cenex, he is on the Parish Council at St.
Thomas Catholic Church and a member of the Roscoe American Legion.  Anna is a
member of the St. Thomas Altar Society, having served as its president and
secretary in the past, and is a member of the American Legion Auxiliary.

ELROY AND JEAN GOETZ

        Back row: Virginia, Michele, Sharon and Marie; front row: James, Angie,
        Elroy and Joan Goetz

Elroy was born March 2, 1936, to Roy and Katherine Deis Goetz.  He grew up in
Hillside Township and attended Hillside country school.  Elroy served in the U.
S. Army from October 1958 to July 1960.  He served as a director of the Roscoe
Farmers Union from 1971 to 1980.  He married Jean Alwin June 8, 1963, at
Watertown, S.D.

Jean was born June 1, 1940, in Watertown, S.D. She graduated from Watertown
High School in 1958.  Jean graduated from Aberdeen Presentation School of
Nursing in 1962 and was employed at the Brown Clinic in Watertown prior to
marriage.  Elroy and Jean have been farming one-half mile west of Roscoe in Glen
Township since their marriage.

They have four daughters: Marie born April 16, 1965. She was the 1982 Roscoe
Girls State delegate.  Sharon was born May 11, 1966 and Virginia October 18,
1967. Virginia designed the Roscoe Centennial emblem. Michele born January 30,
1971.  The girls are all students of Roscoe Public School.

Elroy's hobbies are fishing and hunting.

MIKE AND LAURA GOETZ

Mike M. Goetz was a son of Micheal Goetz, who was born in 1870 in Odessa,
Russia, and his wife Aloisa Meier, who was also born in Russia in the year 1871.
Mike was the youngest of seven children, George was the oldest, born in 1898
and he died in 1979.

Johanna was born in 1900, and married Frank Malsom in 1920.  Katherine was born
in 1901, and married Valentine Roth in 1923.  Margaret was born in 1904 and
married George Hutmacher in 1962.  Roy was born in 1907, married Katherine Deis
in 1928.  Roy died in 1979.

Pete was born in 1908, and married Ludwena Malsom in 1933.

Mike was born in Hillside Township May 21, 1910, and married Laura Kaiser April
30, 1935.  Her father was Mathew Kaiser, who was born in 1882 and died in 1931.
Her mother was Katherine Rausch, who was born in 1890.  The family consisted of
two sons, Robert, the oldest, was born June 24, 1936 at Onaka, S.D. He is
married and has one daughter, and is living at Grants Pass, Ore.  Second son
Jim, was born June 6, 1938, at Yakima, Wash. and he is married and has two
girls and one son.

Mike stayed with his parents on the family farm until he got married April 30,
1935.  They set up farming in Hillside Township on a farm owned by John
Heckenlaible, farming until the fall of 1937.  Due to the drought and depressed
farm prices, they decided to sell out and try their luck out west.  After the
farm sale they hooked a trailer onto the 1933 Ford car and headed for Yakima,
Wash., got a job on an irrigation construction project, wages at that time
being 55 cents per hour, and this was union wage and this work lasted until
1940.  Another move was undertaken, this time moving to Son Diego, and going
into the Home Building Construction work.  The wages by this time had increased
to 85 cents per hour, also union wages.  This work lasted until 1941 when the
war broke out, and then he started work in an aircraft factory, being the lead
man in the painting department, staying in this work until 1943.  At this time
he entered civil service. This work was in Naval Housing Maintenance in the
purchasing department and at times worked in the vehicle body shop until 1963 a
transfer was mode to the Naval Air Station, as supervisor of air craft
maintenance. He was in this position until his retirement from civil service in
1970.  After this retirement, he did miscellaneous work, mainly auto body work
and auto painting and also house interior and exterior pointing until 1975, at
which time a move was made back to Roscoe, S.D. where they purchased the Jacob
Miller residence.

Laura has worked in a fruit cannery in Yakima, Wash. for three years, and after
moving to San Diego she also worked in aircraft work, mostly in the sheetmetal
department for four years.  She worked in the school and hospital food
department for five years, being a homemaker in the between times.

Hobbies are fishing, bowling, traveling and Mike was a pitcher in the different
baseball leagues in the Roscoe and Loyalton area.

JOHN J. AND KATHERINE GORNE

        Back row: Jacob, Rose, Emil and John; front row: Mellita and Katherine
        Gorne

John J. Gorne was born January 13, 1869 in Crimea, Russia.  Katherine Scherer
was born June 20, 1873, also in Crimea.  They were married in February, 1894,
and came to this country and the Roscoe area in 1905.

They had nine children: Margaret, born and died in Russia; Albert, died in 1913
and is buried in Cloyd Valley Cemetery; Jacob, married Elsie Walz, had four
children: Ervin, Lorraine, Orville and Janice.  They were Roscoe area farmers;
Anton, married Freida Galster, retired farmer living in Balsom Lake, Wis. and
has one son, Raymond; John, lived on the family farm, died in 1959 and is
buried in the Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe; Rose, married Art Hintz, lives at
Bowdle, S.D. They were farmers and laborers and have one son, Gordon.

Emil, married Viola Miller, is a retired farmer and businessman.  He now lives
in Colton, Ore. and has one son, Ivan; Mellita, who is married to Elmer Ellwein
and are semi-retired on their farm northwest of Roscoe. They have two sons
Dennis and Larry; and Matilda Julian of Huntsville, Ala., who is retired and
has a stepson and step-daughter.

John died March 29, 1931 and Katherine March 27, 1959.  They are both buried in
Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

JACOB AND ELSIE GORNE

Jacob Gorne was born February 1, 1898 at Crimea, Russia, the son of John J. and
Katherine Scherer Gorne.

They came to the United States and the farm southwest of Roscoe in 1905.

Elsie Walz was born July 30, 1900 at rural Bowdle, S.D. to Fredrick and
Margretha Becker Walz who came to the United States to Freeman, S.D. in 1885
and the Bowdle area in 1900.

        Back row: Orville, Lorraine and Erwin; front row: Jacob, Janice and
        Elsie Gorne

Jacob and Elsie were married March 28, 1922 at Theodore Lutheran Church
southwest of Bowdle.  They settled on a farm southwest of Roscoe until they
retired in 1956 and moved into Roscoe.

Their children are: Ervin, a factory laborer, living in Minneapolis; Lorraine,
married Erwin Baer, and lives on the Gorne family farm; Orville, married

Bookkeeping and Tax Service in Aberdeen; Janice, married Larry Dyer.  She's a
former parochial school teacher and is now a librarian in Grand Island, Neb.

Jacob died March 24, 1967 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.
Elsie lives with her daughter and son-in-law, Lorraine and Ervin Baer.

ALLAN AND SHARON GRAU

        Front row: Amy and Christina; back row: Allan, Jodi, Sharon and Brandi
        Jo Grau

Allan John Grau was born to Albert William and Gladys (Bent) Grau May 29, 1949

LEONE (JOHNSON) GREGOR (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Leone's parents moved to Roscoe in 1886 from Sedan, Kan. and several years
later moved to a farm three miles north of town.  Among the teachers she
remembered were Nellie Decker, Eva Elford and Ruth Witter.  Her father died in
1899 and they moved to town and then to Iowa in 1901 or 1902.  She remembers her
mother telling about holding a train ready for women and children in case of an
Indian raid.

FRED AND IRENE GUTHMILLER

Fred Guthmiller was born January 18, 1905 in Neudorf, Russia to Jacob and
Mathilda Bieber Guthmiller.  The family came to the United States in 1909 and
settled in the Hillsview, S.D. area.  In 1914 the family moved to the Roscoe
area.

Irene Lutz Guthmiller was born December 25, 1909 in Greenway, S.D. to Jacob and
Emelia Ulmer Lutz. Irene's parents were born in the United States.

        Back row: Morris Hettich, Dennis Kotschevar, Gunnar Eriksson and Melvin
        Himmerich; middle row: Arlene Hettich, Inez Kotschevar, Betty Eriksson
        and Renetta Himmerich; front row: Robert and Shirley Guthmiller, Linda
        Roehrich, Irene, Fred, Sharon and Gerald Guthmiller

Fred and Irene were married January 9, 1928 in Ipswich, S.D. They built their
first home three miles north and two and one half miles east of Roscoe which was
one-half mile east of Fred's parent's farm.  They moved to Roscoe in 1965.
Fred did carpenter work and still builds cabinets.  They remodeled the house in
which they live which was built by Gene Licht.

Their children are: Renetta, Betty, Inez, Robert, Gerold, Arlene and Linda.

Renetta was born November 14, 1929, married Melvin Himmerich September 14,
1947, and they reside in Minneapolis.

Betty Ann was born March 9, 1932, married Gunnar Eriksson on April 21, 1954,
and resides in Rollo, N.D.

Inez was born August 20, 1937, married Dennis Kotschev on July 2, 1961, and
they reside in Enderlin, N.D.

Robert was born November 17, 1938, married Shirley Ernst August 21, 1960 and
resides in Aberdeen.

and resides on the family farm northeast of Roscoe.

Arlene was born January 13, 1942, married Morris Hettich November 21, 1961 and
lives in Aberdeen.

Linda was born February 5, 1948, married Charles Roehrich in 1978 and lives
near Berlin, N.D.

The Guthmillers have 29 grandchildren and 11 great- grandchildren.

ROGER AND PHYLLIS GUTHMILLER

        Phyllis, Michael, and Roger Guthmiller

Roger Guthmiller was born September 19, 1945 to Alvin and Magdaline (Mohr)
Guthmiller at Aberdeen, S.D. He lived on a farm 12 miles north and one mile
west of Roscoe with his parents.  He attended rural school and belonged to St.
John's Lutheran Church at Hosmer.  In 1965 he began working for Spitzer
Implement in Roscoe.  In 1974 he began working for the Edmunds County Highway
Department where he is presently employed.

July 1, 1968 he married Phyllis Ehnes, daughter of Simon Ehnes, at St. Thomas
Church in Roscoe.

Phyllis was born March 23, 1946 on her  parents' farm near Roscoe.  She
attended Roscoe Public School during her elementary and secondary education,
graduating in 1964.  She attended South Dakota State University at Brookings,
S.D. where she graduated with a B.S. degree in Home Economics in 1968.  In
1968-69 she taught in the Selby School system, 1969-79 in the Ipswich Public
School system and 1979-83 in the Roscoe school system.

Roger and Phyllis have one son, Michael, born August 23, 1969.

ERHARD AND ANNA HAAR

Erhard Haar was born in Freeman, S.D. October 11, 1904, the son of Benjamin and
Emelia Shortsman Haar. In the fall of 1922, he moved with his family to the
Roscoe area.  They lived northwest of Gretna for about a year and then settled
on a farm 11 miles southwest of Roscoe.  Erhard attended Auto Mechanics School
in Aberdeen in 1927 and 1928 after which time he worked at the Yellowstone
Garage in Roscoe which was owned by John Lammle and John Spitzer.

He married Anna Hofer February 23, 1931 at Webster, S.D. She was the daughter
of Adam and Julia Schipke Hofer.  She was born October 4, 1908, one and one-half
miles east of Roscoe.  This is where she grew up, attending the Roscoe school
through the ninth grade. They moved out on what had been the Paul Schmidt, Sr.
farm in 1933.  That some year Erhard traded jobs with George Goetz and worked
as a janitor for five and one- half years at the Roscoe school.  They then
moved on Anna's parents farm to help care for her ailing mother for four years.
The next move was south of Beebe for two years and then on a farm seven miles
southwest of Roscoe where Paul Schmidt, Jr. had lived.  They lived there for 11
years when they moved back to Roscoe and Erhard worked for H. E. Preszler, then
Bob Auchampach as an auto mechanic before he went to work for Biebers at the
Roscoe Auto Co. where he remained about 15 years until he retired.

They have five children, Dorothy, married to Victor Schlomer, lives in Glenham,
S.D. and has four daughters.

Gilbert, married to Betty Krumm, lives in  Aberdeen and has three children.

Armond, married to Geraldine Way, lives in Aberdeen and has three children.

Arnold, lives at Mina Lake, owns the Silver Dollar Bar at Aberdeen, and has two
children.

Ruth lives in Aberdeen and works at Safeguard Industries and for her brother,
Arnold, at the bar.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church where Anna is a member of the
Ladies Aid.

MR. & MRS. WALTER HAAR

        Walter and Lorena Haar

Walter Haar was born February 9, 1912, at Freeman, S.D., the son of Benjamin
Haar, who was born in Russia July 10, 1874, and died in April 1953, and Emelia
Shortsman Haar, who was born in Menno, S.D. They came to the Roscoe area in
1922.

Lorena Kilber was born September 8, 1917, in Cloyd Valley Township, to Philip
and Maria Froch Kilber, who were both born in Russia and came to America in 19
10.

Walter and Lorena were married in the Lutheran Church in Bowdle, S.D. October
31, 1937, and settled on a farm southwest of Roscoe.  They had five children:
Duane, married JoAnn Bonn from Groton, lives on the family farm and they have
three children; Shirley, married Jude Sexton from Aberdeen, living in Sturgis,
S.D. They have four sons and Shirley works in the Fort Meade Hospital; Darwin,
living in Bismarck, N.D. and has his own business; Karen, married to Myron Nakoa
from Los Angeles, Calif. and working for an insurance company; Lorene, married

        Darwin, Duane, Lorena, Walter, Karen and Shirley Haar

        Kim, JoAnn, Duane and baby Colleen Haar
