EDWIN AND IRENE HAAR

Edwin Haar was born July 1, 1916 near Bowdle, S.D. to Albert and Pauline
Aldinger Haar.  This is where he grew up, attended the local country school,
farmed and trucked with his father.  October 17, 1939 he married Irene Dumdei,
who was the daughter of John and Marie Maier Dumdei.  She was born January 5,
1921 at Northwood, N.D. She moved with her family to a farm near Bowdle when
she was a baby.  She grew up on this farm and attended the school near her home.

        Irene, Edwin, Dawaine, and Dale Haar

Edwin and Irene made their home on a farm near Bowdle until 1956 when they
moved to Ipswich, S.D. and operated the "Friendly Drive-in".  In 1961, they
moved to Roscoe and Edwin did custom combining, going south every summer with
his operation to follow the harvest back up north.  In 1971, they went into
partnership with their son, Dale, and his wife, Gloria, in the business they
call Haar's Recreation, for eight years.  Now they are semi-retired with Edwin
still doing some livestock trading.

They have two sons, Dawaine, married to Lila Job, has six children and lives in
Bowdle and Dale, married to Gloria Merkel, has three sons and lives in Roscoe
where they own and operate Haar's Recreation.

DALE AND GLORIA HAAR

Dale Haar was born March 14, 1943 in rural Bowdle, S.D. to Edwin and Irene
Dumdie Haar.  He lived on the farm and attended country school until he was 12
and moved to Ipswich with his family where they operated a Drive-In.  He
attended high school in Ipswich.  They moved to Roscoe in 1961.

Gloria Merkel was born July 8, 1948 in Hoven, S.D. to Albert and Gloria Bogema
Merkel.  She grew up on a farm near Bowdle where she attended the country
school near her home for four years and finished her school years in Bowdle.
In 1967 she worked in cafes in Ipswich.

Dale and Gloria were married September 25, 1971 in Bowdle.  They made their
home in Roscoe where they bought Hiebs Recreation and the business become known
as Haar's Recreation.  They still operate this business at the present time.

They have three sons, Dennis, born September 6, 1972; Doran, born October 17,
1973 and Darcy, born February 21, 1976.  They are students at the Roscoe school
and skilled pool players at Haar's Recreation.

BERTON AND FRIEDA HAM

The Berton Ham family moved to the Loyalton, S.D. community in 1946.  They
purchased the Harry Cole farm, where they lived until 1968 when they retired
and moved to Roscoe.

Berton is the son of Robert H. Ham and Mary Schaich, and was born near Hoven,
S.D. Frieda Erlenbusch Ham is the daughter of Daniel Erlenbusch and Katherine
Saur and was born near Lowry, S.D. in a sod house.

While living near Loyalton, Berton was a member of the Loyalton School Board
and served as Vermont Township treasurer for several years.

Berton and Frieda were married January 22, 1938 in Selby, S.D. They have two
children, Dwayne, who is married to Kathleen Johnle and is presently living in
Bowdle, S.D. and Myrna, who is married to Roger Roehl, living in Taipei, Taiwan.

GEORGE AND ANN HAMMOND

George Wilber Hammond was born in 1901, to Wallace and Lillian Hammond, near
Canton, S.D. He attended Bartles School near Parker, S.D. In the spring of
1914, they moved to Elmwood, Wis.

In 1918, George came to Aberdeen, S.D. and was employed by Jim Anderson,
hauling bundles on a threshing machine rig.  In the fall, he organized a
partnership with his brother Earl and uncles, Clyde and Elford Ettles.  They
moved to the Anderson ranch in McPherson County.  In the spring of 1924, he and
his Uncle Clyde broke land in Walworth County, South of Glenham, S.D. In the
spring of 1927, George moved back to McPherson County and farmed.  The crops
were good, but back payments took most of his earnings, as he had bought a new
tractor from Spitzer and Lammle at Roscoe.  In the fall of 1928, he came to
Roscoe and asked Spitzer and Lammle to work for his room and board, if that was
all he could earn, but in the spring they handed him a check for $50 per month.
He stayed at Spitzers for eight years, farming together and in 1930, they
asked him to run their threshing outfit on half, and he worked that way until
he bought a truck and started trucking.  George built a 22 foot trailer and
hauled livestock and hundreds of horses to eastern markets and hauled back hay
during the drought years, and also cars and trucks for Martin Scherf, Ford
dealer in Roscoe.

In 1937, George married Ann Schauer, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Andrew Schauer of
Roscoe.  They established a home in Roscoe where they reside now.

In 1947, they sold the truck line to Arnold Hettich of Roscoe.  They then owned
and operated the Hammond Road Construction, employed five or six men during the
summer and fall.  Ann kept the books.  Miles and miles of roads were built and
when the State advertised for volunteers to plow snow in the winter of 1956,
George called the highway department and told them that he had two tractors
with snow plows, and they said, "Hold the line a minute," and that's when he
was hired to go to Gettysburg, S.D. to plow snow.  The next day at 6 p.m., they
came to deliver the plows to Gettysburg. George asked where they should start
plowing the next morning and he said, "Hell, morning, you start tonight!  "They
plowed snow around the clock for seven days and nights.  The eighth day they
delivered the plows back to Roscoe.

In the fall of 1959, they also moved lots of dirt in the Herreid and Pollock,
S.D. country.  Son-in-law Vern Ernst worked for him at the time, so Vern and
Dody moved their trailer home to Herreid and Ann and George lived in with them
during that time.  Some of the crew were lodging in the Herreid Hotel.

In the fall of 1973, George retired due to poor health. As a self-taught
painter, he decided to take up painting after his retirement.  Some of his
paintings went all the way to Canada and the Philippine Islands.

He served as Justice of Peace in Roscoe from 1942 to 1944, and was on the
Farmers Union board for 17 years.  In 1966, he was elected to the City Council
and served as President of the Board.  He served as Mayor of Roscoe from 1957
to 1959.  They belong to St. Paul's Lutheran Church where Ann taught Sunday
School for a few years and was an active Ladies Aid member.

George and Ann were blessed with two daughters: Georgia Ann (Dody), married to
Vern Ernst, son of Eugene and Mary Ernst of Aberdeen.  They have four children,
Todd, Cheryl, Shelly and Sheila, all living in Rapid City, S.D.

Sonja, married Al Reetz, son of Mr. and Mrs. Edward Reetz of Neshkora, Wis.
Sonia and Al live in Santa Cruz, Calif.  They have two children, Randy and
Tammie.

All in all, they have a wonderful family and have led a very active and
exciting life.  Ann is still busy with daycare.  She provided loving care for
quite a few youngsters in the past 30 years and they loved them all. In recent
years, Ann has taken core of Dale and Bonnie Roesch's children, Jessica, Eddy
and Lindsey.  For the past five years, she has taken core of Megan and Bethany
Garner, children of Reed and Pam Garner.  All five are lovely children and seem
like their own grandchildren.  They keep them young and happy!

KATHERINE HEIL LAMMLE HAUCK

Katherina Heil was born in Gluckstal, South Russia on March 20, 1860 to Ludwig
and Elizabeth Huber Heil.

November 22, 1881 she was married to John Lammle in Bergdorf, Russia.  They
were life-long Lutherans.

They had nine children, three of whom died in infancy.

The Lammle family came to America February 16, 1893 and settled on a farm in
Bon Homme County where they lived for eight years, then moving to McPherson
County.

Mr. Lammle died December 30, 1910.  In 1915 Katherina married Fred Hauck and
they moved to Hosmer, S.D. Mr. Hauck died May 12, 1922.  In 1934 Mrs. Hauck
moved to Roscoe to make her home with her children, John, William and Phillip
Lammle, Margaret (Mrs.  Christ Schumacher).  A son, Jacob, lived in Hosmer and
a daughter, Katherine Weisser, lived in Missoula, Mont.

Mrs. Hauck passed away January 16, 1952 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery.

REINHOLD AND ALVINA HAUCK

Reinhold Hauck was born May 4, 1907, in McPherson County, the son of Michael
and Katherine Pietz Hauck. In 1929, he married Alvina Scherbenske, daughter of
William and Matilda Fey Scherbenske.  She was born October 1, 1910, near Artas
in Campbell County.  They farmed six miles west of Roscoe until 1968 when they
retired and moved to Roscoe.

Reinhold worked port-time for Spitzer Implement for several years.  He served
several years on the Glen Township board, was a F.H.A. board member and a
deacon in the United Church of Christ in Hosmer.

They had seven children, Viola is married to Dan Schumacher.  They are dairy
farmers near Baldwin, Wis. and have three children, Buddy, Rosie and Bonnie.

Mervin is married to Margaret Gauer and is a self employed trucker in Ipswich,
S.D. They have two children, Terry and Tricia.

Rosella is married to Harold Catlett and lives in Minneapolis, Minn. where she
is a nurse.  They have two children, Debbie and Harold, Jr.

Virginia is married to Charles Meister and lives at Groton, S.D. She works at
3-M in Aberdeen and they have three children, Debbie, James and David.

Roger married Sandra Opp and they live in Aberdeen where he is a trucker.  They
have three children, Kevin, Darin and Ryan.

Gloria is married to Kenneth Nelson, is a factory employee in Sheboygan, Wis.
and has one son, Ringo.

Rick married Katherine (Kitty) Ulmer and is a farmer near Roscoe.  They have
four children, Brian, Sonia, Jacqueline and Tracy.

Reinhold passed away March 31, 1982 and is buried in Bethany Cemetery in
Hosmer.  Alvina lives in the Hosmer Senior Citizens Home.

RICK AND KITTY HAUCK

        Back row: Sonia, Brian and Rick; front row: Jacqueline, Kitty and Tracy
        Hauck

Rick Hauck was born September 7, 1946 near Roscoe to Reinhold and Alvina
Scherbenske Hauck.  He went to school in Roscoe and graduated in 1964.  He
served in the United States Army from September 1966 to August 1968 where he
spent one year in Ft.  Hood, Texas and one year in Vietnam in the Armored
Division.  In 1967, he married Katherine (Kitty) Ulmer of Hosmer, S.D. She was
the daughter of Walter and Fern Crawford Ulmer. She was born in Aberdeen, S.D.
June 14, 1948, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1966 and attended Stewarts
School of Hairstyling in Aberdeen for one year.

They live on Rick's home farm six miles west of Roscoe.  He has served on the
Glen Township board, is post commander of the Roscoe Legion, member of the Rod
and Gun Club and is an ASC committeeman.  Kitty is a member of the American
Legion Auxiliary where she has served as on officer.  They are members of St.
John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer where Rick was a deacon and Kitty has been a
Sunday School teacher and member of the ALCW.

They have four children, Brian, born February 17, 1968; Sonia, born June 3,
1971; Jacqueline, born August 30, 1974 and Tracy, born May 29, 1978.

BERNARD AND PAMELA HEIER

        Melissa, Barney, Jason and Pam Heier

Bernard (Barney) Heier was born May 29, 1951, at Aberdeen, S.D. to Adam and
Margaret Hopfinger Heier of Hosmer, S.D. He attended Hosmer Grade and High
School, graduating in 1969.  Following graduation, he worked for the Hosmer
Lumber Company.  November 4, 1970, he was drafted into the Army and spent from
July 1971 to July 1972 in Vietnam.

Pamela Schatz Heier was born August 5, 1953, at Hoven, S.D. to Norman and
Esther Wolff Schatz of Tolstoy, S.D. In March 1965 they moved to a farm south
of Roscoe where she attended the Montpelier country school and later attended
the Roscoe Grade and High School graduating in 1971.  Following graduation, she
attended the Lake Area Vocational Technical School in Watertown, S.D. for
dental assisting.  In May 1972, she graduated and started working for Dr. D. J.
Carrels in Aberdeen, S.D.

They were married September 2, 1972, at Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer,
S.D. Since then they have lived in Aberdeen, Huntington Beach, Calif. and
Ipswich, S.D. before moving to Roscoe in October 1976. Barney is a
self-employed carpenter and Pam is employed by N.E.S.D.C.A.P. as
Edmunds-McPherson County Headstart Homevisitor. They have two children, Jason,
born August 19, 1976 and Melissa, born November 15, 1978.

They attend St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe. Barney has served as the
Commander of the Roscoe American Legion and is post president of the Sportsman
Club.  Pam is a member of the American Legion Auxiliary and St. Thomas Altar
Society.

JACOB AND MARGARET HEIL

Jacob Heil, Jr. was the son of Jacob and Dorothea Krein Heil.  He was born in
Odessa, Russia November 24, 1882.  He came to America when he was nine years
old.  He lived with his parents at Menno, S.D. until he took up a homestead as
a young man in Hillside Township in Edmunds County.

March 5, 1905, he married Margaret Bauer from Leola, S.D. She was the daughter
of Gottfried and Elizabeth Eisenman Bauer and was born June 19, 1884 in
Gluckstal, South Russia.  She came to this country as a young girl.

Mr. Heil was a farmer all his life until 1945 when they moved into the city of
Roscoe.  During his last 13 years he did part-time carpentry and also part-time
veterinary work.

        Emma, Lula, Lydia, Jake, Elizabeth and Arthur Heil

The Heils had 12 children.  Six of them are still living.  They are Emma (Mrs.
Albert Wegner), Cresbard, S.D.; Elizabeth (Mrs.  Cecil Mount), Aberdeen, S.D.;
August, Elko, Nev.; Lydia (Mrs.  Henry Trefz), Roscoe; Mahlon, Blue Jay,
Calif.; and Elmer, Arcadia, Calif.

The six deceased children ore Arthur, Margaret, Jake, Bertha, Lula and one
daughter died in infancy.

Mr. Heil died September 9, 1956 and Mrs. Heil died in September 1967.  They are
both buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

ALBERT AND BARBARA HEILMAN

        Michael, Albert, Karen, Barbara, and Anthony Heilman

Albert Heilman was born February 22, 1936 at Bowdle, S.D., the son of Lawrence
and Regina Heilman. He lived on a farm north of Bowdle and attended school in
Bowdle.  Albert was in the Army from 1959 to 1961. He received his basic
training at Fort Leonard Wood, Mo., and then was stationed at Fort Huachua,
Ariz.

Barbara Ann Mardian was born December 4, 1944 to Anton, Jr. and Barbara
Fuhrmann Mardian.  She attended school in Roscoe.

Albert and Barbara were married October 26, 1965.  In September 1966 they
settled on the farm three miles west of Roscoe where Barbara grew up and where
they live at the present time.

They have three children Anthony, born August 12, 1967; Michael, born May 23,
1969 and Karen, born February 19, 1971.

They belong to St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe where Barbara is a member
of the Ladies Altar Society. Albert is a member of the Roscoe American Legion.

ANTON AND MAGDALENA HEILMAN

Anton L. (Tony) Heilman was born January 7, 1900, on a farm north of Bowdle,
S.D. He was the son of Ludwig and Magdalena Uhrich Heilman, who were both born
in Russia and were married there before coming to this country.  He was the
youngest of three children. Magdalena (Lena) Gabriel Heilman was born June 30,
1903, on a farm southwest of Hosmer, S.D. She was the daughter of John and
Claudia Harty Gabriel, who were born and married in Russia.  They came to
America on March 12, 1898, from Odessa, Russia.

        Back row: Clarence, Lenora, Christ; front row: Anton, Florence, Floyd
        and Magdalena Heilman

Tony and Lena were married February 5, 1924, in the Holy Trinity Catholic
Church in Hosmer.  They lived on a farm northwest of Bowdle until the fall of
1945, when they moved to Roscoe.  Tony followed the carpenter trade with Fred
Menzia for three years.  He was later employed at the Farmers Union where he
worked until the time of his death November 24, 1952.  He is buried in
Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

They had five children: Christ, married to Agatha Malsom, lives in Hosmer and
they have eight children.

Lenora, married to Pius K. Malsom, lives on a farm north of Roscoe.  They have
one son.

Clarence, married to Joan Brendel, lives in Aberdeen, S.D. and has two
daughters.

Florence, married to Marvin Lehr, lives in Britton, S.D. and has two sons.

Floyd, married to Virginia Miller, lives in Winner, S.D. and has three
daughters.  All three sons were in the service.  Christ and Floyd were
stationed in Korea and Clarence was stationed in the United States.

In the spring of 1953, Lena and the two youngest children, Florence and Floyd,
moved to Aberdeen.  May 3, 1959, Lena married Jacob Heilman, Sr. and went to
live on his farm northeast of Bowdle.  Jake died March 6, 1965 and she moved
back to Roscoe for a short time. She returned to Aberdeen and lived there until
her death August 27, 1968.  She is also buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.  With her second marriage, she gained two step-daughters, Johanna
Bitterman of Bowdle and Rose Stoltz of Dickinson, N.D. and four step-sons Pete,
Valentine, Jacob Jr. and Francis, all of Bowdle.

BERNARD AND GERTRUDE HEINTZMAN

Barnard Heintzman was born December 14, 1881 in Wolkov, Russia, to Bernard and
Gertrude, nee Kunz, Heintzman.  He came to the United States in 1901 and
settled on a farm near Hosmer, S.D. for a time and then moved to a farm near
Onaka, S.D. for a few years. In 1908, he married Gertrude Volk at Roscoe.  She
was the daughter of Frank and Scholastica, nee Mangold, Volk.  She was born
March 8, 1884 in Mannheim, Russia and came to the United States in 1902.  She
settled in Roscoe where she worked as a chambermaid at the James Hotel which
was in more recent years owned by Rena Schneider.  After her marriage, she
lived on her husband's farm near Onaka until 1916.  Here they started their
family of nine children.

        Julia, Lizzie, Magdalena, Gertrude and Scholostica; seated: Bernard
        Heintzman

Eva lived only nine months and is buried at Roscoe.

Magdalena was born in January 1910 and died in August 1972.

Gertrude was born September 13, 1911 and is now living in Roscoe.  Scholastica
was born November 20, 1912 and is now living in Aberdeen.  She was married to
Mike Melia June 17, 1958, who passed away September 12, 1964 and is buried at
Conde, S.D.

Juliana was born May 4, 1914.  She married Arthur Epp June 24, 1963 and lives
at Eau Claire, Wis.

Bernard was born May 20, 1915 and died June 14, 1915.

Frank was born June 29, 1916 and died July 21, 1916.

Lizzie was born July 27, 1917 at Roscoe and is now living in Roscoe.

Eva Katherine was born September 10, 1920 and died the same day.

The family moved to a farm one and one-half miles northeast of Roscoe in 1916.
Mrs. Heintzman passed away October 11, 1936.  Bernard retired from farming when
his health failed and he moved to Roscoe November 2, 1942.  He passed away June
5, 1969 and he along with his wife, Gertrude, and the children who preceded
them in death are all buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

PETER AND VERONICA HEISZLER

Peter Heiszler was born in 1872 in the Odessa area of Russia.  He was raised
there and immigrated to the United States with his wife, Veronica, and their
first child in 1901.  Veronica Meier Heiszler was born in 1873 also in Russia.

        Back row: B. K. Dutenhoeffer, George, Wendelin, Peter, Veronica, Ann,

        (Heiszler) Dutenhoeffer holding Stanis, and Ann Dutenhoeffer, Peter
        Jr., and Mike Heiszler

They homesteaded in rural Edmunds County in Hillside Township where they raised
seven children.  Mary who married B. K. Dutenhoeffer and lived in Roscoe.  Both
are deceased.

Mike married Mary Malsom and lived in Seattle, Wash.  He died in 1980.

George married Bertha Heinzman and lived in Aberdeen, S.D. and both are
deceased.

Lena married Jake Gefre and lived in Toppinish, Wash.  She is deceased.

Wendelin married Mary Heinzman and lived in Seattle, Wash.  He is deceased.

Ann married Tom Goetz and lives in Onaka, S.D.

Peter married Elsie Weber and lives in Onaka.  She is deceased.

the church cemetery.  He died in 1937 and she in 1946.

DENNIS AND CAROL HETTICH

Dennis Hettich was born October 3, 1947 in Aberdeen, S.D. to Jacob and Velma
Korb Hettich.  He graduated from Hoven High School in 1965.  He was in the Sea
Bees from June 1966 to October 1969.

Carol Vilhauer Hettich was born March 1, 1950 in McPherson County to Alvin and
Elia Haar Vilhauer.  She is a graduate of Northwestern Lutheran Academy,
Mobridge, S.D. She attended the School of Commerce in Aberdeen, S.D. graduating
in 1969 with a secretarial degree.

Carol and Dennis were married in Roscoe October 24, 1970.  They resided in
Aberdeen where Dennis was employed as a bookkeeper at United Building Center.
In April 1972, they moved to Clear Lake, S.D., where Dennis managed a lumber
yard for UBC.  They moved to Hoven in February, 1973 where Dennis went into
carpentry, self-employed.  After three years they moved to Roscoe and purchased
the farmer Sauer home.  In 1981, they built a new home at the same location.
Dennis became the city maintenance man of Roscoe in March 1982, doing carpentry
as a side line.

        Back row: Carol and Dennis; front row: Nell and Kirk Hettich

Auxiliary.

Two boys were born to this union, Neil and Kirk.  Neil was born April 15, 1971
and Kirk was born August 4, 1974.

FRED HETTICH FAMILY

Fred Hettich was born July 2, 1889 at Bergdorf, Kersohn, Russia and came to the
United States with his parents at the age of 12 on the ship Kaiser Willhelm in
the year 1901.  His father's name was George Hettich and his mother's name
Magdalina Fisher Hettich They settled five miles west of Ipswich and lived
there for a couple of years,  then moved four miles south and three miles east
of Roscoe in Montpelier Township and lived there the rest of his life.

January 3, 1917 Fred Hettich and Rosalia Kohler were married in the Lutheran
Church at Herrick, S.D. and she came to Montpelier Township.  Rosalia Kohler
Hettich was born February 24, 1898 at Gnadau, Kubanskoe, Albast, Russia and
came to this country with her parents at the age of nine on the ship Potsdam in
1907. They settled at Naper, Neb. and a few years later moved to Herrick, S.D.
Her parent's names were Alvin Kohler and Christina Martins Kohler.

In the year 1918 they built the house that Norman Schatz's now live in.  It has
been moved from the original place and some addition added.

        Back row: Raymond, Wilbert, Arnold, Laurence, Ervin, Ernest and Harold;
        front row: Ella, Deloris, Rosalia, Lenora, Esther and Elsie Hettich

To this union 15 children were born, three dying in infancy.  Harold was born
December 10, 1917, married Norma Huber from Selby, S.D. and is now the
postmaster at Selby, S.D.

Arnold, born May 18, 1920, married Verna Scheid of Roscoe and is now a trucker
and living in Roscoe, S.D.

Lenora, born September 16, 1922, married Lew Sawyer of Stockton, Calif. and is
a widow living in Stockton, Calif.

Ernest was born August 13, 1924 and married Freida Himerich of Selby, S. D. He
has a bowling alley and construction firm in Selby, S.D.

Lawrence was born October 4, 1925, married LaVerna Heib of Hosmer, S.D. and is
now farming northwest of Bowdle, S.D.

Raymond, born June 9, 1927, married Frances Krambeal of Ipswich, S.D. and is
working in a store at Missoula, Mont.

Ervin, born February 24, 1929, married Leola Russell of Selby, S.D. and is
divorced.  He then married Sue Herborn of Phoenix, Ariz. and is now selling
real estate in Phoenix, Ariz.

Wilbert, born March 1, 1931, married Viola Lacher of Roscoe, S.D. and is now
the rural mail carrier at Selby, S.D.

Deloris, born December 28, 1932, married Myron Lehr of Roscoe, S.D. and is
farming at Roscoe, S.D.

Esther, born September 28, 1934, married Arnold Lammle of Roscoe, S.D. She
divorced and is working for the BIA at Riverside, Calif.

Ella, born December 21, 1935, married Harry Adam of Aberdeen, S.D. and they are
now co-owners of a warehouse grocery at Harvey, N.D.

Elsie, born May 14, 1937, married Clifford Hanson of Pollock, S.D. and lives in
Spearfish, S.D. where she works at the college.

Fred Hettich passed away October 4, 1941.  Mrs. Hettich continued to live on
the farm a few more years. August 11, 1946 she married William Miller of Roscoe,
S.D. and moved to Roscoe.  Mr. Miller passed away June 23, 1964.  She continued
to live in Roscoe until the fall of 1976 when she went to live at the Good
Samaritan Center at Selby, S.D. until she passed away February 1, 1980.

Three sons served their country, Harold in the Army, Lawrence in the Navy
during World War II and Wilbert serving his time during the Korean Conflict.

Thirty-two grandchildren and 27 great-grandchildren have carried the Hettich
name from Anchorage, Alaska to Huntsville, Ala. and from California to Detroit,
Mich.

MR. AND MRS. MICHAEL HETTICH

        Back row: John, Jacob, Mike, Elizabeth and Christ; front row:
        Christine, Alvina and Fred Hettich (This picture taken in Russia in
        April 1911)

Mr. and Mrs. Michael Hettich homesteaded in the Roscoe area in May 1911. They
came from South Russia and settled northeast of Roscoe.  Six children were born
in South Russia to Michael and Elizabeth Scheuffle Hettich.  They were
Christian, born December 12, 1897, deceased and buried in Roscoe; John, born
October 7, 1899, deceased and buried in Hosmer, S.D.; Jacob, born June 14,
1901, deceased and buried in Roscoe; Fred, born July 19,1903, now living in
Aberdeen, S.D.; Alvina Fischer, born December 19, 1905, living in Roscoe; and
Christina Clabough, born April 27, 1908, deceased and buried in Salem, Ore.

Three children were born in the United States.  They were Emma Lohnes, born
April 26, 1911, living in Ipswich, S.D.; Edward, born February 27, 1914,
deceased and buried in Lodi, Calif.; and Rosa Bradshaw, born August 8, 1916,
living in Salem, Ore.

        Back row: Jacob, Christian, Fred and John; Middle row: Edward, Alvina,
        Christina and Emma Hettich, Fred Roesler; front row: Minnie Roesler,
        Rose, Ruth, Michael, Molly and Katherina Hettich, Lydi and Anna Roesler

The mother, Elizabeth, died March 25, 1918.  She is buried in the Roscoe
Cemetery.

Michael was remarried to Katherine Schilling Roesler, in April 1919.  Katherina
also had four children: Fred, lives in Billings, Mont.; Lydia Stoecker,
deceased, is buried in Billings; Anna Knittel, living in Roscoe; and Minnie
McAllister, living in Billings.

Two more children were born to Michael and Katherina Hettich.  They are Ruth
Mittlestadt and Amelia Rose Schaff, both living in Montana.

The Mike Hettichs farmed in the Roscoe area, living at several different farms.
They retired in 1936 and moved into town.  He died in 1950.  His second wife,
Katherina, continued to live in Roscoe until her death in 1961.  They are both
buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

CHRISTIAN AND AMELIA MOLLY HETTICK

Christian Hettick was born December 12, 1897 in South Russia to Michael and
Elizabeth (Schuffle) Hettick.  Amelia (Fischer) Hettick was born July 23, 1899
to George and Christina (Waltz) Fischer.

Christian and Molly both grew up in rural Edmunds County and were married in
October of 1921. They lived on a farm in the Beebe area until  they retired in
Ipswich in 1952.  Christ passed away in February, 1970 and Molly passed away in
January, 1972.  Both are buried in Morningside Cemetery north of Roscoe.  Christ
and Molly were members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.  Christ was a
township supervisor and a member of the Ipswich Co-op Elevator, Hospital
Auxiliary and the BB Extension Club.

The couple was blessed with four children, Vera, deceased, Melvin of Mina, Earl
of Roscoe and Shirley Bloemendaal of Rapid City.

        Back row: Melvin, Shirley and Earl; front row: Molly and Christ Hettick

EARL AND BERNICE HETTICK

        Back row: Dale, David and Donald; front row: Deanne, Bernice, Earl and
        Denise Hettick

Earl Hettick was born March 16, 1928 on a farm north of Beebe to Christian and
Amelia (Fischer) Hettick.  He grew up and attended rural school in the Beebe
area. He lived on the farm with his parents until he entered the armed forces
in 1951.  After his discharge in 1953, he went to work for a road construction
firm.

Bernice (Winkler) Hettick was born November 29, 1933 on a farm southwest of
Roscoe to Theophil and Emma (Buechler) Winkler.  She attended Roscoe Public
School for 12 years.  After her graduation in 1952, she was employed at Sherf's
Super Value until 1954.

Earl and Bernice were married in May of 1954 and traveled around South Dakota
while Earl worked on a road construction crew.  In 1957, Earl left the
construction field, and the couple moved to a farm south of Roscoe.  They lived
there until 1965 when they moved to their present home in Roscoe.  Earl worked
for Spitzer Implement for three years and Bernice worked part- time at JoAnn's
Apparel.  In 1969, the couple purchased Roscoe Hardware, which they still own
and operate.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church and the Roscoe Commercial Club.
Earl is a member of the Rod and Gun Club, the American Legion and has served on
the church council.  He is a past commander and county commander of the
American Legion.  Bernice is president of the American Legion Auxiliary and
secretary-treasurer of the Alumni Association.  She is a past member of the
Garden Club and How and Why Extension Club.

The couple has five children, Donald, an electrician at Roscoe Hardware,
married to Sondra (Schumacher) Hettick; Dale, a carpenter in the Roscoe area,
married to Lori (Ernst) Hettick; Deanne, secretary at the Ipswich State Bank,
living in Roscoe and Denise, employed at K.O. Lee in Aberdeen.  David, a fifth
grader, attends school at Roscoe.  They have one grandson, Christian and a
granddaughter Janette Anne.

DONALD AND SANDRA HETTICK

        Sandra, Christian and Donald Hettick

Donald Hettick was born September 14, 1955, in Quinn, S.D. to Earl and Bernice
Winkler Hettick.  When Earl quit his construction job, they returned to Roscoe.
Don spent his earlier years on a farm south of Roscoe and later they moved to
Roscoe.  He graduated from Roscoe High School in 1973 and received his
electricians degree from the North Dakota State School of Science in Wahpeton,
N.D. in 1975.

Sandra Schumacher Hettick was born August 7, 1955, in Ipswich, S.D. to Ivan and
Arvella Preszler Schumacher.  She spent her earlier years on farms near Hosmer
with her parents.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1973 and received
her BS degree in Education from NSC in Aberdeen, S.D. in 1977.

Don and Sandy eventually met and were married June 17, 1978, in Hosmer, S.D.
They have lived in Roscoe since that time.  Don works at Roscoe Hardware as an
electrician and plumber.  Sandy has been the high school English teacher in
Roscoe since the 1979-80 school year.

They both belong to the Roscoe Commercial Club.  Don also serves on the City
Council, the Financial committee in St. Paul's Lutheran Church, and is a member
of the Rod and Gun Club and the Volunteer Firemen.  Sandy also belongs to REA,
SDEA, and NEA.

They have a son, Christian Donald, who was born May 12, 1981.

DALE AND LORI HETTICK

Dale Hettick was born January 28, 1958 in Ipswich, S.D. to Earl and Bernice
Winkler Hettick of Roscoe, S.D. Lori Ernst was born July 5, 1961 in Aberdeen,
S.D. to Morris and Dorothy Pederson Ernst of rural Roscoe. Both Dale and Lori
grew up in the Roscoe area and attended Roscoe public schools.  Dale graduated
from high school in 1976 and attended Lake Area Vocational Technical Institute
in Watertown, S.D. in the field of carpentry.  Lori graduated from high school
in 1979 and attended Presentation College in Aberdeen.

Dale and Lori were married June 13, 1981 at St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Roscoe.  Their home is located in the northeast part of Roscoe close to the
City Park.  Dale is self-employed as a carpenter in the Roscoe area and Lori is
a registered nurse at the Ipswich Community Hospital.

They have a daughter, Janette Anne, born February 1, 1983.

JACOB AND EMELIA HETTICH

        Back row: Floyd, Marvin, Willard and Kenneth; front row: Emelia, Nancy
        and Jacob Hettich

Jacob Hettich was born June 14, 1901 in Bergdorf, South Russia and came to the
Roscoe area in the spring of 1911 with his parents, Michael and Elizabeth
Scheuffle Hettich.  Emelia Kost was born September 28, 1904 at Menno, S.D. in
Hutchinson County, the daughter of Nicholaus and Sophia Horst Kost who came to
the United States and Menno in 1903.

Jacob and Emelia were married at Menno January 4, 1925 and came to Roscoe and
settled on a farm six miles west and one and one-half miles south.  They farmed
in several places and bought a farm five miles south and one and one-half miles
west of Roscoe in 1942.

Their children are, Marvin, born July 6, 1926, married Leta Hanks June 15,
1949, and farming one mile north of Loyalton since 1950.

Willard, born May 2, 1928, married Rose Doane August 28, 1950, and farming the
home place since 1959.

Floyd, born February 16, 193 1, married Doria Persell in 1950, moved to
California in 1955 where he worked for an aircraft company for many years and
also a bus company.

Kenneth, born March 17, 1935, married  Jane Weekly September 17, 1966.  He did
custom combining for several years, lives in Rapid Valley near Rapid City, S.D.
where he is employed with Barber Transportation.

Nancy, born March 29, 1950, married Larry Accola October 21, 1972.  She was a
registered nurse for several years and now resides in Milwaukee, Wis. where her
husband is vice president of a bank.

Jacob died June 3, 1977 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

MARVIN AND LETA HETTICH

Marvin Hettich was born at Roscoe July 9, 1926, to Jacob and Emelia Kost
Hettich.  Jacob was born in South Russia in 1901 and came to South Dakota in
1911. Emelia was born in South Dakota in 1904.  Marvin entered the Army in
January 1945 and served in the Philippines for one year.

Leta Hanks Hettich was born November 28, 1927 at Sturgis, S.D. to Fred and
Ethel Ward Hanks.

Marvin and Leta were married June 15, 1949 at Hot Springs, S.D. They settled on
a farm one mile north of Loyalton in 1950, after having worked in the Black
Hills following their marriage.  They still farm and raise livestock on this
same location.

They have six children: Muriel is a legal secretary and recently moved from
Orlando, Florida to Farmington, Utah; Jocelyn, a substitute teacher, is married
to William Barlow, has two little girls and lives in Gimli, Manitoba which is a
resort town near Winnipeg; Winnette works for the Department of Natural
Resources in Anchorage, Alaska; Norman is a farmer near Loyalton, S.D.; Greg
farms for Norman; and Elaine is a student in Roscoe High School.

WILLARD AND ROSE HETTICH

        Back row: Kent and Susan Peter, Gary and Marilyn Surratt, Debbie and
        Terry Hettich, Linda and Terry Wendt; front row: Willard, Rose, Julie
        Ann, Randy and Toni and baby Joel Jack Hettich

GOTTLIEB AND JULIANNA HEYNE

Gottlieb Heyne was born September 19, 1886, in Russia, the son of George and
Christina Docktor Heyne. He came to America with his parents in 1893, from
Neudorf in the Odessa region of South Russia, and homesteaded land two miles
east and one-half mile north of Hosmer, S.D. Julianna Wiederrich was born
January 7, 1889 in Russia, the daughter of Jacob and Philipina Will Wiederrich.
She also came to America with her parents from Neudorf, South Russia in 1893.
They homesteaded one-half mile east of Hosmer.

They were married March 30, 1909, and settled on a farm about six and one-half
miles northwest of Roscoe. They had five children, Edwin, born March 5, 1910,
married to Lydia Nehlich, who was born February 8, 1913.  They have five
children, Marvin, Eileen, Leo, Roman and Judy.  He died July 29, 1948.

Arthur was born April 24, 1912 and married Anna Nehlich who was born March 28,
1910.  They have four children, Leora, Marlene, Irmgard and Allen.  He died
July 17, 1978.

Edward was born September 5, 1913, is married to Ida Preszler, who was born May
6, 1914 and lives on the' family farm.  They have three children, James,
Marcella and Dennis.

Alma was. born February 13, 1916, married William Nichols, born September 15,
1917, and lives in California.  They have one daughter, Joanne.

Martha was born November 1, 1917, and married Clyde Howe and lives in Kansas.
They have three children, Totten, Gregory and Barbara.

Gottlieb died January 10, 1945 and Julianna died October 9, 1964.  They are
both buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

EDWARD AND IDA HEYNE

Edward J. Heyne was born near Hosmer, S.D. September 5, 1913, the son of
Gottlieb and Julianna Wiederrich Heyne.  Ida Preszler was born near Hosmer May
6, 1914, the daughter of John and Louisa Handel Preszler.

They were married April 18, 1937, in Hosmer and settled on the Heyne family
farm which is located about six and one-half miles northwest of Roscoe.  They
have three children, James, born March 20, 1941, married to Rosella Malsam, who
was born October 11, 1948.  They have one son, Scott James.

Marcella, born November 13, 1944, married James Mohr, who was born April 8,
1941.  They have two children, Jeffrey and Carol.

Dennis, born May 31, 1952, married Joyce Kirschenmann, who was born November 2,
1956.  They have one son, Ryan Scott.  Dennis served in the Army from 1972 to
1974.  All three children live on farms in the Roscoe-Hosmer area.

        Back row: James, Marcella and Dennis; front row: Ida and Edward Heyne

Edward has held several offices in Sangamon Township, was secretary and
treasurer for the North Roscoe Telephone Company, was a director for the Roscoe
Farmers Union and was an ASCS committee man.

Ida belongs to the Lutheran Ladies Aid and the How and Why Extension Club.
They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe and have lived in the
Roscoe area all their lives.

JAMES AND ROSELLA HEYNE

James J. Heyne, son of Edward and Ida Preszler Heyne, was born March 20, 1941
at Ipswich, S.D. He attended rural grade school and his freshman year at Roscoe
High.  He attended the next three years and graduated from Hosmer High School.
He has been farming all his life.

September 30, 1972 James married Rosella Malsom, daughter of Anton and
Catherina Zacher Malsom. Rosella was born October 11, 1948 at Hoven, S.D. She
attended four years of rural grade school.  The remainder of her grade school
and high school was attended at Hosmer where she graduated.  She attended a one
year secretarial school at Aberdeen, S.D. She worked for five years in Aberdeen
for an insurance company until she married.

June 7, 1981 their son, Scott James Heyne, was born.

FRED J. AND CHRISTINA HIEB

Fred J. Hieb was born near Hosmer, S.D. August 18, 1893, the son of Jacob and
Elizebeth Renschler Hieb. March 27, 1919 he married Christina Werre at
Aberdeen, S.D. She was the daughter of John Philip Werre and Christina nee
Hottman, who were both born in Russia and came to the United States in the
early 1900s. She was born April 20, 1895 near Hosmer.  They farmed north of
Roscoe in Blaine Township in McPherson County where he served on the country
school board. They moved to Roscoe when they retired.  They were members of the
Lutheran Church.

They had four children, Herbert J., married Irene Mohr, was a farmer, mechanic
and business man.  He died March 16, 1975.

Erwin A. married Alice Krebs, was a farmer and is a business man.  He owns and
operates Hieb Implement in Roscoe.

LaVerna, Mrs. Lawrence Hettich, is a beautician and homemaker, lives on a farm
near Java, S.D.

Alfred died in infancy.

Fred died March 18, 1967 and Christina died November 22, 1956.  They are both
buried in Hosmer, S.D.

ERWIN AND ALICE HIEB

        Back row: Gloria, John, Don; middle row: Paul, Erwin, Marjorie; front
        row: Julie and Alice Hieb

Erwin A. Hieb was born October 17, 1922 near Hosmer, S.D. to Fred J. and
Christina Werre Hieb.  Both of Erwin's parents were born in South Dakota.  They
lived in Roscoe from 1947-49, and then moved to a f arm near Hosmer.  Christina
died in 1956.  Fred moved to Roscoe in 1957 and remained there until his death
March 18, 1967.  Both are buried in Hosmer.

Alice Krebs Hieb was born December 26, 1925 in Hosmer to Henry and Caroline
Maier Krebs.  Both her parents were born in South Dakota, lived most of their
lives in Hosmer and are buried there.

Erwin and Alice were married June 22, 1947 at the Congregational Church in
Hosmer, S.D. They moved to Roscoe May 30, 1957 from a farm 17 miles northeast of
Roscoe.  Erwin and Alice are owners of Hieb Implement.

Erwin served in the Armed Forces where he was stationed in Frankfurt, Germany.
He is a member of the Roscoe American Legion, post substitute rural mail
carrier, and school bus driver for 17 years.  Alice is post president of the
Roscoe American Legion Auxiliary and Roscoe PTA, President, Secretary and
Treasurer of the Lutheran Ladies Aid; Vice-President of Northwestern Lutheran
Academy in Mobridge; and Past President, Secretary and Treasurer of Rural
Letter Carriers Auxiliary.  They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Roscoe.

Three children were born to this union, Marjorie, Linda Kay, and John.

Marjorie was born January 31, 1948, is a graduate of Roscoe High, married Don
L. DeBoer of Springfield, S.D. January 25, 1968, and has two children, Julie
and Paul. They live in Denver, where Marjorie does secretarial work.

Linda Kay was born January 7, 1951 and died two days later.

John was born July 12, 1957, is a graduate of Roscoe High and married Gloria
Strivens of Redfield, S.D. June 28, 1980.  They live in Aurora, Colo. where
John is a country truck driver for Westman Commission Comp. in Denver.

ROBERT AND DELORIS HIEB

        Cora, Cynthia, Deloris, Galen, Robert and Glen Hieb

Robert Hieb was born March 28, 1928, in Hosmer, S.D., the son of Rudolph and
Maria Walth Hieb.  Rudolph Hieb was born December 29, 1884, in Neudorf, South
Russia, came to the United States in 1893 and settled in Hosmer.  He died
February 3, 1972.  Maria Hieb was born February 28, 1891 in McPherson County
and died in June 1976.  They are both buried in Hosmer.

Deloris Kappes Hieb was born September 11, 1933, the daughter of Adam and
Katherine Grosz Kappes of Long Lake, S.D. Adam Kappes was born July 3, 1896 in
McIntosh County, N.D. and died February 1, 1971.  Her mother, Katherine, was
born July 21, 1900, in South Russia and came to this country as a small child.
She died October 25, 1976, and they are both buried in Ashley, N.D.

Robert served in the Army for two years from March 1951 to March 1953.  Robert
and Deloris were married at Long Lake on July 19, 1953.  They settled on a farm
six miles east and one mile north of Roscoe, until 1978, when they sold the
farm.  In 1979, they built a new home seven miles east of Roscoe where in days
past, the town of Beebe was located.

They have four children: Glen, a minister, married Jackie Instad of St. Paul
June 21, 1980.  They live in Daowagiac, Mich. and have a daughter, Angela, and a
son, Jared.
