and they live in Denver.

Cynthia, a secretary, married Steve Malon of Rapid City, S.D. May 19, 1979, and
lives in Rapid City.

Galen, a diesel mechanic, has been in the Army since 1981, and is stationed at
Fort Carson, Colo.

REV. AND MRS GLEN HIEB

        Angela, Glen, Jackie and Jared Hieb

Glen Hieb was installed as pastor of St. John's Lutheran Church, Wisconsin
Synod, in Dawagiac, Michigan July 6, 1980.

Glen was born at Ipswich, S.D. June 12, 1954, to Robert and Deloris Kappes Hieb
of Roscoe.  He attended the Roscoe school through the eighth grade and
graduated from Northwestern Lutheran Academy at Mobridge.  He attended
Northwestern College in Watertown, Wis. and the Seminary in Mequon, Wis.

He spent his summers during Seminary with his parents near Roscoe, worked for
Spitzer's Implement and Wiedrich Construction.  He enjoyed participating in
Slo-Pitch Softball as a Player-manager.

Glen was married to Jackie Instad of St. Paul, Minn. June 21, 1980.  They have
a daughter, Angela, born April 1, 1981 and a son Jared, born October 19, 1982.

JOHN AND ELIZABETH HIMMERICH

John Himmerich was born March 30, 1876, in Malinova, Cherson, South Russia, the
son of Philipp and Katherina Martin Himmerich.  At the age of 13, in May 1889,
he came to the United States with his parents and family, from Neu Gluckstahl,
South Russia, via Bremen, Germany, aboard the S. S. Rhein, landing in Baltimore,
Md. They came to Eureka, S.D. and settled on a farm southwest of there.

Elizabeth Nehlich Himmerich was born December 19, 1875, in Gluekstahl, South
Russia, to Solomon and Rosina Schumacher Nehlich.  She came to this country May
21, 1898, with the George Schilling family, via Hamburg, Germany, arriving in
the port of New York. They also came to and settled in the Eureka area.  She
was the babysitter for the Schilling children.

John and Elizabeth were married in the Neudorf Geminde Lutheran Church, located
southwest of Eureka, January 15, 1899.  They filed on a claim of land about six
miles northwest of Roscoe in Sangamon Township, where they built a small house
of sod, which always had a dirt floor.

Their first five children were born in this house.  In 1904, they acquired more
land from Frank Horton, and built a new farm about one mile east of the original
homesite.  A larger adobe house was built, complete with wood floors.  The move
to the new house was mode in 1905.  In about 1912, the outside of the house was
covered with wood siding, giving it a modern appearance.

Here they raised their family of four sons and four daughters.  They were
Jacob, Lydia (Mrs.  Fred Schmitt), John, Frederick, Rose (Mrs.  Helmuth
Preszler), Anna (Mrs.  Arthur Preszler), Arthur, and Martha (Mrs. Theodore
Blumhardt).  Another son, Christian, died in infancy.

Mr. Himmerich never became a tractor farmer.  He bought his first automobile in
1917.  Mr. and Mrs. Himmerich were of the Lutheran faith.  They helped to build
the Hoffnung (Hope) Lutheran Church north of Roscoe.  This was dedicated in the
spring of 1909.  Mr. Himmerich was an active member and an officer of that
church until his death.  Mr. and Mrs. Himmerich are both buried in that
cemetery.  The church itself has since been removed.

Mr. Himmerich held numerous township and school district offices.  In the
harvest season of 1928, he met with an accident which caused injuries that
eventually led to his death February 5, 1929.

Mrs. Himmerich was remarried to John Bieber of Bowdle, S.D. in 1930 and lived
in Bowdle until Mr. Bieber died August 6, 1932.  She then purchased a home in
Roscoe and came to live.  May 26, 1934, she married Ludwig Dietterle of
McLaughlin, S.D. and they lived in Roscoe until her death December 13, 1949.
After her death, Mr. Dietterle returned to McLaughlin.  Due to ill health, he
became a resident of the Baptist Nursing Home in Bismarck, N.D. in 1964.  He
lived there until his death June 20, 1970.  He is buried in McLaughlin.

JOHN AND CLARA HIMMERICH

John Himmerich, Jr. was born December 18, 1901, in a little sod house about six
miles northwest of Roscoe, to John and Elizabeth Nehlich Himmerich.  In 1905,
the family moved to a farm about one mile east of the original homesite, where
they built a larger adobe house to accommodate the growing family.  He had four
brothers and four sisters, one brother, Christian, having died in infancy.

He received his education in a little country school in Sangamon Township, two
miles north of his home.  His first teacher was Miss Cleo Crawford, who later
became Mrs. John Pickett, and Miss Bertha Elford was one of his later teachers.
He finished his education with three years at the Lutheran Academy in Eureka,
S.D.

He farmed with his father until the time of his marriage to Clara Adelia
Preszler at Hosmer, S.D. November 29, 1925.  Clara was the daughter of a
neighbor, John and Louisa Handel Preszler.  She was born June 17, 1904, near
Menno, S.D. in Hutchinson County. She came to the Hosmer area with her family
in 1906. After their marriage, John and Clara built a farm three miles north
and two miles west of Roscoe, where they lived until 1930, when they moved back
to John's boyhood home.  There they farmed through the depression and drought
of the 30s, until September 1942 when they moved to Roscoe and went into the
grain elevator business.  He remained in this business until August 18, 1969,
when he suffered a stroke which forced him to retire.  He continued to be in
ill health.

March 5, 1972 Clara also suffered a stroke and as a result of this they both
became residents of the Americana Nursing Home in Aberdeen, until the Colonial
Manor Nursing Home was opened in Ipswich, S.D. in April 1972.  John continued
to live there until his death November 27, 1980.  He is buried in Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe.  Clara is still a resident at the Colonial Manor at this
time.

John was a member of the Roscoe Commercial Club, the Volunteer Fire Department,
the Rod and Gun Club, president of the Roscoe Cemetery Board for several years,
served on the City Council, and as Mayor of Roscoe for 10 years.  He was the
secretary-treasurer of the Edmunds County Republican Party and served as
secretary of St. Paul's Lutheran Church for 24 years. Clara is a member of the
Lutheran Ladies Aid.

They have five children, Melvin, married to Renetta Guthmiller of Roscoe, is
living in Minneapolis, Minn. and works for the Univac Corporation.  They have
seven children, Kathy, Ginger, Neal, David, Anita, Renetta Jean, and Joyce.

Dolores, married to Harold Sahli of Roscoe, and lives in Roscoe where they own
and operate the Red Owl grocery store.  They have two children, Bruce and Robin.

Leo, married to Georgia Binfet of Onaka, S.D., lives in Roscoe where he is
manager of the Roscoe Grain and Feed Company.  They have three children, Stacy,
Darin and Jason.

Harry, married to Opal Tory of Britton, S.D., and lives in Roscoe where he
teaches in the high school.  They have two children, Shane and Annette.

Diana, married to Barry Erickson of Langford, S.D., lives in Tulare, S.D. where
she teaches second grade and Barry is superintendent of the school.  They have
four children, Boyd, Troy, Jennifer and Megan.

John is remembered by those who knew him for his "dry" sense of humor.  He was
an avid baseball fan all his life and a true fisherman as well, being content to
bait his book, cost it into the water and letting the worm shift for itself
while he took a nap.

LEO AND GEORGIA HIMMERICH

        Stacy, Leo, Jason, Darin and Georgia Himmerich

Leo was born in a sod house March 17, 1932 in Edmunds County about five and
one-half miles northwest of Roscoe, S.D. to John and Clara Preszler Himmerich.
Both of his parents were born in the United States.  They are of German
heritage.

Georgia Binfet Himmerich was born October 6, 1940 in Edmunds County about 13
miles southwest of Roscoe, S.D. to Leopold and Josephine Gisi Binfet.  Both of
her parents were born in the United States.  They are of German, Italian and
French heritage.  Georgia spent her childhood days on a farm one mile east of
Onaka, S.D.

Leo and Georgia were married November 20, 1961 at St. John the Baptist Catholic
Church in Onaka.  They made their home in Roscoe where they purchased the
former John Voegele home located in the Morgan addition of town.

Leo graduated from Roscoe High in 195 1. He served in the U. S. Navy from
1951-55 where he served aboard the USS Ashland, LSD-1 in the Mediterranean,
Greenland and Caribbean areas.  After the service, he attended Northern College
in Aberdeen, S.D. for two years, working part-time for Hartung Construction.  He
then became employed with Roscoe Grain and Feed in 1958.  Leo became manager of
the elevator in 1965 and continued to be manager when the elevator was
purchased by South Dakota Wheatgrowers in 1976.

Georgia graduated from Cresbard High in 1958 and from Presentation Jr. College
in 1960 with a two year Elementary Teacher Certificate.  She taught elementary
school in Miller, S.D. and Roscoe for a total of seven years.  She was on Avon
representative for two years before becoming secretary for the Roscoe South
Dakota Wheatgrowers where she is presently employed.

Leo is a member of the Roscoe Volunteer Firefighters, where he served four
years as firechief, served on the School Board for eight years, is a member of
the Roscoe American Legion and serves on the Cemetery Committee.

Georgia is a past member of Garden Club, past president of Church Council,
served on the Edmunds County 4-H Subcommittee, and is a member of the Roscoe
American Legion Auxiliary where she served as secretary and president.

Leo and Georgia are members of the Roscoe Commercial Club where Georgia served
as secretary and Leo as president.  The Himmerich family are members of St.
Thomas Catholic Church where Georgia is a member of the Altar Society and a
catechism teacher.

To this union were born four children, Stacy, Scott, Darin and Jason.

Stacy was born October 29, 1962 at Faulk County Memorial Hospital in Faulkton,
S.D. She attended and graduated from Roscoe School in 1980 and is enrolled at
South Dakota School of Mines and Technology in Rapid City, S.D.

Scott was born March 9, 1964 at the Faulk County Memorial Hospital.  He died at
the Sioux Valley Hospital in Sioux Foils, S.D. May 6, 1964 and is buried in the
cemetery at Roscoe.

Darin was born December 16, 1965 at the Faulk County Hospital.  He is a student
at Roscoe High School.

Jason was born June 19, 1972 at St. Luke's Hospital, Aberdeen, S.D. He is a
student at Roscoe Grade School.

HARRY AND OPAL HIMMERICH

        Harry, Opal, Annette and Shane Himmerich

Harry Himmerich was born September 17, 1939, on a farm five and one-half miles
northwest of Roscoe in Edmunds County.  He is the son of John and Clara
Preszler Himmerich.

September 6, 1964, he married Opal Tory of Britton, S.D., at Bethlehem Lutheran
Church in Aberdeen, S.D. Two children were born to the marriage.  Shone born
December 10, 1965, in Aberdeen, S.D. and Annette born October 17, 1972, in
Aberdeen, S.D.

Harry attended grade school and high school at Roscoe where he graduated in
1958.  He spent two years in the U. S. Army from 1959-1961, serving part of
that time in Heidelberg, Germany.  The next few years were spent attending
Northern State College in Aberdeen, S.D. Upon graduation from college in
January, 1966, he began teaching high school in Hosmer, S.D. He took a teaching
job in Roscoe in 1967 and has been teaching and coaching here since.

Opal was born at Britton, S.D., April 15, 1940, to Russell and Mary McLaughlin
Tory.  She attended rural school northeast of Britton through the seventh grade.
She then attended school in Britton, where she graduated in 1958.  Upon
graduation from the Aberdeen School of Commerce in 1959, she was employed by
the Aberdeen City Street Commissioner from June 1959 to November 1965.  She has
served as Roscoe City Finance Officer since 1970.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church, where Harry has served as
secretary and also as Chairman of the Church and Opal is a member of St. Paul's
Lutheran Ladies Aid.  They are members of the Roscoe Commercial Club.  Harry
has served as Chairman of Edmunds County Republican Party and is presently
State Committeeman.  He has served as Secretary of the Roscoe Volunteer Fire
Department since 1975 and is a member of the Roscoe American Legion, South
Dakota Education Association, Roscoe Education Association, National Education
Association and National Coaches Association.

FRED AND FLORENCE HIMMERICH

Fred, born February 27, 1904 in rural Roscoe, was the third son and fourth
child of John Himmerich and Elizabeth Nehlich.  His brothers and sisters were
Jacob, Lydia, John, Rose, Anna, Arthur and Martha.  He grew up on the family
farm, working with and for his parents and brothers until he joined the United
States Army in August of 1926.  He was discharged before Christmas, 1927, and
returned to Roscoe.

Shortly thereafter he met Florence Lucille Barnard, the seventh and eighth
grade teacher and girls basketball coach at the Roscoe School.  She was born
March 6, 1905 in Chicago, Ill., the fourth surviving child and third daughter
of Allison Frank Barnard and Jesse Lincoln Angell.  Her brothers and sisters
were Theron Allison, Dorothy Angell, Mary Louise, Oromel Rugg, and Hollis
Lincoln.  She arrived in Roscoe in the summer of 1927 after receiving a two
year teaching degree from Wessington Springs College, Wessington Springs, S.D.

Fred and Florence were married May 28, 1929.  Shortly thereafter, they
travelled to Washington state where Fred enlisted a second time in the United
States Army in November 1929.  He served as a trombonist in the Fourth Infantry
Band, stationed in Spokane, Wash., until his discharge June 4, 1932.  During
this enlistment, Florence travelled to Grand Forks, N.D. to give birth to the
first of their five children, twins, Allison Frank and Maurice Fred on October
16, 1930.  Fred and Florence returned to Roscoe in mid 1932 to prepare to begin
farming the following year.  Their third son, Robert Theron, was born December
13, 1932.  Susie Louise, their first daughter, was born February 2, 1935.

After experiencing three crop failures in five years because of the drought,
the family again moved to Washington state in 1936.  By the following year they
were able to re-establish themselves in agriculture, first at Lowell, then near
Arlington and later near East Stanwood.  In 1939, they returned to South
Dakota, this time to a farm near Aberdeen, S.D., a place that served as home
base until 1953.

In addition to farming, Fred began his railroad career in 1943.  The family
enjoyed some degree of financial success during the war years as did much of the
American population.  Dianne Elizabeth, their second daughter and fifth
surviving child was born February 5, 1947.  The family was residing in their
Eighth Street S.W. home in Aberdeen at the time.  Seniority permitted a full
time railroad career for Fred by 1953, and, with the Santa Fe Railroad, Fred
and Florence spent the next 13 years in the vicinity of Son Bernardino, Calif.

After retiring in 1966, they enjoyed travel and short term residences in
Wisconsin, South Dakota, and California.  Fred and Florence established their
permanent residence in Roscoe in 1977.  Here they celebrated their 50th wedding
anniversary in July of 1979.  The occasion was a gathering of all their children
and grandchildren.  Even with a permanent residence, the couple continued to
travel extensively from South Dakota to California, to Virginia and Wisconsin
to be with their children and grandchildren.  They were enroute to southern
California where Fred was to be the best man in the wedding of their oldest
grandchild, James Scott Beem, when they were killed simultaneously in an
automobile accident near Agar, South Dakota on Tuesday, April 8, 1980. They were
buried in Roscoe's Morningside Cemetery April 15, 1980.

The twins, Allison Frank and Maurice Fred have followed quite different
careers. M. Fred, from a very young age, aspired to a religious vocation.  He
was ordained in 1962 after completing his studies at Nashota, Wis., and is now
serving as rector of St. Paul's Episcopal Church in Watertown, Wis.  Fred
married Carol June Roycraft of Oshkosh, Wisconsin, in December of 1958.  They
have five children, four daughters and one son.

A. Frank enlisted in the United States Navy in June 1949 after graduating from
Anaheim Union High School, Anaheim, Calif.  He served 23 years as an
electronics specialist before retiring as Lieutenant Commander in 1972.  He
married Jeanne Louise Beck of Punxsutawney, Pa. in December 1957.  Frank
continues his electronics profession as a Navy Department civilian in
Arlington, Va., where they have made their home since 1963.  Frank and Jeanne
have two sons.

Robert Theron (Bob), after graduating from Central High School in Aberdeen in
1951, entered the United States Marine Corps.  He retired as a Major after over
21 years of service in 1973.  Since that time he has pursued graduate studies
at California State University Fullerton and University of California Los
Angeles where he is a doctoral candidate in Latin American History.  He has
been a Lecturer in History at California State University Fullerton since 1976.
Bob married Eva Margaret Valencia in 1953.  They have one son.  Bob and Eva
make their home in Tustin, Calif.

Susie Louise has been a professional photographer since graduating from
Aberdeen's Central High School in 1953.  She married Richard Alan Beem in 1953
and have made their home in Orange, Calif. ever since. They own a photography
studio in Garden Grove, Calif. and commercial property in Santa Ana, Calif.
Sue also teaches marketing for the vocational studies department of the Garden
Grove Unified School District.  They have five children (three sons and two
daughters) and two grandchildren.

Dianne Elizabeth (Diny), upon graduating from Pacific High School, Son
Bernardino, Calif. in 1965, began a career in the travel industry along with
continuing her college education at the University of Hawaii, Pepperdine
University and several local community colleges.  Diny has taken on the task of
collecting information in an effort to write a Himmerich Family History.  She
makes her home in Orange, Calif. where she is a Travel Marketing Director.

FRED AND CHRISTINA HOFFER

        Back row: Art, Martha, Magdalena, Jacob, Anna and Edwin; front row:
        Philipina, Mervin, Fred, Christina, Lorraine, and Edna Hoffer

Fred Hoffer was born December 30, 1885 at Neudorf, South Russia to Michael and
Kathrina Kirschenmann Hoffer.  He came to this country as a young man in 1903.
His parents remained in South Russia.

Christina Wiederrich Hoffer was born March 4, 1886 in Neudorf, South Russia to
Jacob and Philipina Will Wiederrich.  Jacob was born February 2, 1862 and died
August 25, 1943.  Philipina was born January 1, 1865 and died in December 1934.
They are both buried in Hosmer, S.D. Christina came to this country with her
parents when she was six years old.

Fred and Christina were married January 19, 1909 at Hosmer.  They lived on a
farm northwest of Roscoe for many years.

They had 11 children: Philipina, born February 9, 1910, died April 1, 1912;
Martha, living in Hosmer; Jacob, married to Alma Blumhardt and farming
northwest of Roscoe; Anna, married Jacob B. Voegele, is a widow and is living
in Hosmer; Magdalena, married Manford A. Beckman, is a widow and lives in Anoka,
Minn.; Edwin, married Lucretia Sunday Cervantis, both have died and Edwin is
buried at Roscoe; Edna, married Jacob Bauer and lives in Fargo; Philipina,
married Ervin Reede and lives in Tacoma, Wash.; Arthur, married Clara Adam and
lives in Los Cruces, N.M.; Lorraine, married Harold Roemmich, lives in
Mellette, S.D. and Mervin, married to Lorraine Hofer, lives in Anoka, Minn.

Fred died April 24, 1956.  Christina died November 15, 1955.  They are both
buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

JACOB AND ALMA HOFFER

Jacob Hoffer was born June 26, 1913 to Fred and Christina Wiederrich Hoffer who
were both born at Neudorf, South Russia.  Fred Hoffer was born December 30,
1885 and came to this country at the age of 17. Christina Wiederrich was born
March 4, 1886, and came to the United States with her parents when she was six
years old.

Alma Blumhardt was born May 1, 1911 to Frank and Karolina Walth Blumhardt.
Frank was born March 18, 1885 and died July 9, 1939.  Karolina was born
November 20, 1888 and died November 25, 1976.  They are both buried at Bowdle,
S.D.

Jacob and Alma were married October 7, 1934.  They live on a farm northwest of
Roscoe.

Three children were born to them, Joan, was born July 22, 1937 and died April
5, 1938; Ronald, married to Elda Ruby, lives on a farm north of Roscoe, they
have two sons, Byron and Timothy; and Harvey, married Charlene Tibbetts, has
two sons, Paul and Chad.  He is a carpet layer and is farming.

RONALD AND ELDA HOFFER

        Byron, Elda, Ronald and Timothy Hoffer

Ronald Hoffer was born December 30, 1938 at Leola, S.D. to Jacob and Alma
Blumhardt Hoffer.

Elda Ruby Hoffer was born October 16, 1943 at Ipswich, S.D. to Bruce and Anne
Keisz Ruby.  Her father was born April 19, 1919 and died November 9, 1944
during WWII along the French German border.  He is buried in France.  He had
always lived in South Dakota. Her mother was born February 14, 1918 and always
lived in the Ipswich area.

Ronald and Elda were married January 7, 1962 at Roscoe.  Ronald spent two years
in the U. S. Army being stationed in California and Fairbanks, Alaska.  They
reside on a farm two miles north of Roscoe.

Two children were born to this union, Byron, born April 18, 1963, is a graduate
of Roscoe High and is farming with his father; and Timothy, born June 28, 1966,
is a student at Roscoe High School.

HARVEY AND CHARLENE HOFFER

        Paul, Harvey and Chad Hoffer

Harvey Hoffer was born March 25, 1946 at Aberdeen, S.D. to Jacob and Alma
Blumhardt Hoffer, who have always lived in the Roscoe area.

Charlene Tibbitts was born to Donald and Ruth Tibbetts of Cavour, S.D. April
14, 1949.

Harvey and Charlene were married August 25, 1968 at Pierre, S.D. and made their
home in Pierre.  Harvey is now farming with his father at Roscoe.

They have two children, Paul, born May 20, 1972 and Chad, born October 20, 1973.

ADOLPH AND DOROTHEA HUBER

        Adolph and Dorothea Huber (granddaughter Dorothy Faw)

Adolph Huber was born in Herreid, S.D. June 12, 1903, the son of Gottlieb and
Fredricka Leidle Huber. February 29, 1926, he married Dorothea Pudwill, who was
also born at Herreid September 23, 1907, to Fred and Katherine Schell Pudwill.

They moved to a farm south of Roscoe in April 1962 where they lived until 1964
when they moved to Mobridge, S.D.

They had four children: Virginia Baer of Roscoe; Leland, living at Selby, S.D.;
Marvin, living in Mobridge; and Rosalie Faw, living in Roscoe.

Adolph passed away June 9, 1973 and is buried in Mobridge.  Dorothea is still
living in her home in Mobridge.

RANDOLPH AND ERNA HUBER

        Sandra, Randy, Erna and Carol Huber

Randy Huber was born in Bently, N.D. November 14, 1917.  His father was
Gottlieb Huber and his mother, Christina Dockter.  His parents were both born
in Russia and came to the United States in 1894.  They lived in the Hosmer,
S.D. area most of their lives.

Randy served four and one-half years in the Army during World War II.  He was
active in combat missions in North Africa, Sicily and Anzio Beachhead in Italy,
Southern France, Germany and ended his service in Salzburg, Austria.  After the
service, Randy was engaged in farming, did carpenter work for 18 years, until
he became owner of Randy's Building Supplies in 1967, which he still operates
at the present time.

Erna Hieb Huber was born in Hosmer March 23, 1925. Her father was Rudolph Hieb,
who was born in Russia and came to the United States around 1890.  Her mother
was Mary Walth.  They lived in the Hosmer area.  Erna graduated from Hosmer
High School in 1943 and received her degree in Elementary Education in 1976
from NSC.  She began her teaching career in 1944.  She has taught schools in
Edmunds, McPherson and Campbell Counties and at the present time is teaching
kindergarten in Roscoe.

Randy and Erna were married in St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer May 19,
1947.  They moved to Roscoe in 1947, and are still residents of Roscoe.  They
are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church.

They have two daughters: Sandra was born in Ipswich, S.D. August 17, 1951.  She
graduated from Roscoe High School and the University of Vermillion, majoring in
Elementary Education.  She taught school in Sioux Falls, S.D., Firth and
Blackfoot, Idaho.  She married Tim Thie of Sioux Falls, who is also a graduate
of Vermillion.  Tim taught school for some years in Blackfoot and at the
present time is a sales person for Idaho Potato Packers. They live in Blackfoot
and have one daughter, Darcy Ann, born April 7, 1980.

Carol was born in Ipswich October 3, 1954.  She graduated from Roscoe High
School and received her Secretarial degree at NSC.  She started her secretarial
work in Pierre, S.D., working for the Pierre Motor Vehicle Dept. and at the
present time is employed at the Hartwell Insurance Corporation in Idaho Falls,
Idaho.  She married Ken Erickson of Racine, Wis.  Ken was a journalist in
Pierre, writing the Pierre news for the Rapid City Journal.  Ken is director of
Adult Education in Southeastern Vocational Technology School in Idaho Falls.

Randy and Erna are both members of the Commercial Club.  Erna also belongs to
the Lutheran Ladies Aid, SDEA and REA.  Randy is a member of the Volunteer Fire
Department and the American Legion.  He served on his church council in various
jobs for about 20 years and on the City Council for two terms.

Randy is well known in the area for his musical ability. He started playing his
banjo with dance bands at the age of 14.  Later he joined the Rhythm Kings,
playing with Gottfried Adam, another local young music man.

He farmed his own band, the Moonlight Serenaders, and for several years
entertained in many dance halls throughout the area.  In the years after the
Serenaders had disbanded, he played as a substitute with Jake and Milton Aman's
bands and still serves as a "5th wheel" on Milton Aman's band.  Randy recalls a
highlight in his musical career when he was serving in the Army in Germany
during WWII when the company commander decided that some special entertainment
was in order. Randy and Gottfried Adam were called on to play for a dance in
the city of Strasburg, Germany, which turned out to be a well attended and
appreciated success by both the servicemen and the local people.  Randy plays
five instruments and has always enjoyed his role as a entertainer.

PHILIPP AND KATHERINA HUFT

Philipp and Katherina Huft came to South Dakota in December 1899 from Bergdorf,
South Russia.  Philipp was born August 29, 1869.  About 1894, he married
Katherina Hettich, daughter of Michael Hettich, in Bergdorf.  She was born
October 24, 1871.

Their children were Christane, born March 26, 1896, resides in Lodi, Calif.;
Christ, born April 1, 1898, died in 1915; John, born January 9, 1899, died June
25, 1945; Fred, born in April 1902, died January 19, 1977; Katherina, born
September 15, 1904, married John Rohrbach, lives in Aberdeen since 1963; Jack,
born October 20, 1906, died September 10, 1974; Mary, born December 18, 1908,
lives in Son Francisco, Calif. and Edwin, born April 9, 1911 died April 20,
1977.

Philipp Huft died in Ipswich, S.D. October 30, 1950.  His wife Katherina died
in Ipswich April 24, 1956.  They ore both buried in Morningside, Cemetery at
Roscoe.

PHILIP AND LEONA HUFT

Philip Huft was born February 20, 1924 at Bowdle, S.D. to Jacob and Johanna
Wald Huft, who lived in the Bowdle area and are now deceased.  Leon Haux was
born January 19, 1930 at Java, S.D. to Henry and Emma Pleinis Haux.  Leona's
father is deceased and her mother lives in Selby, S.D.

Philip and Leona were married at St. John's Lutheran Church in Bowdle June 26,
1949.  They moved on a farm four miles north and two miles west of Roscoe in
September, 1955.  They moved to Roscoe in 1969, buying the Mardian Hide and Fur
place.  Philip worked in the City Liquor Store, S.D. Wheat Growers Elevator and
is now working for the county.  In his spare time he repairs small motors, lawn
motors, etc.  Leona worked a short time in Scherf's Store, the cafe in Roscoe,
in the Hosmer Nursing Home for three years and is now employed in the Colonial
Manor Nursing Home in Ipswich.

        Back row: Cleta, Leona, Philip and Cheryl; front row: Jesse Vargeson,
        Emma Haux and Charlotte Huft

They have six children, Wayne Philip, born April 1, 1952, graduated from Roscoe
High School in 1970, joined the Air Force and served overseas in Thailand. He
married Pam Herd and lives in Amarillo, Texas where he works for an oil
company.  They have two sons and a daughter.

        Wayne, Misty, Pam, Chad and Shane Huft

Dale was born January 12, 1956, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1974 and
joined the Air Force in October, 1974.  He served in England for three years
and is now stationed at the Air Base in Grand Forks, N.D. He married Janet
Manning February 7, 1980 in England where her parents are in the Air Force.
They have one daughter.

Richard was born December 18, 1956, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1975
and is living in Whittier, Calif.  He works in a chrome plating factory.

        Dale, Janet and Elizabeth Huft

Cleta was born July 29, 1959 and graduated from Roscoe High School in 1977.
She married Rick Vargason July 9, 1977.  They both work for the Mel Pickard Bee
Farm and have a son and a daughter.

Cheryl Emma was born February 13, 1966, the year of the big snow storm which
started March 4, and they were without electricity for four days.  She is a
junior at Roscoe High.

Charlotte Ann was born January 8, 1972 and is in the fifth grade at the Roscoe
school.  Both girls are active in 4-H.

        Richard Huft

ADOLF AND HADWIG JESCHKE (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Adolf Jeschke was born in May of 1877 and came to Roscoe in 1897 in a covered
wagon from Milwaukee, Wis.  He married Hadwig Beyers, daughter of Christ Beyers
in 1901.  They farmed in Byrant Township until 1944 when they retired.

Hadwig was born in Germany in July, 1883 and came with her parents in 1884 to
America and settled in Montpelier Township.

Mr. Jeschke was one of the original signers of the constitution of the St. Paul
Lutheran Church in 1901 where Mrs. Jeschke was also very active.  Mr. Jeschke
passed away in 1965 and Mrs. Jeschke in 1973 and both are buried in Morningside
Cemetery.

DAVE JESCHKE FAMILY

        Dave and Marie Jeschke

Dave Jeschke was born in March 1903, five miles southeast of Roscoe.  His
parents were Adolf Jeschke and Hedwig Beyers Jeschke.  He was raised on his
father's homestead and lived there until 1927.

One of the outstanding events in his young life was when he visited his
grandfather, J. G. Jeschke and on his way home was encircled by a pack of
wolves.  His father came by on horseback and began shooting at them.

Another happening that comes to his mind was his uncle, Adolph Beyers,
permitted him to sit with him driving a seven team horse-power threshing
machine, with his Uncle Albert Beyers hand-feeding grain bundles.  At times his
uncle, George Harrison, relieved him of the job.

In May of 1927, Dave married Pauline Schwind of Cascade, Iowa.  To this union
three children were born, James of Roscoe, Donald of Palmer, Alaska and Betty
Ann of Montavillo, Ala.

He rented a farm two miles south and two and one- half miles east in Montpelier
Township and lived there until 1935.  He then bought a farm four miles south and
one-half east.  In October 1977, he sold the farm to his oldest son, James.  In
December 1974, his wife Pauline passed away after a long illness.

In August 1977, he married Marie Dexter of Huntsville, Mo.  In the spring of
1978 they moved to Roscoe into his parents previous home.  They spend their
winters in their rural home in Huntsville.

HERMAN AND CAROLINE JESCHKE

Herman Jeschke was born in Germany in 1874 to Gottlieb and Wilhelmina Birgholt
Jeschke.  He came to the Roscoe area in 1897 to establish a homestead for the
rest of his family to came to the United States.  He married Caroline Hettick
December 26, 1903, in a school house near Roscoe, as there was no Lutheran
church in Roscoe at that time.  Caroline was born in Bergdorf, South Russia,
and came to this country in 1900.

Herman and Caroline homesteaded on a farm southeast of Roscoe.  The couples 12
children were all born on that farm.

        Back row: Reuben, Milton, Reinhold, Oscar and Bill; front row: Kenneth,
        Caroline and Melvin Jeschke

The children are: Emma, deceased; William, deceased; Freeda Ferguson, living in
a nursing home in Ellendale, N.D.; Reinholdt, deceased; Edna Teletzke, living
in Long Beach, Calif.; Reuben, now living on a farm near Roscoe; Irene McClain,
living in Oxnard, Calif.; Viola, deceased; Oscar, retired and living in Roscoe;
Milton, deceased; Kenneth, living at Mobridge, S.D.; Melvin, living in
Aberdeen, S.D.

The family later moved to a farm north of Loyalton, S.D. Herman died in October
1934.  The family continued to live on the farm until 1963, when Caroline
entered a nursing home.

She passed away in 1969.  They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.

Mr. and Mrs. Jeschke were charter members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Roscoe.

REUBEN AND BERNICE JESCHKE

Reuben Jeschke was born April 13, 1913 in Bryant Township, the son of Herman
and Caroline Hettich Jeschke.  He has lived in the Roscoe area all his life. in
1937, he married Bernice Semmler, daughter of Jacob and Hulda Maas Semmler.
Bernice was born seven miles south of Roscoe in Montpelier Township, January
28, 1920.

For six years they lived on three different little farms near Roscoe.  In 1943
they bought a half section of land with the help of their friendly banker.
They, like many people their age went through some very hard years. Since the
land they bought didn't have any improvements, they got busy drilling a well,
moved up an old house, built a cattle shed and hen house.  The building went on
for many years, and in 1955 they put up a barn.  Mixed farming was their
business, for 28 years they milked cows, raised chickens, sold eggs and cream,
like most other farmers did those days.  They still call this place home.

They have three children: Yvonne Rau, living on a farm north of Java, S.D.;
Arlyce Gimbel, living in Moorcroft, Wyo. and working in the first National Bank
in Gillette, Wyo.; DeWayne, who with his wife Shirley and three children,
JoAnn, Justin and Jolene, lives and farms on the land homesteaded by his
great-grandfather, Gottlieb Jeschke, and his grandfather, Herman Jeschke.
DeWayne is a fourth generation farmer.

Reuben and Bernice have eight grandchildren: Cheryl Rau Granum, Bradley Rau,
Todd Rau, Scott Rau, JoAnn Jeschke, Justin Jeschke, Jolene Jeschke and Lona
Gimbel.  They have one great-granddaughter, Courtney Granum.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

DEWAYNE AND SHIRLEY JESCHKE

DeWayne Jeschke was born December 13, 1939, at Roscoe, the son of Reuben and
Bernice Semmler Jeschke.  They are living on the farm east of Roscoe.

Shirley Walth was born to Milbert and Melitha Guthmiller Walth October 16, 1943
at Eureka, S.D. Her parents ore living in Aberdeen where both are employed at
the Elks Lodge.

DeWayne and Shirley were married June 10, 1962 at the Lutheran Church in
Hosmer, S.D. They lived in Pierre from June of 1962 to July of 1968.  During
those years, Shirley worked at two different cafes and for five years worked at
the South Dakota Division of Criminal Investigation.  DeWayne worked at a gas
station and for the South Dakota Department of Highways their last year in
Pierre.

In July of 1968, they moved on the farm with Reuben Jeschkes and farmed there
until the fall of 1971 when they bought the former Bill Fischer farm.  At
present, they are still on the same farm.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

Three children were born to DeWayne and Shirley, JoAnn, November 19, 1964,
Justin James July 12, 1968 and Joleen March 24, 1971.

OSCAR AND MARTHA JESCHKE

Oscar Jeschke was born near Beebe, S.D. to Herman and Caroline (nee Hettich)
Jeschke.  He married Martha Voegele, daughter of Jacob and Lydia  (nee
Schumacher) Voegele of Roscoe, in 1941 in Lawrence, Kansas.  Oscar was
attending Aircraft Mechanic School there.  The couple moved to Buffalo, N.Y.
upon Oscar's graduation.  Here he was employed by Curtis Wright Aircraft.

In 1943, they moved back to Roscoe and purchased a farm three miles north of
Roscoe.  In 1946 they purchased her parents, the Jacob Voegele's farm when the
Voegeles retired.  The farm is located one-half mile northeast of Roscoe and
was previously known as the "Old Judge Lightner Farm".

Here the Jeschkes built a new home in 1950 and lived here until their
retirement in July 1981.  Their son Collin and his wife Gail purchased the farm
and live there now.

The Jeschkes had four children.  Sharron, the oldest, is married to Harris
Swanson.  They live southeast of Ipswich on a farm.  They have four children.

Curtis lives at Mitchell, S.D. where he works with Emsco Chemical.  He is
married to Sue Emerson.  They have four children.

Collin is married to Gail Gutjahr.  They live on the family farm near Roscoe.

Tamara lives at Hosmer, where she is Activity Director at the Hosmer Senior
Citizens Home.  She is married to Don Neifer.

Oscar and Martha have been active in many community activities.  Oscar has
served as Chairman of Edmunds County Democrats, township supervisor and
treasurer in his church, St. Paul's Lutheran.  Martha taught in the rural
schools in the community.  She was also the first woman on the Roscoe School
Board and the first woman on the Edmunds County FHA Board.  She also served in
her church as organist, choir director, and Ladies Aid president.

The Jeschkes retired in 1981 after Mr. Jeschke suffered a heart attack.  They
built a new home in Roscoe, their favorite town.

SAM AND BERTHA JOHNLE

Sam Johnle was born November 26, 1908, near Bowdle, S.D., to Fred C. Johnle,
who came to Bowdle from Russia, and Elizabeth Miller Johnle, who was born in
Walworth County.  Bertha Baer was born December 16, 1913, in Walworth County,
the daughter of Michael Baer, who was born near Selby, S.D. and Katherine Ernst
Baer, who was born in Crimea, Russia and came to the Roscoe area in 1899.

        Berth, Kathy, Donald and Sam Johnle

Sam and Bertha were married November 29, 1936, in Theodore Lutheran Church near
Hoven, S.D. They moved to a farm in the Roscoe area in 1938.  They retired and
moved to Roscoe in 1973.

They have two children, Donald, living in Roscoe and Kathy (Mrs.  Dwayne Ham)
living in Bowdle.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe, where Bertha is a
member of the Ladies Aid. They are both members of the Roscoe Senior Citizens.

Sam passed away June 21, 1982 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

ALBIN N. AND OLIVE L. JOHNSON

Albin Johnson was born August 9, 1898 in Sands Township, Polk County,
Minnesota, near Alvarado, Minn., the son of Carl Johnson and Anna Ringdahl
Johnson.  He married Olive Erickson September 2, 1922 at Warren, Minn.  Olive
was the daughter of Helga and John Erickson.  As a young girl her parents moved
to Oslo, Minn. where she attended school.  Albin also spent his early years
near Oslo, attending school there and later attending Business College at
Crookston, Minn.

At the time of their marriage in 1922, Albin was employed at the bank in
Gonvick, Minn.  They spent their early years of marriage in Gonvick.  Their son,
Emmett, was born in 1926.  In 1927, they moved to Hendrum, Minn. and worked in
the bank and lived there for one year.  In 1929, they moved to Larimore, N.D.,
where Albin was employed by the Elk Valley State Bank of Larimore.  They made
Larimore their home until 1948, when they moved to Los Angeles, Calif. for
several years.  They then returned to Minnesota, living at Hendricks, Hawley
and later Walhalla, N.D. In July of 1954, they moved to Roscoe, where Albin
bought controlling interest in the First State Bank of Roscoe from William and
Charles Christen.  They retired in 1964 and continued to live in Roscoe until
the fall of 1979 when they moved to the Aberdeen Nursing Center, Aberdeen, S.D.

Olive died March 27, 1980 and was buried at Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.
Albin moved to Mother Joseph Manor in Aberdeen, S.D., where he still resides.
Their only child, Emmett Hardy, lives in Roscoe with his wife, Terry, and
family.

EMMETT H. AND THERESE M. JOHNSON

Emmett Hardy Johnson was born to Albin N. Johnson and Olive Erickson Johnson
September 26, 1926 at Bemidji, Minn.  He lived at Gonvick, Minn. until three
years of age when the family moved to Larimore, N.D. He married Therese Marie
Schissel October 3, 1951 at Ossion, Iowa.  Terry was born at Colmar, Iowa.  Her
parents were Alphonse A. and Mary Becker Schissel. She has four brothers and
one sister.  One sister died February 15, 1982.

Emmett attended Larimore Public School and the University of North Dakota.  He
received a B.S. degree in Industrial Engineering in 1950 and on Accounting
degree in 1954.  He served twice in the United States Army, first in 1946 and
again during the Korean Conflict.

Terry attended St. Aloysius School at Colmar, Iowa and Colmar High School.  She
then attended St. Francis School of Nursing in LaCrosse, Wis. and received her
degree in Nursing.

After their marriage in 1951 they lived in Denver, Colo., Grand Forks, N.D.,
and Minot, N.D. While in Minot, Emmett was employed by an accounting firm. They
moved to Roscoe September 15, 1956 when he joined his father at the First State
Bank of Roscoe.  He is currently President at the bank.

Emmett is a member of the American Legion, VFW, BPOE, Roscoe Commercial Club
and is a retired member of the Roscoe Fire Department.

Terry is a member of the American Legion Auxiliary, St. Thomas Altar Society,
Roscoe Commercial Club and the S.D. Nursing Association.

They are members of the St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.

        Back row: Mark Wehde, Mary, Nyla Singer and Brian; front row: Nancy,
        Cindy, Mark and Paula Johnson

They are parents of five children. Mark was born September 14, 1952 at Denver,
Colo.  He was four years old when they moved to Roscoe.  He graduated from
Roscoe High School and attended Northern State College at Aberdeen, S.D.,
graduating with a Masters in Guidance Counseling in 1977.

He worked one year with C.E.T.A. at Creston, Iowa. Since May of 1979, he has
lived at Boone, Iowa where he is a paramedic at Boone County Hospital.  He
married Paula Kluge at Ames, Iowa September 27, 1980.  Paula is also a
paramedic with the hospital.  They have one son, Nathan Anthony, born January
23, 1982.

Cindy was born May 6, 1955 in Minot, N.D. She graduated from Roscoe High School
and then attended the University of South Dakota, where she graduated with a
B.S. degree in social work and psychology.  She has managed the business office
at a Rapid City furniture store for the post four years.

Brian was born April 12, 1960 in Aberdeen, S.D. He graduated from Roscoe High
School in 1978.  He is a senior at South Dakota School of Mines and Technology
and is working on a degree in Electrical Engineering and Physics.

Mary Kay was born April 5, 1961 in Aberdeen.  She graduated from Roscoe High
School and completed three years at South Dakota State University at Brookings.
She is now attending the University of North Dakota and is majoring in
Physical Therapy.

Nancy was born December 23, 1967 in Aberdeen and is currently attending Roscoe
High School.

GIDEON AND WANITHA JUNG

Gideon Jung was born October 30, 1916 near Hosmer, S.D. to Frederich and
Caroline Will Jung.  His father, Frederich, was born March 16, 1876 in Russia
and came to the United States in 1889.  He died June II, 1959.  His mother,
Caroline, was born April 16, 1878 in Russia and came to this country in 1885.
She died March 26, 1962. Both are buried at Hosmer.

Wanitha Neifer Jung was born July 15, 1916, north of Roscoe.  She is the
daughter of John and Rosina Hieb Neifer.

Gideon and Wanitha were married in the Reformed Church in Hosmer March 8, 1936.
They settled on a farm near Hosmer until October 1940 when they moved to a
farm west of Roscoe, which has been their home ever since.

They have one son, Allan, who is married to Marlys Dickhaut, and also lives on
the home farm.  They have four children.

ALLAN AND MARLYS JUNG

Allan Jung was born September 17, 1936 at Hosmer, S.D. to Gideon and Wanitha
Neifer Jung.  He moved with his parents to the present farm home in 1940 from a
farm northeast of Hosmer.  Allan attended the rural school near his home.

Marlys Dickhout Jung was born to Dan Jr. and Verona (Buchmann) Dickhout April
21, 1940 at Hazen, N.D. They moved to the Tolstoy, S.D. area in 1945.  She came
to the Roscoe area at the time of her marriage.  She attended rural school near
her home and graduated from Plainview Academy in Redfield, S.D. in 1959.

Allan and Marlys were married December 27, 1959. They settled on the family
farm west of Roscoe.  They have three children: Allan F., born December 11,
1960, married Shirley Huber, June 14, 1980 at Bowdle, S.D.; Kathleen L., born
December 3, 1961, graduated from Presentation College in Aberdeen, S.D. May 8,
1982; Karen S., born December 19, 1964, a student at Roscoe High School, and
John J., born December 2, 1968, also a student at Roscoe.

Allan has served for several years as Deacon and Treasurer of the Hosmer
Reformed Church.  Marlys has served as a Sunday School teacher for several years
and F.H.A. Chapter Mother for three years.

Besides farming, they raise Simmental cattle and sell quality Simmental bulls.

ALBERT AND BELLE KILBER

Albert Kilber was born June 18, 1899 to Jacob and Christina Huber Kilber at
Freeman, S.D. As a child his parents and family moved to a farm near Roscoe,
S.D. where he grew up and also farmed.  Albert had eight brothers and four
sisters of whom Otto is the only one living.  He lives in Minneapolis, Minn.
Albert married Belle Merkel May 6, 1928 at Fort Pierre, S.D.

Belle Merkel Kilber was born January 2, 1907 to Ludwig and Eva Hoffman Merkel.
She lived in Bowdle, S.D. until her marriage to Albert Kilber.

Albert and Belle lived on a farm near Bowdle, S.D. until 1943 when they moved
back to the homestead near Roscoe and farmed there until 1958 when they retired
and moved into Roscoe.

They had one son, James, who also farms on the homestead near Roscoe.

Albert passed away July 25, 1980 and is buried in the Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.  Belle is residing in Roscoe.

JAMES AND ESTHER KILBER

James Kilber was born February 6, 1929 to Albert and Belle Merkel Kilber at
Roscoe, S.D. He grew up on the farm attending school in Bowdle and Roscoe.  He
graduated from Roscoe High in 1949.  He entered the Marine Corp in 1951 and
after his discharge in 1952 he returned to farm with his father.  September 14,
1958 he married Esther Heier in Aberdeen.

Esther Heier Kilber was born February 16, 1925 at Akaska, S.D. to Samuel and
Christina Schanzenbach Heier.  She grew up there and graduated from Glenham
High School in 1942.  After graduation she lived in Minneapolis, Minn. where
she worked in a defense plant.  Later, she moved to Kingsley, Iowa and worked
for Council Oak Grocery Store.  In 1948, she came to Aberdeen, S.D. and clerked
at National Tea Store until her marriage to James Kilber in 1958.  Since 1968
she has worked part-time at Sahli's Red Owl in Roscoe.

They lived on the farm until 1970 when they moved to Roscoe.  Jim is still
operating the farm.

Jim belongs to the Roscoe American Legion, the Volunteer Fire Department, Rod
and Gun Club, and Commercial Club.

Esther is a member of and post president of the Roscoe American Legion
Auxiliary, president of the Lutheran Ladies Aid and a member of the Commercial
Club.

ROBERT H. AND EMMA KILBER

Robert H. Kilber was born to Henry and Fredricka Dachtler Kilber in the
Freeman, S.D. area December 2l, 1899 and died January 13, 1979.  He was married
to Emma Albrecht in March 1925.  Emma was born to Alfred and Wilhelmina Miller
Albrecht in February 1905 and died in January 1952.

Nine children were born to Robert and Emma; Lester, Calvin, Rupert (died in
infancy), Arlo Wayne, Ramona, Robert, Jean, Donna and Delbert.

They were members of the Lutheran Church and were engaged in farming all their
married lives on a farm eight miles southwest of Roscoe.

Robert and Emma are both buried in the Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

LESTER AND LENORA KILBER

Lester Kilber was born October 19, 1925, to Robert and Emma Albrecht Kilber,
who were both born in the Roscoe vicinity.  Lester graduated from Roscoe High
School and spent most of his time working on the farm with his parents.  He
worked at Scherf's Super Valu a short time where he met Lenora Scheid who was
working next door at Joe Prisbe's Cafe.

Lenora Scheid Kilber was born February 3, 1926, at Long Lake, S.D. to Adolf and
Anna Spitzer Scheid who were both born in the Long Lake area.  They lived on a
farm two miles north of Long Lake.  In 1940, they purchased a farm nine and
one-half miles southeast of Roscoe.  Lenora worked at the C&C Cafe in Ipswich,
S.D. for several years, then went to work at Joe Prisbe's Cafe in Roscoe.
Lester and Lenora were married October 12, 1947, at St. Paul's Lutheran Church.
They started farming on his grandfather's farm, six miles west and three and
one-half miles south of Roscoe, where they are still living and dairy farming.
They ore milking 50 cows at the present time.

They have seven children, four boys and three girls.

Carmen Lee was born April 16, 1950, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1968,
attended NSC for one year and then went to Minneapolis where he got a part-time
job while he finished school with Graco Manufacturing. He was drafted into the
Army and was stationed in Seoul, Korea.  After the Army, he went back to his job
with Graco and is assistant manager.  He has one son, Ryan, who was born
October 4, 1973.

Corrine Mae was born September 10, 1951, graduated from Roscoe High School in
1969 and went to Minneapolis where she was employed in a bank.  Later she was
transferred to a bank in Aberdeen, S.D. where she met Clarence Heinz, who was
attending NSC.  They were married June 30, 1973 and made their home in Pierre,
S.D. where Clarence built their first home.  He worked for the South Dakota
Highway Department. Corrine worked at a Pierre bank.  In 1981, they moved to
Boise, Idaho, where Clarence works for Boise Cascade Company and Corrine works
as a secretary. They were blessed with twins, Jeff and Jill, born January 11,
1978.

Carlo Jean was born in 1954, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1972 and
worked for Control Data for a short time.  August 18, 1973 she married LeRoy
Beitelspacher.  They purchased the Ken Eisenbeisz farm a mile from his parents
where they are at the present time.  Carla also works as a secretary at the
Bowdle Hospital.  They have two sons, Brent, born April 28, 1976, and Colin,
born June 5, 1978.

Cornel Dean was born February 18, 1956, graduated from Roscoe High School in
1974, and from NSC in Aberdeen and is teaching in Flasher, N.D.

Cindy Lou was born June 16, 1957, on Father's Day which was a happy day for her
Dad.  She graduated from Roscoe High School in 1975 and worked for Safeguard
Industries in Aberdeen for a time.  She is now employed at the First State Bank
of Roscoe.  June 25, 1977, she married Darwin Rohrbach.  They live in Roscoe
where Darv works for Spitzers, Inc.  They have a daughter, Ericka Lynn, born
August 12, 1980.

Craig Lester was born June 8, 1959, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1977,
worked for Spitzers, Inc. and is now majoring in computer programming at USD in
Vermillion, S.D.

Last but not least, Clint Allen was born June 9, 1963. He tipped the scale at
12 pounds 10 ounces!  He graduated from Roscoe High School in 1981 and is
attending NSC, majoring in Business Administration. Lester and Lenora are
members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church, where Lester is a member of the church
board and Lenora is a member of the Ladies Aid.

RUBEN AND MATHILDA KIRSCHENMANN

Ruben Kirschenmann was born near Hosmer, S.D. January 4, 1909 to John and
Christina Kurle Kirschenmann.  Ruben's father, John, was four years old when he
came to America with his parents from South Russia.  They settled two and
one-half miles north of Hosmer, S.D. They later moved two and one-half miles
south of Hosmer, where Ruben grew up.

Mathilda Schick was born southwest of Hosmer, S.D. to William and Rosena Oster
Schick September 17, 1914.  Mathilda's parents came from South Russia in their
earlier years and homesteaded between Hosmer and Bowdle, S.D.

Ruben and Mathilda were married November 6, 1932 in Hosmer Congregational
Church.  They moved to a farm 11 miles south of Roscoe where they farmed for 13
years.  They then sold the farm and purchased the Roscoe Hardware from the
Adam's Bros. in 1945.  They built on and remodeled the store.  They handled
electrical appliances, hardware, plumbing, heating, and did electrical work.
They operated the store until 1969 when they sold it to Earl and Bernice
Hettick, who are the present owners.

Ruben served as an Edmunds County Commissioner for 12 years.  He was Mayor of
Roscoe and served on the City Council for several years.

Mathilda is a member of the Loyal Homemakers, having held the office of
secretary; Senior Citizens, having held the office of secretary-treasurer for
several years; board member of N. E. South Dakota Community Actions Program;
delegate to Area '4' Senior Citizens Nutrition Program; and post member of the
Congregational Ladies Aid, Garden Club and Legion Auxiliary.

        Loren Kirschenmann

        Harley Kirschenmann

Mathilda and Ruben have two children, Loren and Harley.  Both boys grew up in
Roscoe and are graduates of Roscoe High School.  Loren was born July 15, 1936.
He married Sharon Fischer July 5, 1959.  Their two daughters are Cheryln Lynn,
born December 11, 1968 and Stacey, born December 3, 1972.  They live in
Richardson, Texas where Loren works for Texas Instruments.

Harley was born November 10, 1941.  He married Carol Trefz November 23, 1962.
Their two sons are Robb James, who was born July 21, 1967 and Todd, born
February 17, 1971.  They live in Idaho Falls, Idaho where Harley works for
E.G.G.

Ruben died March 8, 1978 and is buried in the Roscoe Morningside Cemetery.
Mathilda resides in Roscoe.

        Carol, Robb, Todd and Harley Kirschenmann

HARLEY AND CAROL KIRSCHENMANN

Harley Kirschenmann was born in Roscoe in 1941 to Rueben and Mathilda Schick
Kirschenmann.  He attended grade and high school here, graduating with the
class of 1959.  Carol is also a former Roscoe resident, born to Henry and Lydia
Heil Trefz in Mound City, S.D. in 1942.  Her family moved to Roscoe in 1955.
She attended the Roscoe School and graduated in 1960.  While in Roscoe, Carol
worked for JoAnn's Apparel and Harley worked in his fathers' Hardware Store.

Carol attended NSC in Aberdeen.  Harley graduated from Northwestern Electronics
Institute in Minneapolis in 1962 and USD in 1967.  He received his Masters
Degree from Pepperdine University in California.

Harley and Carol were married in St. James Congregational Church in Roscoe in
November 1962. Harley's work has taken them to many places in the post 20
years, such as Charleston, S.C. working with Lockheed Aircraft.  In 1969 they
became residents of Crystal, Minn. when he worked for the Honeywell Corp., who
transferred them to their San Diego, Calif. location where they made their home
for six years.  In 1976, they moved to the "Great Northwest", Idaho Falls,
Idaho, where they are presently living.  Harley is employed by E. G. and G,
Idaho Inc. as the manager of the Quality Department.

They have two sons, Robb, age 15 and Todd, age 11.

CONRAD AND REGINA KNITTEL

Conrad and Regina Guenthner Knittel came to the United States from Russia in
1903.  They settled on a farm south of Bowdle, S.D. A few years later they
moved on a farm north of Roscoe and in the spring of 1918 they built a
homestead southeast of Roscoe in Montpelier Township.

In the fall of 1918, tragedy struck the family when they contacted the Spanish
flu.  October 10, a son Reinhold, age 18 died.  The father, Conrad, died October
15, at the age of 46.  A daughter, Leontina, age 17, and the mother, Regina,
age 38, both died November 4.

The family consisted of Reinhold, Leontina, Lydia (Mrs. Jacob Geist) deceased,
Frieda Casey, deceased, John of Roscoe; Edward, deceased; William, deceased;
Arthur, of St. John, Wash.; and Edwin of rural Aberdeen. Two sons died in
infancy.  All deceased members of the family are buried in Morningside Cemetery,
Roscoe, except Frieda, who is buried in Bowdle, S.D.

JOHN AND ANNA KNITTEL

John and Anna (nee Roesler) Knittel were married at St. Paul's Lutheran Church
in Roscoe November 24, 1935 by the Rev. J. P. Scherf.  They lived on the
Knittel homestead for 42 years until they retired and moved to Roscoe.

John was born near Bowdle, S.D. to Conrad and Regina (nee Guenthner) Knittel.
In 1918, his family built the homestead southeast of Roscoe where he lived most
of his life.  John was supervisor of Montpelier Township for 35 years.

Anna was born near Lowry, S.D. She lived with her uncle, Christ C. Schilling,
and later with her Uncle George C. Schilling at Akaska, S.D. In 1926, she lived
with her parents, Mr. and Mrs. Michael Hettich on a farm south of Roscoe.
Later she was employed in Aberdeen until her marriage to John Knittel.  Anna is
a member of St. Paul's Lutheran Ladies Aid.

WILLIAM AND ELIZA KNITTEL

William Knittel was born July 17, 1890 at Freeman, S.D. He was the son of
George and Margaret Ruede Knittel.

Eliza Walz was born in Neudorf, South Russia July 12, 1892, the daughter of
Wilhelm and Christina Guthmilleer Walz.  They came to the United States in 1897
and settled on a farm nine miles southeast of Hosmer.

William and Eliza were married February 18, 1913 at Hosmer and made their home
at Avon, S.D. Later they moved to Freeman, S.D. and in 1927 they came to the
Roscoe area, settling one and one-half miles northeast of Roscoe.

They had four children, Esther (Mrs.  Ed Werre), Helen (Mrs.  Christ Werre),
Gerhardt and Irene (Mrs.  Clarence Fordham).

They belonged to St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

Eliza died November 22, 1937 and William died February 16, 1967.  They are
buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

PETER J. AND JOSEPHINE KOTSCHEVAR

Peter J. Kotschevar was born February 9, 1899 in Farming, Minn. to Peter and
Katherine Lauer Kotschevar.  Peter's mother was born in Vadrill, Germany and
immigrated to the United States in 1883.

Josephine Deis Kotschevar was born April 13, 1906 near Onaka, S.D. to Lorenz
and Helen Weber Deis. Josephine's father was born in Mannheim, Russia in 1881
and came to the United States with his parents, Jacob and Catherine Deis and
homesteaded in the Onaka, S.D. area in 1948.

Peter arrived in South Dakota from Richmond, Minn. in May of 1921.  He met
Josephine and they were married in Onaka October 18, 1927.  They homesteaded
five miles south and one mile east of Roscoe.

In 1972 Peter and Josephine retired from farming and moved to Roscoe where they
purchased a home.

Peter and Josephine are affiliated with the First Presbyterian Church at Roscoe.

Josephine died December 28, 1980 and is buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

        Josephine, Arnold, Dennis, Delores, and Peter Kotschevar

Three children were born to this union: Arnold, Dennis and Delores.

Arnold was born September 1, 1928.  He is married to Marilyn Anderson.
Arnold's two children are Alan and Carol.  Arnold is a banker in Duluth, Minn.

Dennis was born November 11, 1931.  He is married to Inez Guthmiller.  Their
children are Brian, Karen, Joel and Julie.  Dennis is with the Insurance
Department at the Enderlin, N.D. Bank.

Delores was born October 25, 1932.  She is married to Ken Nelson.  Their
children are Deanne, JoLee, and Chris.  Delores is a secretary at the National
Bank in Rapid City, S.D. Ken is the Pennington County Agent.

RAYMOND KRAFT AND WAYNE LEBOLDUS FAMILIES

Although their address is Onaka, they feel they are also tied to the Roscoe
community.  Roy and Dwayne farm in partnership, 11 miles south and three and
one- half miles west of Roscoe, on the farmstead that was previously owned by
John and Lena Malsom, who are now living in Roscoe.  They no longer have dairy
cattle but still grain farm and raise stock cattle and calves.

        Back row: Diane, Roy, Delaine, and Donna; front row: Dale, Mary, and
        Darlene Kraft.

Ray and Mary live on the south side of the road.  Roy is active in the Onaka
Legion, Onaka Church Council, and the Hillside Township Board.  He also served
on the Land O'Lakes voting unit board.  Mary has served as on officer on the
Parents Advisory Council and Parents Music Association in Roscoe.  She also is
a catechism teacher in Onaka and works in the Onaka Grocery Store and is a
waitress at the Gold Nugget in Bowdle.

Ray and Mary have five children, all of which attended Roscoe Public School,
beginning in kindergarten through their present school year.

Delaine, born July 18, 1963, graduated from Roscoe in 1981 with honors and is
