Cystic Fibrosis.

Darlene, born September 19, 1973, was the youngest rider in the 1980
Bike-a-thon for Cystic Fibrosis at Roscoe.  She rode 11 miles.

        Judie, Karen, Chad, and Dwayne Leboldus

Dwayne and Judie live on the north side of the road. Dwayne was an active
member of the United States Army Reserves from 1966 until 1972 and is now a
member of Onaka Legion, Roscoe School Board and the Edmunds County Soil
Conservation District.  In 1981, Dwayne received the Outstanding Supervisor
Award from the South Dakota Association of Conservation Districts.

Judie was an active member in the Farmerettes Extension Club from 1976 to 1979.
Just a part of the various services the club provided for the community were:
they became a kitchen band and entertained for the Bi-Centennial celebrations
in both Roscoe and Onaka; provided some playground equipment for the Roscoe
Park; and they constructed new gold drapes for the Roscoe School gym-stage.

Judie was a catechism teacher in Onaka and is presently the clerk in the Onaka
Post Office.  Dwayne and Judie have two children.  Three of their family
members have attended Roscoe Public School.  Chad, born August 9, 1969, serves
as an altar boy at the St. John's Catholic Church in Onaka, and he completed the
full 18 miles in the 1980 Bike-a-thon for Cystic Fibrosis. Karen, born March
26, 1971, was crown bearer for the Homecoming Coronation at Roscoe in 1977 and
she completed the full 18 miles in the 1980 Bike-a-thon for Cystic Fibrosis at
Roscoe.  Chad and Karen have attended Roscoe Public School beginning in
kindergarten through their present year.

Their father, Dwayne, also attended Roscoe Public School during his eighth
through senior years, graduating in 1963.  Dwayne is currently a Roscoe School
Board member.  Dwayne's father, Mike Leboldus, was also a Roscoe School Board
member from June 1960 until June 1964.  Hillside Township joined the Roscoe
School District in 1958.

Mike and Mary live and grain farm two miles south of the K and L Dairy
location.  Mike and Mary's daughter, Marietta, also attended Roscoe Public
School in 1958 through 1961, graduating in 1961.  Marietta and her husband,
Wolter Stewart, and their daughter, Kari Lynn, now live in California.

JOHN AND MARTHA KRANZLER

John H. Kranzler was born October 3, 1918, northeast of Hosmer, the son of Fred
and Magdalena Eckman Kranzler, John grew up on a farm and attended rural
schools.  He served in the Army during WWII from February 24, 1942 until
September 7, 1945, being stationed in Germany for much of that time.

Martha Stabbe Kranzler was born at Hosmer May 21, 1922, the daughter of
Ferdinand and Regina Reede Stabbe, who came to this country from Zuerichtal,
Russia.

Johnny and Martha were married November 4, 1945. They settled on his family
farm and lived there all their married life.

They were blessed with three sons: Harvey is a teacher in Alexandria, Minn., is
married and has two daughters, Tonia and Sara; Rodney lives in Pierre, S.D.
where he works for the State in Social Services.  He's married and has one
daughter, Nicole; Fred lives in Aberdeen, S.D. and works as a draftsman for
U.B.C. He is married and has one son, Jonathon.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe where Martha is a
member of the Ladies Aid and John served on the Church Council.  John also was a
member of the North Bryant Township board for many years.

John was killed in a highway accident November 19, 1979 and is buried in
Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe. After his death, Martha built a new home in
Roscoe and moved into town July 3, 1980.

KENNETH AND ADELINE KRENZ

Kenneth Krenz was born June 17, 1904 at Amery, Wis. to Frank A. and Edith
Kennedy Krenz.  He grew up and went to school in that area.  June 2, 1933, he
married Adeline A. Brunmaier of Roscoe at Ipswich, S.D. Adeline was born at
Artas, S.D. July 20, 1909 to John and Mary Oster Brunmaier.  She grow up and
attended school in the Bowdle area.

Kenneth and Adeline made their home in New Richmond, Wis.  They moved to Lodi,
Calif. where they lived about 10 years.  While in Lodi he drove a truck for a
fruit company.  In 1945, they went back to Wisconsin for about a year and then
came to live in Roscoe in 1946. Here he was a trucker for Schneider Truck Lines
until about 1957.  He went to North Dakota to work for Tennefor Construction
Company for about six years, maintaining their home in Roscoe during that time.
After this, he retired and they still live in Roscoe where they plant a large
garden every year and enjoy reaping the harvest.

They have three children, all born in Lodi, Calif.

Helen Mary was born December 14, 1937, is married to Lee Kolasch and lives in
Rapid City, S.D. They have two children.

Lucille, born December 26, 1942, is married to Roger Ackerman, has five
children and lives in Aberdeen, S.D.

Kenneth, born April 21, 1944, is a custodian in the Rapid City school system.

TONY AND PAULETTE KUB

Tony Kub was born to Frank and Mabel Kub February. 28, 1952 in Aberdeen, S.D.
He married Paulette Ehnes November 27, 197 1. Paulette was born May 13, 1951 in
Aberdeen, to Simon and Anna Mary Ehnes.  Tony was raised on the home place
located 14 miles southeast of Roscoe in Cleveland Township.

He attended a country school near his home, Holy Cross School and Ipswich
Public School.  He stayed on the farm to help his father until 1972 when he
entered the military service.  Upon completion of his service term, he returned
to the farm where he and his family still live.

Paulette attended Roscoe Public School and graduated in 1969 and then attended
Stewarts School of Hairstyling in Aberdeen.  After graduation, she worked at
Dorothy's Beauty Salon in Ipswich.  In 1975, Paulette worked in the Roscoe
Beauty Shop and in 1976, purchased the ownership.

        Tony and Paulette have two children, Justin, born December 13, 1976 and
        Brandy, born March 9, 1979.

JOSEPH AND ANNA KUKRALL, JR.

Joseph Kukrall, Jr. was born November 24, 1898 in Bon Homme County, S.D. Anna
Kadlec Kukrall was born February 11, 1900 at Tyndall, S.D.

Joseph and Anna were married November 23, 1920 at Tyndall, S.D. They farmed
near Geddes for over two years.  In February of 1923, they moved to the Harry
Cole farm (now owned by John Beyers).  October 1, 1923 they moved to the
present farm (Kukrall Bros.) They rented until 1946, then purchased the farm
from John and Elizabeth Pfieffer, who previously purchased it from the Rural
Credit Board.

        Back row: Clarence, Joe, Anna, Lorraine, and William; front row: Delano
        and Roy Kukrall.

After Mr. Pfieffer's death of a heart attack, they mode payments to Mrs.
Pfieffer and the Rural Credit Board. Joseph and Anna came to the Harry Cole
farm with 200 chickens, four cows, four horses, old machinery, and no cash.
Anna sold some of the chickens for $75 and bought a milk cow which ended up a
three-titter.  From this meager beginning, they and their three sons, Roy,
William and Delano, tore down most of the old buildings over a period of years.
A modern nine room house was built in 1949 and a new garage, machine shed,
cattle shed, hog-farrowing building and various other improvements were mode.

Eight children were born to Joe and Anna: Wilbert of Canton, S.D.; Rene of
Torrence, Calif.; Clarence of Roscoe, S.D.; Willard (deceased in 1970); Roy,
William and Delano of Roscoe, S.D. and one daughter, Lorraine, (Mrs.  Dale
Gebhard) of Wecota, S.D.

Joe died of a heart attack November 11, 1954.  Anna continued to live on the
farm with Roy, William and Delano until her death on September 27, 1980 at the
age of 80 years, seven months and 16 days.

CLARENCE KUKRALL FAMILY

Clarence Kukrall was born November 18, 1923 near Loyalton to Joe and Anna
Kadlec Kukrall.  He was raised and educated in Montpelier Township.  In 1941 he
entered the Civilian Conservation Corps.  After being discharged in 1942, he
returned home to help on the farm until 1944, when he entered the Navy.  In
1946 he was discharged and returned home.  During this time he worked for his
father and also for Jake Baer.  Later he worked for George Hammond in the
construction business.  It was during this time that he met Doris Ernst.

Doris Ernst was born October 27, 1927 near Roscoe to Robert and Ida Schick
Ernst.  She was raised and educated in the Roscoe area.  She married Reinhold
Schumacher September 15, 1947.  To this marriage was born one daughter, Sharon,
August 12, 1948.  After Reinhold's death in 1952, Doris worked at the cafe in
Roscoe that Clarence's brother, Willard, operated.

During this time she met Clarence and in 1956 they were married.  They
continued to live in Roscoe until Clarence purchased a farm one mile south of
Loyalton.

        Wayne, Clarence and Doris Kukrall

        Robert, Ryan, Michelle, and Sharon Rohrbach

In 1958 their son, Wayne was born.  Wayne attended school at Loyalton until the
sixth grade when the Roscoe and Loyalton schools consolidated.  He attended
Roscoe school and graduated in 1976.  He is presently farming with his father.

Sharon attended grade school at Loyalton through the eighth grade, then
attended Roscoe High School and graduated in 1966.  She attended Aberdeen
School of Commerce and Stewarts School of Hairstyling.  Sharon married Robert
Rohrbach of Hosmer in 1968 and is presently working for Minnegasco in the
billing department.  Bob is a building superintendent for Physicians and
Surgeons Building.  They have two children, Michelle, age 11 and Ryan, age 8.

KUKRALL BROTHERS

Roy was born May 4, 1927 at Loyalton, S.D. and served in the United States Army
from 1950 to 1952.

Delano was born April 9, 1933 at Loyalton, S.D. and served in the United States
Navy from 1955 to 1957.

William was born June 23, 1931 at Loyalton, S.D.

After Roy and De1ano were discharged from the service they returned to the
family farm to resume their farming careers.

William, Delano and Roy are members of the Roscoe Presbyterian Church.

Parents were Joseph F. Kukrall and Anna Kadlec Kukrall.

JOHN AND ELIZABETH LACHER

John J. Lacher was born August 19, 1899 in Zeeland, N.D., the son of John and
Eva Schumacher Lacher. Elizabeth Sahli was born December 2, 1899 at Hosmer,
S.D., the daughter of Theophil and Margaret Heilman Sahli.

John and Elizabeth were married November 23, 1921, in Hillsview, S.D. They
farmed near Zeeland, N.D. until 1930, when they moved to a farm four miles
south of Roscoe.  In 1956, they moved to Aberdeen where John worked as a
janitor at St. Luke's Hospital until he retired in 1967.

They had six children: Barbara, born August 24, 1922, married George Fettig
February 5, 1946, lives in San Mateo, Calif.; Margaret, born February 29, 1924,
married Fred W. Miller November 10, 1946, lives at Roscoe; Julia, born April
18, 1926, married Anton Gruenstein September 23, 1946, lives in Aberdeen;
Pauline, born March 30, 1929, married Melvin Voegele June 21, 1951, died June
17, 1967; Viola, born August 21, 1931, married Wilbert Hettich May 10, 1954,
lives in Selby, S.D.; Valentine, was born July 29, 1934, married Anna Norton
September 25, 1962, and lives in Aberdeen.

Elizabeth died May 23, 1974 and John died April 6, 1982.  They are both buried
in St. Mary's Cemetery in Aberdeen.

JACOB AND MARIA LAIB

        Back row: Evelina Schumacher, Anna Beck; front row: Gwen Schumacher and
        Marie Laib (four generations).

Jacob and Maria Hoffman Laib were both born in Russia and came to the United
States in 1899 after their marriage.  In 1905, they settled near Bowdle, S.D.
Throughout the following years they moved to a farm northwest of Roscoe and
later to a farm south of Roscoe, the present Ruben Beck farm.

They later moved to Roscoe where Jacob worked for Martin Scherf until his death
June 4, 1944.  Maria Laib continued to live in Roscoe until the fall of 1961.
She then made her home with her daughter and son-in-law, Mr. and Mrs. John
Beck, at Ipswich, until her death June 1, 1974.

JOHN AND CHRISTINA LAMMLE

John Lammle, son of John and Katherine Heil Lammle, was born December 10, 1886
in Groganut, Russia.  He came to America with his parents February 16, 1893 and
settled in rural Tyndall, S.D. They moved to McPherson County, South Dakota in
1901.  Later John homesteaded in Canada for a few years.

November 23, 1909 he married Christina Spitzer of McPherson County, near
Hosmer.  Christina was born December 19, 1889 in Marienthal, Russia to Daniel
and Christina Bieber Spitzer, and came to America with her parents May 4, 1892,
to McPherson County.

John and Christina moved to a farm north of Roscoe and in 1914, with their
children Lydia, Adeline and William, they moved to the town of Roscoe.  Armond,
Leona and Merilyn were born in Roscoe.

For some years John drilled wells, then became a garageman at Roscoe Auto
Company and a John Deere dealer in partnership with John Spitzer.  Later that
partnership was dissolved and he managed the Roscoe Auto Company and John
Spitzer the Implement business.

He became a United States citizen January 12, 1922. While in Roscoe he was an
elder in St. Paul's Lutheran Church for six years and served on the city
council and the school board.

Christina was active in St. Paul's Lutheran Church Ladies Aid.  She was a
wonderful seamstress and sewed for many of the people.

Due to ill health John retired and they moved to St. James, Minn. in 1944.  He
passed away February 27, 1951.  Christina passed away August 9, 1978.  They are
buried in Mt.  Hope Cemetery in St. James.

Their six children are Lydia, married to Gordon Wolner; Adeline, married Walter
Bartsch, now of New Ulm, Minn.; William, married Martha Melheim, living in St.
James; Armond, married Avis Porter and lives in Roscoe; Leona, married to Duard
Pickett, lives in Simi Valley, Calif. and Merilyn, married to Gerald Vielhaber
of Byron, Minn.

They had 14 grandchildren and three great-grandchildren.

ARMOND AND AVIS LAMMLE

Armond Lammle was born at Roscoe March 25, 1917 to John and Christina Spitzer
Lammle.  He married Avis Porter at Webster, S.D. September 15, 1941.  Armond
attended school at Roscoe and worked with his father at the Roscoe Auto Co.
until his induction into the Army March 25, 1941.  He served in the South
Pacific during WWII and received his discharge in October 1945. Armond and Avis
moved to St. James, Minn. where Armond was partsman for St. James Implement.  In
1976 they moved to Roscoe where he is partsman for Spitzer Implement.

Avis Porter was born at Java, S.D. June 9, 1919 to Harry L. and Marie Larsin
Porter.  She moved to Roscoe with her parents in 1931 where her father was
employed at the Roscoe Depot for the Milwaukee Railroad. She attended the
seventh and eighth grades at Roscoe and graduated from Bath High School.  She
attended USD, Vermillion, S.D. and taught in the Roscoe School District from
1940-1942.

Armond and Avis have five children, Jon Armond, born August 26, 1946 at St.
James, married Jurene Wolf August 6, 1966 and is living at Parker's Prairie,
Minn. They have two children, Timothy, Jon and Susan Lynn.

Leslie Lee, born October 14, 1947 at Aberdeen, S.D. married Kayla Lind June 20,
1971 and lives in Denver, Colo. and has one son, Christopher Justin.

Glen Irwin, born October 22, 1950 at St. James, married Eileen Hove October 6,
1973 and lives in Alexandria, Minn. with their two children, Jessica Caroline
and Justin Armond.

Lori Renee, born June 22, 1955 in Rochester, Minn., married Calvin Olson April
17, 1982 and lives in Manhattan, Kan.

Alan Clay, born August 18, 1961 in St. James, lives in Alexandria.  He
graduated from Roscoe High School in 1980 and attended AVTI at Alexandria,
Minn.  He is employed at Extrusion in Alexandria.

PHILIPP AND MARIE LAMMLE

Philipp Lammle was born May 21, 1892 in Kroganon, Russia to John and Katherina
Heil Lammle.  He came to America with his parents, sisters and brothers,
February 16, 1893, and settled on a farm in the Avon, S.D. area.  Later they
moved to McPherson County and farmed near Hosmer, S.D.

He married Marie Fischer from Greenway, S.D. November 23, 1915.  She was born
October 27, 1893 and was the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Carl Fischer.  They
continued to farm until 1943, when they moved to Roscoe and operated Lammle
Trucking.  He retired in 1967.

They had two children.  Helmuth married Esther Job April 30, 1936.  They moved
to Redwood Falls, Minn. where he did carpenter work.  In 1945 and 1946, he
served his country in the military service.  In 1949 they moved back to Roscoe
where he was in partnership with Eugene Job, doing excavating for four years.
In 1953 they moved back to Redwood Falls and again did carpenter work.  They
had two children, Lloyd, living in Florida and Janette, married to Kenneth
Woscek, living on a farm near Redwood Falls; and Esther Lammle, who married
Calvin Walth April 18, 1943.  They lived on a farm near Hosmer until 1955 when
they moved to New Ulm, Minn.  Calvin worked for the State Sign Shop for one
year and then for Kraft Cheese Mfg. until he passed away of a heart attack May
5, 1976.  Esther worked for Wear Well Garment Co., Windings, Inc., Kroft and
Schells Gardens until retirement.  They had two daughters.  Donna, a beautician
and has her own Aquarius Hair Design Salon in New Ulm, and Diana, living in San
Francisco, Calif. and works as office manager for On-Line Business Systems, Inc.

Philipp passed away at the age of 77 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.  Marie is living at the Colonial Manor Nursing Home in Ipswich, S.D.

WILLIAM AND CAROLINE LAMMLE

William Lammle was born September 17, 1899, in Tyndall, S.D. to John and
Katherine Hell Lammle.  He came to McPherson County with his parents in 1901.
They settled northeast of Hosmer where he grew up and attended country school.
In 1915 he came to Roscoe and worked as a clerk in Ben Redman's grocery store.
In 1916 to 1917 he worked with his brother, John, drilling wells and later he
worked in the Schumacher Bros.  Blacksmith Shop.  He bought cream from 1919 to
1921. In 1922 he went into the Implement business, purchasing the stock from
Peter Malsom.  The building was remodeled in 1923 and again in 1946.  This
burned down completely in 1947.  He rebuilt the some year of cement block tube
and after 33 years of continuous business he sold it to Robert Auchampach in
1955.

He married Caroline Schumacher in 1919.  She was the daughter of Solomon and
Rosina Bieber Schumacher. They established their home in Roscoe where they lived
until 1971 when they moved to Auburn, Neb.

They have four children: Irene (Jordeth) living in Miller, S.D., has two
children; Erma (Peterson) living in Pasco, Wash.; Walter, married to Angeline
Dutenhoeffer, lives in Shenandoah, Iowa and has four children and Arnold,
living in Edgemont, Calif.

Mr. Lammle served in various offices.  He was City Treasurer for 13 years, City
Council member one year, President of Commercial Club three years, Chairman of
Volunteer Fire Department five years, Church Officer 30 years and City Auditor
26 years.

Caroline passed away in 1975 and Bill in 1978.  They are buried in Auburn, Neb.

JOHN P. AND ROSA LANDFRIED

        John A. Langfried, Jr.

John P. Landfried, Sr. was born in 1872 in Sturgeon Bay, Wis.  He came to the
Roscoe area in 1903 and settled in Montpelier Township.

Rosa Wagner was born in 1887 in Wis.  She came to Roscoe when she married John
Landfried in about 1906.  They made their home in Montpelier Township until
1913 when they moved to Bryant Township.  This is where they lived until 1921
when they moved to a farm one-half mile west of what is now Ralph Preszler's
farm in Glen Township, living there until 1923, moving one mile west and one
mile north to what was known as the Borsberry place.  They moved to town in
October 1937.

John P. died in January 1938.  Rosa continued to make her home in Roscoe.  She
died in 1969.  They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery.

They had ten children, Minnie (Mrs.  Ed Knittel) living in Aberdeen.

Esther (Mrs.  George Price), is deceased.

Emma is living in Sacramento, Calif.

Ida (Mrs.  Val Buechler) is deceased.

John A. is living in Roscoe.  He is a retired railroad man, and served in the
Army from February 1942 to September 1945.

William, living in Toole, Utah, served in the Army December 1941 to 1946.

Louis, living in Tacoma, Wash., served in the Army from January 1941 to 1961.

Christina (Mrs. Frank Peterson) lives near Tolstoy, S.D. Rosie (Mrs. Ben
Daniels) lives near Hecla, S.D. Anna (Mrs. John Daniels) lives near Hecla.

HENRY LEFEBVRE

Henry LeFebvre was born in Wisconsin.  His father had several blacksmith shops
and Henry acquired the one in Middleton in Dave County.  As he accumulated a
small amount of money, he began dealing in real estate.  A couple of fellows
who owned land near Roscoe asked him to sell their property.  He went to Roscoe
and sold their property, advertised in several states and did very well selling
farms.  He then built the Farmers State Bank building located on the southwest
corner of main street on Highway 12.

Apparently things went good for several years, then the dry years arrived and
this among other things wiped him out.  He then went to work as a foreman at
Northern Pacific Railroad in St. Paul and was employed there for several years.
He then purchased a blacksmith shop in Mora, Minn.  This was about 1925 or
1926.

He had his son as a partner.  In the winter of 1939, his doctor informed him
that he had cancer.  He died in September 1941.  His grandson, Maurice
LeFebvre, who submitted this history, stated that he lost the best friend and
advisor that he ever had.

FRANK AND KATHERINE LEHMAN

Francis Xaviar Lehman was born June 26, 1884 in Carsonville Township, Becker
County, Minn. to Frank X. and Mary Friedman Lehman.  Frank X. Sr. and his wife
Mary were both born at North Washington, Iowa and are also both buried there.

They had three sons, all of whom came to Roscoe in the early 1900's.  While at
Roscoe, Frank worked as a welldriller for many miles around the area.  When he
was working in the Eureka, S.D. area, he met Katherine Kapp and they were
married November 25, 1913 in Eureka.

Katherine Kopp was born November 19, 1892 to Joseph and Sophia Kemmel Kapp in
South Russia and came to the United States at an early age.  Her parents are
both buried in the Eureka Cemetery.

Frank and Katherine had four children, all of them born in Roscoe.  The
youngest died in infancy and is buried at Roscoe.

In 1922, the family moved to Mandan, N.D. where Frank was employed by the
Northern Pacific Railroad. Their children, Edna, Alice and Sidney went to school
and graduated there.  During World War II Frank was transferred to Green River,
Wyo. to work on the Union Pacific Railroad.  They lived there until the time of
his death March 25, 1948.  He is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

Katherine passed away July 27, 1970, and is also buried at Roscoe.

Alice Lehman Gray passed away in December 1979 and is buried in Centralia,
Wash. where she was living at the time.

Sidney Lehman is presently living at Jamaica Plains, Mass. and Edna L. Hille
lives in Roscoe.

ADOLPH AND MINNIE LEHR

        Back row: Eugene, Edwin, Elmer; front row: Minnie, Adolph and Martha
        Lehr.

Adolph Lehr was born November 28, 1889, near Hillsview, S.D. to Andrew and
Freidericka Aman Lehr, Sr. He was raised there and attended school there.  His
wife Minnie was born December 6, 189 1, in Hutchinson County, S.D. to Christian
and Amelia Ganske Sayler. Her family moved to a farm northeast of Hosmer, S.D.
and she attended school there.

Adolph and Minnie were united in marriage November 26, 1912, north of Hosmer.
They moved to a farm north of Hosmer and lived there about eight years. During
this time, a son Edwin, was born August 1, 1913. About 1920, they moved to
Hillsview.  Two sons were born here, Elmer on May 19, 1922 and Eugene, on
August 21, 1927.  In 1928, they moved their family to a farm about eight miles
northeast of Roscoe.  They farmed there until 1949 when they retired and moved
into Roscoe.  Adolph passed away at his home August 24, 1951, at the age of 61
and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.  Minnie still lives in Roscoe
with her son Edwin.

Edwin attended school in Hillsview and at a farm school near the family farm.
He entered the Armed Forces in February, 1942.  He was stationed at Camp
Barkley, Texas for a short time before being sent to the South Pacific Islands
and then on to the Philippine Islands.  He was returned to the United States and
discharged in September, 1945.  He then farmed with his parents until December
1949 when he moved to town with them and continued farming until 1969.

Elmer attended school also in Hillsview and at the farm school near the family
farm.  He married Martha Pietz September 18, 1949 and they moved on the family
farm where they still live.

Eugene also attended the farm school near the family home and then on to Roscoe
High School.  Upon graduation in 1946, he attended the State School of Science
in Wahpeton, N.D. February 21, 1951 he entered the Armed Forces and was
stationed at Fort Warden, Wash.  After his discharge, February 20, 1953, he
returned to Roscoe and farmed for two years. In the fall of 1954, he moved to
Aberdeen and worked for Dakota Sash and Door until April 1979.  He then worked
for the South Dakota Department of Labor in Aberdeen where he is presently
employed.  He married Marilyn Wiseman June 30, 1972 at Aberdeen where they live
now.

ELMER AND MARTHA LEHR

        Back row: Marlin, Dennis and Dean; front row: Martha and Elmer Lehr.

Elmer Lehr was born May 19, 1922 to Adolph and Minnie Sayler Lehr at Hillsview,
S.D. He lived in Hillsview and attended school there until 1928. His family
moved to a farm eight miles northeast of Roscoe in Bryant Township.  He then
attended a nearby farm school.

Martha was born August 20, 1931, to John and Magdalena Burrer Pietz on a farm
northeast of Hosmer, S.D. She lived here and attended the Blaine Township
school, graduating from the eighth grade in 1944.  She then worked on the farm.

Elmer and Martha were united in marriage September 18, 1949 at the Hoffnunsfeld
Congregational Church by the Rev. R. Knaus.  This church was located north of
Newtown, S.D. They moved to his parents homestead northeast of Roscoe and have
lived there since.  Elmer and Martha belong to the United Church of Christ in
Roscoe.

To this union were born three sons: Dean Curtis, born June 28, 1951, is
presently an auto mechanic at Roscoe Auto Co. He married Marilyn Heilman
October 14, 1972, at Hosmer and are currently living in Roscoe. They have three
children.  Marlin Elmer, born September 28, 1954, is presently working at
Liebelts Homes in Aberdeen, S.D. He married Susan Lunders June 9, 1978 at
Hosmer and they are currently living in Aberdeen.  They have one child.  Dennis
James was born October 6, 1960, and is presently farming with his parents
northeast of Roscoe.

DEAN AND MARILYN LEHR

        Back row: Marilyn, Stephanie, and Dean; front row: Brian and Samantha
Lehr.

Dean Lehr was born June 28, 1951 at Ipswich, S.D. to Elmer and Martha Pietz
Lehr of Roscoe, S.D. His wife Marilyn was born December 13, 1953 at Eureka,
S.D. to Christian and Agatha Malsom Heilman of Hosmer, S.D.

Dean was raised on a farm northeast of Roscoe.  He attended farm school for the
first three years and then entered the Roscoe Public School system where he
graduated in 1970.  In the fall of 1970, he entered the Lake Area Vo-Tech
School at Watertown, S.D. receiving an Automotive Mechanical Degree in 1971.
He is employed at the Roscoe Auto Company.

Marilyn grew up on a farm west of Hosmer and attended school in Hosmer,
graduating in 1972.

Dean is an active member of the Roscoe Volunteer Fire Department and is

Marilyn was active in the Roscoe Women's Softball Association and is currently

Dean and Marilyn were blessed with three children: Stephanie Lynn was born

A daughter, Mary, was stillborn October 21, 1979, a twin to Samantha.  She is

ANDREW AND KATHERINE LEHR

Andrew Lehr was born May 26, 1896 near Hillsview, S.D. to Andrew and Regina
Aman Lehr.  He grew up there.  He served in the Army during WW I as a U.S.
Guard in Kansas.

Katherine Barth was born September 25, 1900 north of Eureka, S.D. to Wilhelm
and Barbara Henne Barth.  As a young girl she moved to a farm seven miles
northeast of Hosmer where she grew up.

Andrew and Katherine were married November 30, 1922 at Katherine's family home
where the marriage took place due to her father's illness.  The ceremony was
performed by Rev. Brown from St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer.  They
settled on a farm about three miles east of Hillsview where they lived about
four years.  In 1927, they moved to a farm five and one-half miles east and
north of Roscoe.  In 1948, they moved to Roscoe.

        Andrew, Marvin, Raymond and Katherine Lehr

They had two sons, Raymond, married to Hildegard Schaible, has three children,
Dwight, Raeleen, and LaDell, is semi-retired and lives in Roscoe; Marvin,
married to Florence Heilman, has two sons, Glenn and Clyde, lives in Britton,
S.D. where they own a motel, the Sale Barn and he does some farming east of
Hecla, S.D.

They belong to St. James Congregational Church in Roscoe.  Andrew was a member
of the Roscoe American Legion.

Andrew died June 29, 1963 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.
Katherine is a resident in the Senior Housing Unit in Roscoe.

RAYMOND AND HILDEGARDE LEHR

EMANUEL LEHR FAMILY

        Helen and Emanuel Lehr

Emanuel Lehr, son of Andrew Lehr, Sr. and Fredricka Aman Lehr was born at
Hillsview, S.D. in McPherson County April 3, 1902.  He grew to his manhood on
the farm that his parents homesteaded.  June 30, 1924 he married Martha Docktor
at Ashley, N.D. Martha was born December 6, 1904 near Venturia, N.D. to Jacob
Docktor and Margaret Stoecker Docktor.

Four children were born to them.  One child, Leslie, died in infancy and the
living are: Virginia, who married Walter Mastel of Ipswich, S.D. They have two
sons, Gregory and Thomas and they all live in Sioux Falls.  Leah, married
Arthur Dabbert, of Roscoe, S.D. and now lives in Russell, Kan. They have two
children, Bruce and Carla.  Myron Patrick, married Deloris Hettich of Roscoe,
S.D. and is living on the farm east of Roscoe. They also have two children,
Carey and Karen.

They lived at Hillsview until 1945 when they moved to the Roscoe area on the
farm east of Roscoe known as the Adam Hofer farm.  Due to well failure the farm
was moved to the present location where Myron now lives. Martha Lehr passed
away September 23, 1953 at Roscoe.

September 10, 1955 married Helen (Opp) Fisher. Helen was born at Leola, S.D.
October 28, 1911 to Daniel D. Opp and Katherina (Hoff) Opp.  Helen has a son,
Marshall, who finished his education at Roscoe.  He now lives in Chaska, Minn.
with his family.

Mr. and Mrs. Lehr continued to live on the farm a couple more years when they
moved the school house that was four miles west of Roscoe, S.D. to town and
remodeled it for a house where they are still living.

MYRON AND DELORIS LEHR

        Sharon, Carey, Deloris, and Pat Lehr, Karen and Scot McIntosh

Myron (Pat) Lehr was born March 17, 1931 to Emanuel Lehr and Martha (Docktor)
Lehr at Eureka, S.D. He lived on the farm that his grandparents homesteaded in
the Hillsview area until he was 14 years old.  His parents then moved to the
Roscoe area.  Myron spent two years in the Army during the Korean Conflict 1952
to 1954. He married Deloris Hettich of Roscoe, S.D. June 20, 1953.  Deloris was
the daughter of Fred Hettich and Rosalia (Kohler) Hettich and was born December
28, 1932.

They lived on the Lehr farm for a year then moved to Rapid City, S.D. for a
couple of years.  Farming and ranching always on their minds, they returned to
the farm in 1957 and made it their home ever since.

Two children were born to them.  Carey, born October 19, 1954, married Sharon
Lout of Aberdeen, S.D. They live by Aberdeen and he works with his
father-in-law. They have a daughter Angela Lee, born February 3, 1982.

Karen was born July 15, 1957.  She married Scot McIntosh of Roscoe, S.D. They
lived at Eureka for a few years, then moved to a ranch northeast of Hot
Springs, S.D.

Someday they plan to retire out in the Black Hills area.

HENRY AND EMILY LIEBER

        Emily and Edwin Lieber

Henry and Emily Leibel Lieber were married in Wisconsin and came to South
Dakota in 1900.  They settled on the last homestead in Edmunds County which was
the southeast corner of section 23.  Here they broke their seven acres which
was required.

The immigrants, as they were called, would ride as for as Faulkton on immigrant
cars.  Then they hitched their horses to a wagon and came the rest of the way
to their destination.  It was said Mr. Lieber hauled his belongings of two
cows, one pig, two horses, 12 chickens and a stirring plow on that car.  They
lived on the homestead until 1907 when they moved to Roscoe to run "Liebers
General Store" which was located where Hieb's Repair is now.  In 1911, they
moved back to the homestead until the time of their death.

Henry died in 1929 and Emily in 1955.  Both are buried in Ipswich.

To this union four children were born: Edwin, Blanche, and Walter, all born in
Wisconsin and since deceased, and Charlotte, who was born in Gatham, Wis. in
1896 and attended schools in the Roscoe area.  She now lives in Hot Springs,
S.D.

The parents of the Liebers came from Germany.

RICHARD AND NOAMIE LEIDHOLDT

Richard Leidholdt, one of the 12 children of Jacob and Margaret Bietzke
Leidholdt, was born July 31, 1907, in a sod house 12 miles southwest of Roscoe.
This is where he grew up, went to country school and farmed with his father
until 1934 when he was married.

Noamie Martz was born on a farm near Hosmer, S.D. April 11, 1909.  She was the
daughter of Daniel and Eva Wiedrich Martz, one of seven children.  She grew up
here and attended the rural school near her home.

Richard and Noamie were married by Rev. J. P. Scherf January 21, 1934. They
lived in Hosmer until they moved on a farm south of Roscoe in 1935 and in 1936
they moved to Roscoe where they have lived ever since. Richard did some farming
and did custom wood sawing. He worked on the railroad coal chute for a short
time and on Highway Construction for many years until his health forced him to
retire.  Noamie worked for many years at the Schneider Hotel.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church.

The Leidholdts have always raised a large garden and enjoyed having their own
fresh vegetables every year. They are happy to call Roscoe their home.

STANLEY AND VIVIEN LESTER

Stanley (Mike) Lester was born in Faulkton, S.D. February 22, 1913.  His father
was E. T. Lester, who came to this area from Wisconsin.  His mother, Pearl
Hardin Lester, was born and raised in South Dakota. The Lester family lived on
a farm near Onaka, S.D.

Vivien (Babe) Oban Lester was born near Loyalton May 7, 1916, to T. I. Oban,
Sr., who was born in New Hampshire in 1872 and came to South Dakota when he was
14 years old.  Her mother, Lula Barton Oban was born in Vermont in 1881 and
came to this area when she was three years old.

Mike and Babe were married July 15, 1935 and lived on her parents farm near
Loyalton.  They have two sons, Larry and Richard.

Larry went to Ag. School in Brookings, S.D. after finishing grade school in
Loyalton.  He married Deloris Kracke on June 30, 1955, and lives in Baker, Ore.
on a cattle ranch.  They have two daughters, Vickie and Diana.  Vickie is
married to David Hainsworth and lives in Dallas, Ore. and has two children,
Katie and Scot. Diana is a student at Dallas High School.

Richard started High School in Roscoe and graduated in Sweet Home, Ore.  He
married Deb Kramp November 3, 1979 and lives one and one-half miles south of
Loyalton.  He has three boys, one son, Michael Charles, nine, and two stepsons,
Gregory Jon Troop Lester, 16, and James Kramp, four.

EUGENE AND EVADNE LIGHT

Mr. and Mrs. Eugene Light were a couple of the earlier residents of this part
of South Dakota.  Both came to Edmunds County from the state of Wisconsin.
Eugene Light came to Roscoe in 1903, attracted to this part of the country by
the opportunity afforded to own a farm of his own by the process of
homesteading.  Mrs. Light (Evadne Korsrud), a teacher from the River Falls,
Wisconsin Teachers College, accepted a teaching position in Edmunds County in
1907 largely through the influence of the Hazzard Teachers Agency of
Minneapolis.  That teachers agency was interested and influential in placing
needed teachers in this part of the midwest at that time.

        Ed, Eugene, Marjorie, Evadne and Lynn Light

Mr. Light, during his first year in Edmunds County, engaged in farming in
partnership with a brother, Jacob, who was also known to many of the earlier
time residents.  The latter left South Dakota to live in Neosho, Wis.  Mr.
Light while continuing with his farming also formed a well drilling business
with Mr. Fred Huck.  That business carried on for several years.

Eugene Light and Evadne Korsrud were united in marriage on Thanksgiving Day in
1908.  During the first four years of their marriage the Lights lived on their
farm three miles north of Roscoe.  Here their first child, a son, Gordon, died
in infancy.

Among the most vividly remembered occurrences of those times were frequent
prairie fires, sometimes started by coal burning railroad locomotives. Some of
the years, at times, were extremely dry, and with the almost uninterrupted
spaces of land on which the grass was thick and tall, there was a considerable
hazard to be reckoned with by those living in isolated places.

During the first year the Lights were living on their farm, a prairie fire,
extending for many miles, destroyed their home along with those of other
farmers who lived in the area between Hosmer and Leola.  Later, the Lights lost
another home to fire.

Travel wasn't always easy in those days.  On one occasion, Mr. Light responded
to a call for jury duty by walking to Ipswich along the railroad tracks.  Jury
duty was a responsibility not to be taken lightly.  On that same occasion the
judge ordered the sheriff to bring in a juror who had failed to appear for
duty.  After hearing the juror explain that the bad weather was his reason for
not appearing, the judge sentenced him to jail for the duration of the trial.

Mr. and Mrs. Light moved to Roscoe in 1912 and a short time later, Mr. Light
became manager of the Potter Garrick Company grain elevator.  He remained in
that position for 29 years.  While in Roscoe Mr. Light built two houses that
still remain in use.

The Light family belonged to the Roscoe Presbyterian Church, of which Mr. Light
was a trustee.  He was active in Civic affairs being a member of the city
council for many years, and a member and president of the board of education.
Mrs. Light was also active in community and church groups. During her years
there she also assisted each of the children, Lynn, Edgar and Marjorie as they
each in turn assumed duties in publishing the Roscoe Independent from 1925 to
1942.  The first talking movie in Roscoe was started by Lynn in 1934.

Eugene Light passed away in 1957 and Mrs. Light in 1962.  Lynn passed away in
1979.  Marjorie and Mr. and Mrs. Edgar Light reside in Aberdeen.

ISHAM AND FLORENCE MAHON

Isham S. Mahon was born January 15, 1883, at Altamont, Ill. where he grew up
and attended school. When he was a young man he moved to Chicago with his
mother.  In 1905, he came to South Dakota and settled in the Sand Lake area
where he worked as a farmhand.  Later he homesteaded in Stanley County.  He
worked for Norbeck and Nichelson Well Drillers for 11 years after which he
moved to a farm nine miles northeast of Roscoe.  March 30, 1927 he married
Florence Cole at Aberdeen.

Florence Ann Cole, the daughter of Asa and Brigette Murray Cole, was born on
her parents homestead near Columbia, S.D. August 11, 1884.  She attended rural
school and grew up in this area. She continued to live on the farm with her
mother and brothers while she taught school for four years.

The Mahons lived on the farm northeast of Roscoe until 1942 when they moved to
Roscoe.  Mr. Mahon retired from farming in 1947.  They were members of the
First Presbyterian Church in Roscoe.  Florence was also a member of the United
Presbyterian Women and the Roscoe Area Senior Citizens.

I. S. Mahon died September 17, 1965.  Florence continued to live in her home
until 1974 when she made her home in a Senior Citizen apartment in Roscoe.  At
the time of her death April 19, 1980, she was a resident of the Colonial Manor
at Groton, S.D. I. S. and Florence were both laid to rest in Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe.

JOHN J. AND LENA MALSOM

John J. Malsom was born January 10, 1920 to Jacob and Otillia Kirkgasler Malsom
on a farm near Onaka, S.D. His father, Jacob, was born December 8, 1898 near
Strasburg, Russia.  Jacob came to America in 1900 and settled with his parents
near Roscoe.  John's mother was Russian born.  She died July 23, 1923.  Jacob
was remarried to Margaret Bosch February 10, 1934 at Hague, N.D. Jacob died
July 30, 1950.  Margaret resides in Casper, Wyo.

Lena Gisi Malsom was born February 10, 1919 to George and Catherine Deis Gisi
on a farm near Onaka, S.D. Her father, of Italian ancestry, was born near
Ipswich, S.D. after his parents, Jonathon and Mary Eva Cook Gisi, immigrated to
America from Russia.  Lena's mother Catherine, of German ancestry, was born in
Russia and immigrated to America in 1901 when she was 12 years old with her
parents, Jacob and Catherine Bokenmeier Deis.

John married Lena June 18, 1941 in Merphisburough, Tenn. where John was serving
in the U.S. Army.  To this union were born four children, Julie, Alan John,
Tim, and Selena.

Julie was born June 19, 1946.  She is a graduate of Roscoe High School and
received a two year Elementary Teaching Certificate from Presentation Junior
College in Aberdeen, S.D. She taught in Salem and Redfield, S.D. Since June of
1969, she has been employed with Republic Airlines as flight attendant.  She
resides in Hopkins, Minn.

        Back row: Selena, Julie, and Tim; front row: Lena and John Malsom.

Alan John "Sonny" was born April 30, 1947.  He is a graduate of Roscoe High
School.  He did a tour of duty with the Army Secret Service in Europe and
Vietnam. He was killed in a truck accident near Faulkton, S.D. on Highway 45
February 27, 1969 and is buried in the cemetery in Roscoe.

Tim was born February 5, 1950.  He is a graduate of Roscoe High School.  He is
a graduate in drafting of Northern College, Aberdeen, S.D. and also of Lake
Area Vo-Tech in Watertown.  He married Joanne Roth, daughter of Lucy and George
Roth, November 24, 1972. They have one son, Jason, who was born August 22,
1977.  They reside in Brookings, S.D. where Tim is employed with UPS and Joanne
is employed with 3M.

Selena was born March 6, 1954.  She is a graduate of Roscoe High School and
Lake Area Vo-Tech as a medical assistant.  She married Craig Unterbrunner May
25, 1974.  They have three children, Eric, born July 24, 1977; Sarah, born May
25, 1979; and Aaron, born March 27, 1982.  Craig is self-employed as owner of
Thermo-King Refrigeration in Sioux Falls, S.D. where they reside.

        Alan John Malsom

John and Lena resided in the Onaka, S.D. area after their marriage and in 1965
sold their farm and moved to Roscoe.  John was employed at S.D. Wheatgrowers
Grain Elevator until his retirement in February of 1982. He still drives the
Roscoe School bus.

Lena is a graduate of Central High School of Aberdeen, S.D. and attended
college at Aberdeen Northern and Spearfish where she earned her four year
degree in Elementary Teaching.  Lena's teaching career has spanned over 40
years.  She taught in Hosmer, Hillside, and Madison Townships and at Loyalton.
The post 22 years she taught in Roscoe where she is presently semi-retired as
a part-time aide.

John is a member of Roscoe Rod and Gun Club, Commercial Club, and the American
Legion where he served as commander.

Lena is a member of the Commercial Club, the American Legion Auxiliary where
she served as president and secretary, St. Thomas Altar Society where she
served as president and secretary and is a past member of Ivy Circle Garden
Club where she served as treasurer.

MATHEW G. AND MARION MALSOM

        Back row: Harlon, Pius, and Mathew, Jr.; middle row: Mariann, Frances,
        Marilyn, Angeline and Diane; front row: Marion and Mathew G. Malsom.

Mathew G. Malsom was born October 24, 1923 near Hillsview, S.D. Marion Schoch

and three miles south of Roscoe.  They farmed there until their retirement in

Mathew was in the armed service from April 1945 until November, 1946 and had
served in Korea.  They are of the Catholic faith.

Their children are, Diane, married to Robert Allbee, now farming in the
Loyalton area.

Marilyn, married to Raymond Wik, is a housewife and they ore living in

Frances Ann, married to Phillip Monks, is a housewife, living in Louisville,

Mathew J. Jr., married to Londa Preszler, is the second generation living on

Pius P. is married to Susan Schumacher and is farming in the Roscoe area.

Harlan W., married to Ramona Grismer, is a mechanical engineer now living at

MATHEW J. (JR.) AND LONDA MALSOM

        Londa, Mathew, and Kevin Malsom

PIUS P. AND SUSAN MALSOM

PIUS K. AND LENORA MALSAM

Lenora Heilman Malsam was born April 25, 1926 on a farm northwest of Bowdle,

ROBERT AND THERESA MALSAM

Robert Pius Malsam was born at St. Luke's in Aberdeen, S.D. October 17, 1946,
to Pius K. and Lenora Heilman Malsam.  He received his schooling in rural

ANTON AND MARTHA MARDIAN, SR.

Anton Mardian, Sr. was born September 24, 1884 in Russia, the son of Jacob and
Agnes Thomas Mardian. He came to the United States with his parents when he was
18 years old and settled in the Hosmer area.  He married Martha Schmidt at
Roscoe November 22, 1910. Martha was born September 22, 1891 in Russia to Paul
and Caroline Daness Schmidt.  They came to this country and settled near Roscoe
when Martha was seven years old.

        Back row: Paul, Jacob, John, Wendlyn, Anton, Jr., and Stanley; front
        row: Pauline, Agnes, Anton, Sr., Martha, Caroline, and Helen Mardian.

Anton and Martha lived on a farm three miles west of Roscoe, where they lived
until 1944 when they retired and moved to Roscoe.

They had six sons and four daughters.  Wendelin was born October 7, 1911, He
married Eva Kraft and lives in Mobridge, S.D. They have nine children.

Agnes, born October 2, 1912, married Clarence Schaefer, lives in Sioux Falls,
S.D. and has one son.

Caroline, born June 9, 1914, married Adam Baumgartner and had one son.  She
later married Fred Peabody and had two daughters.  She lives in Shelton, Wash.

Anton, Jr., born June 17, 1916 and married Barbara Fuhrmann.  He has one
daughter and lives at Roscoe.

Paul, born April 28, 1918, married Mary Geier, has seven children and lives in
Aberdeen, S.D.

Helen, born February 9, 1920, married Stanley Alford, has three children and
lives in Bremerton, Wash.

Jacob, born December 22, 1921, married Goldie Brakage, has two sons and lives
in Aberdeen, S.D.

John, born December 18, 1923, married Hildegard Goetz and had two children.
John passed away in February, 1963.

Hildegard married Bob Jones in April of 1977 and lives in Mobridge, S.D.

Pauline, born November 13, 1926, married Wilbert Gillette, and had two
daughters.  She later married Leo Pigman and lives in Hartford, S.D.

Stanley, born June 22, 1928, married Beverly Jones, has two daughters and lives
in Englewood, Colo.

Anton died May 4, 1966 and Martha May 21, 1982. Both are buried in Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe. They were members of St. Thomas Catholic Church.

ANTON JR. AND BARBARA MARDIAN

Anton Mardian, Jr. was born June 17, 1916, at Roscoe to Anton, Sr. and Martha
Schmidt Mardian.

Barbara Fuhrmann was born February 21, 1914 near Roscoe to Joseph and Helena
Mangold Fuhrmann.

Tony and Barbara were married October 22, 1938. They lived on a farm northwest
of Roscoe (where Ralph Preszler is now living) from the spring of 1939 until
1944, when they bought a farm three miles west of Roscoe from Michael Brossart.
This had been where Tony's family lived from 1928 to 1944.  In 1951 they built
a new house which they badly needed, as the one they lived in was quite old and
very cold in the winter. The new house had running water and a bathroom.

        Barbara, Anton, Jr., and Barbara Ann Mardian

They have one daughter, Barbara Ann, who was born December 4, 1944.  She is
married to Albert Heilman and has two sons and one daughter.  They live on the
Mardian family farm west of Roscoe.

In 1967, Tony and Barbara bought a basement home one-fourth mile west of Roscoe
and built a new home on it the some year, moving in November 19, 1967. They
still live there.

Tony and Barbara are members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe where Tony
serves on the church council and Barbara is a member of the Ladies Altar
Society.  Tony served in various offices in Glen Township for many years, and
is treasurer of the District Democratic Party.  He has also served on the
Community Building Corporation.

PAUL AND ARLENE MARDIAN

Paul Mardian was born May 6, 1939 to W.P. and Eva Kraft Mardian in Roscoe.  He
graduated from Roscoe High School in 1958 and married Arlene Cunningham
December 27, 1958 at Onaka.

Paul and Arlene lived in Lawton, Okla. for their first eight months, where he
was serving in the United States Army.  In August of 1959 he was assigned duty
in Korea until September of 1960 when his military service ended.

They returned to Roscoe where he worked with his father in the scrap metal
business.  In 1964 they moved to Davenport, Iowa where Paul continued to work in
scrap metal.

In 1968 Paul and his three brothers took the business to Mobridge after their
father decided to retire.  The business soon expanded into North Dakota and
Nebraska.

In 1977 Paul took over management of the operation in Mandan, N.D. and moved
his family into a new home in Bismarck in 1978.  Paul and Arlene are presently
living in Bismarck.

They have three children, Allen, who is 22 and has served in the Army.  He is
presently a student at South Dakota School of Mines in Rapid City studying
Electrical Engineering.

Pam, 21, is a graduate of Minot State College and is employed as a receptionist
in the office of the North Dakota State Public Utilities Commission.  She is
married to Ron Guy of Minot.  They make their home in Bismarck.

Nancy, who is 18, and is a graduate of Century High School.  She is attending
Mary College in Bismarck where she is studying accounting and business law.

HAROLD AND BERNEICE MARGESON

        Back row: Peggy, Berneice, and Kay; front row: Harold Margeson.

Harold L. Margeson was born in Dell Rapids, S.D. September 1, 1920.  His
parents were Clarence and Florence Margeson of Tacoma, Wash.  His father and
only sister are deceased and his mother still resides in Tacoma and is 82 years
old.  There are also six brothers all residing on the West Coast.

Berneice I. Schipke was born in Roscoe, S.D. November 29, 1919.  Her parents
were Henry and Hannah Christen Schipke.  Her father, Henry, was born and raised
on the Schipke homestead, one mile east of Roscoe and her mother was born and
raised in Albany, Minn.

Harold and Berneice were married in St. Paul's Lutheran Church August 11, 1945,
at which time Harold was still serving his country in WWII.  Upon his discharge
from the military, they resided in Aberdeen for three years where both were
employed at the Jewett Wholesale Drug Co. In 1948, they moved to Roscoe where
Harold was affiliated with the First State Bank and retired from there after 34
years of service. Berneice was employed as Business Manager of Roscoe
Independent School District for 22 years, retiring in 1974.  They have been
members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church since residing in Roscoe.

Two daughters were born to them.  Kay was born in 1947 and graduated from
Roscoe High School in 1965. She attended Northern State College and Aberdeen
School of Commerce.  She married Gary Prisbe in 1969 and they have two
children, Brian, 11, and Heather, 9. They reside in Madison, Wis. where both
are employed at the University of Wisconsin, Madison.

Peggy was born in 1950 and attended school in Roscoe.  She graduated from high
school in 1969 and attended Northern State College where she graduated in 1973
with a B.S. degree in Education.  She resides and teaches in Faulkton, S.D.
where she has completed 10 years of teaching.

Harold and Berneice have both belonged to various organizations over the past
years and have enjoyed contributing to the growth of Roscoe.

Future retirement plans are indefinite.

EDWIN AND IRENE MARTZ

        Back row: Janice and Vicki; front row: Irene and Edwin Martz.

Edwin Martz was born May 15, 1921 at rural Hosmer to Daniel and Eva Wiedrich
Martz.  He grew up there and attended rural school.

W. F. MATZ (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Mr. Matz moved with his parents, Fredrich and Caroline Matz and eight brothers
and sisters to a farm nine miles south of Roscoe in 1886 when he was 12.

He recalls the hard work, gathering and selling buffalo bones and getting
together with the neighbors. His mother came to be remembered as "Grandma Matz"
because she helped with neighbors through many illnesses.

He remembered the winters being hard, especially the blizzard of January 12,
1888.

BERNARD AND EMMA MAYER

Bernard Mayer was born October 3, 1896 at  Hosmer, S.D. He died November 24,
1977 at Rapid City, S.D. and is buried at National Cemetery at Sturgis,  S.D.
His father, Jacob Mayer, born February 12, 1856,  Ukraine, Russia, died
February 24, 1937 at Hosmer,  S.D. His mother, Karlina (Hieb) Mayer, born
January  6, 1871, Ukraine, Russia, died July 16, 1941 at Hosmer, S.D. The date
of arrival in the United States is unknown.

Emma (Job) Mayer was born February 2, 1903 at Leola, S.D. Her father, Ludwig
Job, born January 18, 1869 in Russia, died March 28, 1956 at Leola, S.D. Her
mother, Rosina (Kranzler) Job, born November 26, 1873 in South Russia, died
August 13, 1960 at Leola, S.D. Date of arrival in the United States is unknown.

Bernard Moyer served in the United States Army in World War I from August, 1918
to March, 1919.  Bernard and Emma were married at Leola, S.D. February 25, 1922
and established their home in Roscoe in February, 1922.

Bernard was in partnership with John Spitzer in the Ford Garage for several
years.  He also was in the banking business, farmed, and an investigator for the
WPA program.  In July, 1934 he became the Postmaster of the Roscoe Post Office
and served until he retired in October, 1963.  In November, 1963, they moved to
Arizona and lived there 13 years.  Due to Bernard's health they returned to
Rapid City in the summer of 1976 where Emma still lives at this time.

Bernard and Emma had three daughters born and raised in Roscoe, Bernice,
Lorraine and Vivian.

Bernice married Dewain Egge of Aberdeen and is presently living in Munich,
Germany.  Dewain is employed by the Boeing Company.  They have two daughters
and three grandchildren.

Lorraine married Russell Maxey of Huron and lives in Rapid City.  Lorraine is a
medical technologist at Medical Arts Center.  Russell is employed by Nutrena
Feeds. They have three daughters and one grandchild.

Vivian married Gerald Evans of Mankato, Minn. and lives in Rapid City.  Vivian
is a claims representative with the Social Security Administration.  Gerald is
employed by Control Data.  They have one son.

JACOB J. AND ANNA MARIE MAYER

        Back row: Howard, Elmer, Harold, and Cornelia: front row: Jacob,
        Franklin, and Anna Marie Mayer.

Mr. Jacob J. Mayer was born in McPherson County, South Dakota September 30,
1894, the son of Jacob Mayer, Sr. and Karolina Hieb.

February 1, 1916 he married Anna Marie Nehlich who was born November 13, 1895.
The marriage was conducted by the Rev. R. Tauber.  Anna Marie Nehlich was the
daughter of Christ Nehlich and Anna Marie Mutschler.  After their marriage, the
couple moved to a farm in central McPherson County, South Dakota until 1927,
when this farm was sold, and the farm three miles north of Roscoe, South Dakota
was purchased. The family lived and farmed here until the untimely death of Mr.
Mayer, which occurred February 7, 1959. The family had previously moved to a
residence on the southwest side of Roscoe.  Following the death of Mr. Mayer,
the larger acreage place was sold to the Earl Hetticks, and Mrs. Anna Marie
Mayer purchased the George Wilson residence, at which residence she is still
residing.

October 10, 1968 Mrs. Anna M. Mayer was united in marriage with Mr. Jake
Keller, and this marriage lasted until the death of Mr. Keller April 26, 1980.

The marriage of the Mayers was blessed by the following children.

Elmer R. Mayer was born April 27, 1917, and married Hertha Hieb, and they are
still living in the City of Roscoe.

Harold F. Mayer was born May 30, 1919, and is single, and is a resident of the
Colonial Manor in Ipswich, S.D.

Cornelia Mayer was born July 20, 1921, and is married to Erwin Ellwein, and
they are living in the City of Ipswich.

Howard Mayer was born November 9, 1923, and married Carol Penor.  They are
living in the town of Mansfield, S.D.

Franklin Mayer was born June 1, 1933, and married Florence Butler, and they are
in the plumbing business in Custer, S.D.

ELMER R. AND HERTHA MAYER

Elmer R. Mayer was born in McPherson County, S.D. April 27,1917, the son of
Jacob J. Mayer, who was born September 30, 1894 and died February 7, 1959.
Jacob J. was the son of Jacob Mayer, Sr. and Katharina Hieb, and was married to
Anna Marie Nehlich who was born November 13, 1895.  She was a daughter of Christ
Nehlich and Anna Marie Mutschler.

In 1927, Elmer moved with his parents to the farm located three miles north of
Roscoe.  He married Hertha Hieb October 8, 1941.  She was born May 15, 1920, at
Kramer, N.D. She was the daughter of Conrad C. Hieb, born November 18, 1890 and
died November 5, 1945, who was the son of Christian Hieb and Katherina Gross.
He was married to Katherine Martel who was born September 24, 1887 and died
March 17, 1925.  She was the daughter of Philip Martel and Rosena Mehlhaff.

        Hertha, Scott, and Elmer Mayer

Elmer and Hertha farmed with his parents for one year, then purchased the Jacob
Guthmiller farm in the spring of 1942, farming until the fall of 1946, when they
purchased the place now known as Eddie's Place.  They also purchased the home
where the Gerhard Rauchers now reside.  Operating Al's Place until due to a
heart attack, it was sold to Milbert and Martha Ellwein in the fall of 1956.
In 1957, he started to work as a part-time postal clerk in the Roscoe Post
Office, and worked in this capacity until 1964 when he was appointed acting
postmaster until 1967, again returning to the former position of postal clerk,
staying in this position until his retirement from the Post Office in 1977.

With a partner, Harold E. Ellwein, he started the Roscoe Real Estate &
Insurance business on February 1, 1958.  He acquired a Real Estate Brokers
license and Mr. Ellwein holding a previous Real Estate Salesmans license.  They
were in that business until in 1976 when the business was sold to the present
owners, Leland and Tracy Treichel.

He was secretary and treasurer of the Roscoe Commercial Club from 1948 until
1957, life members of the St. James Congregational Church, being secretary
beginning in 1948 and secretary and treasurer since 1963 until the present
time.  In 1962, following Harvey Haug, he also assumed the position of
secretary and treasurer of the Roscoe Morningside Cemetery, still holding this
position at the present time and served a term on the City Council.

They are semi-retired, Elmer still does income tax preparing, and related
services.  Hertha still does limited sewing, and is a great help in the cemetery
work, stoking out the graves and such work.  Hertha and Elmer spent much of
their time traveling.  They have covered all of the states in the United
States, except Maine, have traveled all of the provinces of Canada, State of
Alaska, Mexico at different points, and have taken two European trips, covering
seven countries.

        Judy, Shane, Nicole, and Leslie Mayer

Their family consists Of three sons.  The oldest son, Leslie E. Mayer, was born
April 8, 1943, at the farm home located three miles north and two miles east of
Roscoe, S.D. The doctor was Dr. Calvin Schad.  He married Judy Jensen of
Esmond, N.D. July 3, 1980, and they have a daughter Nicole and a son Shane, and
at present are residing at 1307 Val Dr. Marysville, Calif., 95901 where he is
employed by the Department of Defense.

        Sharyn, Bryanna, Sasha, Dale, and Jordan Mayer

The second oldest son, Dale D. Mayer, was born August 17, 1945, at the farm
home also located three miles north and two miles east of Roscoe, S.D. Dr.
Calvin Schad was the doctor.  He married Sharyn K. Hendrickson of Aberdeen,
S.D. August 18, 1967.  They have a daughter, Sasha, and twins, Bryanna and
Jordon, and at present are residing at 1315 Norwood Dr., Aberdeen, S.D. where
Dale is employed by the Brown County Implement Co. (John Deere) at Aberdeen.
Sharyn teaches Special Education in the Aberdeen schools.

The youngest son, Scott L. Mayer, was born October 2, 1956 at St. Luke's
Hospital in Aberdeen, S.D. He has taught English at Aberdeen Central High,
worked for Senator Jim Abourezk in Washington D.C., at Harrah's Casino at Lake
Tahoe, Nev., for the South Dakota Tie Line, two summers for George McGovern,
and at present is working for the office of Secretary of State of Minnesota, in
St. Paul, Minn. and is attending the Martha Mitchell School of Law, and is an
active member of the Democratic Farm Labor party in Minnesota.  His present
address is 4530 W. 38th St., Apt. 224, St. Louis Park Minn. 5541

ALBERT AND CATHERINE MEIER

Albert Meier, son of Frank and Katherine (Mardian) Meier, was born March 13,
1915 at Aberdeen, S.D. At an early age, his family moved to Roscoe where Al
attended the local school.  After schooling, he began working for the Milwaukee
Railroad, an occupation he held in various capacities.

Catherine Van Kempen Meier, daughter of Anton and Catherine (Welters) Van
Kempen, was born November 26, 1918 at Cologne, Minn.  At the age of five, with
her parents and two older brothers moved to Andover, S.D., where her father was
employed by the Milwaukee Railroad.  Upon completion of high school, she
attended the University of Minnesota in affiliation with St. Mary's Hospital of
Minneapolis for a period of time.  On January 23, 1939, she married Albert
Meier who at that time was employed as a roundhouse foreman in Andover. In May
of 1940, they moved to Roscoe where Al continued his work with the railroad
until a serious accident in 1957, forced him into an early retirement. He later
worked for Merle Clarke and J. L. Healy Construction companies.  Al was a
member of the Catholic Order of Foresters, served as Justice of the Peace for
several years, was a member and at one time Fire Chief for the Roscoe Volunteer
Fire Department. He was also a post member of the Roscoe Commercial Club and
the Aberdeen Elks Lodge.  Al passed away May 3, 1980 following a bout with
throat cancer.

Anton Van Kempen (Kate's father) upon the death of his wife in December of 1943
at Andover, moved to Roscoe and made his home with Al and Kate until his death
in November of 1963.  Tony was an avid gardener and his many friends and
neighbors, reaped in the harvest that his garden would produce.

Kate worked for several years in various business places in Roscoe.  She was
office nurse for Dr. Calvin Schad now of Rapid City.  When the doctor left
Roscoe, in November 9, 1956, she began working  at the First State Bank of
Roscoe and worked there until November of 1980 when she retired.  She held the
position of City Treasurer for 19 years.

        Carole Meier Taigaafe

Al and Kate have three children, a daughter Carole, who is working at the
Madigan Army Medical Center in Tacoma.  Carole is married to Jim Taigaafe,
retired Sgt. Major of the United States Army with 30 years of service and who
is presently working for the postal department in Tacoma, Wash. where they make
their home.  They have a daughter Michelle.  A daughter Denice died in 1972 as
the result of an accident.

        DeDe Meier Diegel

Daughter Donna (DeDe) Diegel is the Administrator of the Sargent Manor Nursing
Home in Forman, N.D. DeDe does not always lean to the administrative rule in
life but has won several trophies for stock car racing, demolition derbies and
fast pitch soft-ball.  She was named to the South Dakota State All Stars for
soft-ball in 1973.  DeDe has three sons, Jay, Tate and Troy.

        Tom (T-Bone) Meier

Son Thomas (T-Bone as he is known in Roscoe) is employed by Northern States
Power of Minneapolis.  He and his wife Sharon live in Brooklyn Center, Minn.
They have four children, Camey, William, Dawn and Thomas, Jr. T-Bone is very
active in sky diving and is a member of the Freedom Flyers who perform at
various air shows.

ANDREW AND MAGDALENA MEIER

        Back row: Lawrence, Carolyn, George, Shirley, and Andy; middle row:
        Betty, Adele, Roy, Loretta, and Henry; front row: Margaret, Magdalena,
        Andrew, and Bernadine.

Andrew Meier was born May 10, 1896 in Odessa, Russia and came to the United
States in October 1900. His parents were Rochus and Elizabeth Meier.  Rochus
was born in Russia, August 15, 1866 and died in 1948. Elizabeth Brigadeen was
born in Russia in 1870 and died in November 1954.

Andrew lived with his parents on the homestead four miles west and two and
one-half miles south of Roscoe, S.D. He lived there until he married Magdalena
Ehnes in January 1921.  In 1922 he built a farm one-half mile northeast of the
homestead.

Magdalena was born December 4, 1899 in Bowdle, S.D. Her parents, Henry Ehnes,
was born March 15, 1875 and Margaret Kelsch, was born March 12, 1876 in Russia.

Andrew and Magdalena were blessed with 13 children, five boys and eight girls.

George, born October 1921, married Virginia Ulmer, living in Nevada City,
Calif. and have two children, Mary and Mark.

Betty, born April 3, 1923, married Sebastian Daschle, living in Aberdeen, S.D.
and have four children, Thomas, David, Gregory and Steven.

Henry, born May 9, 1924, married Clara Geier, living in Sacramento, Calif. and
have six children, Andrea, Peg, Eric, Carla, Karen and Henry, Jr.

Bernadine, born November 21, 1925, married Charles Morgen, living in Son Diego,
Calif. and have eight children.  Mary Kay, deceased; Charles Jr.; Mary Lynn,
Larry, Joe, Jack, Jill and Jim.

Lawrence, born January 27, 1927, married JoAnn Fix, living in Rapid City, S.D.
and has three children, Susan, Craig and Kris.  In 1979, he married Anita
Bittman.

Roy, born May 23, 1928, married Erma Weishaar, living in Roscoe, S.D. and they
have five children, Annette, Sonia, Mark, Jeffery, deceased, and Jennifer.

Margaret, born October 5, 1929, married to Bernard Veith, living in Viola, Kan.
and they have one child, Tony.

Carolyn, born April 26, 1931, married Edward Heier, are living in Aberdeen,
S.D. and have three children, Lisa, Lori and Ryan.

Loretta, born November 12, 1932, married Alvin Abeln, living in Groton, S.D.
and have five children, Douglas, Donald, Dale, Robert and Denise.

Francis, born August 31, 1934, died September, 1934.

Shirley, born August 15, 1935, married Hurley Gellhaus, living in Aberdeen,
S.D. and they have four children, Debbie, Dean, Mark and David.

Andy, born December 1, 1937, married DeAnn Imberi, living in Rapid City, S.D.
and they have one child, Michele.

Adele, born March 16, 1940, married Robert Davies, lives in Sioux Falls, S.D.
and they have four children, Rebekah, Cheri, John and Greg.

Andrew and Magdalena have 44 grandchildren and 23 great-grandchildren.

Besides farming, Andrew did carpentry work.  He retired from the farm in 1948
and made his home in Roscoe, S.D. where he had a small lawn mower repair shop
and he retired from this about 20 years later.  Both Andrew and Magdalena are
residing at this place now.

ROY AND ERMA MEIER

Roy Meier was born May 23, 1928 to Andrew B. and Magdalena (Ehnes) Meier in
Glover Township, Roscoe, S.D. He was the sixth in a family of 13.

In May, 1950 he married Erma Weishaar, the daughter of Jacob and Martha
Guthmiller Weishaar.  To this marriage there were five children, three girls,
and two boys.

Annette, born July 15, 1951, married to Dave DeRouchey, and living in Newton,
Iowa.

Sonia, born February 27, 1954, married to Bill Leisen, and living in Aberdeen,
S.D.

Mark, born January 6, 1956, married to Zody Fuquay, and living on the home farm.

Jeffery, born January 28, 1966, died in May 1977.

Jennifer, born April 15, 1968, living at home.

Roy and Erma have four grandchildren, Nicholas, Cody, Sabrina and Blake.

Up until April of 1982, the family was farming on the home place (birthplace of
Roy) four miles west and two miles south of Roscoe.  Then son, Mark, and his
family took over the farm and Roy started a Woodworking Shop in Roscoe.

MARK AND ZADY MEIER

Mark Meier was born January 6, 1956, in Ipswich, S.D. to Roy and Erma Weishaar
Meier.  They lived on a farm southwest of Roscoe.  Mark graduated from Roscoe
High School in 1974.  November 13, 1976 he married Zady Fuquay in Upland,
Calif.  Zady was born December 15, 1955 in Ypsilanti, Mich. to Van and LaVea
Hadden Fuquay.  She graduated from Alta Loma High School in California.

Mark and Zady lived in California for the first three years and then came to
live in Roscoe and take over the family farm.  They have two children,
Nicholas, born October 25, 1979 in Montclair, Calif. and Sabrina, born April
28, 1981 in Ipswich.

They are members of the St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.  Zady is a member
of the Ladies Altar Society and plays on the Women's Softball team.  Mark
belongs to the Sportsmans Club.

WENDLIN AND HELENA MEIER

        Carolina, Wendlin, and Helena Meier

Wendlin Meier, Sr. was born March 10, 1880, in Odessa, Russia.  His father was
Frank Meier and his mother was Claudia Ungemacht.  Mrs. Frank Meier died and is
buried in Russia.  Frank Meier, along with his sons, Rochus, Wendlin, Frank,
Sebastian, Joe and one daughter, Catherine, immigrated to the United States
probably in 1906.  One daughter, Marcelline, was married and remained in Russia
and later, immigrated to Germany with her family.  Two sons, Michael and Martin
also remained in Russia.  It is believed they may have been sent to Siberia.
The Meiers landed in New York and found their way to the Roscoe area.  Wendlin,
Sr. and Rochus homesteaded land southwest of Roscoe.

Wendlin married Helena Schmidt at Bowdle, S.D. June 17, 1907.  Helena was born
in Russia, April 15, 1888. They had six children.

Clementina was born November 6, 1909, married Martin Gabriel in November 1930
and four children were born to them- John and Raymond died in infancy and
Dorothy and Shirley Ann.  Martin passed away September 4, 1976 and is buried in
Ipswich.

Wendlin, Jr. was born April 29, 1912, married Viola Juhl at Pembina, N.D. and
they have one daughter, Delores.  They lived in North Dakota for some time,
where they farmed and he worked on his father-in-laws' threshing rig.  From
there they moved to Seneca, S.D. where they operated a cream station.  Later
they moved to Minneapolis, Minn. where he worked in a factory.  They left for
California where he owned and operated a body and fender business.  Wendlin
died of Hodgkins disease December 2, 1966 and is buried in Lancaster, Calif.

Carolina was born December 9, 1913, married Bernard Gabriel of Onaka September
14, 1930 and they lived and worked on the Bert Beckler farm east of Onaka.
They moved to Onaka and operated a cream station from 1933 until 1941, when
they purchased a grocery store.  They ran the store until 1942, when they
purchased a cream station from Jacob Fix, Sr.  This was closed in 1968 and
Bernard then went into road construction, which he still operates.  They have
two daughters, Lorraine, born April 28, 1933 and Gladys, born May 22, 1935.

Frank Meier was born January 26, 1917, and lives at Gold Hill, Ore.

Paul was born July 13, 1920, and married Agnes Vetch, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.
Tony Vetch of Edgemont, S.D. They live at Rapid City, S.D. where he is employed
at Ellsworth Air Force Base.  Their children are Jeannette, Paulette, Becky and
Patti.

Joseph was born January 24, 1925 and married Lorraine Schmidt of Aberdeen,
where they reside.  Joe is employed at Safeguard Industries.  They have eight
children, all of whom live in Aberdeen.  They are Karen, Janet, Sondra, Gary,
Diane, Ronald, Julie and Vicki.

Wendlin and Helena moved to Roscoe about 1939. They are members of St. Thomas
Catholic Church. Wendlin passed away June 25, 1954 and is buried in Morningside
Cemetery in Roscoe.  Helena is a resident at the Colonial Manor in Ipswich.

MARTIN AND ELIZABETH MENZIA

Martin Menzia was born in Mannheim, Russia October 20, 1881, the son of Conrad
and Rosina Macini Menzia. In 1906 he married Elizabeth Volk, daughter of Frank
and Jeannette Mangold Volk.  She was also born in Mannheim, Russia, February
20, 1879.  They came to the United States and Roscoe in 1908.  Martin worked on
the Milwaukee Railroad, retiring in about 1940.

They had eight children, Conrad and Ferdinand (twins), Ann, Gabriel, John, Mary
and Frank.  Their first son died in infancy.

They were members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe where Elizabeth was a
member of the Ladies Altar Society.

Elizabeth died in Ipswich, S.D. December 16, 1962 and Martin died April 24,
1966 in St. Paul, Minn.  They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

        Back row: Frank, Conrad, Ferdinand, Gabriel, and John; front row: Ann,
        Martin, Elizabeth, and Mary Menzia.

CONRAD B. AND JULIA MENZIA

Conrad B. Menzia was born May 12, 1909 in the Roscoe area to Martin and
Elizabeth Volk Menzia.  He attended the Roscoe School and as a young boy worked
out on various farms until he went to work for the Milwaukee Railroad.  After a
few years he worked mostly as a carpenter which he is still doing.

November 9, 1932 he married Julia Fuhrmann at Roscoe.  She was the daughter of
Joseph and Helena Mangold Fuhrmann.  Julia was born February 6, 1912 at Roscoe.
She grew up on a farm about 12 miles southwest of Roscoe and attended the
rural school near her home.

C.B. and Julia made their home in Roscoe.  In 1951 they moved on a farm near
Seneca and remained there until 1959 when they again made their home in Roscoe.

They have one daughter, Genevieve, who was born December 14, 1933.  She
graduated from Roscoe High School in 1952 and worked at Sahli's Store until her
marriage.  In 1954 she married Arnold Schumacher of Roscoe.  They now live in
Rapid City and have one daughter, Joan Rae.

C.B. and Julia are members of St. Thomas Catholic Church where Julia is a
member of the Ladies Altar Society.  C. B. is a member of the Roscoe Volunteer
Fire Department.

        Arnold, Joan Rae, and Genevieve Schumacher

GABRIEL AND MARY MENZIA

Gabriel F. Menzia was born February 4, 1913, at Roscoe to Martin and Elizabeth
Volk Menzia who came to this country from Mannheim, Russia in 1908.  He
attended the Roscoe school.  At the age of 17, he went to work for the
Milwaukee Railroad.  He entered the Army June 6, 1942 and received his training
at Camp Walters, Texas.  He was shipped overseas, serving in North Africa,
South France and Italy.  He received his discharge September 24, 1945.  After
his return home he went back to work on the railroad.

On May 30, 1955, he married Mary Kulekowsky in Minneapolis, Minn.  Mary was
born in Minneapolis September 15, 1917 to Peter and Anna Dombrowski Kulekowsky
who had came to this country from Russia. They are both deceased and buried in
Minneapolis. Mary attended schools in Minneapolis.  During WWII, she was
employed at Twin City Ordinance and later at the Feinberg Sausage Co.

        Kathleen, Gregory, and Patricia Menzia

Gabe and Mary made their home in Roscoe.  They have three children, Kathleen,
born September 5, 1956 and lives in Lewistown, Mont.; Gregory born August 25,
1957, lives in Aberdeen and Patricia born November 12, 1958 and lives in
Pringle, S.D.

Gabe retired February 27, 1976 after having worked for the railroad for a total
of 42 years.  They belong to the St. Thomas Catholic Church where Mary belongs
to the Ladies Altar Society and where Gabe and Mary have been the custodians
for many years.  Gabe is a member of the Roscoe American Legion.

STEVE AND ELIZABETH MENZIA

Steve Menzia came to Roscoe in 1907 or 1908 with his parents, four brothers and
one sister.  He was born September 25, 1893, in Odessa, Russia, the son of
Conrad K. and Rosina Menzia.  He served his country in World War I. Steve
married Elizabeth Klein November 22, 1922 at Strasburg, N.D. She was born March
15, 1900, in Strasburg, Russia, the daughter of Casmir and Magdalena Klein.

Steve worked for the Milwaukee Railroad for 42 years until he retired.  He was
a coal shed foreman for many years.  Elizabeth was a homemaker who spent most of
her time cooking and baking, her specialty was baking bread.  She also did a
lot of gardening and enjoyed making flowers with crepe paper.

        Roger, Wendlein, Joe, Adam, Aloyce, Steve, and Paul Menzia, Catherine
        Crisp and Maggie Kulas

They had nine children.  The family belonged to St. Thomas Catholic Church in
Roscoe.  Elizabeth died May 2, 1955 and Steve died August 9, 1968.  They are
both buried at Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

Their nine children are the following.

Magdalena (Maggie), was born in Roscoe November 9, 1923.  She went to
Milwaukee, Wis. from Dodge, Wis. in 1942, where she worked in a factory until
she married James Kulas in 1947.  They have four daughters, and two sons.  She
is a homemaker who loves to cook and bake like her mother.  She enjoys
crocheting for her five grandchildren.  Her husband is retired from Miller
Brewery and they live in West Allis, Wis.

        Conrad C. Menzia

Conrad (Connie), was born in Roscoe February 27, 1926.  He was drafted to the
service in September 1944, went to the Pacific in 1945 and fought in the battle
of Luzon.  He then went to Germany until 1948, when he returned home and
married Bibiana Orasch from Dornbirn, Austria in February 1949.  In 1950 he
went to Korea where he was a prisoner of war for two and one-half years.  After
he came home, he went back to Germany.  He was a 20 year serviceman and after he
completed his time, he lived in New Jersey where he died June 15, 1968.  He is
buried in Arlington National Cemetery at Washington, D.C. His wife still lives
in New Jersey where she works as a nurse.

Catherine was born in Roscoe February 25, 1928 and married Nickolas Crisp of
Ipswich, S.D. February 8, 1949 in Roscoe.  Her husband worked on the railroad in
the Ipswich area until 1952, when they moved to Milwaukee, Wis.  Nick is
employed at McQuay-Perfex Inc. where he repairs heat transfer products.
Catherine is a homemaker, belongs to Christian Women Society and does volunteer
work at Mount Carmel Nursing Home.  They are members of Our Lady of Lourdes
Parish.  They have four children, two sons and two daughters.

Paul (Popeye) was born in Roscoe February 4, 1930. He joined the service in
1948 and served until 1949. In 1950 he went to Milwaukee, Wis. where he has been
working for Babcock & Wilcox.  He was married to Catherine Berg of Milwaukee in
