June 1952.  They have four sons and one daughter.  She is a homemaker and they
live in West Allis, Wis.

Joseph (Joe) was born in Roscoe March 14, 1934.  He went to Milwaukee in 1951,
started working for Allis Chalmers in September 1952 until 1955, when he went
to the service for two years.  He returned to Allis Chalmers and has been
working there ever since.  He married Donna Petroski of Milwaukee and they have
six sons.  She is a homemaker and works part-time at the Ramada Inn.  They live
in New Berlin, Wis.

Adam was born May 21, 1936 in Roscoe.  He went to Milwaukee in 1956 where he
worked at Louis Allis for one year.  He joined the service and served two years
in the Army.  He returned to Louis Allis for another year.  In 1961, he married
Emily Heidorn of Racine, Wis., a licensed practical nurse.  They have three
daughters and two sons.  Adam is a foreman at American Motors where he has
worked for 21 years.  He has been active in Scouting for the post 12 years
serving as Scoutmaster for the last eight years.  They are very active in their
church where he has served as chairman of the Church Festival, chairman of the
New Year's Eve Party and is Past President of the Home and School Association.
They live in Cudahy, Wis.

Wendlein (Wendy) was born May 12, 1939 in Roscoe. He married Virginia Mitzel of
Onaka, S.D. May 28, 1960.  They have four daughters, and ore presently living
in Georgia.

Aloyce was born April 9, 1942 in Roscoe.  Upon graduation from Roscoe High in
1960, he relocated to Milwaukee to work at the Caterpillar Tractor Co. He
started his career as a welder apprentice and after three years, he became a
journeyman.  He is presently a planning processor at Caterpillar and has been an
employee there for 22 years.  He married Margaret Kulas of Milwaukee in 1963.
She is employed at Mitchell Savings and Loan in Mukwonago, Wis. and is a new
accounts specialist and teller.  They have three children, one daughter and two
sons.  As a family, they enjoy snow skiing, volleyball, fishing and hunting.
They have lived in Mukwonago, Wis. since 1975.

Roger was born in Roscoe August 15, 1944.  He went to Milwaukee in 1955 when
his mother died, to live with his sister Catherine and brother-in-law, Nickolas
Crisp. He lived with them for 15 years.  He served two years in the Army.  In
April 1971, he married Joan Schous of Milwaukee.  They have two sons.  She is a
homemaker and Roger works for Caterpillar as a welder.  They live in Grafton,
Wis.

The children of Steve and Elizabeth Menzia often talk about Roscoe because it
is where their roots are and where they had many happy times.  Catherine's
husband Nickolas said Roscoe was the Biggest Little Town in the World!  He and
his friends could always have a good time there.

There are 35 grandchildren and six great-grandchildren.

GOTTLIEB AND LOUISE MILLER FAMILY (As submitted by Olga Miller, San Luis
Obispo, Calif.)

My youngest brother and sister and myself were born and raised in Roscoe. I,
(Olga) was born in 1914, my brother Henry in 1917, now lives in Aberdeen, S.D.,
and my youngest sister, Martha was born in 1920 and now lives in Yankton, S.D.
My oldest brother, Gust and two older sisters, Mathilda and Ella, were born in
Aberdeen, S.D. Us four older children started school in the old wooden school
house.  My Dad worked on the railroad and at the old Round House.  My parents,
Gottlieb and Louise Miller, and brother Gust are now deceased.

We attended and were members of the Roscoe Lutheran Church.  My brother, Gust,
and sisters Mathilda and Ella were confirmed in the old Lutheran Church.  I was
confirmed in the Wecota, S.D. Lutheran Church where we became members after
moving away from Roscoe.  This is where my parents and brother are buried.
Roscoe has always meant home to me even though we very seldom get back there.
After leaving Roscoe, my family farmed in the Jeschke neighborhood where we
went to school.  I remember the night the Jamison Hotel burned.  The Roscoe
Fire Dept. was so small compared to what they have today.

As my sister Mathilda and I read the Roscoe paper every week, it is so
gratifying to read of folks we still remember.  Two of my sisters, Mathilda and
Ella live here in San Luis Obispo, Calif., near me, the rest live in S.D. We
keep in touch quite often by telephone or letter.

We hope that everyone in Roscoe will have a great and wonderful time at the
centennial.

        Gottlieb and Louise Miller

WILLIAM AND IDA MILLER

William Miller was born October 20, 1884, in Hoffnungstahl, South Russia, the
son of Adam and Anna Miller.  Ida Finkbeiner was born in Bessarabia, South
Russia, December 23, 1890, to Henry and Lydia Finkbeiner.

William and Ida were married February 23, 1911, in South Russia and came to
South Dakota in 1911. They settled in the Lowry, S.D. area and operated a
blacksmith shop.  In 1918, they moved to a farm near Firesteel, S.D. until
1936, when they moved to a farm south of Roscoe until his retirement in 1947.
Ida died January 27, 1945.  William remarried August 11, 1946, to Rosalia
Hettich and lived in Roscoe until his death June 23, 1964.

William and Ida had 11 children, Anna, born January 12, 1912, married John C.
Miller January 4, 1939 and lives in Rapid City, S.D.

Elsie, born February 3, 1914, married Alfred Winkler October 30, 1941, and
lives at Roscoe.

Lydia, born September 16, 1919, married John Kranzler April 16, 1947, and lives
at Spearfish, S.D.

Edna, born November 7, 1918, married Ted Schnabel March 25, 1947, and lives at
Pierre, S.D.

Fred W., born August 15, 1920, married Margaret Lacher November 10, 1946, and
lives at Roscoe.

Emil, born July 27, 1922, married Sally Valenzuela April 27, 1947, and lives in
Ontario, Calif.

Irma, born April 19, 1924, and died in 1930.

Emelia (Joy), born February 23, 1926, married Clarence Voegele March 30, 1950
and lives in Roscoe.

Paul, born April 17, 1928, married Bernice Ackerman October 10, 1949, and died
March 13, 1980.

Edwin, born July 20, 1930, married Leona Walth April 8, 1951, and lives in
Denver, Colo.

Elizabeth (Betty), born May 30, 1933, married Ray Jung February 11, 1956, and
lives in Santa Monica, Calif.

FRED W. AND MARGARET MILLER

        Back row: Kay, Fred, Jr., Bonnie, Wayne, and Dale; front row:
        Margaret and Fred Miller

Fred W. Miller was born August 15, 1920 in Firesteel, S.D., the son of William
and Ida Finkbeiner Miller.  They moved to the Roscoe area in 1936.  Margaret
Lacher was born February 29, 1924, in Venturia, N.D., the daughter of John and
Elizabeth Sahli Lacher.  Her family came to Roscoe in 1930.

Fred and Margaret were married November 10, 1946 in Roscoe.  They settled on a
farm nine miles southwest of Roscoe.  In 1957, they moved to a farm four miles
south of Roscoe.  Fred was a schoolboard member for 15 years.  Fred moved to
Gettysburg, S.D. in 1974, and worked at Cameron's Super Value as a butcher and
meat cutter.  He was later joined by his wife and youngest daughter.  The farm
was run by his two oldest sons and daughter.  In October 1976, they moved back
to the farm near Roscoe where they are now living.

They have five children, Dale Dennis, born April 23, 1952, married to Kathy
Geditz October 9, 1976 and is a station attendant at Farmers Union in Roscoe.

Fred Wilbert, born April 24, 1956, is an assembly man at K. O. Lee's in
Aberdeen.

Wayne Eric, born March 27, 1957, is a Medical Lob Technologist in Van Nuys,
Calif.

Bonnie Kay, born January 9, 1960, is an Elementary Education teacher.

Kay Lynn, born May 8, 1964, is attending college in Spearfish, S.D.

OTTO AND VIOLA MOHR

        Back row: Harley and Merle; front row: Viola and Otto Mohr.

Otto Mohr was born on a farm 13 miles north of Roscoe on a snowy December 17,
1924, to Gottlieb J. and Bertha M. Albrecht Mohr, who were both born in south
central McPherson County, to German-Russian immigrants.  He received an eighth
grade education and grew up on his parents' farm.  He married Viola G. Walth at
the Jehovah Lutheran Church north of Roscoe by the Rev. F. Lehner October 21,
1945.  She was born June 22, 1923, the daughter of John F. and Magdalena Walth,
who were both born of German-Russian immigrant parents in the Fairfax, S.D.
area.  Her birthplace was on a farm 18 miles west of Leola, S.D. where she grew
up and received an eighth grade education in the rural school nearby.  Otto and
Viola lived on the some farm where Otto was born until the spring of 1963, when
they sold their farm and property and came to live in Roscoe where they opened
what became known as Mohr's Cafe.  They operated this business until the fall
of 1968 when poor health in the family forced them to give up the cafe
business. in the spring of 1969, Otto started working for the City of Roscoe in
the Liquor Store where he was manager for about 10 years.  Viola did
babysitting and housecleaning until Donnie's Cafe opened up.  She has been
employed there ever since. Otto also drove school bus for about 14 years, served
as alderman for his ward in Roscoe for two years and was Mayor of Roscoe for
two years.  At present Otto is disabled and putters around on hobbies.

Two sons were born to Viola and Otto: Merle and Harley.

Merle is married and lives at Mina Lake and operates Mohr's Custom Fencing at
Aberdeen.

Harley is also married and lives on a small farm near Bath, S.D. He raises
horses and dogs, and is also in the fencing business.

BEN AND AALTJE MULDER SR.

Ben Mulder, Sr. was born in Beerta, Holland, July 11, 1876.  At the age of 15
he immigrated to America.  For many years he worked in parts of Minnesota, Iowa
and southern South Dakota.  He then came north and worked in the Roscoe-Ipswich
area as a farm laborer. June 2, 1902 he was united in marriage to Aaltie
Stubbe, also of Beerta, Holland, at Ipswich, South Dakota.

Mr. and Mrs. Mulder worked as a hired couple for Bruno Nobbe for two years.  In
1904 they homesteaded on a farm in North Bryant Township on the southeast
one-fourth section 7-124-70 north of Roscoe.

Mr. and Mrs. Mulder were blessed with three boys and five girls.  The first
three were born in a sod house.

Their son, George, married Lillian Maier (both deceased) and had two children.

Daughter Amanda married Herman DeVries (both deceased) and had six children.

Edward, a son, is retired and living at Aberdeen.

Katie, a daughter, married George Dommer (both deceased).  They had one child,
deceased.

A daughter, Matilda, married John Nehlich, Jr., (she is deceased), and had four
children.

A daughter, Lydia, married Elsa Tiggelaar, deceased, and is living in Willow
Lake, S.D. They had three children.

A son, Ben, Jr., married Lenora Maier, is semi-retired, and living near Roscoe.
They had five children.

Emma, a daughter, married Christ Nehlich, deceased. She is Lunch Room
Supervisor at the Ipswich Public School, is living in Ipswich and had three
children.

The Mulder family lived on the farm for 14 years.  In 1916, they moved to
Hosmer, S.D. where Mr. Mulder was owner of the Hosmer Drayline for one year.
The following year they moved back to the home place in N. Bryant Township and
resided there and farmed until Mr. Mulder's retirement.

August 20, 1937 Mrs. Mulder passed away at the age of 57.  In 1940 Mr. Mulder
sold the home place to one of the son's and moved to a home in Roscoe.

In 1953 Mr. Mulder purchased a small house and moved it to the John Nehlich
farm where he lived most of his retiring years.  He also spent some of his last
years with two of his daughters.

December 11, 1959 Mr. Mulder passed away at the age of 83 at the Ipswich
Community Hospital.  Both Mr. and Mrs. Mulder are buried in the Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe.

BEN AND LENORA MULDER

Ben Mulder, Jr. was born June 4, 1916 in Hosmer, S.D., the son of Ben Sr.
Aaltie (Alice) Stubbe Mulder, who were both born in Holland.  Ben came to the
United States in 1876 and Aaltie came in 1880.  When Ben, Jr. was one year old
his parents homesteaded on a farm located eight miles north and one mile east
of Roscoe. This is where he grew up and worked on his dad's farm until June 18,
1945 when he married Lenora Maier at Webster, S.D.

Lenora was born August 19, 1915 at Bowdle, S.D. to Jacob A. and Margaretha Will
Maier who were both born in South Dakota and lived in the Bowdle and Hosmer
area.  Ben and Lenora continued farming and after three years also went into
construction work in Aberdeen until 1977.  At the present time they are still
doing some farming and Ben also does some carpenter work.

They were blessed with six children, James, lives on a farm north of Eureka,
S.D., married Jean Ottmar and has three children.

        Barry, Maxine, Mark, Shiela, Jim, and Leslie Mulder

Leslie lives in Rochester, Minn. where he is a parts manager for Oldsmobile and
Datsun.

Maxine lives in Cheyenne, Wyo. where she is a beautician.

Sheila lives at Richmond Lake, S.D. and is the Brown County Deputy Treasurer,
married to Keith Enderson.

Mark lives in Mina, S.D. working for Edmunds County Highway Department and is
married to Karen Filegar.

Barry lives in Eureka, working for James Ottenbach.

Ben served on the North Bryant Township and school boards for several years and
has been the town marshall in Hosmer.  He has lived in North Bryant Township
since age one.

They are members of the First Presbyterian Church in Roscoe where Ben has also
served on the church board.

EDWARD MULDER

Edward Mulder, third eldest child of Ben and Aaltie Stubbe Mulder, was born
September 27, 1906, in a sod house in North Bryant Township, eight miles north
and one east of Roscoe.  He received his education in the rural school, with
the exception of one year when his family lived in Hosmer, S.D. At that time
there were only two and one-half or three months of school held during the year.

He worked on the farm with his parents most of his early life.  April 12, 1942
he was drafted and served four years in the United States Army, three and one-
half years were spent overseas in the Pacific theater and Japan in WWII.  After
returning from the service, he was a lineman for the R.E.A. for two years and
then spent three years on the dam site at Pierre, S.D. and also did some farm
labor.  In 1960, he started working for Steel Structures in Aberdeen, S.D.
until his retirement in 1971.

At the present time he resides at 312 N. Kline in Aberdeen.

WILLIAM AND MAGDALENA NAASZ

William (Bill) Naasz was born December 12, 1903, in Hoffnungstahl, South
Russia, the son of William and Mary Vederspoon Naasz.  He came to this country
with his family in 1904 and settled in the Eureka, S.D. area where Bill grew to
manhood.

Magdalena (Maggie) Rohrbach was born October 12, 1906 near Hosmer to Jacob and
Christina Lammle Rohrbach.  This is where she grew up and attended the country
school three and one-half miles from her home. Bill and Maggie were married
November 7, 1929 near Hosmer, S.D. They made their first home on a farm south
of Bowdle for six years.  After that they lived on a farm north of Bowdle for
three years and then moved to Bowdle.  From here, Bill walked every day to the
W.P.A. construction site near Java where he worked as they had no car at that
time.  They later moved to Java for a short time and then to Hosmer, picking up
odd jobs in that area and in North Dakota.  In 1942, they made the move to
Roscoe where they have lived until the present time.  Bill worked for Edmunds
County Highway Department and on road construction.

In 1962, Bill and Maggie and two of their boys, Gerry and Verlin, went to
Bellfield, N.D. to work on construction for the summer months.  Maggie did the
cooking for the crew and managed the hotel where they stayed.  Bill retired in
1972.

They have six children, Darlene, married to Johhny Guthmiller, lives in
Aberdeen and they have one daughter.

Luella, married to Pierre Rousseau, lives in Son Jose, Calif. and they have
three children.

LaVerne, lives in Columbus, Ohio and has three children.

Howard, with his wife Cheryl and five daughters lives in Littleton, Colo.

Gerald, his wife Pat and two children, live in Denver, Colo.

Verlin, with his wife Denise and three children, live in Rockford, Ill.

Bill and Maggie are members of the World Wide Church of God.  They put out a
fairly large garden every year and they both enjoy working in it.

MR. AND MRS. JOHN NEHLICH, SR.

John Nehlich was born June 10, 1873 in Gluckstal, South Russia.  He was
baptized in the Lutheran faith.  He received his education at the Gluckstal
German Community School and learned to read the German language fluently.  John
was gifted with a good singing voice.  Singing when visiting friends was a
great enjoyable past-time.  He played the organ and accordion by ear.  John was
confirmed in his church school.  He served in the army while living in Russia.

        Back row: Martha, Christ, John, Edward, Arthur, and Lydia; front row:
        John, Sr., and Johanna Nehlich,

He married Magdelina Schaible in Russia, and in 1900, they and other relatives
came to Americo and settled in the Roscoe-Hosmer area where they farmed.

A daughter, Katie, was born and Mrs. Nehlich died a few days after the
daughter's birth.  Mrs. Nehlich was buried near the rural Hosmer Lutheran
Church.

March 10, 1901, Mr. Nehlich married Johanna Heiser, who had been born March 18,
1878.  They continued to farm in the Roscoe-Hosmer area except for a few years
in which they lived at Hepbrune, N.D. and later at Reliance, S.D. Then they
returned to the Roscoe-Hosmer area.

Mr. Nehlich read the German sermons at the rural Lutheran Church on Sundays and
led the singing, when no minister was available.

Their children were Katie, Mrs. Fred Bosche, who has died.

Rosie, Mrs. Art Jung, has died.

John, farmed near Beebe until 1977.  He was married to Mathilda Mulder who died
in 1975.  In 1977 he married Irene Voegele Borkirchert.  It was at this time
that he retired and moved to Roscoe, S.D.

Mary, who was married to Helmuth Preszler, has died.

Ann was married to Art Heyne.  They lived at Watertown until after Art had
died, then Ann made her home in Aberdeen.

Christ, was married to Emma Mulder, and they farmed near Beebe.  Christ died of
a heart attack in 1968 at the age of 57 years.

Lydia, lives at Aberdeen, was married to Edwin Heyne and he was killed in a
train accident.

Martha, Mrs. Art Bauer, lives in California and her husband was killed.

Art Nehlich is married to Florence Stuckle and lives in Post Falls, Idaho.

Eddie Nehlich was married to Irma Kirschenmann who died in October 1981 and is
buried at Rapid City.  Eddie lives at Newark, Calif.

The youngest of the children, a boy, died during the flu of 1919.

Art and Eddie served in the United States Army in World War II and it was at
this time that Mr. and Mrs. Nehlich retired from farming and moved to Roscoe.
They became members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church at Roscoe.  They observed
their Golden Wedding in March of 1951 at the Roscoe American Legion Hall.

Mrs. Nehlich died January 21, 1953 and Mr. Nehlich died March 3, 1953.  They
are buried at the Morningside Cemetery north of Roscoe, S.D.

CHRIST AND EMMA NEHLICH

Christ Nehlich was born at Reliance, S.D. to John and Johanna Heiser Nehlich,
who immigrated from Russia in the early 1900s.  In 1917, the Nehlich family
moved to the Hosmer area and in 1924, he moved with the family to a farm in No.
Bryant Township, where he received his education in the rural school.

In 1940, he was married to Emma Mulder, youngest daughter of Ben Mulder, Sr.
also of Roscoe, at Aberdeen, S.D. In 1952, Christ and his family purchased the
Corner Bar in Roscoe, known as Bosch's Corner Bar, operating this business for
one year.  They then moved to a farm in Huntley Township and resided in the
Roscoe-Ipswich area until the time of Christ's death January 20, 1968.  Christ
was an active member on township, school and church boards during the time he
resided in No. Bryant Township.

Emma Nehlich, the youngest daughter of Ben and Aaltie Stubbe Mulder, was born
on a farm in No. Bryant Township and received her elementary education in the
rural school and graduated with the class of 1937 from Roscoe High School.

She was employed as a waitress at Steen's Cafe in Ipswich for a few years and
since 1960 she was employed by the Ipswich Public School in the Food Service
Deportment.  After her husband's death, Emma moved to Ipswich and has been
employed as school lunch supervisor at the Ipswich Public School for the post 23
years.

Three children were born to the Christ Nehlichs.

Terry married Audrey French of Aberdeen and has four children, Gregory, Tara,
Kyle and Timothy.  He is route manager for Coca-Cola Co., Aberdeen, S.D.

Patricia married Larry Noe of Chestnut, Ill. and is employed with Illinois
Power Co. They have two girls, Vikki and Cindi, living at Decatur, Ill.

Todd was employed with the Milwaukee Railroad for 10 years, but is now employed
as a Security Guard with 3-M at St. Paul, Minn.  He married Mary Keller of
Aberdeen and has one son, Christian Edward.  Their family lives in No. St.
Paul, Minn.

MR. AND MRS.  JOHN NEHLICH, JR.

        Mathilda, Jaci, and John Nehlich

John Nehlich was born June 5, 1904 near Hosmer, S.D. to John and Johanna
Nehlich (nee Heiser).  He was baptized in the Lutheran faith in the rural
Lutheran church east of Hosmer, S.D. They lived near Hosmer for a number of
years, then the family moved to Reliance, S.D. Later they lived at Hepburn,
N.D., then the family moved back to the Roscoe-Hosmer area where John was
confirmed at the Hosmer Lutheran Church.  John farmed with his dad in the
Roscoe area until September 15, 1938 when he married Mathilda Mulder.

Mathilda Mulder was born at Hosmer February 14, 1911. She became a member of
the Presbyterian Church at Roscoe.  She attended rural school near Roscoe, S.D.
Her parents were Mr. and Mrs. Benjamin Mulder (nee Alice Stubbe).  They lived
north of Roscoe, S.D.

After John and Mathilda's marriage they moved to a farm four miles northeast of
Beebe, where John farmed until 1977 when he retired from farming and moved to
Roscoe, where he still lives.

The Nehlichs have four children.  They are, Lester, who has been custodian at
the Roscoe Public School for several years.

Arty lives on the family farm and farms there.

Curtis lives at Oakes, N.D. and is the manager of the Coop there.

Jaci, (Mrs.  Jim McIntosh), lives in the rural Eureka area, and is the
secretary at the McIntosh Clinic at Eureka, S.D.

John's parents lived for awhile in Roscoe, where his dad died in the spring of
1953 and his mother died in the summer of 1953.  John's brother, Christ, died in
1968 at the age of 57 years.  He has two brothers, Art who lives at Post Falls,
Idaho and Eddie, who lives at Newark, Calif.  His sisters are Martha Bauer, who
lives in California and Mrs. Ann Heyne and Mrs. Lydia Heyne, both living in
Aberdeen, S.D.

Mrs. John Nehlich (Mathilda) died May 14, 1975 at the age of 64 years.  A
stepsister, Mrs. Fred Bosche, (Katie) and a sister, Mrs. Helmuth Preszler,
(Mary) have also died.

John has 12 grandchildren and one great-grandchild. Lester is married to Anna
Martz.  Their children are Scott and Mrs. Russell (Peggy) Person.  They have two
children, Shannon and Nicholas and live in Bowdle.

Arty is married to Delaine Gross from Bowdle, S.D. Their children are Darian,
Lori, Shawn and Darla.  They all attend the Roscoe Public School.

Curtis is married to Josephine Unser, Ipswich, and their children are Kim,
McChelle, Wendy and John.

Jaci is married to Jim McIntosh of rural Roscoe.  They were in the same high
school class together.  Their children are Jesse and Jed.

June 16, 1977 John Nehlich and Irene Borkirchert were united in marriage.  They
live in Roscoe and ore both retired.

LESTER AND ANNIE NEHLICH

Lester Nehlich was born April 5, 1939 in Ipswich, S.D. to John and Matilda
Mulder Nehlich.  Annie Martz was born in Bowdle, S.D. September 18, 1943 to
John and Martha Banik Martz.  Lester and Annie both grew up on farms near
Roscoe and attended Roscoe area schools.

They were married in Hosmer, S.D. June 9, 1961, and established their home in
Aberdeen where they lived for four years.  They moved to Roscoe in 1971 and are
both presently employed at the Roscoe Public School. They are members of the
Roscoe Presbyterian Church.

They have two children, Peggy Sue, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1979,
married to Russell E. Person, of Bowdle, September 8, 1979 and they have two
children, Shannon Marie and Nicholas James.  They are presently living at
Bowdle.

Scott Lester is a student at Roscoe High School and will graduate in 1984.

T. I. AND LULA OBAN

T. I. Oban, Sr. was born in New Hampshire in 1872 and came to this area in
about 1886.  Lula Barton Oban was born in Vermont in 1881 and came to South
Dakota in 1884.  They were married April 26, 1897 and lived around Loyalton all
their married lives.

They had seven children, Ralph was born November 4, 1897 and died August 20,
1980.  He married Edna Gange September 19, 1919, who now lives in Loyalton.
They farmed in South Dakota all their lives, and had 10 children, three boys
and seven girls.

Gertrude was born April 22, 1899 and married Raymond Smith August 21, 1920.  He
died in June of 1965.  Gertrude lives in Upland, Calif.  They had two boys, Ray
was school janitor for 25 years in Loyalton before moving to California.

Chester, born in 1901 and died February 22, 1966.  He married Jenett Murray and
they had two boys and one girl.  Chester was highway superintendent for years
and lived in South Dakota all their lives.

Zeldia was born May 4, 1903 and married Phil Bukaske November 8, 1920.  He died
in 1956.  They had four children, two boys and two girls.

Thomas was born in 1906 and died August 21, 1966. He married Esther Walton and
they had six children, three boys and three girls.  They always lived in South
Dakota.

Richard was born August 21, 1911 and died April 14, 1962.  He married Clarice
(Toots) Lester in June, 1936. They had two adopted boys and lived in South
Dakota and North Dakota.  Toots still lives in New Rockford, N. D.

Vivien was born May 7, 1916 and married Stanley Lester July 15, 1935.  They
have two sons and live near Loyalton.

T. I. Oban was section foreman on the railroad from Roscoe to Orient for 12
years.  He also engaged in farming.  He built up three or four sets of farm
buildings and owned close to 20 quarters of land.  He also had the first
milking machine, the first combine and the first 32 volt light plant in Edmunds
County, in about 1914.

VERNON AND VERNA OBAN

        Back row: Kathy, Judy, Verna, Dean and Karen; front row: Vernon, and
        Johnny Oban.

Vernon Oban was born at Loyalton, S.D. August 20, 1936 to Ralph and Edna Ganje
Oban.  Ralph was born and raised in the Loyalton area.  Edna came to the
Loyalton area about 1914 from the Mellette area and married Ralph in 1919.
Vernon grew up on the farm at Loyalton and served two years in the military
service in the United States and Germany.  Upon returning from the service
Vernon became actively engaged in the business of farming.  He married Verna
Buechler at Bowdle December 26, 1959.  They made their home with his parents on
the farm.  His parents moved to Loyalton and Vernon took over management of the
farm.

Verna was born September 7, 1939 at Bowdle to David and Maggie Aipperspach
Buechler.  David Buechler was born and raised in the Bowdle area where he
farmed. Maggie Aipperspach was born in North Dakota.  The family moved to the
Artas, S.D. area where she grew up. She moved to the Bowdle area when she
married David December 5, 1926.  Verna graduated from Bowdle High School in
1957, attended Northern State College, and taught in rural school at Ipswich and
Loyalton.

Vernon and Verna have five children.  The family ore all members of St. Paul's
Lutheran Church in Bowdle. Dean came to Roscoe school as a fifth grader from
Loyalton and graduated from Roscoe High School in 1978.  He is presently living
at Elk River, Minn. selling life insurance.  Koren came to Roscoe as a third
grader from Loyalton and graduated from Roscoe High in 1980. November 28, 1981
she married Clarence Gauer at Ipswich where they presently reside.  Clarence
farms in the Ipswich area and Karen is employed at K. O. Lee of Aberdeen.  Judy
and Kathy are presently students at Roscoe High School and are both active in
sports, music, F.H.A., church, 4-H and community activities. Johnny Ray is a
fourth grade student at Roscoe Elementary School.

Vernon and Verna are active in church and community affairs.  Vernon is a 4-H
leader, a member of the township board, member of the Edmunds County Extension
board and VFW member.  Verna is a 4-H leader, Sunday School superintendent,
member of the Loyal Homemakers Extension Club, a Yellowstone Trail CowBelle
member and a member of the American Lutheran Church women.  She currently
serves Edmunds County Extension as chairman and just completed a three-year
term as state citizenship chairman.

RICHARD AND EDNA OPP

Richard Opp was born July 21, 1918, in Venturia, N.D. His parents were Simon
Opp and Marie Wolf Opp.

He married Edna Waelhof at the Zeeland Farm Church February 13, 1944.  Edna was
the daughter of Gotthilf and Eva Brandner Waelhof of Zeeland, N.D. After
marrying, they farmed in North Dakota until moving to this area in 1948 where
they now farm.  They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

Three children were born to Edna and Richard.  Sandra is married to Roger Hauck
from Roscoe.  They live in Aberdeen where Sandra works as a secretary and Roger
is a truck driver.  They have two children, Kevin, age 14, and Darin, age one
and one-half.

Bruce is married to Jacqueline Krogman from Hoven, S.D. They live on the family
farm near Roscoe where Bruce is farming and Jackie is a teacher. Beverly
resides in Custer, S.D.

BRUCE AND JACKIE OPP

Bruce Opp was born July 9, 1953, in Aberdeen, S.D. to Richard and Edna Waelhof
Opp.  He married Jacqueline Krogman July 26, 1980, at Hoven, S.D. She was the
daughter of Clemens J. and Agnes Sons Krogman of Hoven.

They are living on the family farm east of Roscoe where Bruce is engaged in
farming with his father and Jackie is teaching.  They have no children.

MRS. MARTHA PENFIELD (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Mrs. Martha Penfield, sister of Ruby Collin, came to South Dakota in 1892 and
married Hiram L. Ellis in 1898 in Roscoe.  Her father and mother, Mr. and Mrs.
Elijah Cole, lived on a farm northeast of Roscoe until 1901, when they moved to
Ipswich where they lived the rest of their lives.

JOHN AND ELLA PESICKA

        John, Bill and Ella Pesicka

John, "Jack", Pesicka was born in 1907 at Bowdle to Joseph and Marie Gleich
Pesicka who came to this country from Russia.

Ella Brunmaier was born March 13, 1913 near Artas, S.D. to John and Mary Oster
Brunmaier.  Her mother was born on the ship coming to the United States from
Russia.

Jack and Ella were married in 1933 and moved to the Roscoe area the same year.

Their children are Michael Kenneth, married to Edna Tennant, lives in Onida,
S.D. and has two daughters.

William John, married Harlene Lay, lives in Aberdeen and has four children.

Mary Barbara, married Don Erickson and lives in Onida with her four children.

Wayne, married to Gloria Weller, died in a motorcycle accident in California in
1968, had two daughters.

Valentine Donnie, married Alice Rempher, lives at Pierre and has three children.

Gerald, lives at Le Seuer, Minn. and has one daughter and one stepson.

HAROLD AND ADELIDA PETERSON

        Harold Peterson

Harold Donald Peterson, son of Emma Woodruff and W. C. Peterson was born in St.
Paul, Minn.  October 15, 1921.  As a child he moved with his family to the
Roscoe area where he attended school.  He finished High School through American
School of Chicago by correspondence. Harold entered the Army at Ft.  Meade June
28, 1940 and served in New Guinea.  He was discharged at Ft. Snelling, Minn.
June 26, 1945.

January 21, 1945 he married Adelida A. Schumacher, the daughter of Christian
and Margaretha Lammle Schumacher who was born April 6, 1922.  Adelida graduated
from Roscoe High School in 1940 and attended the Remily Beauty School in
Aberdeen in 1941. After her apprenticeship she received her manager's license
and started her own beauty shop, which she operated off and on and in various
locations in Roscoe, the last of which was in her own home.

Harold and Adelida mode their home in Roscoe where he was Roscoe school
custodian for many years.  Before moving to Aberdeen in 1980, he had been an
employee of the Pickard Bee Farm.  While in Roscoe they were members of St.
Paul's Lutheran Church where Adelida served as organist for many years.  Harold
was a member of the Roscoe American Legion in which capacity he prided himself
in often being firing squad commander.  He was also a member and chief of the
Volunteer Fire Department.  Adelida had been a member of the American Legion
Auxiliary and the Priscilla Club.  She was the City Editor for The Roscoe
Independent for many years.

They had three children, Harlow, born in 1946, a daughter, Melody Rose, born in
1954 and another son, Timothy, born in 1958.  Harlow, his wife Marie and their
two children, Rebecca Ann and Robert Harold, reside in Aberdeen.  Melody
married Lance Bonen and resides in Hosmer, while Timothy is also an Aberdeen
resident.

In November of 1981, Harold entered the Veteran's Hospital in Sioux Falls, S.D.
He came back to Aberdeen for Christmas and was cared for by the Hospice Program
(caring for the terminally ill and dying, at home) and January 3 he passed
away.  He was buried in the Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

Until the time of his illness, Harold was the manager of the Dorian Apartments
and custodian of the Midwest and Capitol Building, Adelida having taken over in
that capacity since that time.  Since moving to Aberdeen, they have been
members of Trinity Lutheran Church there.

JOHN AND CLEOPATRA PICKETT

John Pickett came to Roscoe in the early 1900s from Salem, ill. to homestead.

Cleopatra Crawford came to Roscoe in 1908 to teach school.

John and Cleopatra were married in the Roscoe Methodist Church June 10, 1910.

After farming for a few years north of Roscoe, they moved to town.  John then
worked for the Milwaukee Railroad as night watchman.  After many years with the
railroad, he retired and became Roscoe's town marshall. In their later years,
they moved to a farm in the Powell area south of Ipswich.

Four children were born to this union, Randolph, Carl, George and Irene.
Randolph and Carl gave their lives serving their country in World War II.
George lives in Whitewood, S.D. and Irene, who is Mrs. Milton Sandquist,
resides in Aberdeen, S.D.

DANIEL AND MARGARET PIETZ

Daniel Pietz was born February 26, 1895 near Hosmer, S.D., the son of Michael
and Magdalena Pietz.  He was a WWI veteran, receiving his discharge December 10,
1918 at Camp Cody, N.M. He married Lydia Huft March 27, 1921.  They had two
children, Irene (Mrs.  Fred Schlepp) of Melrose Park, Ill., and Harold of
Billings, Mont.  Lydia passed away and later Dan married Lydia Sayler.  They
had one daughter, Leona, Mrs. Robert Washam) of Laclede, Mo.

        Back row: Harold, Irene, Milton and Elmer; front row: Leona, Arlene and
        Ruby Pietz.

When his second wife died, he then married Margaret Schiermeister of Zeeland,
N.D. She was born August 17, 1903, the daughter of Jacob and Margaret Reinboldt
Schiermeister.  They had four children, Ruby (Mrs. Wm. Thompson) of Roscoe;
Milton of Lewiston, Mont.; Arlene, (Mrs.  Glen Stratton) of Columbia Falls,
Mont. and Elmer of Billings, Mont.  There are 24 grandchildren and nine
great-grandchildren.

The Pietz's moved to Roscoe in the fall of 1952.  Mr. Pietz died in January
1953 and Mrs. Pietz died in February 1971 and both are buried in the Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe.

ERVIN AND ESTHER PRESZLER

Ervin Preszler was born near Hosmer, S.D. June 18, 1917, the son of John and
Louisa Handel Preszler.  He grew up on the farm where he was born and attended
the country school near his home.  He married Esther Weishaar in a little
country Lutheran church northeast of Hosmer October 12, 1941.  She was the
daughter of John and Magdalena Ehman Weishaar, born December 12, 1922, also on
a farm near Hosmer.  She attended the rural school near her farm home.

For the first year of their marriage they made their home on Ervin's family
homestead southeast of Hosmer.  From 1942 to 1945 they lived on a farm west of
Hosmer and in 1945 they moved to the former H. E. Preszler farm west and north
of Roscoe where they still live at this time.

        Back row: Duane, Betty and Larry; front row: Esther and Ervin Preszler.

They have three children.

Betty (Mrs. Albert Malsom) lives in Livermore, Calif. and has two daughters,
Michelle and Laurie.

Duane married Andrea Meier, and lives in River Falls, Wis.  They have three
children, Lora, Amy and David.

Larry lives in Livermore, Calif. and has two children, Chandra and Darin.

Ervin and Esther are members of the U.C.C. Church in Hosmer where Ervin serves
as treasurer and Esther belongs to the Women's Fellowship group.  Ervin served
for many years as treasurer of the Glen Township board.

HELMUTH AND ROSE PRESZLER

Helmuth E. Preszler was born October 9, 1902, near Menno, S.D. in Hutchinson
County, the son of John and Louisa Handel Preszler.  In 1906, he moved with his
family to a farm northwest of Roscoe.  This is where he grew up, went to the
country school near his home and farmed with his father until the time of his
marriage.

Rose Himmerich was born December 28, 1905, to John and Elizabeth Nehlich
Himmerich, about five and one- half miles northwest of Roscoe in the new adobe
house built by her father and other family members.  She grew up on this farm
and with her brothers and sisters, she walked the two miles to the country
school north of their home.

Helmuth and Rose were married at Hoffnungs (Hope) Lutheran Church north of
Roscoe November 14, 1926. They settled on a farm west and north of Roscoe and
lived there until 1944, when they moved into Roscoe where he bought the Roscoe
Auto Company from John Lammle and operated it for two years when he sold it to
the Farmers Union.  He continued to work there for several years when he
decided to do some farming again.  In 1955, he bought the Standard Service
Station from Edwin Schumacher, which he operated until 1964, when ill health
forced him to sell the business to Henry Trefz.  When his health improved, he
worked as a custodian at the Roscoe School.

They had four children, Lorene, married to Elmer Kranzler, has two daughters,
Ruby and Bonnie and they live in Webster, S.D. where they own and operate a
motel; Ardella, married to Calvin Kilber, has five children, Dawn, LeRoy, Joy,
Clyde and Glenn and lives on a farm near Roscoe; Elmer, married to Alma Delzer,
has one daughter, June.  He is the bookkeeper for Delzer Construction Company
and lives in Sturgis, S.D. and Jerold, married to Lila Bauer, has two daughters,
Michelle and Julie, works with computers and lives in Oakhurst, N.J.

Helmuth served as supervisor for several years in Glen Township and on the
Roscoe School Board for 23 years.  They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran
Church where Rose is a member of the Ladies Aid.

Helmuth passed away July 29, 1972 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.  Rose is a resident in the Senior Housing in Roscoe.

EUGENE AND GERTRUDE PRESZLER

Eugene Preszler was born at Hosmer, S.D. to Christopher and Magdalena Schnabel
Preszler May 31, 1908.  His parents and all his grandparents came to the Hosmer
area in 1900 from Menno, S.D. He was the fifth of six children, attended school
in Hosmer and farmed with his dad until he married.

Gertrude Ulmer Preszler was born in Menno, S.D. September 5, 1911 to Edward and
Anna Caroline Ulmer, the oldest of six children.  Her parents moved to Hosmer
from Menno in 1917 when Gertrude was five years old.  All her grandparents
lived in the Menno area.

Eugene and Gertrude were married in September 1930.  In 1931, they settled on a
farm four miles west and one mile south of Roscoe.  They have lived there for
51 years.

They had three sons, Ralph who married Betty Streifel and lives on a farm near
Roscoe.  Harry died when he was nine years old and Kurt, a lawyer, was killed
in an airplane crash at Aberdeen in April 1977.  He was 31 years old.

RALPH AND ELIZABETH PRESZLER

        Back row: Ronald, Paulette, Anita, Gregory and Kurt Preszler, Kurt
        Creagan, Scott Fayant, and Mark Preszler; front row: Betty, Jared,
        Sarah, and Ralph Preszler, Heather Fayant, Debra and Jamie Creagan.

Ralph G. Preszler was born April 12, 1931 at Hosmer, S.D., the son of Eugene
and Gertrude Ulmer Preszler. Eugene was born near Hosmer in 1908 and Gertrude
was born at Menno, S.D. in 1911 and came to the Hosmer area in 1916.

Elizabeth (Betty) Streifel was born in Aberdeen, S.D. April 13, 1930 to John
and Regina Schmidt Streifel. John was born in Zeeland, N.D. in 1902, and Regina
was born in Russia also in 1902.  She came to this country in 1907.

Ralph attended the rural school near his home west of Roscoe and both Ralph and
Betty graduated from Roscoe High School in 1949.  Ralph worked for the Farmers
Union State Office and in May, 1951 he entered the Army.  Betty graduated from
beauty school in Watertown, S.D. in 1951 and worked in the Vanity Beauty Shop
in Aberdeen.

Ralph and Betty were married July 12, 1952 at Fort Leonard Wood, Mo.  After his
discharge from the service, Ralph attended NSC at Aberdeen for three years.

They bought a farm northwest of Roscoe in 1957 and still live there.  They
enjoy farm life and wouldn't think of living anywhere else.  Betty opened a
beauty shop in their basement in 1958 and operated it there for 19 years.  In
1976 they built a new addition to the house which included an office, garage,
and a new beauty shop.  Betty really enjoyed getting her shop out of the
basement.  She has had many pleasant experiences and has thoroughly enjoyed
working with the people in the community.  She says Roscoe is the greatest
place in the world to live.

Ralph is a member of the Reformed Church in Hosmer and Betty belongs to St.
Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.

They were blessed with four children.  Ronald Ralph was born June 3, 1953, and
married Paulette Dewald, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Edmund Dewald of Bowdle, S.D.
September 9, 1977.  They live southwest of Roscoe on the former Milbert Ellwein
farm.  He also works as a mechanic for Kearns Large Equipment in Aberdeen. They
have two children, Sarah Marie, born September 18, 1979 and a son, Jared, born
March 1, 1982.

Debra Kay was born February 25, 1954, worked as a veterinarian's assistant in
Sioux Falls, S.D. and married Kurt Creagan from Minneapolis, Minn.  June 25,
1977. They have one daughter, Jamie Elizabeth, born November 2, 1980.  They
live in Parker.

Gregory was born January 22, 1955 and is in the contracting business in
Vermillion, S.D. He married Carol Gauer, daughter of the late Jack Gauer and
Ricky Gauer of Ipswich.  Greg and Carol have since divorced. They have one son,
Kurt Allen, born September 14, 1977.  Greg married Anita LaRocque in August,
1982.

Mark Allen was born April 25, 1957.  He attended Lake Area Vo-Tech in
Watertown, S.D. majoring in Agriculture Business.  Since 1978 he has farmed in
partnership with his parents.  He bought the former Andy Senger farm where he
now lives.  He married Kitty Schumacher in November 1982.

RONALD AND PAULETTE PRESZLER

        Jared, Paulette, Ron and Sarah Preszler

Ronald Preszler was born June 3, 1953 in Aberdeen, S.D. to Ralph and Betty
Streifel Preszler of Roscoe. Paulette Dewald was born November 12, 1953 in
Hoven, S.D. to Edmund and Frieda Stuckel Dewald of Bowdle, S.D.

Ron and Paulette were married September 10, 1977 in Bowdle.  They lived in
Aberdeen where Ron worked as a mechanic at Kearns Machinery and Paulette worked
in the business office at Northern State College.  They moved to the Milbert
Ellwein farm south of Roscoe in December 1980.  Ron kept his job at Kearns
until April 1982 when he became a full-time farmer.  Paulette gave up her job
and became a full-time mother and homemaker when they moved to the farm.

They have two children, Sarah Marie, born September 18, 1979 and Jared Ronald,
born March 1, 1982. They are members of St. Thomas Catholic Church.

THE FRANK PRICE FAMILY

The Prices lived in a house that was in the middle of the block east of the
schoolhouse. in addition to other jobs for many years, Mr. Price was Justice of
the Peace. He was also a respected and obeyed school custodian. In the dining
room of their home, Mrs. Price set aside a corner of the room for items
students could purchase, such as paper, pencils, notebooks, candy and gum.

Mrs. Price was a sister of John Bishop.  The children in the Price family were
Howard, Mary, George, Maude, Merrit, Arthur, Donald and Violet.

Howard married Mae Stewart who was a teacher. When he resigned as postmaster,
he became the carrier on the rural route.  Later he and his wife moved to
Aberdeen where they ran a delivery service.

George and Arthur married sisters, Esther and Ida Landfried.

Donald, with three other boys, made up the first class to graduate from Roscoe
High School in 1922.  He moved to Illinois.

Violet lived in Minneapolis.  She married a man from there.

Frank (Francis) Price was born in 1865 and died in 1947.  Marie Bishop Price
was born in 1865 and died in 1945.  They were laid to rest in Morningside
Cemetery at Roscoe.

GEORGE AND ESTHER PRICE (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

George Levens Price was born August 4, 1894, at Aurora, Ill.  He came to South
Dakota with his parents, Mr. and Mrs. F. A. Price in 1895.

George did a hitch in Uncle Sam's army for six months in 1918 during World War
I. After this he worked ten years in the roundhouse at Roscoe and two years for
the Snyder Oil Co. filling station.  He began to work for George O. Wilson on
the farm in 1935 until he retired.

George was married to Esther Lena Landfried November 26, 1933.  They had one
child, Lizzetta Marie. George passed away in 1974 and Esther in 1976.  They are
both buried in the Roscoe Cemetery.

JEROME AND LEORA PRISBE

Jerome Prisbe was born July 30, 1932 in Detroit, Mich., the son of Joseph and
Cecelia Prisbe.  After the death of his parents, he came to Roscoe to live with
his brother, Joe, and sisters, Justine and Germaine in 1943.

Jerome married Leora Heyne, daughter of Arthur and Anna Nehlich Heyne, October
24, 1955.  They have two sons, Todd, 18, presently attending S.D. School of
Mines and Technology in Rapid City, S.D., and Brad, 13, a student at Roscoe and
living at home.

Jerome graduated from Roscoe High School in 1950 and worked for Spitzers
Implement until he entered the Army in 1953.  He spent two years in the Army, 15
months of this service in Germany.  He has held various offices, including one
term on the city council, two terms on the school board, post president of
Commercial Club, is a member of the Roscoe Volunteer Fire Department and the
Roscoe American Legion Post No. 259.

Leora, after graduating from Roscoe High School in 1951, was employed in the
First State Bank of Roscoe until 1964.  She is a member of the Commercial Club
and the Ivy Circle Garden Club.

After his discharge from the Army in 1955, Jerome worked for Scherf's Super
Valu until his appointment as postmaster in September 1966.

JOE AND ANN PRISBE

Joe and Ann moved to Roscoe in late 1945 and have made their home here since.
Joe, a native of Detroit, Mich., was born December 23, 1920 to Joseph and
Cecelia Prisbe.  He attended St. Lawrence and Chadsy High, went to work at Ford
Motor Co. after high school where he met Ann Spitzer, daughter of John and
Rosina Spitzer.  Joe and Ann were married at St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Roscoe November 4, 1944 and lived in Dearborn, Mich. for another year before
moving to Roscoe.  Prior to coming to South Dakota, Joe and Ann both worked at
the Ford Bomber Plant which Ford Motor Co. converted into the B-36 Plant during
World War II.  Joe was supervisor of the plant commissary and Ann was a
department office clerk in the assembly plant.

Ann was born on a farm near Eureka, S.D. October 21, 1917, moved to Roscoe with
her parents when she was about a year old, attended grade and high school in
Roscoe.  After graduation she worked at the Roscoe Post Office from 1936 to
1939 when she resigned to go to Detroit.

Upon returning to Roscoe, Joe and Ann owned and operated a cafe for a short
time.  Having sold the business, Joe then worked for Sherf's Super Valu, also
for the City of Roscoe as manager of the Municipal Liquor Store for several
years.  In 1956, they purchased the inventory of the Variety Store and built a
new store in 1960 which they still operate.  Joe also has been with the U.S.
Post Office as a substitute clerk for the post 18 years.

Joe and Ann have two sons and two grandchildren.

Gary was born December 2, 1946, is married to Kay Margeson of Roscoe, and lives
in Madison, Wis.  They have two children, Brian, age 10, and Heather, age 8.

Douglas was born May 6, 1951 and is married to Diane Francis of Big Stone City,
S.D. and they reside in Aberdeen, S.D.

D. W. (TOM) AND CLARA REIDT

        Verne, Clara, Tom, Darlene, Floyd, Roger, and Clifford Reidt

D. W. Reidt came to this area in the spring of 1924.  He farmed with his
brother Bodie Reidt, south of Loyalton. He was born in Alma, Wis.  July 31,
1895, the son of Jacob and Eliza Lehman Reidt.  He served four years in the
Army during WWI.  In 1925, he married Clara Garrison, daughter of Charles L.
and Ida Mitchell Garrison.  Charles and Ida homesteaded in Hillside Township in
1917.  They came to this area from Carlyle, S.D. (a small town, no longer in
existence, which was located along what is now Highway 20).

This is where Clara was born in 1906.  Clara had three sisters, Violet,
married, living in Michigan; Lillian, married Ole Olson in 1925 and Florence,
married to Jake Guthmiller, living in Michigan. Tom and Clara lived in Hillside
Township on the Garrison homestead after Charlie's death in 1925.  They had six
children, Clifford graduated from Roscoe High School and served in the Army
during WWII.  He married Verdella Aman and has three children, Fay, Lendon and
Craig.  They live in Powell, Wyo.

Ethel died in June 1941 at the age of 13 and is buried at Roscoe.

Verne graduated from Roscoe High School and served in the Army during the
Korean War.  He married Lorraine Wiedrich and lives on the home farm south of
Roscoe.  They had three daughters, Cindy, (Mrs.  Larry Heinz), Diann, and
Nancy, who died in infancy.

Floyd graduated from Roscoe High School and served in the Army after the Korean
War.  He married Virginia Finney and lives on a farm adjacent to the home farm.
They have two children, Linda and Kay.

Darlene married Delbert Haight and lives in Rapid City, S.D. They have one son,
Russell.

Roger married Carlene Miller of Powell, Wyo.  They have two sons, Tylen and
Trevor.  Roger was killed in a traffic accident in Wyoming in July 1968 and is
buried in Powell.

Tom and Clara lived and farmed in Hillside Township until 1952 when they moved
to Igloo, S.D. In 1956 they moved to Rapid City.  Tom died in July 1980.  Clara
died in August 1982.  Both are buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

FLOYD AND VIRGINIA REIDT

Floyd W. Reidt, son of Tom and Clara Garrison Reidt, was born in 1935 at
Roscoe.  He lived there with his parents until 1952, and when they moved to
Igloo, S.D., he came back and lived with his brother Clifford, so he could
graduate from Roscoe High School in 1954.  He served in the Army from 1955 to
1957.  In 1958, he joined his brother Verne on the home farm in a ranch-
farming-dairy partnership.

In August 1959, Floyd and Virginia Finney were married in Faulkton, S.D.
Virginia, a native of the Faulkton area, worked in the Faulk County ASCS office
where she continued working until the summer of 1962.  They originally lived in
a trailer on the home farm until the fall of 1961, when the partnership
purchased the former Oscar Boer farm and they moved there.

Floyd and Virginia have two daughters, Linda Marie, born June 1962 and Kay
Lynne, born December 1971. Linda graduated from Roscoe High School in 1980, and
is a business major at NSC in Aberdeen, S.D. and will graduate in December
1983.  Kay is a student at the Roscoe Public School.

Floyd, Virginia and their girls participate in school and community affairs as
well as being very actively involved in Edmunds County 4-H activities, showing
Holsteins and Quarterhorses in both open and 4-H events.

Floyd is the third generation to farm the land in Hillside Township homesteaded
by his maternal grandfather, Charles L. Garrison.

VERNE AND LORRAINE REIDT

        Back row: Diann and Verne Reidt, Garett, Amanda, Larry, and Cindy
        Heinz; front row: Lorraine Reidt.

Verne Reidt was born at Roscoe, the second son of D. W. and Clara Garrison
Reidt.  Verne married Lorraine Wiedrich February 8, 1953.  She is the daughter
of Gottlieb and Martha Kirschenmann Wiedrich of Roscoe. Verne and Lorraine
graduated from Roscoe High School.  When first married, Verne served in the
Army, stationed at Paulsbury, New Jersey.  After his discharge, they lived in
Eureka, S.D. for one year working for Obenauer Construction.  Following that he
worked for Seely Construction.  In the fall of 1954 they moved to the Reidt's
home place south of Roscoe.

Their first daughter, Cindy, was born January 21, 1954.  She graduated from
Roscoe High School and the Medical Institute of Minnesota.  She is a
veterinarian technician and presently working part-time at the Ipswich Vet
Clinic.  Cindy is married to Larry Heinz of Ipswich and they are farming with
Verne and Lorraine. They have two children, Amanda, age three and Garett, age
two months.

The Reidts second daughter, Diann, was born January 19, 1960, graduated from
Roscoe High School, attended one year at SDSU and one year at NSC.  At the
present time, she is manager of a Pred Store at the Empire Mall in Sioux Falls,
S.D. Their third daughter, Nancy, was born July 19, 1966, and was deceased the
same day.

Verne has been active in the American Legion, serving as commander for two and
one-half years, was chairman of the local Farmers Union for 18 years and
chairman of the Edmunds County Livestock Imp.  Assn. He served on the draft
board, church council and as a 4-H leader for 12 years.  Lorraine has been
active as a 4-H leader for 15 years.  She is a member of the American Legion
Auxiliary where she has served in all the offices. She is a member of the
County Extension where she serves as an officer.

Their farm operation includes raising beef cattle and registered dairy cattle.
They raise small grain, corn and alfalfa.  As a hobby, Verne restores Model A.
Fords.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

THE FRED ROESCH FAMILY

        Fred Roesch     Rosina Roesch

Fred Roesch, Sr., his wife and children, John and Rose, came to the Roscoe
territory in March 1899, when the snow was melting and water filled the low
places, making moving difficult.  They had arrived in the Eureka, S.D. area
from Russia in October 1898.  During the winter months they bought up livestock
and such implements as may be needed for farming.  During that time, they also
bought the farm five miles north of Roscoe, which at that time was owned by the
Davison Bros, who then moved to Canada.  This farm was recommended very highly
as a combination farm and ranch.  The Hooper and Riegen Ranch was added to this
farm unit, also 300 cattle, mostly Black Galloway, and all the equipment, hay
racks and ox yoke etc. purchased from Shorrs, the tenant who was at that time
living on the Hooper-Riegen Ranch.  Mr. Shorrs sold out because a prairie fire
had burned him out.

A claim was filed for homesteading the northwest one-fourth of 31-124-70.  This
farm was located (as the crow flies) between Ipswich and Eureka.  The main road
was through this yard and many a pioneer stopped in to water their team or to
spend the night.  Neighbors were few and for apart in those days.  Still
remembered are the kind deeds of their good neighbors, the Linkenmeyer family
to the east, the George Harrison family to the south and the Christian Diegel
family to the south- west.

Seven more children were born on this farm, Eva, Mrs. A. F. Isaak, deceased,
lived in Santa Barbara, Calif. and had two children, Alice and Allegra.

Fred and his wife, Lorraine, live in Panorama City, Calif., and have three
children, Barbara, Fredrich, Jr. and Sally.

Marie, Mrs. H. P. Neff, widowed, lives in Tacoma, Wash. and has two children,
Susan and William.

Jacob, deceased, lived in Aberdeen, S.D. and had three children, J. J.,
deceased, and Richard and Marilyn.

Martha, Mrs. A. O. Larvie, widowed, living in Hermiston, Ore., and has three
children, Armond, David and Ann Marie.

Edward, and his wife Irene, live on the family farm north of Roscoe, have three
children, LaVaughn, Dale and Judy.

Annie, Mrs. L. E. Larson, is widowed and living in Aberdeen.

The two older children who were born in Russia, John, who in 1920, built a farm
across the road from his family, and his wife Emma, live at the Colonial Manor
in Ipswich, S.D. His six children are Ruth, Melvin, Esther, Bernice, Donald and
Jean.

Rose, Mrs. A. W. Schumacher, widowed, lives at the Americana Nursing Home in
Aberdeen and has four children, Virginia, Constance, Evelyn (deceased), and
Ronald.

Mr. Roesch wrote in his records, that on this farm, we prospered, we enjoyed
many blessings, we also had grief and hard times, but such is life.

THE JOHN ROESCH FAMILY

John Roesch was born July 6, 1892, in Glucksthal, Russia, to Fredrick and
Rosina Treftz Roesch.  They came to America aboard the Kaiser Friedrick in
1898, and homesteaded five miles north of Roscoe.  He was drafted into the Army
June 24, 1918.  After brief training, he was sent with the 88th Division to
Alsace Lorraine, France.  After the Armistice was declared, he remained on in
France until June 1919, when he returned home and continued to farm with his
father.

        Back row: Melvin, Esther, Ruth, Martha, John and Jean; front row:
        Bernice, and Donald Roesch.

        Back row: Donald, Bernice and Melvin; second row: Ruth, Esther and
        Jean; front row: John and Emma Roesch.

November 16, 1920 he married Martha Wiederrich, daughter of Jacob and Philipina
Will Wiederrich, of Hosmer, S.D. They built up a farm across the road from his
parents.

They were blessed with six children, Ruth was born September 16, 1921 and
married to Raymond Beyers June 4, 1944.  They have four children, John, Robert,
Mark and Patty.

Esther was born April 7, 1925 and married to LeRoy Buechler May 10, 1950.  They
have two children, Jerry and Danny.

Melvin was born February 26, 1927 and married Marvelyn Fuhrman February 12,
1955.  They have three children, Michael, Marty and Melody.

Bernice was born December 6, 1928 and married Reuben Beck June 21, 1950 and has
three children, Gregory, Richard, and David.

Donald was born September 26, 1931 and married Marilyn Jackson January 31, 1953
and has two children, Pamela and Douglas.

Jean was born November 19, 1938 and married Tom Uhrich, July 6, 1959 and has
two children, Steven and Kristen.

Mrs. Roesch died March 31, 1940.  She is buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.

December 30, 1944, John married Emma Ills Blumhardt, widow of Reinhold
Blumhardt.  She was the Edmunds County Treasurer at that time, first having
completed her husband's term of office after his death and then one elected
term.  They continued to live on the farm until July 1955, when they bought a
home in town and his son Donald took over the farm.  John continued to help on
the farm until 1981 when at the age of 89, ill health forced him to retire.  He
died on September 14, 1982.

MELVIN J. AND MARVELYN ROESCH

        Back row: Marty, Melody, Michael; front row: Marvelyn and Melvin Roesch.

        Carol, Linda, and Michael Roesch

Melvin Roesch was born February 26, 1927 at Roscoe to John and Martha
Wiederrich Roesch.  Melvin's father, John, was born in Odessa, Russia and came
to Eureka, S.D. in 1898.  One year later he moved to the Roscoe area where he
still resides.  After his wife, Martha's death, he was married to Emma
Blumhardt.

Marvelyn Fuhrman Roesch was born January 22, 1933 at Mansfield, S.D. to Ernest
and Myrtle Naessig Fuhrman. Myrtle was originally from Webster, S.D.

Mel and Marvelyn (Mike) were married February 12, 1955 at Aberdeen, S.D. Melvin
lived north of Roscoe all his life and Marvelyn moved to Roscoe from Aberdeen
after their marriage.  They settled on a farm northeast of Roscoe and have
lived there ever since.  Melvin served two years in the United States Army,
served on the Farmers Union, Presbyterian Church, North Bryant Township and
Roscoe School Boards.  Marvelyn is a member of the How & Why Extension Club,
Priscilla Library Club, served on several 4-H committees and presently works
part-time at Donnie's Cafe and Lounge.

They belong to the Presbyterian Church, Shrine, Masons and Eastern Star.

They have three children, Michael Jon, married to Carol Jean Thompson and farms
in partnership with his dad.  They have a little girl, Linda Michelle.  Marty
Jeff, is service department foreman in Aberdeen for Sheehan Equipment Company
and Melody Jill is a veterinary assistant in Pierre, S.D. for Oahe Veterinary
Clinic.

EDWARD AND IRENE ROESCH

        Back row: Dale and Bonnie Roesch, Clyde, LaVaughn, Marcia and Michael
        Pietz; front row: Jessica, Eddy, Ed (holding Lindsey), and Irene
        Roesch, Judy (holding Andrea), Terry and Amy Cox.

Edward and Irene remain on the farm his parents moved on in 1899, located five
miles north of Roscoe. He married Irene Ulmer, daughter of Solomon and Joanna
Ulmer of Hosmer, S.D. They have three children, LaVaughn, Dale and Judy.

LaVaughn married Clyde Pietz of Hosmer.  They now live in Aberdeen, S.D. and
have two children, Marcia and Michael.

Dale married Bonita Pietz of Hosmer.  They live on a farm four miles north and
one mile west of Roscoe and have three children, Jessica, Eddie and Lindsey.

Judy married Terry Cox from Hosmer.  They live in Aberdeen and have two
children, Amy and Andrea.

Edward's father, Fredrick Roesch, spoke often of the open area their farm is
located on, prairie fires that burned from here to Ipswich, plowing fire guards
to stop them.  Water was a big factor at that time, for livestock and for human
use.  Mr. Roesch spoke of pulling a well drilling rig from the Loyalton area to
this farm with 12 horses, to drill an artesian well, about seventeen-hundred
feet deep, drilled 24 hours, day and night for about six weeks.  His mother and
Rose cooked for the help.  The well had very poor quality drinking water.  It
could only be used for livestock.  They hauled the water for human use from
other wells.  The artesian well had so much gas, that if a match was lit on top
it would burn awhile.  This well filled with sand or something and was of no
value.  It was thought "Norbeck & Nichols" drilled this well. A new well was
drilled three-hundred-twenty feet deep and it is still in use.

Edward's brother John built a farm just across the road from their Dad, about
1920, after returning from World War I. He remembers helping him break up some
of the sod with a walking plow and four horses.  Edward rode the lead horse or
the one that walked in the furrow.

The homesteaders dug many wells by hand.  Nearly every large slough had a place
showing where someone had dug for water on the edge, dust storms in the 1930s
were extremely hard.  After it rained in 1934, the land that had rested
produced crops that were good but the prices were very low, corn was seven
cents a; bushel and wheat was below 25 cents per bushel.

He and his sister Annie planted over 3,000 trees by hand in the later 1930s to
prevent more dust storms. One year he and Annie harvested with the header.  Fred
Fetzer threshed for them that year and had a total of 78 bushels of wheat.
Christ Diegel threshed for them many years, before the neighbors, John
Himmerichs, had a steam engine used for threshing.  When it was no longer in
use, it was wrecked.  He bought the big wheels and used them for water tanks.
He believes the boiler was used in the Roscoe School for a number of years.

He remembers going to the county fair in Roscoe.  This was on the east side of
town.  His Dad would take along about everything they had to show it, just
about like Ma and Pa Kettle!  They came home with quite a number of prizes and
ribbons.  The horse races were especially good.  As he recalls, the
Weisenburgers and the Diegels were the big contributors, of course there were
others. Today the breeds of horses used then would be Arabian and Appaloosa.
They always had well trained horses and his Dad would run a lot of cattle.  His
Dad bought the Hooper and Reigen Ranch over east with about 300 Galloway
cattle.  He used to go quite a distance to get good herd bulls.  He would leave
early in the morning with a team and wagon, sometimes put another box on top of
a triple box, so the bull couldn't jump over the top.  Cattle at that time were
about two and one-half cents per cwt.  However, Galloway were very much in
demand--probably due to the long hair that breed had and the cold winters here.

All the roads at that time were as the "crow flies". There are still wagon
trails on the land, the road to Hosmer led through their yard.  To the
northeast of their farm was the road "short cut" to Roscoe.  When a road got
too deep, a new track would be started between the old tracks.

They sometimes delivered loads of hay to town. Nearly everyone in town had
their own barn for a milk cow or two, also chickens and laying hens.  People
would carry their egg basket to the store and trade for groceries.  A twelve
dozen egg crate was very popular for farm to market use.

The older model cars would advertise that their trunk holds several cans of
cream, then they would come out with a five or six, up to eight or nine or 10
gallon cans to fit the certain car trunks or space in the certain makes of cars
at that time.  There were a number of cars at that time that aren't heard of
any more, like the Star Essex, Durant, Overland, Auburn, Saxon, Hudson,
Maxwell, Page, Hupmobil, Kaiser, Fraser.  These were all short lived.  His Dad
bought his first car in 1915, an Overland, no doors in front, carbrite lights,
a touring car.  His Mother spoke of a car that used to drive through their yard
(a chain drive).  She asked the driver to be careful not to run over the
children.  Top speed on these was about 18 miles an hour--down hill!

At this writing, they now live on an all-weather black top road, speed limit 55
miles per hour, Highway No. 247 that ties in a highway system from Highway 12 to
North Dakota to Canada.  At least four milk trucks go past their farm each day.
They have a wonderful rural electric system for lights and power, a private
telephone, many four wheel tractors are used now, and so many things that make
life much easier.  Sunflowers are seen as the main crop in some areas.  The
price of fuel and heating has gone from 17 cents per gallon to $1.25 per gallon
as well as gasoline.

DALE AND BONNIE ROESCH

        Eddie, Dale, Jessica, Bonnie, and Lindsey Roesch

Dale Edward Roesch was born June 26, 1944 at the home of his parents, Edward
Roesch and Irene nee Ulmer Roesch.

Bonita nee Pietz Roesch was born June 11, 1945 at St. Luke's Hospital in
Aberdeen, S.D. to Arnold Pietz and Vera nee Walth Pietz.

Dale attended a country school through the fifth grade when bus service began
and he completed his education at the Hosmer Public School System.  After
graduation from high school in 1962, Dale attended South Dakota State
University at Brookings, S.D. and Dunwoody Technical Institute in Minneapolis,
Minn.

June 19, 1966 Dale and Bonnie Roesch were married at St. John's Lutheran Church
at Hosmer, S.D. From 1965 to 1969 Dale farmed near Aberdeen, S.D. on the Jake
Roesch farm.  During this time he was also employed part-time at the REA
Express Company, Nelson Equipment, and Sveen Oil Company.  He was a member of
the 452nd Ordinance Army Reserve Unit from 1965 to 1972 and was stationed at
Ft. Bliss, Texas and Ft. Huachuca, Ariz.

Bonnie graduated from Hosmer High School in 1963 and from Northern State
College in 1967 with a Bachelor of Science Degree in elementary education and
minors in the fields of music and special education. While in school, she
worked at the South Dakota State School in Redfield and as a salesperson for
the Sarah Coventry Company.  In 1970 she received a Master of Science Degree
from Northern State College in the field of guidance and counseling.  She
taught in the Leola Public School System in 1967 and 1968 and was employed by
the Roscoe Public School System from 1969 to 1979.

In the fall of 1969 Dale and Bonnie moved to Roscoe. Dale farmed with his
father and also worked as a salesperson for Spitzer Implement.  They purchased
the former Christ Diegle farm in 1972, where they reside to this date.  In
addition to farming, Dale runs a crop- spraying and trucking service.

Three children have been born to this family, Jessica, January 11, 1969;
Edward, June 4, 1976 and Lindsey, March 20, 1980.

Dale is currently serving on the Roscoe School Board and as president for the
Roscoe Commercial Club.

CHRIST AND CHRISTINA ROHRBACH

Christ and Christina Bieber Rohrbach came to this country from Russia in 1904.
They settled in the Hosmer, S.D. area for two years and then moved to a farm 10
miles north of Roscoe where they lived about 15 years.  In 1921 they moved to
Roscoe where they retired.

Their children are Jacob, John, Christina (Kemnet), Katherine Schumacher and
Carolina Huft.  All are deceased except Caroline who lives in Spokane, Wash.

ENOCH AND LEA ROTH

Enoch Roth was born April 17, 1913, five miles northwest of Venturia, N.D.
where he grew to manhood. His parents were John and Christina Rempfer Roth.
They were born and raised in the Venturia area where they spent all their
lives.  John Roth passed away May 16, 1968 and Christina passed away September

Lea Fischer was born December 23, 1914 to Christ C. and Elizabeth Bertsch

in McPherson County. Christ passed away January 11, 1975 and Elizabeth passed
away August 23, 1971.

September 22, 1935 Enoch and Leo were married. They spent the first 12 years on
a farm near Venturia where four generations of Roths lived.  Then they lived
for three years on a farm five miles west of Ashley, N.D. They spent the next
eight years in the implement business in Wishek and Ashley, N.D. For the past 19
years they have lived in Roscoe where Enoch is engaged in the Watkins business.
They attend the First Baptist Church in Ipswich.

To this union five children were born, Robert Delane, who died in infancy.

Evangeline (Mrs.  Virold Maeckel) living in Coral Springs, Fla. and they are in

KASPER AND JULIA SAHLI

Kasper Sahli was born March 1, 1896, to Theophil and Margaretha Heilman Sahli
on a farm near Hillsview, S.D. in McPherson County.  The family moved to a farm
two and one-half miles west of Hosmer, S.D. in 1898.  In 1906 they sold out and
the Sahli family moved to Buffalo Park, Kan. where they farmed four years.  In
the fall of 1910, they returned to S. D. to a farm 4 miles southwest of Hosmer.
He had five sisters and one brother.  He received- his education in the rural
schools near his home in Hosmer Township.  His first grade teacher was Mrs.
John Bishop of Roscoe.

Julia Gabriel was born October 13, 1901, on a farm near Hosmer to John and
Claudia Harty Gabriel.  She attended the same rural school as Kasper did.  They
were married at Hosmer October 28, 1918.  Kap continued farming with his father
for two years and then they moved to Zeeland, N.D. where they operated a cream
buying business.  In September 1925, they moved to Roscoe where they again
operated a Cream and Produce business until 1935.  Kap became a field
representative for the Beatrice Creamery which required him to travel to all
the Beatrice Cream Stations in his district which covered a territory extending
into a large part of North and South Dakota.  This kept him on the road five or
six days every week.

After four and one-half years, they made a decision which changed their lives
very much.  In June 1939, they bought a General Merchandise and Grocery Store
known at that time as the Roscoe Cash Store.  This store was located in the
building which is now Hieb's Repair. The name of the store was changed to
Sahli's Red and White.  In 1946, they bought the Finder building and tore it
down and also the John Fetzer Cream Station which they sold to John
Schlichenmeyer who moved it out to his farm.  This became the new location of
the store when they constructed a new building on this site. In 1954, their son
Harold became a partner in the business and in 1956 they joined the Red Owl
franchise and it became Sahli's Red Owl.  Julia worked with Kap in the store
from the very first day.  They sold their remaining share to Harold in 1961 and
although they were retired, they spent many hours helping in the store.

They were members of St. Thomas Catholic Church where Kap served as trustee in
1931 and 1932 and Julia was a member of the Ladies Altar Society.  He served as
city treasurer and on the city council for several years, as city mayor for two
years and was a member of the Roscoe Cemetery Board and the Roscoe Volunteer
Fire Department.

They had four children, Mary, married to Larry Deis of Onaka, S.D. and is
living in Philip, S.D. They have two daughters, Juliann and Leanne and four
grand- daughters and one grandson.

Harold is married to Dolores Himmerich and is living Roscoe.  They have one
son, Bruce, one daughter, Robin, and one granddaughter.

Barbara is married to Eugene Schwan and is living in Aberdeen.  They have two
daughters, Rosemarie and Patricia, one grandson and one granddaughter.

Elizabeth Jean is married to Alvin Schmitt and is living in Roscoe.  They have
one son, Tim.

Kasper passed away October 15, 1973 and Julia passed away on their wedding
anniversary, October 28, 1981.  They are both buried in Morningside Cemetery in
Roscoe.

HAROLD AND DOLORES SAHLI

Harold Sahli was born in Roscoe July 24, 1928, the son of Kasper and Julio
Gabriel Sahli.  He received his education in the Roscoe school graduating from
Roscoe High School in May 1946.  He worked in the family grocery store
throughout his high school years and continued to work there after his
graduation.

Dolores Himmerich Sahli was born on a farm northwest of Roscoe July 5, 1930, to
John and Clara Preszler Himmerich.  She attended a rural school in Sangamon
Township for six years.  In September 1942, she moved to Roscoe with her family
when her father became manager of the Farmers Elevator.  She graduated from
Roscoe High School in May 1948 and worked at the First State Bank of Roscoe
from September 1948 through December 1951.

Harold and Dolores were married November 22, 1948 at Roscoe.  Harold entered
the Army January 9, 1952, receiving his basic training at Ft.  Jackson near
Columbia, S.C. Dolores joined him there and worked at McCrory's Department
Store in Columbia.  After basic, Harold was stationed at Ft.  Richardson,
Alaska until December 13, 1953.  Dolores joined him there also and they lived
off base in Anchorage, Alaska, where Dolores was employed as a teller at the
First National Bank of Anchorage.  Upon their return to Roscoe, Harold
purchased a partnership in his father's grocery store and remained there,
eventually buying his father's share in 1961 and became the sole owner.  They
are still operating this store at the present time.

        Back row: Bruce, and Harold; front row: Robin and Dolores Sahli.

They have two children, Bruce, born February 20, 1959, is married to Cynthia
Roushar of Bowdle, S.D. and they live in Roscoe where Bruce is a Star-Route Mail
Carrier and they both work part-time in the family store.  They have two
children, Sarah Roe and Scott Ryan.

Robin Gayle was born August 1, 1961 and is a student at NSC in Aberdeen, S.D.

Harold and Dolores are members of the Roscoe Commercial Club and St. Thomas
Catholic Church and Roscoe Golf Association.  Harold also belongs to the Roscoe
American Legion, Faulkton VFW, Volunteer Fire Dept., Rod and Gun Club, and
Roscoe Cemetery Board. Dolores is a member of the Roscoe American Legion
Auxiliary and St. Thomas Altar Society.

BRUCE AND CINDY SAHLI

Bruce Sahli was born February 20, 1959 in Belle Fourche, S.D., the son of
Harold and Dolores Himmerich Sahli.  He grew up in Roscoe where he went to

and Loisa Feichtner Sauer, who settled near Java in 1901, having came from South
Russia.

Christ and Fern were married in 1924 in Java and moved to the Roscoe area in

teacher and elder in the Presbyterian Church in Roscoe.

They had six children, Lorrayne (Mrs.  Devon Adams) lives in Gulf Breeze, Fla.
