irrigation firm.

Dennis lives on a farm near Roscoe.

Christ died June 4, 1977 and Fern died in 1962.  They are buried in Sunset
Memorial Gardens in Aberdeen, S.D.

FRED AND EMELIE SAUER

Fred Sauer was born December 15, 1886 in Peizon, Poland near Warsaw.  He came
to this country in 1907 to avoid having to serve in the Russian Army, as Poland
was under Russian rule at that time.  He settled in Chicago working there in
the winters and coming to South Dakota to work on farms near Freeman every
summer.  He married Emelie Keinast November 15, 1914 at Freeman.

Emelie Keinast was born in Zogaroff, Poland, January 6, 1896.  Her parents were
August and Marie Serbe Keinast.  Her mother died when she was six years old and
the family of four children was separated after her father found that he was
unable to provide for them and care for them.  At the age of eight, Emelie was
sent to live with a family named Schultz, who took her with them to live on a
farm near Berlin,,Germany.  She was forced to work very hard to pay for her
board and room.  After about a year, Emelie returned to Poland where she lived
with an aunt.

Her father remarried, and he and his wife and Emelie's sister came to the
United States.  In 1912, after waiting a week in Bremerhavn, Germany for the
ship "Kaiser Wilhelm II", Emelie and her two brothers also made the journey to
the United States arriving in New York City April 12.  They went on to Chicago
where they were reunited with their family.  Emelie met Fred while she was
living in Chicago.

After their marriage they made their home near Freeman until March 23, 1921,
when they moved to a farm four and one-half miles southeast of Roscoe.  They
lived there for three years during which time they never had a good crop.  They
moved to Roscoe December 17, 1923 and lived in a two-room shack on the site
where, in 1926, they built a new home.

Emelie's father, stepmother and a brother also moved to the Roscoe area in 1921
and lived on what had been the Lightner farm just northeast of Roscoe.

Fred continued to farm on land near town until 1961 when he sold his land to
the American Legion which is the site of their new building and the golf course.

They have three children, Emanuel, born September 4, 1917, married Loraine
Schumacher, was postmaster and operated a grocery store in Keldron, S.D. for 35
years.  They live in Morristown, S.D. and have two children.  Esther, born
August 4, 1921, lives in Aberdeen where she is employed by the J. C. Penney Co.
Leona, born February 20, 1924, married to Clarence Sauer, lives in McLaughlin,
S.D. and has one daughter.

Fred passed away February 5, 1971 and was laid to rest in Morningside Cemetery
at Roscoe.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church where Emelie is a member of the
Ladies Aid.  She was a past member of the American Legion Auxiliary.

NORMAN AND ESTHER SCHATZ

        Norman, Monte, and Esther Schatz

Mr. and Mrs. Norman Schatz purchased their farm from Melvin Jeschkes and moved
to this community in February of 1965.

Norman Schatz was born March 8, 1922 at Tolstoy, S.D. to Robert and Beatha
(Auch) Schatz.  Esther Wolff was born March 5, 1922 to Edd C. and Louise
(Seidel) Wolff at Bison, S.D.

Norman attended the Tolstoy town school and also some rural schools in that
area.  Upon graduation from the eighth grade, he continued helping his Dad with
the farming and assisting the neighbors working on the threshing rigs. in the
fall he went with his Dad to the Sioux Falls, S.D. and the Minnesota areas
picking corn by hand for five cents a bushel.

In October of 1941 he was called into the service and was located in Kentucky
and Washington and he also served in the England and European areas until 1945
when he received an honorable discharge.

Upon his return from the service he decided to go back to farming, renting the
Beach Farm south of Tolstoy. December 31, 1947 he was married to Esther Wolff.
Esther graduated from the Onaka High School in 1940 and attended NSC in
Aberdeen, S.D. She taught school for 12 years in Tolstoy town school and
surrounding country schools, her last being the Beach School next to their home.

Norman and Esther are the proud parents of two children, Pamela Kay, born
August 5, 1953 at Hoven, S.D. and Monte Lee born March 1, 1959 also at Hoven,
S.D.

Pamela, better known as Pam, married Bernard Heier. Her history is in their
family write-up.

Monte graduated from Roscoe Grade and High School and in 1981, graduated with a
B.S. degree from S.D.S.U. at Brookings, S.D. with a double major in Political
Science and Economics. in the fall of 1981, he entered the Law school in
Vermillion, S.D. and this summer has been working as a clerk in the
Ryan-Scoville Law firm in So. Sioux City, Neb.  He will continue his school in
the fall and also work part-time in the firm.

Mr. and Mrs. Schatz are also the proud grandparents of two grandchildren, Jason
and Melissa Heier of Roscoe.

The Schatz's are members of the Lutheran Church, Norman a voting member and
Esther a member of the Ladies Aid.  Norman is also a member of the American
Legion.

MR. AND MRS. ANDREAS SCHAUER

        Katherine and Andrew Schauer

Andreas Schauer, an early pioneer, came to Dakota Territory in 1901.  He was
born in Neudorf, Russia, October 5, 1873.  In October 1895 he was married to
Katherina Klein and in 1901 he and his wife immigrated to the United States.

The Schauer family made their temporary home with a relative whose homestead
was located west of Eureka in McPherson County.  Here Mr. Schauer helped them
break up land and sow their crops.  He experienced the hardships of typical
pioneer days.  Their work was oil done with their hands.  The only materials
available were sod and stones.  His wages were the meager sum of 25 cents per
day.

Some time later they settled on their homestead eight and one-half miles north
of Roscoe.  Their first home was a sod house, their livestock consisted of two
horses and a cow.  Food was not too plentiful at that time, and the settlers
did not make any more trips than necessary.  Mr. Schauer said it would take
three or four days to get supplies from Ipswich or Aberdeen at that time.

In February 1907, Mrs. Schauer passed away.  She was preceded in death by three
children who died in infancy. Their other children were, Rudolph, living in
Roscoe; Martha, Mrs. Fred Aipperspach, Wishek, N.D.; Rose, Mrs. Fred Schaible,
Eureka, S.D. and Andrew 11, who lost his life serving his country in the first
World War.

In May 1907, he was married to Katherine Sandmeier and to this union eight
children were born.  They are, Mrs. Lydia Diegel, Mitchell, S.D.; Christina,
Mrs. Ed Stern, Aberdeen, S.D.; Esther, Mrs. Fred Fetzer, Cody, Wyo.; Bertha
Diegel, Dearborn, Mich.; Ann, Mrs. George Hammond, Roscoe; Olivia, Mrs. J.
Stephan, Savannah, Go.; Andrew of Aberdeen and Harold, who gave his life for
his country in the second World War.

These first years were very lean ones.  People were poor, but with courage and
determination they cared for their crops and in time reaped good harvest, and as
time went on, gradually paid off their debts and saved money to buy more land.
Mr. Schauer raised his own tobacco at that time.  The winters in those days were
much more severe and stormy than they are now and drought years were also
experienced.

These pioneers knew when Sunday came.  They had no churches and no ministers,
so the father read to the family from his prayer book.  Later they held
services in their homes and school houses.  In 1908 the Iowa Synod Church was
organized about five and one-half miles north of Roscoe, where services were
held every Sunday, conducted by members of the congregation with an occasional
visit by a minister.  Mr. Schauer was active in serving this church.  He was
also a member of the school board at various times.  Sometime later the
Schauers became members of the St. Paul Evangelical Lutheran Church of Roscoe.

The Schauers moved to the city of Roscoe in 1930 where they lived until January
1958 when due to advanced age they became residents of the Lutheran Senior
Citizens Home In Eureka, S.D. Mr. Schauer died March 30, 1961 at the age of 87
years and Mrs. Schauer died September 17, 1965 at 83 years of age.  They are
both buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

RUDOLPH AND EMMA SCHAUER

Rudolph Schauer was born November 29, 1902 in a sod house in Edmunds County,
near Hosmer, S.D. to Andrew and Katie Klein Schauer.  Both of his parents were
born in Russia.  His father is buried in Roscoe Cemetery and his mother is
buried in Hosmer.

Emma Opp Schauer was born June 28, 1905 at Eureka, S.D. to Christian and
Christina Neuharth Opp.  Christian was born in Russia and came to the United
States at the age of three.  Christina was born in McPherson County in South
Dakota.  Both of her parents are buried at Eureka, S.D.

Emma and Rudolph were married October 27, 1925. They farmed near Roscoe until
1936 when they moved to Roscoe.  Rudolph did trucking then for 30 years.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church and Emma was a member of
Lutheran Ladies Aid.

Emma died April 27, 1974 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

To this union were born four children, Lester, Herbert, Mildred, and Angie.

Lester and family live in LaPuente, Calif., where he manages a Chevron Filling
Station.

Herbert and family live in Groton, S.D. where he is employed with the
Burlington Railroad.

Mildred Dieringer lives in Portland, Ore. and her husband, Raymond, is deceased.

Angie Haynes lives in Vancouver, Wash. where her husband, Roland, is a railroad
engineer.

ELROY AND MARGARET SCHERBENSKE

        Elroy, Ernest, Margaret, and Eric Scherbenske

Elroy Scherbenske was born at Eureka, S.D. January 6, 1943 to Rueben and Edna
Miller Scherbenske.  He grew up near Leola, S.D. and attended country school. in
1963, he married Margaret Moser who was the daughter of Jacob and Maggie
Zimmerman Moser.  She was born in Eureka in April 1942 and grew up in Ashley,
N.D. where she attended school.

Elroy and Margaret worked on farms near Leola and Wetonka until they moved to
Roscoe in 1970 where Elroy works for Hieb Implement.

They have two sons, Ernest, born July 1, 1965 in Ellendale, N.D. and Eric, born
January 8, 1980 in Sioux Falls, S.D.

They are members of Peace Lutheran Brethren Church in Aberdeen, S.D.

MARTIN AND VALBORG SCHERF

        Marie, Valborg, Martin and Paul Scherf

Martin Scherf was born in Fairmont, Minn., August 24, 1902 to the late Rev. and
Mrs. J. P. Scherf.  When he was two years old he moved with his family to
Balaton, Minn. where he received his elementary education and three years of
high school.  He was employed in a garage there.

In 1921, the family moved to Roscoe and Martin was valedictorian of the first
graduating class of Roscoe High School.  He attended Northwestern College in
Watertown, Wis.  His early positions in Roscoe were held in the elevator and he
served as school janitor.

In 1925, Martin and his brother, Arthur, started an I.G.A. grocery store which
they operated in partnership until 1953.  Martin also was a partner with the
Bieber brothers in the Roscoe Auto Company for several years.

From 1942 until 1963 Martin operated a farm east of town where he raised
turkeys, geese, and Aberdeen Angus cattle.  He also raised Shetland ponies as a
hobby.  In 1946 he shipped 12,000 horses to Poland under the U.S.D.A. Lend
Lease Program.

In 1963 Martin sold his farm and the family moved to Mobridge where he was in
the home construction business.

In addition to his many business interests, Martin served on the Lutheran
Church Mission Board and on the Board of Regents for the Northwestern Lutheran
Academy at Mobridge.  He was also a board member for the former Aberdeen
Pheasants Baseball Club.  In 1956 his name was included in the first Who's Who
in South Dakota publication.

Martin married Valborg Johnson of Canton, S.D. February 7, 1938.  Valborg had
come to Roscoe in the fall of 1926 to teach in the high school.  Her first
letter home informed her family that she would only stay in Roscoe until
Christmas, there were no trees and the water tasted bad.  Valborg's stay in
Roscoe lasted 37 years.

Martin and Valborg had two children, Paul and Marie. Paul married Kathy Repp of
Volin, S.D. They have three children, Elizabeth Kay, Sarah and Shawn.

Paul and his family live in Gillette, Wyo.  Marie married Gary Gramm of Hosmer.
They have two children, Mark and Susan.  Valborg now lives with Marie and her
family in Brookings.

Martin died June 12, 1964 and is buried at Mobridge.

        Back row: Rosemary, Leonard, Jr., Sebastian, Joe, Barbara and Lorraine;

JULIUS AND CAROLINE SCHIPKE

In the month of May, the year 1883, Julius and Caroline Lukow Schipke came to
the Dakota Territory and homesteaded by Roscoe on Section 29-123-70 or one mile
east of Roscoe.  Julius was the son of Carl and Francisca Gross Schipke, Sr.
born in Breslau, Germany and migrated to America in 1870 and obtained his
second papers in 1880.  They first settled in Madison, Wis.  Caroline Schipke
was born in Germany and came to America in 1875, and settled in Kankakee, Ill.
where she married Julius Schipke.

To this union were born six sons, Ernest, Wilhelm, Henry, Julius, Walter and
Arthur, and five daughters, Anna, Caroline, Emily, Jessie and Minnie.

Julius passed away in the year 1915.  Caroline passed away in 1925.  They were
also one of the group of the area to hold church services of the Lutheran faith
in their home.  The pioneers of their time.

HENRY AND HANNAH SCHIPKE

Henry J. Schipke, known to his friends as "Hank", was born October 21, 1892, on
a farm one and one-half miles east of Roscoe, the son of Julius and Caroline
Schipke, pioneers of Dakota territory and one of a family of 11 children.  He
attended the Roscoe Public School and after his graduation from the eighth grade
he was eager to further his education and then enrolled in the Martin Luther
College, New Ulm, Minn. and the following year attended Mankato Business
College, Mankato, Minn.  He then returned to Roscoe where his career in banking
started in 1912 at the First State Bank as bookkeeper and assistant cashier.

In 1915, he was united in marriage to Hannah Christen and to this union three
children were born, William of Orange, Calif.; Hazel (Mrs.  John Lindberg) of
Seattle, Wash. and Berneice (Mrs.  Harold Margeson) of Roscoe. There are six
grandchildren.

As the son of a pioneer, "Hank" took advantage of the fertile soil in the
territory and his interest in gardening became more and more apparent after his
own home had been built in 1919 on a barren corner lot.  Spending many hours
planting and working with the soil, he was able to prove that it was possible
to have a true garden spot of beauty on the South Dakota prairie land.  He was
on avid sportsman, hunting and fishing when the seasons were open.

For a number of years, he was a member of the town council, served as clerk of
the Board of Education for 18 years and also as a board member.

The career he had chosen kept him in the banking business for 39 years.  He
became cashier in 1944, was also a stockholder and director in the organization
until his death May 16, 1951.

Mrs. Schipke died March 21, 1963.  Both are buried in Morningside Cemetery at
Roscoe.

THE WALTER SCHIPKE FAMILY

June 29, 1926.  Walter was born August 29, 1902.  Esther was born March 2, 1896.
To this union was born a son, Roman, October 7, 1927. In the year 1930, October
7, Esther passed away and is buried in Albany, Minn.

        Walter and Esther Schipke

1932, from Bremerhaven, Germany.  They lived on the farm until 1959 when they
retired and built a home in Roscoe.  Walter held different positions on the
Roscoe School Board and in Bryant Township.

Walter passed away July 14, 1971.  Bertha lives in Roscoe in her home at the
present time.

PAUL AND CAROLINE SCHMIDT, SR.

Paul Schmidt, Sr. was born January 29, 1866 In Germany and later migrated to
Odessa, Russia with his family.  His wife, Caroline Daness, was born April 17,
1866, also in Germany and went to Russia with her parents.  They were married
in Russia and January 6, 1907, they and their eight children left South Russia,
arriving near Roscoe February 7, 1907.  They lived with his nephew and wife,
Mr. and Mrs. Gabriel Uhrich for a few weeks.  They bought a farm five and
one-half miles southwest of Roscoe.  They lived there until 1932 when they
retired and moved into Roscoe.

The children born in Russia were, Helen (Meier) born in 1888, living in the
Colonial Manor in Ipswich, S.D.; Martha (Mardian) born in 1891, died in 1982;
Paul Jr., born 1894, died 1980; Stanis, born 1897, died in 1983; Agatha, born
1899, died 1973; Katherine (Heintz), born in 1900, died 1979; Regina (Streifel)
born in 1902, lives in Roscoe and Johanna (Niederoski), born 1904 and lives in
St. Paul, Minn.

Two daughters were born in Roscoe.  They were Elizabeth (Sahli), born in 1909
and lives in Gladstone, Ore. and Margaret (Olson) born in 1910 and lives in St.
Paul, Minn.

They were members of St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.  Paul passed away
February 19, 1941 and Caroline passed away September 19, 1941, both at age 75.
They are buried at Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

PAUL P. AND LUDWINA SCHMIDT

Paul P. Schmidt, Jr. was born in Russia in 1894 and came to the United States
and the Roscoe area with his family February 7, 1907 when he was 13 years old.
He was the son of Paul and Caroline Doness Schmidt.

Paul grew up on the farm and worked with his father until he bought his own
farm.  He married Ludwina Grosze in Hague, N.D. in 1919.  They continued to live
on the farm until 1947.  Paul was a county assessor for many years, served on
the school board for several years and was also treasurer of St. Thomas Catholic
Church in Roscoe.

They have eight children, Anton, who lives in Seattle, Wash. where he is a
foreman for a construction company.  His wife, Lorraine, works at the Seattle
First National Bank.

Katherine is married to Wayne Kies, lives in Coeur D Alene, Idaho and has four
children.  Wayne works for Kaiser Refrigeration and Katherine is a supervisor
for North Shore Convention Bus.

Regina is married to Ray Combs and resides in Seattle where both are retired.
They have two children, Theresa, married to Chuck Green, lives in Seattle and
has two children.  Theresa is a secretary for a union firm and Chuck is
self-employed.  Elizabeth is married to Nort Hall who is Sales Manager for
Freeway Chevrolet in Spokane, Wash.  They have three children and live in Coeur
D Alene, Idaho.

Frank also lives in Coeur D Alene, where he works for Kaiser in the Sanitation
Maintenance Department.  His wife Johanna teaches school and they have six
children.

Adam and his wife Lorraine live in Stonewood, Wash. Adam works in Seattle for
O.K. Tires as a lineman. Adam has five children and Lorraine has four.

John resides in Portland, Ore. and works for Boeing as a machinist.  He has
three children.

Paul and Ludwina moved to Aberdeen in 1947 where he was employed by the city
for 10 years.  In 1966 they moved to Coeur D Alene, Idaho.  Ludwina died October
29, 1976 and Paul January 26, 1981.  They are both resting in St. Thomas
Cemetery in Coeur D Alene.

STANIS AND KATHERINE SCHMIDT

        Back row: Bernie, Joe, Lou, and Paul; middle row: Sr.  Clarice (Helen),
        Stanis, Katherine, and Martin; front row: Dolores, and Leo Schmidt.

Stanis Schmidt was born in Odessa, Russia in 1897 and came to the United States
with his parents, Paul and Caroline Daness Schmidt in 1907 and homesteaded
south of Roscoe where the family farmed for many years.  Stanis is one of 15
children of whom six are still living, Regina Striefel in Roscoe; Helen Meier
in Ipswich; Johanna and Margaret in St. Paul, Minn. and Elizabeth in Portland,
Ore.

Katherine Heintzman was also born in Odessa, Russia in 1900.  She was 11 months
old when her parents, Martin and Claudia Goetz Heintzman came to the United
States.  They first settled near Onaka and later farmed near Roscoe, after
which they moved to Aberdeen where they spent many years.  Katherine was the
oldest of 12 children.  Martin was a brother to Ben Heintzman, a long time
resident of Roscoe.

Katherine and Stanis were married in 1921 at Roscoe in St. Thomas Church.  They
spent 40 years in farming, then retired to Roscoe in 1960 where Stanis worked in
construction and Katherine was an active member in the Altar Society and the
Quilting Bee.  She was noted for her baking, cooking and needlework.

Katherine died in 1976 after a short illness.  Stanis moved to Bowdle where he
lived with his son-in-law Steve Heilman and granddaughters, Vicki and Doris.  He
died January 5, 1983.  Stanis and Katherine are buried in Morningside Cemetery
at Roscoe.

Joe and Viola (Weber) live in Aberdeen and own an antique-second hand store.
Paul and Lena (Lingor) moved to Yakima, Wash. after farming a few years with
Paul's father.  They owned a radiator shop in Yakima until 1981 and now both
Paul and Lena are enjoying a more active life of traveling and fishing.

Lou and Emil (Kilber) are retired and living in Prescott, Ariz.  Lou was
manager of the White Drug Cafe in Aberdeen for many years.  She is now very
active in her parish as organist, teacher and pastoral minister.  Emil is a
great gardener and excels in woodworking.

Martin and Vera also live in Yakima, Wash., just three blocks from Paul and
Lena, so they see each other often.

Martin is working with Pepsi Colo Company and is looking forward to retiring
from there in a few years. Then he hopes to pursue his hobby in carpentry.
Vera is busy with the grandchildren.

Bernie and Edward Dunn live in Enumclaw, Wash. where Bernie is employed with
Super Valu Company and Ed is a loan officer in the local bank.  Before moving
to Enumclaw, Bernie and Ed were managers of the Montgomery Store in Prosser,
Wash.

Leo and Sally (Reinhold) moved to Tempe, Ariz. two years ago to be closer to
the college that their children are attending.  Leo is still in barbering and
was fortunate to get his shop in Tempe.  Sally is employed in the local library
several days a week.  Prior to moving to Arizona, Leo and Sally were in
Coldwater, Mich. and Chicago.  They are enjoying the warm weather in the south.

Dolores worked in the banks in Aberdeen and Great Falls, Mont. before marrying
Steve Heilman of Bowdle. They farmed several years and purchased the Amoco
Station in Bowdle which Steve is still managing.  Dolores died in February,
1980 after a lingering illness of cancer at 42 years of age.  Helen, Sister
Clarice, is a member of the Benedictine community of Mother of God Priory,
Watertown, S. D. Currently she is working as pastoral associate at St. Mary's
in Plymouth, Minn.

JACOB AND EVA SCHMITT

        Back row: John, Christ, Jacob; front row: Fred and Mary Schmitt.

Jacob Schmitt, Sr. was born June 21, 1860, in Bergdorf, South Russia.  He
married Eva Doyle in 1888 and they came to the United States and settled in
McPherson County.  Several moves were made before they settled on a farm in
Sangamon Township in 1899, about seven and one-half miles northwest of Roscoe
where they lived the rest of their lives.

They had nine children, Eva was born in January 1890 and died in 1898.

Fred was born February 9, 1892 and died February 17, 1964.

Christ was born in June 1896 and died in October, 1911.

Jacob, Jr. was born December 27, 1895 and died January 1, 1968.

John was born May 3, 1897 and died December 23, 1944.

Mary was born March 3, 1900 and died in November of 1919.

David was born October 28, 1902, and died October 6, 1973.

Margaret was born January 1, 1904, and lives in Freeman, Neb.

Edward was born May 2, 1906 and died March 31, 1961.

They were members of the Hoffnung (Hope) Lutheran Church north of Roscoe.  Eva
Schmitt died July 4, 1909 and was the first person to be buried in their church
cemetery.  Jacob died March 3, 1943 and is also buried in the country church
cemetery.

FRED AND LYDIA SCHMITT

Fred Schmitt was born near Eureka, S.D. February 9, 1892, to Jacob and Eva
Doyle Schmitt.  In 1899, he moved with his parents to a farm in Sangamon
Township, about seven and one-half miles northwest of Roscoe.  He served in the
United States Army during WW I from May, 1918, to June, 1919.  He was stationed
in France for ten months of this time.  December 14, 1920 he married Lydia
Himmerich In the Hoffnung (Hope) Lutheran Church north of Roscoe.  Lydia was the
daughter of John and Elizabeth Nehlich Himmerich and she was born in a two-room
sod house northwest of Roscoe October 12, 1900.  They settled on a farm two
miles northwest of Roscoe and lived there until 1926 when they moved to Cloyd
Valley Township.

        Fred and Lydia Schmitt

        Back row: Alvin, Leontina, Arthur; front row: Alton, Lydia, and Marvin
        Schmitt.

They have five children, Leontina, born January 21, 1922, married to Warren
Whitbeck, has three children and lives in Billings, Mont.

Arthur was born September 7, 1924 and lives in Roscoe.

Alvin was born January 1, 1926, married Jean Sahli, has one son and lives in
Roscoe.

Marvin was born January 28, 1929, married Joan Richards, has three sons and
lives in Billings.

Alton was born May 19, 1931, married Georgia Bond, has two children and lives
in Holdrege, Neb.

Fred was a member of the American Legion and they are members of St. John's
Lutheran Church in Hosmer. Fred died February 17, 1964 and is. buried in Bethany
Cemetery in Hosmer.

Lydia remained on the farm with her son, Arthur, until 1979, when they moved
into Roscoe, where they live at the present time.

ALVIN AND JEAN SCHMITT

Alvin E. Schmitt is the son of Fred and Lydia Himmerich Schmitt who were born,
raised and lived in the Edmunds County area all of their lives.  Jean Sahli
Schmitt is the daughter of Kasper and Julia Gabriel Sahli, who were also born
in Edmunds County and lived most of their lives here.  Alvin and Jean are both
graduates of Roscoe High School.

Alvin and Jean were married October 1, 1963 in Roscoe.  To this union one son,
Tim Alan, was born December 22, 1964.  Tim will be a senior in the Roscoe High
School during the 1982-83 school year.

        Tim, Jean, and Alvin Schmitt

Alvin served two years in the Army f rom January 1945 to December 1946.  His
service took him to the South Pacific where he spent thirteen and one-half
months on Tinian Island.  After his return home, Alvin held various jobs over
the years until March 14, 1960, when he became Agent for the Standard Oil Co.
in Roscoe. January 29, 1976 Standard changed from Agent to Jobber system, so
Alvin became a Jobber for Standard, the position he still holds.  September 1,
1978 he was appointed Veterans Service Officer for Edmunds County and is still
serving in this position.

He has been a member of many organizations in this community, including the
Roscoe Volunteer Fire Department for more than 20 years, having served as the
fire chief for one year, was Commander of the American Legion for two years,
and served on the town board for two years.  Jean worked in her father's and
brother's grocery store, and also as a cook and waitress at the Roscoe school
for several years.  The last few years she has been a housewife and answered the
telephone for Alvin's Standard business.  She has also been a member of
different organizations and held offices.

The Schmitts want to wish Roscoe a very Happy Anniversary and hope it will be a
very fine town for the next 100 years!

FRED AND SADIE SCHNEIDER (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Her father, J. S. Penfield, came to Dakota from Madison, Wis. in August, 1884.
He filed on a homestead, built a sod house, went back to Wisconsin and married
Gertrude Young in the spring of 1885. They returned to the homestead in South
Dakota.

There were seven Penfield children.  Sadie married Fred Schneider in 1906 and
they moved to Washington in 1938.

JOHN SCHNEIDER (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

John Schneider, brother of P.A. Schneider, born in 1878, moved to Roscoe in
1900 and lived in the Heinrich house.  After two years he homesteaded to the
present Norbert Fuhrmann place.  John and his brother Pete followed the
frontier to Tolstoy and started a meat market in 1907 which they ran for five
years.  In 1913 he moved to a farm north of Beebe where he lived until 1942
when he moved to Roscoe and died in 1961.

PHILIP ALBERT (P.A.) SCHNEIDER

        P. A. Schneider

        Rena Schneider

P. A. Schneider was born to Philip and Katherine Schneider near Freeman in
1895.  During his early years this family moved to Webster, S.D. In 1900 his
family packed up their belongings into a covered wagon and drove the stock to
Roscoe where they took up their residence in the Peter Heinrich house.  In 1903
this family moved 14 miles southwest of Roscoe and settled on a homestead which
is known as the Roy Goetz farm, later on the Joe Fuhrman farm and to the Henry
Heiser farm.  In 1907 they moved to Tolstoy where he hauled water for the
railroad from Watertown to Akaska.

In 1916 Phil moved to Beebe where he met and married Rena Krueger, who was born
in 1900, at Fostoria, Iowa.  They had two sons, Chet born in 1919 and Orval,
born in 1923.

In 1925 Phil built a garage and did some repair work. A year later, he started
a grocery store and cream station.  A kerosene stove exploded in 1928 and burned
all the buildings they had built.  Later that year they purchased the
"Woodman's Hall", where Lawrence Welk had played.  It was remodeled into a
store with living quarters in the back.  It was changed into a night club in
1935 until 1942.  During this time, Rena helped run the store while Phil did
custom work, baled hay and hauled gravel.

In 1938, Phil purchased and remodeled the former Farmers State Bank building in
Roscoe into a tavern which they operated for six months then sold it to Pete
Goetz. in the fall of 1940, he started hauling gravel with the help of his two
sons.

While Orval was in the Army, 1942, Phil and Rena moved all the buildings from
Beebe, except the garage, to Roscoe.  They operated the "Schneider's Place"
which sold beer and lunches for many years until it was destroyed by fire and
torn down.  It stood on the corner of Jerome Fischers Sales lot.  In 1945, the
hotel was purchased from Helen Cozad, that was built by Theodore Muenz, which
Rena operated as "Schneider's Hotel" along with the bus depot until the time of
her death in 1982.

Phil continued working with his sons, Chet and Orval, operating the trucking
business known as "Schneider's Trucks" until his death in 1963.

CHESTER P. AND CLAUDIA SCHNEIDER

Chester Philip Schneider was born at Beebe, S.D. May 21, 1919, to P. A. (Phil)
and Rena Krueger Schneider.  He attended country school one mile south of Beebe,
grades one through eight.  It was when he was in the sixth grade that he
compiled his own oral essay entitled, "Edmunds County, First Settled in 1882 by
Drew" copy can be found on page 11 of the Roscoe Diamond Jubilee Book.  He won
at local, county, and state level competition, representing some worthy prizes,
namely a 1923 Motel T Touring car from his father, which in 1934, he chopped
off the rear seat and erected a small box on the back, thus transporting
rubbish within Beebe and operated a small delivery service to the surrounding
Beebe community.  This was the beginning of Chet's 30 years of trucking.

Some of his grade school teachers were Mrs. Dave Jeschke and Mrs. Hilda Schurr.
He graduated from Ipswich High School in 1937, at which time he purchased a
new 1937 GMC truck from the Gottlieb Schaible Motor Co. in Hosmer, S.D. In
1940, he graduated to his first semi truck and continued to build the
"Schneider Truck Line" to several units.  June 1, 1946, he took his brother
Orval into partnership and on May 15, 1948, his father Phil, became another
partner.

The three continued in partnership until December 1963, when partner Phil
passed away.  At this time Orval went to work for Cenex in Roscoe and Chet
continued by himself again until August 1, 1965.  Chet suffered a stroke
February 26, 1964, thus causing him to sell out to Eugene McDonald of Selby,
S.D. After selling out, it took Chet two years to recuperate and August 1,
1967, he and his wife, Claudia, moved to Beresford, S.D. and served in the
capacity of wardens at a detention home for boys and girls, for three years.  In
1970 they resigned and moved to Denver, Colo. and purchased a self-service gas
station.  They operated this business until 1976 when they sold out and
purchased five acres and a home, 17 miles north of Denver, which they again
sold in 1981 and moved back to Denver and purchased a home at 1780 E. 83rd. Pl.
At this time, Chet went into the Antique Car and Coin business for himself.
He travels a lot, selling and buying antique cards and coins.

He has been a member of the Presbyterian Church since 1940, his Lodge
affiliation is the Masonic Lodge A.F. & A.M. He was raised to a Master Mason
May 21, 1946 and received his 25 year pin May 21, 1971 from Liberty 150 A.F. &
A.M. in Denver.

Claudia L. Aman Schneider was born to Henry and Elizabeth Kallenberger Aman
September 11, 1922, in McPherson County.  She attended grades one through 11 at
the Roscoe Public School.  August 26, 1940 she married Chet at Sundance, Wyo,
and to this union three children were born, Dixie Schneider Enger, who has
three children, Lori, Genna, and Julie.  She is head nurse at Valleyview
Hospital in Thornton, Colo.

Sheila Schneider Brown, who has two children, Camille and Justin, is executive
secretary to Ringwalt Stock Broker in Omaha, Neb.

Rocky Schneider Lindemann, who has two children, Chet and Missy, has been doing
babysitting for the past few years in Denver.

Claudia, after marriage, served as an Avon dealer for about five years, then at
the detention home 'in Beresford, five years in the self-service station in
Denver and after purchasing the five acres development 17 miles north of
Denver, she went to work in 1976 for Saveway Oil in Brighton, Colo. as cashier
for one year.  After that she resigned and went to work in Denver as manager
with three attendants under her, for Pester Oil Co. Claudia has been a member
of the Presbyterian Church since 1940.  She is a very ardent worker and comes
from a family of three brothers and three sisters.

The Chet Schneiders motto is "Start every morning with a smile and get it over
with!"

ORVAL AND JEANNE SCHNEIDER

Orval Schneider, the son of P. A. and Rena Krueger Schneider, was born in
Beebe, S.D. August 10, 1923.  He went to a rural school in the Beebe area and
during his high school years attended Ipswich High where he graduated in 1941.
After graduation Orval joined the Army during World War II from 1942 to 1946.
He took his basic training at Camp Kohler, Sacramento, Calif. and then served
in the Southwest Pacific Theatre which included Australia, New Guinea,
Philippines and Japan.  While in the service his family moved to Roscoe where
they started a trucking business.  After his discharge from the service he
worked with his dad and brother in the Schneider Truck Line.

August 10, 1946 he married Jeanne Hedlin, daughter of George D. and Myrtle
Huemmer Hedlin.  She was born in Chicago, Ill.  August 10, 1926, where she
attended school through the sixth grade when the family of five children moved
to Draper, Wis.  She finished grade school and graduated from Draper High
School in 1944.  Shortly after that Jeanne trained as a depot operator and
worked for the Milwaukee Railroad.  She worked as relief depot operator from
1944 to 1946, working in Roscoe in that capacity.  In the years following her
marriage to Orv, she worked for Bradbury Construction, Goetz Cream Station and
Jo-Ann's Apparel, and at the First State Bank where she is currently employed.

In 1950 Vicki was born, attended school in Roscoe, graduating in 1968, went two
years at Northern State College in Aberdeen and two years at the University of
South Dakota in Vermillion.  After graduation she went to Omaha, Neb. to work
until she moved on to Son Francisco, Calif. where she still resides.  Vicki
married Byron Callas August 9, 1981.

In 1952 Dallas was born and went to school and graduated from Roscoe High
School in 1971.  He attended all of his four years of college at the University
of South Dakota at Vermillion and has made Vermillion his residence where he is
self-employed.

The Schneider Truck Line was in operation until 1965 when it was sold to Eugene
McDonald of Selby.  It was then Orval started working for Farmers Union Oil Co.
where he is still employed.

MR. AND MRS. LUDWIG SCHUMACHER

Ludwig Schumacher was born March 16, 1863 in South Russia to Mr. and Mrs. Adam
Schumacher.  He was married to Christina Heyd.  They came to the United States
in 1898 and settled in the Eureka, S.D. area, later moving to a farm seven and
one-half miles north of Roscoe.  To this union five children were born, Jacob,
John, Katherine (Mrs.  August Hoff), Lydia (Mrs.  Jacob Voegele) and Rudolph.

Mrs. Schumacher died in 1906 and is buried in Hosmer.

        Ludwig and Anna Mary Schumacher

Ludwig was remarried in 1906 to Anna Mary Mutchler Nehlich.  Anna Mary was born
March 16, 1873 to Christ and Margaretha Mutchler in Odessa, South Russia.  She
came to the United States with her family at the age of 12. In 1893 she was
married to Christ Nehlich.  They had three sons and one daughter, Christ, Jr.,
Jake, John and Anna Mary.  Her husband, Christ, died in 1903 and is buried in
Hosmer.

Ludwig and Anna Mary continued to live on the farm north of Roscoe.  To this
union four children were born, Adam, who lives in Roscoe; Rose Buechler of
Bowdle; Hilda Stangle of Java and Philip, who passed away in 1978.  He lived in
Montana and is buried in Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

Ludwig and Anna Mary moved to Roscoe in 1932.

Ludwig died September 19, 1939 at the age of 76.  He is buried in the Hoffnung
(Hope) Lutheran Church Cemetery north of Roscoe.  Anna Mary died June 6, 1955
at the age of 83 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery.

RUDOLPH AND KATIE SCHUMACHER

Rudolph Schumacher was born December 25, 1897 in Gluckstal, Russia to Ludwig
and Christina Heyd Schumacher.  He traveled to America with his family when he
was six months old.  Ludwig died in 1939 and Christina died in 1906.  They are
buried in the Hoffnung (Hope) Lutheran Church Cemetery, north of Roscoe.

Katie (Katherine) Bieber was born September 4, 1902 in the Hosmer area to Jacob
and Katherina Eisenbeisz Bieber.  Her parents were born and married in Russia
and came to America right after they married.  Jacob died in 1939 and is buried
in Bowdle, S.D. and Katherina died in 1947 and is buried in California.

Rudolph and Katie were married March 23, 1924 at St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Bowdle.  They farmed seven miles northwest of Roscoe for eight years and then
moved to his father's farmstead where they lived for 20 years.  They then
retired and moved to Roscoe.  Rudolph did some work for the city and for Roscoe
Grain & Feed. They belong to St. Paul's Lutheran Church at Roscoe where Katie
belongs to the Ladies Aid.

        Rudolph and Katie Schumacher, and Lorine and Emanuel Sauer

Two daughters were born to this union, Lorine and Delores.

Lorine was born January 18, 1926, married Emanuel Sauer and they had three
children, Stan, Joyce, and Myra Jean who died at age three.  They lived in
Keldron, S.D. for 30 years and are now retired in Morristown, S. D.

Delores was born December 10, 1933 and died when she was 10 days old.  She is
also buried in the Hoffnung Cemetery.

ADAM AND MARTHA SCHUMACHER

Adam Schumacher was born in a sod house north of Roscoe August 19, 1907, to
Ludwig and Mary Mutchler Schumacher.  Martha Dobbert was born in the Tolstoy,
S.D. area June 11, 1912, to Christ and Margaret Geist Dabbert.  Her parents
were born in South Russia and came to the United States in 1911.

Adam and Martha were married in 1929.  It was a tough time to get married, with
drought, grasshoppers, low prices--they had them all.  They lived on a farm
seven and one-half miles north of Roscoe from 1929 until 1969 when they moved
to Roscoe where they live at the present time.  At that time, there were no
handouts. There was WPA, a person had to work for a few dollars a day, not per
hour.  When seed or feed was needed, they had to borrow money or make a loan and
pay it back with interest, but as times got better and the rain came, they did
get out on top again.

Adam and Martha were blessed with a son, Arnold, and a daughter, Verdella.
They were their pride and joy and still are, for which they give thanks to God.
Arnold, married Genny Menzia and lives in Rapid City, S.D. They have one
daughter, Joan Rae.  Verdella married Alvin Stahlecker and they also live in
Rapid City.  They have four children, Eric, Darrell, Deanne and Monte.

They are members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.  They live in Roscoe
now and love it as they never did care to live in a big city.  Adam is working
with people in town to keep himself busy and Martha has her work at home like
any other homemaker and they are satisfied.  They think Roscoe is a great place
to live.

MR. AND MRS. PETER SCHUMACHER

Mr. and Mrs. Peter Schumacher brought their family to Americo and the Roscoe
area from South Russia in 1911. They lived north of Roscoe until 1921 when they
moved into Hosmer.  In 1943 they made their home in Roscoe with their daughter
and son-in-law, Mr. and Mrs. August Schumacher.

Mr. Schumacher died in June, 1944 and Mrs. Schumacher in January 1951.  They
are both buried in Morningside Cemetery.

JACOB P. AND CHRISTINA SCHUMACHER

        Jacob P. and Gwen Schumacher

Jacob P. Schumacher was born February 21, 1891 in Russia.  He came to this
country in 1911 and settled near Roscoe.  He married Christina Heupel February
19, 1918 in a country church near Hosmer, S.D. Christina was born March 16,
1896 in Kassel, S. Russia.  She came to the United States in 1899 with her
parents and they settled near Leola, S.D.

They had three sons, Reinhold, born August 15, 1918, and passed away March 28,
1952.  He is buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

Alvin, born October 16, 1921, lives in Roscoe.

Milbert, born November 21, 1924, passed away April 19, 1972 and is buried in
Morningside Cemetery.

Christina Schumacher passed away July 15, 1946 and is buried in the Hoffnung
(Hope) Lutheran Church Cemetery north of Roscoe.

In October of 1951, Jacob came to live with his son, Alvin and family.  He
passed away in September of 1966 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery.

ALVIN AND EVELINA SCHUMACHER

        Back row: Gwen, Keith, and Mark; front row: Evelina and Alvin
        Schumacher.

        Back row: Terry, Jim, and Tommy; front row: Martha and Curtis
        Schumacher.

Alvin Schumacher was born October 16, 1921 at Roscoe, the son of Jacob P. and
Christina Heupel Schumacher.  He grew up on a farm north of Roscoe where he
attended the country school near his home. He graduated from Hosmer High School
and Northern State College in Aberdeen.  He taught in country schools for 12
years and was also a well driller.

Evelina Beck was born January 28, 1930 at Java, S.D. to John and Anna Laib
Beck.  She attended country schools in the Java and Roscoe areas and Ipswich
Public School.

Alvin and Evie were married June 12, 1949 at St. Paul's Lutheran Church in
Roscoe.

They have four children, Curtis, born June 21, 1951 and graduated from Roscoe
High School in 1969.  He married Martha Allen from Columbus, Ga. and entered
the Army in June of 1971.  He is stationed at Ft. Benning, Ga.

Mark, born July 17, 1957, a graduate of Roscoe High School and Northern State
College, is teaching in Java, S.D.

Keith, born November 2, 1961, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1980 and is
a student at NSC.

Gwendolyn, born June 15, 1964, graduated from Roscoe High School in 1982 and is
a student at Stewart's School of Hairstyling.

Alvin is doing some grain farming, owner of Schumacher TV and Electric and is a
school bus driver. Evie is a housewife and custodian at St. Paul's Lutheran
Church.

MILBERT AND EDNA SCHUMACHER

        Back row: Marlys and Ronald; front row: Edna and Milbert Schumacher.

Milbert Schumacher was born November 21, 1924 to Jacob and Christina Heupel
Schumacher, in rural Roscoe where he grew up and attended the country school
near his home.  He attended Hosmer High School. He was in partnership with his
brothers, Alvin and Reinhold, when they owned and operated               the
Milwaukee Bar in Roscoe from 1946 to early 1948.

Edna Geist was born in Roscoe July 15, 1931 to Leopold and Pauline Aman Geist.
She grew up in the Roscoe area, attending schools in Roscoe, Loyalton and a
country school near Roscoe.

Milbert and Edna were married November 14, 1948 at St. Paul's Lutheran Church.
They made their first home in Roscoe where Milbert was doing some trucking and
working for the Milwaukee Railroad.  They moved to Aberdeen, S.D. for about a
year where he worked in a gas station.  In 1951, they moved to his family's farm
north of Roscoe, where they lived for five years before moving back to Roscoe
in 1956.  Milbert was employed at Spitzer's Implement until the fall of 1971
when ill health forced him to retire.

They had two children, Marlys, who married Lee Vold, has two daughters and
lives in Roscoe; and Ronald, who is employed by Pickard Bee Farms and also
lives in Roscoe.

Milbert passed away April 19, 1972 and is buried in Morningside Cemetery.

Edna was employed at the Roscoe school from 1966 to 1980.  She is a member of
St. Paul's Lutheran Church, where she is a member of Ladies Aid.

SOLOMON AND ROSINA SCHUMACHER (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

Solomon Schumacher was born to Adam Schumacher and Elizabeth Fischer Schumacher
August 1, 1860 in Gluckstal, South Russia.  He married Rosina Bieber in 1882
and came to the Hosmer area where they farmed for 22 years.

Rosina was born in 1861, also in Gluckstal, to Gotlieb and Christina Bieber.
The couple had 12 children, Jacob, John, Christ, Fred, August, Edward, Arthur,
Rosina, Mary, Katherina, Martha and Caroline.

After the couple retired in Roscoe in 1920, Mrs. Schumacher died in 1941 and
Mr. Schumacher in 1944.

CHRIST AND MARGARETHA SCHUMACHER

Worthy of mention as a pioneer of the Roscoe territory is the name of Christian
Schumacher.  A son of Solomon Schumacher and his wife Rosina, nee Bieber, he was
born in South Russia March 2, 1887.  At the age of 14 he accompanied his
parents to America and settled with them on a homestead about six miles east of
Hosmer. In November 1909, he married Margaretha Lammle and settled on a farm
nine miles north of Roscoe.

Margaretha Lammle Schumacher was born January 28, 1889, in South Russia.  At
the age of four, she accompanied her parents to America and settled with them
on a homestead 16 miles west of Tripp, S.D. Later she met and married Christian
Schumacher.

After living on their farm seven years, the Schumachers moved to the town of
Roscoe.  Here Mr. Schumacher worked with his brother, Jacob, in the blacksmith
shop for seven years.  His brother-in-law, Fred Guthmiller, bought Jacob
Schumacher's share in the business and in 1920 the two then mode up the
business of the Schumacher-Guthmiller Blacksmith. This partnership was
dissolved in 1925, after which each operated his own blacksmith.

Operating his own smith, the first location of this business was south of
Highway 12 where Schmitt's (Alvin) property now lies.  In the fall of 1925, he
moved his blacksmith to the north side where he stayed until he sold his
business in 1945 to John Meier and retired due to ill health.

Mr. Schumacher was active in the city during his lifetime.  He acted as
alderman for his ward for many years.  He was also a member of the Volunteer
Fire Department.

The Schumachers joined St. Paul's Evangelical Lutheran Church in Roscoe in
1917.  Here he served as an elder until his death October 31, 1950.

Here was a man whose destiny led him to the forge and the anvil and this some
destiny caused on early death.

Mrs. Schumacher (Margaretha) continued to live in their house until 1969, after
which she became a resident of the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home and at one time
was a resident for one year at the Colonial Manor at Ipswich, but until her
last illness, she resided again in Hosmer.

Mrs. Schumacher continued her membership at St. Paul's Evangelical Lutheran
Church at Roscoe.  She passed away at the Ipswich Community Hospital March 31,
1976.

        Adelida and Rosie Schumacher

The Schumachers had two daughters, Mrs. Trougott (Rose) Wiedenmeyer of Rapid
City, who has one son, Harry.  He and his wife Linda and son Russell live in
Romeo, Mich.  The other daughter is Mrs. Harold (Adelida) Peterson of Aberdeen,
S.D. The Petersons have three children, Harlow and Timothy of Aberdeen and
Melody, (Mrs.  Lance Bonen) of Hosmer.  Harlow and his wife Marie have two
children, Rebecca Ann and Robert Harold.

AUGUST AND KATHRINA SCHUMACHER (Excerpt from Diamond Jubilee Book)

August Schumacher was born in South Russia in 1895 and came to America with his
parents, Mr. and Mrs. Solomon Schumacher in 1898.  They homesteaded northwest
of Roscoe.  In 1911, he came to Roscoe and went into the livery and well
drilling business with his two brothers, John and Jake.  Mr. Schumacher
purchased his own garage in 1918 and was joined by his brother, Jake, in 1920.

He was employed by the Standard Oil Company from 1924 until 1960.  He married
Kathrina Schumacher in 1919 and they have three children, Harold, Mrs. Jim
(Ida) Palmer and Mrs. Ray (Alvina) Robbins.  Mrs. Schumacher died in 1964 and
Mr. Schumacher died in 1974.  Both are buried in the Morningside Cemetery.

EDWARD AND ROSE SCHUMACHER

        Rose, Edward and Eileen Schumacher

Edward Schumacher was born May 13, 1903 on a farm east of Hosmer, S.D., to
Solomon and Rosina Bieber Schumacher.  He married Rose Wiedenmeyer October 7,
1923 in Roscoe.  Rose was born in South Russia October 23, 1902 to August and
Christina Schuler Wiedenmeyer.  She came to the United States with her parents
when she was four years old, settling in the Roscoe area.  She attended the
Roscoe school.  Ed was well known in the area, having worked for the city of
Roscoe as the city marshall for 32 years.  He enjoyed being a member of the
Roscoe City Band for many years.

Their daughter, Eileen, was born May 4, 1928.  She attended the Roscoe school,
graduating from high school in 1947.  She served for many years as the organist
in her church.  Eileen is a resident at the Hosmer Senior Citizens Home.

The family belonged to St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe where Rose was a
charter member of the Ladies Aid.

Ed passed away October 27, 1974 and Rose passed away September 16, 1976.  They
were both laid to rest in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

MARTIN AND ANNA SCHURR, SR.

Martin Schurr, Sr. was born in Albertshousen, Germany May 25, 1868.  He came to
the United States in 1892.  His first stop was in Chicago.  Here he met Anna
Hofelt who worked at the same place.  She had came to the United States in
1892.  She was born in Turek, Poland January 12, 1872.

They were married October 23, 1893, in Chicago and this is where their first
child, Mary, was born.  In 1896, they moved to Neilsville, Wis. where William
was born and the family engaged in farming.  Six years later the family moved
again, this time to Handcock, Wis.  This was the place Robert was born.

The farming changed to potato farming and times were hard.  One year potatoes
were 25 cents a bushel in the fall when they were dug, but the neighbors told
him to keep them until spring when the price should be better.  The potatoes
were stored in a potato cellar and in the spring the price was much less with
the potatoes finally being sold to a starch factory for six cents a bushel.

In 1906 it was again decided to seek better things and to move to South Dakota.
Martin would go first to set up a place to live.  He put all their possessions
in a railroad immigrant car and he arrived at Ipswich March 17, 1906, in a
blizzard and could not unload the car until better weather settled in.  He
moved his possessions to a farm in Montpelier Township in Edmunds County.  Two
weeks later, Anna arrived with the family, Mary, William and Robert, who was
two years old at this time. In 1911 they moved to the Link farm in Bryant
Township. In 1918 another move was again undertaken, when Martin, Sr. started
and built a new farm in North Bryant Township, the northwest one-fourth of
34-124-70, this being the farm where Martin, Jr. and Margaret were born.
Farming was continued here and the family grew up, attending the country
schools.  In 1937 Martin, Sr. met his death and a few years later Anna moved to
Roscoe where she lived until her death in 1963.  The children were William,
married and farmed south of Bowdle, S.D.; Mary, married to Albert Beyers,
farming south of Roscoe; Robert, married and farmed northeast of Roscoe;
Martin, married and farmed south of Beebe; Margaret, married now Mrs. Cooper -
Teeter living in Corvallis, Ore.

ROBERT AND HILDA SCHURR

Robert Schurr was born in Handcock, Wis.  August 29, 1903, to Martin and Anna
Hofelt Schurr.  He received his education in the Edmunds County country schools,
which seemed to move every time people moved from one section to another.
There were a couple of times when due to severe blizzards, none of the school
children could return home and had to spend the whole night in the school.
This included Robert and Bill and the school teacher.

Robert farmed with his father until his marriage to Hilda Hauffe June 4, 1929
at Leola, S.D. She, at the time, was a public school teacher in the area.  She
taught in the public school system full time, later teaching part-time until
1948.

After the marriage, they moved to a farm five miles north of Beebe, S.D. The
farm was known as the George Finley place.  They lived on this farm until 1931,
at which time Robert and Hilda moved back to the home farm with Robert's
parents, farming with his dad until the death of his father in 1937.

Crops in the 1930s were not good.  Robert was running a threshing rig and hired
help was a problem in those years.  There was an abundance of help coming
through on the freight trains in those days, but most were inexperienced and
could not stand the strenuous work. One of the men he had hired drove to the
yard from the threshing machine with part of a load of oats to feed the horses.
He drove to the born, threw down the reins which scared the horses and they
took off, and he was holding on to the sides of the wagon box.  They ran around
the yard while the others tried to catch them till they ran into the water
tank.  Some of the good help that was hired was Red Alcorn, Bob Light, Hugo
McManus and the farmers who wanted their crops threshed.

Bobby and Arnold started their education by going to the country school
one-half mile west of the home place.  In 1945 Robert purchased the Hartel and
Henning property in Roscoe and they also purchased the residence that belonged
to W. S. Swan.

Robert and his family moved to Roscoe and a tenant was put on the home farm,
with Robert continuing farming with the boys.  Robert lives in the same
residence and works with Arnold.  Arnold lives on the home farm, and is
married, has a family of two girls and two boys.

Robert, Jr. is a pastor at Red Lake Falls, Minn. and is still single; Elaine
(Mrs.  Monte Brekke) lives in Missoula, Mont. and has two boys and one girl and
Norman is married and lives in Los Angeles, Calif. and has a family of two
girls.

Hilda has been active in 4-H, Eastern Star, Extension Club, Senior Citizens and
Priscilla Club activities and Robert has been an active member of the Masonic
Lodge for 54 years and is still active at the present time.

JACOB AND HULDA SEMMLER

Jacob Semmler was born at Delmont, S.D. August 26, 1894, to Michial and
Caroline Baier Semmler.  He married Hulda Maas at Parkston, S.D. November 15,
1914.  Hulda Maas Semmler was born at Parkston October 26, 1895.  She was the
daughter of August and Wilhelmina Treibwaser Maas.  The Michial Semmlers came
to the United States from Russia about 1877, born of German Russian parents.
The Maas's came from Alt Arzis, Germany in 1876.  All were married in the United
States and their children were also born here.

Jacob and Hulda Semmler came to the Roscoe area in February 1919.  They settled
on a farm seven miles south of Roscoe.  They lived on this farm until the fall
of 1923, when they returned to a farm in the Delmont area.

In the fall of 1926, they moved to Ree Heights, S.D. where Jacob had a shoe and
harness repair shop. In 1930, the drought started, and seems everyone was
moving, looking for some place to make a living. Hearing of the big crop in the
Roscoe area in 1932, they again loaded their few belongings and moved back to
Roscoe in the fall of 1933 and settled on a farm two and one-half miles south
of town.  Due to crop failures here and the very hard times, they decided to
return to their roots.  In the fall of 1936, they returned to Parkston and
settled on a farm there.  In the summer of 1948, Jacob lost his right leg to an
undisclosed disease.  They then retired in Parkston and he went into the
business of rug making.  He also made toy farm buildings and furniture. Jacob
died December 10, 1959 and is buried in a little country cemetery near
Parkston.  Hulda is still living in her home in Parkston and at age 86 is
enjoying good health.  The Semmlers were of the Lutheran faith.

The couple had 12 children.  They are Irene Winter, retired and living in
Parkston; Eugene, working for Lakeside Dairy for 30 years in Sioux Falls, S.D.;
Bernice Jeschke, living on a farm near Roscoe; Raymond, deceased; Lucille
Ketterling, Lisbon, N.D. and cares for foster children; Mildred Sandmeier,
Bowdle, S.D., works for Ozzie's Grocery Store; Olga Hayen, Mitchell, S.D., a
seamstress; Anita Hayen, Sioux Falls, working for Bell Telephone; Marie Jensen,
Omaha, Neb., in the catering business; Vera Jensen, Denver, Colo., husband
works for the U. S. Government Foreign Exchange; Loretta Nelson, Sioux Falls,
S.D. has a collecting agency and David, a salesman in Parkston.

ANDREW AND REGINA SENGER

In February, 1952, Andrew and Regina Dietz Senger moved from Milwaukee, Wis. to
a farm northwest of Roscoe.  There they farmed and raised four children until
their retirement in the fall of 1978.  They then moved back to their birthplace
of Strasburg, N.D., selling the family farm to a neighbor, Mark Preszler.

The family were members of St. Thomas Catholic Church and their children all
attended Roscoe Public School.

Ken graduated from Northern State College in Aberdeen and is employed by the
South Dakota Department of Health in Pierre.  He is married to Dawn Kilber, also
of Roscoe, and they have two sons, Jim, 12, and Eric, eight.

Geraldine also graduated from Northern State College and is employed as a
substitute teacher in the Aberdeen School System.  She married Pius Heier from
Hosmer and they are presently living north of Aberdeen. They have two children,
Andrew, six years, and Carson, three.

Deborah graduated from Wahpeton, N.D. School of Nursing and is on L.P.N. in
Beulah, N.D. She married Ron Barondeau, from Onaka, and they, along with their
daughter Nicole, three, reside in Beulah.

Francine graduated from Northern State College in May of 1982 and is currently
living in Aberdeen where she is employed with Fairway Grocery.

        Back row: Francine, Geraldine, Kenneth, and Deborah; front row: Regina
        and Andrew Senger.

OREN AND MARGARET SOWARD

Oren Soward was born in 1867 in Ohio.  He married Margaret Ney, who was born in
El Dorado, Iowa In 1875.  They came to South Dakota in 1907 and settled seven
and one-half miles south and one-half mile east of Ipswich, then to the
Loyalton area in 1909.

Oren died in Faulkton in 1941 and Margaret in 1969. Both are buried in Ipswich.

Three children were born to this couple, Dave was born in 1909 in El Dorado,
Iowa.  He was married to Mary Allbee in Loyalton and lived on the farm south of
there for many years.  They ran the grocery and hardware store in Loyalton
until the big fire that burned down the store and pool hall.  They later moved
to Hot Springs, S.D. where they retired.  Dave and Mary have four children,
Helen who married Esman Hindi and lives in Longmont, Colo.  They had three
sons, Beau, Reg and Jeff, who died in infancy and are buried in Ipswich;
Marlene (McGovern) now lives In Rapid City and has four children, Kim, Cindy,
Keith and Michelle, all home. Oren, Jr., married Lindy Meiletz at Milbank and
lives in Sioux Falls, S.D. They have four children, Oren, Paula, Jason and
Tiffany, all at home.  Daphne (Conger) is a graduate of Roscoe High School and
lives in Spearfish, S.D. where she works as office manager in the hospital
there.

Rose was married to Lester Duvall in Faulkton but lived in the Ipswich area on
a farm until they purchased the General Store in Loyalton.  After selling the
store, they moved to North Bend, Ore. where they operated a lumberyard until
retirement.  They have three children, Marilyn, Roger and Dwayne.  All are
married.

Rev. John, who married Jerry Long in Ohio, is now retired and living in
Spencer, Wis.  They had no children.

CHRISTINA BIEBER SPITZER

Christina Bieber was born August 19, 1856 in Gluckstal, South Russia, the
daughter of Gottlieb and Christina Bieber.  In October 1881, she was married to
Daniel Spitzer and they came to America May 4, 1892, settling northeast of
Roscoe in McPherson County.  They had only seven dollars left, but their
kindhearted neighbors helped them get started farming.

Mr. Spitzer died February 25, 1918.  Two of their four children died prior to
his death.

Mrs. Spitzer moved to Roscoe to be with her children, John Spitzer and
Christina Lammle.  She had two sisters, Mrs. Rosina Schumacher and Mrs.
Katherine Rohrbach who also spent their later years in Roscoe.  Mrs. Spitzer
died at the home of her daughter, Mrs. John Lammle, April 28, 1938, and is
buried in the Roscoe cemetery.

Grandma Spitzer was well known for all her sterling qualities, a kind and
helpful neighbor and friend.  Being a mid-wife and practical nurse, and there
being no doctor in Roscoe in earlier years, she helped out in many ways.

JOHN AND ROSINA SPITZER

John Spitzer was born in McPherson County, 18 miles northeast of Roscoe
December 14, 1892 to Daniel and Christina Spitzer.  Mr. Spitzer, as a young
boy, grew up in pioneer farming, spent many of his early years walking behind a
hand plow and told his family of the many hardships they had to endure, like
walking a distance of 25 miles pulling a hand sled to get groceries in all
kinds of weather, as there were no cars.  In 1908, at the age of 16, he was
fortunate enough to have his own "bike", so he took it upon himself to set out
for Aberdeen and back in one day.  This was his first trip ever to the Hub City!

One of his hobbies as a young man was photography. He developed and printed his
own pictures, some of which the family still treasures.

October 22, 1916 he married Rosina Kapp of Eureka, S.D. Rosina Kapp was born in
Gluckstal, South Russia February 6, 1895.  She came to the United States and
South Dakota in 1901 with her parents, settling in the Eureka area.  They lived
on the farm for three more years before moving to Roscoe in 1920, where Mr.
Spitzer went into the garage business in partnership with Ben Mayer.  The
Yellowstone Garage was purchased from Jake Schumacher, located where the
present site of the Cenex building is.  They had the dealership in Model T
Fords, Fordson tractors and Durant cars, also bulk oil and gas delivery.  In
1925, Mr. Mayer sold his share of the business to Mr. Spitzer's brother-in-law,
John Lammle.  They continued handling the Ford agency, Advance Rummley
tractors, and added the Case line of machinery.  In 1927, they cancelled the
Case contract and took on the John Deere Implement line.  In 1928, they had the
top record sales in John Deere D tractors and Van Brunt Drills.  During this
time they also purchased the lots next to the building where the old Jamieson
Hotel use to stand.  This some time, they also purchased the old dance hall
from the Edmunds County Fairgrounds in Roscoe, moved it on to the Jamieson lot
for the implement building.

In 1930, the partners drew lots as to who would take over the Ford line and who
would take the John Deere line.  The John Deere line became Mr. Spitzer's new
business venture, along with a trucking business.  He also continued in farming
some land he owned near by.

During the depression days in the 1930s, Mr. Spitzer was elected to be the
W.P.A. supervisor, which took him away from his own work to help the needy find
work.  Several projects were graveling of roads north and south of Roscoe,
including some county roads, surveying of the land of the present City Park and
the planting of many of the trees, and construction of the present City
Auditorium.  This auditorium was a great asset as it was much used for school
and city activities.

In 1933-34, he served as deputy sheriff of Edmunds County.  He served on the
Roscoe City Council at different times and was mayor of Roscoe from 1950 to
1955.  During his term of mayor, the following city accomplishments were
achieved: City Liquor store put under different management and bookkeeping
system to comply with state standards, City ordinance was completely revised in
1954, the well and pump was purchased from the railroad company for a nominal
fee, disposal system extended, street oiling project, curbing and gutters
installed, the present new street lights installed, ladies rest room built.

In 1947, Mr. Spitzer sold the implement business to his son, Erwin and
son-in-law, Ervin Bader.

After his retirement, he spent much time with his favorite hobbies, hunting and
fishing.

The Spitzers have three daughters and one son, Ann (Mrs.  Joe Prisbe), Adeline
(Mrs.  Ervin Bader), Alma (Mrs.  George Keisner) and Erwin.  They all reside in
Roscoe except Alma who lives in Harlingen, Texas. Mr. Spitzer passed away July
14, 1961 and Mrs. Spitzer passed away January 3, 1971, ten years later. They
are both buried in Morningside Cemetery at Roscoe.

ERWIN AND HELEN SPITZER

        John, Glenn, Patricia, Erwin, and Helen Spitzer

Erwin (Erv) Spitzer was born March 21, 1925, the son of John and Rosina Kapp
Spitzer, in the house he still lives in today in Roscoe.  He grew up here,
attending the Roscoe school, graduating in 1943.  Erv entered the Army in
January 1945, received his basic training at Camp Hood, Texas, served in the
Pacific in the Philippine Islands and Japan and received his discharge in
November 1946.  Erv worked for his father in the John Deere Implement business
while he was growing up and returned to the business upon his return from the
Army.

In 1947, Erv and his brother-in-law, Ervin Bader, bought the Implement business
from his father.

September 7, 1948 Erv married Helen Meier.  She was born in Roscoe March 30,
1928 to Frank and Katherine Mardian Meier, who were both born in Odessa, Russia
and came to the United States in about 1910, settling near Hillsview, S.D.
Helen attended the Roscoe school and graduated in 1946.  She worked in the
local cafe and in Ted's Place until the time of her marriage.  Helen has done
secretarial work and bookkeeping for her husband's business since that time.

They have three children, Glenn, born March 23, 195 1, graduated in 1969,
married Dianne Kirschenmann and lives in Aberdeen where he is a Sgt. on the
Police Force. They have two children, Jerome and Troy.

Patricia, born February 6, 1954, graduated in 1972, married Don Caldwell and
lives in Roscoe where they are both employed in the family implement business.
They have one son, Michael.

John was born December 10, 1955 and graduated in 1974.  He lives in Roscoe
where he is also a member of the family implement firm.

In 1963, Erv bought his brother-in-laws' interest in the business and Erv and
Helen became the sole owners.

Erv and Helen have been very active in civic activities. Erv has served on the
Roscoe School Board for seven years, is a member of the Roscoe American Legion,
Rod and Gun Club, Roscoe Cemetery Board, Volunteer Fire Department and the
Faulkton VFW.  They are both members of the Roscoe Commercial Club where Erv has
served as chairman.  Helen belongs to the American Legion Auxiliary.  They are
members of St. Paul's Lutheran Church in Roscoe.

LELAND AND JANE STERN

        Leland, Leonard, Jane, Melody, Laurie and Kelly Stern

JOHN AND REGINA STREIFEL

John Streifel was born March 10, 1902 at Zeeland, N.D., the son of Matt and
Theresa Marquart Streifel. Matt was born October 4, 1877 at Zeeland and died
November 18, 1953.  Theresa was born January 12, 1881 in Kandel, Russia and
came to Hague, N.D. in 1885.  They are both buried in Zeeland.

Regina Schmidt was born July 2, 1902, at Odessa, Russia to Paul and Caroline
Daness Schmidt.  She came to Roscoe when she was five years old.  Both her
parents were born in Germany in 1866 and came to Roscoe in 1907 from Russia.

John and Regina were married November 25, 1923. During the first years of their
marriage, they lived in the Zeeland area.  Later they moved to Aberdeen, S.D.,
and then to Roscoe where they have lived most of their lives.  John worked for
the Milwaukee Railroad.

They had ten children, five of whom are living.

Joseph was born October 6, 1924, married Elaine Ronkar, is a meat cutter in
Seattle, Wash. and has two sons, John and Richard.

George was born March 19, 1926, married Marie Hlebaina and is a school
custodian in Huron, S.D. They have one son, Andrew.

Elizabeth (Betty) married to Ralph Preszler, was born April 13, 1930, is a
beautician and farm wife and lives northwest of Roscoe.  They have four
children, Ronald, Gregory, Debra Kay and Mark.

Goldie Doris was born March 29, 1938, married Richard Betzold, is a homemaker
in Fort Morgan, Colo, and has four children, Brenda, Mike, Becky and Marcy.

Anna Marie was born March 9, 1945, married Glen Wollman, is a homemaker and
helps with her husband's bee business at Sioux Falls, S.D. They have three
children, Jackie, Rocky and Steven.

The Streifels belong to St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.

T. P. AND THORA THOMPSON

Thora Sivesand March 29, 1915 in Redfield, S.D. Thora was born October 26, 1891
at Decorah, Iowa, the daughter of Ole and Natalie Vic Sivesand.

HENRY O. AND LYDIA TREFZ

        Back row: Charles, Jon, Harley, Mork, and David; front row: Judith,
        Henry, Lydia, and Carol Trefz.

Henry Trefz was born August 10, 1917 at Tolstoy, S.D., the son of Fred and Rose
Lippert Trefz.  He grew up and attended schools in and around Tolstoy.  He
worked for Charles Corliss ranch from 1933 to the fall of 1935 when he joined
the CCC's for six months.  When Henry returned, he worked for the Corliss ranch
again for 18 months.  In 1937, he started to work for Alvin Reuer in Bowdle,
S.D. as a mechanic in his garage.  In 1939 Henry moved to Tolstoy and started a
shop of his own.

Henry was married to Lydia Heil April 15, 1939.  She was born April 13, 1917 at
Onaka, S.D., the daughter of Jacob and Margaret Bauer Heil of Loyalton, S.D.

In the fall of 1939, they moved back to Bowdle and he worked for Alvin Reuer
again until the fall of 1940 when they moved to Aberdeen.  There he worked as a
mechanic for Nole Colman until the spring of 1941 when they moved to Mound
City, S.D. where he was a mechanic for Diebert Brothers for eight years.  He had
his own shop for three years.  Henry sold out to the Diebert Brothers and
worked for Ruben Metzinger in the Chevy Garage at which time lie also operated
the Trefz Hardware Store in Mound City for two years.  They came to Roscoe in
the spring of 1955 and he worked as a mechanic for Martin Scherf and the Bieber
Brothers for eight years.  He then took over the Standard Station on the
highway and in 1967 purchased what was the former Lammle Garage and operated
that until 1975, when after back surgery and a second heart attack, he had to
sell out.  After recuperating, he went to work for Ervin Spitzer, John Deere
Co. and worked until August 1981.  He is now semi-retired and hopes to keep on
living in Roscoe.

They have eight children, Harley, a graduate of NSC, married Gloria Laib, and
they have one daughter, Stephanie.  At present he is married to Jaci Johnson
and lives in Jamestown, N.D. He is the Director of Education at the State
Hospital.

Jerome, deceased, was killed in a car accident when he was a senior at NSC.

Carol, attended NSC and married Harley Kirschenmann, son of Ruben and Mathilda
Kirschenmann. They have two sons, Robb and Todd and live in Idaho Falls, Idaho.

David, a graduate of NSC, was married to Margaret Bauman, daughter of Mr. and
Mrs. Gerhardt Bauman of Hosmer, S.D. They have a daughter, Rachel and a son,
Mathew.  David is a science teacher at Webster, S.D.

Judy, a graduate of the University of Colorado, married LeRoy Opp, son of
Frieda Opp of Eureka, S.D. and they have two sons, Jeffry and Nicholas.  She is
presently married to Drew Featherstone.  She is a supervisor of "Excellsiors" a
private school for girls at Denver, Colorado.

Charles, a graduate of the University of South Dakota at Vermillion and he
interned in Rapid City, S.D. He is married to Vicki Adams of Sioux City, Iowa.
He is a Medical Technician at the Sioux Falls Veterans Hospital, Sioux Falls,
S.D.

Jon attended NSC and works as a welder for Magic Manufacturing Company in
Vermillion, S.D.

Mark enlisted in the service for three years.  He married Barbara Klein,
daughter of Mr. and Mrs. John Klein of Ipswich, S.D. After returning from duty
in Germany, he moved to Colorado and is Assistant Manager of Diversified
Manufacturing Company of Denver, Colo.

Christel, Leland, Tracy, and Jeremy Treichel
